choice of inversion convention.

For completeness, all remaining local factors are one.  Away from
\(2p\), both entries are units and the tame Hilbert symbol is trivial.
At a prime above \(p\), \(K/\mathbb Q\) is unramified locally and both
entries come from that unramified subfield.  Their Teichmuller parts
are \(p\)-th powers; their principal parts in \(E\) lie in
\(U_{p-1}=1+p\mathcal O_E\), and the standard unit-filtration
orthogonality gives
\((U_{p-1},U_{p-1})_p=1\), since \(2p-2>p\).  The archimedean places
are complex.  Global reciprocity therefore reduces to the already
trivial products \((\mathsf{RUB}_4)\), one for each prime of \(K\) above
two.  Moreover \((\rho)=1\), so ideal-theoretic Stickelberger or
Jacobi-sum factorizations alone retain no datum that can distinguish
the selected value.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[every unweighted base-field selector cancels]
\label{rem:base-selector-cancel}
The cancellation in \((\mathsf{RUB}_4)\) is not peculiar to the second
argument \(2\).  Retain \(K=\mathbb Q(\mu_N)\), \(E=K(\mu_p)\), and the
selected prime \(\mathfrak q\mid2\).  Label its \(p-1\) lifts
\(\mathfrak P_c\), \(c\in\mathbb F_p^\times\), so that the
automorphism carrying \(\mathfrak P_1\) to \(\mathfrak P_c\) sends
\(\zeta_p\) to \(\zeta_p^c\).  For every \(w\in K^\times\), if
\[
 R_w=(\rho,w)_{\mathfrak P_1,p},
\]
then Galois equivariance gives
\[
 (\rho,w)_{\mathfrak P_c,p}=R_w^c,
 \qquad
 \prod_{c\in\mathbb F_p^\times}
 (\rho,w)_{\mathfrak P_c,p}
 =R_w^{p(p-1)/2}=1.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{RUB}_5\)}
\]
Thus no unweighted selector from the base cyclotomic field can make
global reciprocity isolate one chosen lift above two.  A weighted
eigenspace identity or a Jacobi-sum selector outside \(K\) may evade
\((\mathsf{RUB}_5)\), but ideal factorization alone then leaves local
unit symbols to evaluate.  The same cancellation applies to the
exceptional arm with the current prime \(\ell\) in place of \(p\):
since \(\ell\nmid15h\) and \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\mid4h\), each of the two
base primes above two splits into \(\ell-1\) lifts and its unweighted
orbit product is one.  Thus merely distinguishing the two base primes
does not distinguish their individual lifts.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Teichmuller-weighted selector reciprocity]
\label{prop:weighted-selector-reciprocity}
Let \(M\) be odd, let \(p\nmid M\) be an odd prime, and put
\[
 K=\mathbb Q(\mu_M),\qquad E=K(\mu_p),\qquad
 f=\ord_p(2),\qquad d=\ord_M(2).
\]
Assume \(f\mid d\).  For each prime \(\mathfrak q\mid2\) of \(K\),
choose \(\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,1}\mid\mathfrak q\) in \(E\), and
write
\[
 \mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,c}
 =\tau_c\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,1},\qquad
 \tau_c(\zeta_p)=\zeta_p^c
 \quad(c\in\mathbb F_p^\times).
\]
Normalize every valuation above two by
\(v_{\mathfrak P}(2)=1\).  For a global unit
\(u\in\mathcal O_K^\times\), set
\[
 A_{\mathfrak q}=(u,2)_{\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,1},p},\qquad
 W_{\mathfrak q}(w)
 =\sum_{c\in\mathbb F_p^\times}
 c\,v_{\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,c}}(w)
 \in\mathbb F_p.
\]
Then, for every \(w\in E^\times\),
\[
 \prod_{c\in\mathbb F_p^\times}
 (u,w)_{\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,c},p}
 =A_{\mathfrak q}^{W_{\mathfrak q}(w)}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{WS}_1\)}
\]
If the divisor of \(w\) is supported above two, global reciprocity
becomes the exact weighted identity
\[
 \prod_{\mathfrak q\mid2}
 A_{\mathfrak q}^{W_{\mathfrak q}(w)}
 =\prod_{\lambda\mid p}(u,w)_{\lambda,p}^{-1}.        \tag{\(\mathsf{WS}_2\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 W_{\mathfrak q}(\tau_e w)=eW_{\mathfrak q}(w),       \tag{\(\mathsf{WS}_3\)}
\]
and
\[
 W_{\mathfrak q}(w)=0
 \quad\hbox{if }w\in K^\times
 \quad\hbox{or }w\in\mathbb Q(\mu_p)^\times.        \tag{\(\mathsf{WS}_4\)}
\]
Thus a selector which isolates a chosen lift above two must be
genuinely mixed between the \(M\)- and \(p\)-cyclotomic directions.
Even then, \((\mathsf{WS}_2)\) retains the local factors above \(p\):
they are not determined by the ideal-theoretic weights.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The completion \(K_{\mathfrak q}\) has residue degree \(d\), so
\(f\mid d\) implies \(\mu_p\subset K_{\mathfrak q}\).  Hence
\(\mathfrak q\) splits completely in \(E/K\).  At a place above two,
the tame \(p\)-Hilbert symbol of two local units is one.  Since \(u\)
is a unit, bilinearity therefore gives
\[
 (u,w)_{\mathfrak P,p}
 =(u,2)_{\mathfrak P,p}^{v_{\mathfrak P}(w)}.
\]
Galois equivariance gives
\[
 (u,2)_{\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,c},p}
 =\tau_c(A_{\mathfrak q})=A_{\mathfrak q}^{c},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{WS}_1)\).  If \((w)\) is supported above two,
then away from \(2p\) both entries are units and their tame symbol is
one; the archimedean factors are also one.  The global product formula
therefore gives \((\mathsf{WS}_2)\).  Notice that no triviality is
asserted at the wild places above \(p\).

Reindexing the valuations proves \((\mathsf{WS}_3)\).  If
\(w\in K^\times\), its valuations are constant in \(c\), and
\(\sum_{c\in\mathbb F_p^\times}c=0\).  If
\(w\in\mathbb Q(\mu_p)^\times\), the restrictions of
\(\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,c}\) and
\(\mathfrak P_{\mathfrak q,c2^j}\) to \(\mathbb Q(\mu_p)\) agree
for \(0\leq j<f\).  The valuations are therefore constant on cosets
of \(\langle2\rangle\subset\mathbb F_p^\times\), while each weighted
coset sum is
\[
 c\sum_{j=0}^{f-1}2^j=c(2^f-1)=0\pmod p.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{WS}_4)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the first mixed Jacobi phase]
\label{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase}
Let \(Q=2^D\), let \(M\) be odd, and let \(p\nmid M\) be an odd prime
such that \(Mp\mid Q-1\).  Choose a generator \(g\in\mathbb F_Q^\times\),
put
\[
 z=g^{(Q-1)/M},\qquad
 \operatorname{ind}_p(g^v)=v\pmod p,
\]
and choose characters, extended by zero at zero, by
\[
 \chi_M(g)=\zeta_M,\qquad \chi_p(g)=\zeta_p.
\]
For \(1\leq j<M\), define
\[
 J_j=\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_Q}
 \chi_M(x)^j\chi_p(1-x).
\]
Fix a place above \(p\) of \(\mathbb Q(\mu_M,\mu_p)\), put
\(\pi=\zeta_p-1\), and let an overline denote reduction at that place
---not complex conjugation.  Then \(J_j\equiv-1\pmod\pi\), and
\[
 C_j:=\overline{\frac{J_j+1}{\pi}}
 =\sum_{a=1}^{M-1}
 \overline{\zeta_M}^{ja}\operatorname{ind}_p(1-z^a). \tag{\(\mathsf{MJ}_1\)}
\]
With \(J_0=-1\) and \(C_0=0\), Fourier inversion gives
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_p(1-z^a)
 =M^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
 \overline{\zeta_M}^{-ja}C_j
 \qquad(1\leq a<M).                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{MJ}_2\)}
\]
Consequently the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item every \(1-z^a\), \(1\leq a<M\), is a \(p\)-th power in
      \(\mathbb F_Q^\times\);
\item \(C_j=0\) for every \(j\);
\item \(J_j\equiv-1\pmod{\pi^2}\) for every \(1\leq j<M\).
\end{enumerate}
For prime \(M\), the first-order equivalence is the \(k=2\) layer of
Arul's Jacobi congruence~\cite[Theorem~1.5]{Arul21}; the elementary
argument here also permits composite \(M\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Modulo \(\pi^2\), one has
\(\zeta_p^v=(1+\pi)^v\equiv1+v\pi\).  The constant term of \(J_j\)
for \(j\ne0\) is
\[
 \sum_{x\in\mathbb F_Q^\times\setminus\{1\}}\chi_M(x)^j=-1,
\]
while the trivial-character identity gives \(J_0=-1\) exactly.
The coefficient of \(\pi\) is
\[
 \sum_{v=1}^{Q-2}
 \zeta_M^{jv}\operatorname{ind}_p(1-g^v).
\]
Partition \(v=a+tM\), and put \(N=(Q-1)/M\).  For \(a\ne0\),
\[
 \prod_{t=0}^{N-1}(1-g^{a+tM})=1-g^{aN}=1-z^a,
\]
so the sum of the indices on that block is
\(\operatorname{ind}_p(1-z^a)\).  In the \(a=0\) block the omitted
zero factor leaves
\[
 \prod_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-g^{tM})=N=1\quad\hbox{in characteristic two},
\]
because \(N\) is odd; its index is zero.  This proves
\((\mathsf{MJ}_1)\).  The Fourier matrix is invertible because
\(p\nmid M\), giving \((\mathsf{MJ}_2)\).  Finally,
\(\operatorname{ind}_p(y)=0\) exactly when \(y\) is a \(p\)-th power
in the cyclic group \(\mathbb F_Q^\times\), which proves the three
equivalences.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[ordinary target as one inverse-Fourier coordinate]
\label{cor:ordinary-mixed-jacobi}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:singleton-cyclotomic-kummer}.  Put
\[
 M=N_a,\qquad Q=2^{r^ab},\qquad z=x_a,
\]
and let \(p\ne r\) divide \(\Phi_{r^a}(2^b)\).  Choose \(g\) in the
preceding proposition so that \(g^{(Q-1)/M}=z\), and form its mixed
Jacobi sums \(J_j\) and coefficients \(C_j\).  Then the ordinary
condition at \(p\) is exactly
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_p(y_a)
 =M^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
 \overline{\zeta_M}^{-j}C_j\ne0.                      \tag{\(\mathsf{OMJ}\)}
\]
Indeed \(y_a=1-z\) in characteristic two, and
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_p(\bar\rho)
 =(q_0-1)\operatorname{ind}_p(y_a),
\]
where \(q_0-1\ne0\pmod p\).  Thus mixed Jacobi sums evade the zero
weight in \((\mathsf{WS}_4)\), but their required first local phase is
exactly the original selected Kummer coordinate.  Formula
\((\mathsf{WS}_2)\) relates its ideal weights to the still-essential
local symbols above \(p\); it does not itself prove nonvanishing.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[full-conductor Mirimanoff--ray reciprocity]
\label{prop:ordinary-mirimanoff-ray}
Retain Propositions~\ref{prop:singleton-relative-unit}
and~\ref{cor:ordinary-mixed-jacobi}.  Thus
\[
 N=r^a\ord(\alpha_r),\qquad
 q_0=2^{r^{a-1}b},\qquad
 \rho=\frac{1-\zeta_N^{q_0}}{1-\zeta_N},
 \qquad E=\Q(\mu_N,\mu_p),
\]
where \(p\ne r\) is a current prime, and the Jacobi specialization is
\[
 M=N,\qquad Q=2^{r^ab},\qquad z=x_a.
\]
Let \(\mathfrak q_{\rm C}\mid2\) be the Conway-selected prime of
\(\Q(\mu_N)\), characterized by \(\zeta_N\mapsto
x_a=\kappa_{r^a}\).  Choose a generator \(g\in\F_Q^\times\) with
\(g^{(Q-1)/N}=x_a\), and choose the lift
\(\mathfrak P_0\mid\mathfrak q_{\rm C}\) to \(E\) for which
\(\overline{\zeta_p}=g^{(Q-1)/p}\).  Then
\(\mathfrak q_{\rm C}\) restricts to the actual lower ancestor:
\[
 \zeta_N^{r^a}\longmapsto x_a^{r^a}=\alpha_r.          \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_0\)}
\]
In particular this construction uses the full conductor \(N\), not
only the degree-\(r\) Kummer step.
With this compatible normalization of the \(p\)-power residue symbol,
\[
 \left(\frac{\rho}{\mathfrak P_0}\right)_p
 =\zeta_p^{(q_0-1)\operatorname{ind}_p(1+x_a)},         \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_0'\)}
\]
and \(q_0-1\ne0\pmod p\).  Thus this symbol retains the full
\(p\)-primary ordinary target.

Let \(\lambda\mid p\), put \(f=\ord_N(p)\), and let
\(E_\lambda^0\) be the maximal unramified subfield of \(E_\lambda\),
with residue field \(k_\lambda\).  The current-prime condition gives
\[
 p\nmid N,\qquad |k_\lambda|=p^f,\qquad
 E_\lambda=E_\lambda^0(\mu_p),\qquad
 \rho\in(E_\lambda^0)^\times.
\]
Write \(\tau\) for the
Teichmuller image of \(\zeta_N\), and put
\[
 u=\tau^{p^{f-1}},\qquad u^p=\tau,
 \qquad
 \mathcal L_p(T)=\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{T^i}{i}\in\F_p[T].
\]
For \(v\in\mathcal O_{E_\lambda^0}^\times\), define the Fermat quotient
\[
 q_\lambda(v)=
 \overline{\frac{v^{p^f-1}-1}{p}}\in k_\lambda.
\]
Then the full-conductor local regulator is
\[
 \boxed{
 A_\lambda:=q_\lambda(\rho)
 =\frac{\mathcal L_p(u)}{(1-u)^p}
  -\frac{\mathcal L_p(u^{q_0})}{(1-u^{q_0})^p}.}       \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_1\)}
\]
It detects the complete local Kummer class:
\[
 \rho\in(E_\lambda^\times)^p\iff A_\lambda=0.         \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_2\)}
\]
If \(A_\lambda\ne0\), the character
\(w\mapsto(\rho,w)_{\lambda,p}\) has conductor exactly
\(\lambda^2\); if \(A_\lambda=0\), it is locally trivial.  Hence
\(E(\sqrt[p]{\rho})/E\) is unramified away from \(p\), with exact
finite conductor
\[
 \mathfrak m_\rho=\prod_{\substack{\lambda\mid p\\A_\lambda\ne0}}
 \lambda^2.                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_3\)}
\]

Fix \(\pi=\zeta_p-1\).  For each \(\lambda\mid p\) there is a
nonzero normalization scalar \(c_\lambda\in k_\lambda^\times\), fixed
by the Hilbert-symbol convention, such that a local unit
\[
 w=[\overline w]
   \bigl(1+\pi B_\lambda(w)+O(\pi^2)\bigr)
\]
satisfies Iwasawa's critical-pairing formula
\[
 (\rho,w)_{\lambda,p}
 =\zeta_p^{\operatorname{Tr}_{k_\lambda/\F_p}
       (c_\lambda A_\lambda B_\lambda(w))}.            \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_4\)}
\]
Normalize local reciprocity so a uniformizer maps to arithmetic
Frobenius.  If \(w\in E^\times\) has divisor
\[
 (w)=\prod_{\mathfrak P\mid2}\mathfrak P^{n_\mathfrak P},
 \qquad
 \left(\frac{\rho}{\mathfrak P}\right)_p
 =\zeta_p^{\ell_\mathfrak P},
\]
then global reciprocity is the exact bilinear identity
\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{\mathfrak P\mid2}n_\mathfrak P\ell_\mathfrak P
 =-\sum_{\lambda\mid p}
   \operatorname{Tr}_{k_\lambda/\F_p}
      (c_\lambda A_\lambda B_\lambda(w))\pmod p.}     \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_5\)}
\]
For the specialized mixed Jacobi sums of
Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase}, write
\[
 C_{j,\lambda}
 =\overline{\frac{J_j+1}{\pi}}
\]
at \(\lambda\), where \(1\leq j<N\).  The choice \(w=-J_j\) has
\(B_\lambda(w)=-C_{j,\lambda}\), so
\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{\mathfrak P\mid2}v_\mathfrak P(J_j)\ell_\mathfrak P
 =\sum_{\lambda\mid p}
   \operatorname{Tr}_{k_\lambda/\F_p}
      (c_\lambda A_\lambda C_{j,\lambda})\pmod p.}    \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_6\)}
\]
Thus Stickelberger's explicit valuation vector and the first Jacobi
coefficient meet in one exact ray-class formula, but neither side has
disappeared.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The restriction statement \((\mathsf{MR}_0)\) follows from the exact
order \(N\) of \(x_a\) and the defining Kummer ancestry.  It also shows
that replacing \(N\) by a generic conductor changes the residue of
\(\tau\), and therefore can change the regulator in
\((\mathsf{MR}_1)\).  Formula \((\mathsf{MR}_0')\) is
\((\mathsf{RU}_2)\), with the chosen generator of \(\mu_p\);
\(q_0-1\ne0\) follows because \(q_0\) has exact order \(r>1\) modulo
\(p\).

Put \(m=p^f-1\).  Expanding
\((1-\tau)^{p^f}-(1-\tau)\) modulo \(p^2\), the only interior binomial
coefficients of \(p\)-valuation one occur at exponents
\(ip^{f-1}\), \(1\leq i<p\), and
\[
 \frac1p\binom{p^f}{ip^{f-1}}
 \equiv\frac{(-1)^{i-1}}i\pmod p.
\]
After including the sign from \((-\tau)^{ip^{f-1}}\) and dividing by
\(1-\tau\), this gives
\[
 q_\lambda(1-\tau)
 =-\frac{\mathcal L_p(u)}{(1-u)^p}.
\]
The congruence
\((vw)^m-1\equiv(v^m-1)+(w^m-1)\pmod{p^2}\) makes
\(q_\lambda\) additive on products.  Applying it to the quotient
defining \(\rho\) proves \((\mathsf{MR}_1)\).

Let \(\widetilde A_\lambda\in\mathcal O_{E_\lambda^0}\) be the
Teichmuller lift of \(A_\lambda\).  The Teichmuller decomposition has
the sharper form
\[
 \rho=[\overline\rho]
      \bigl(1-p\widetilde A_\lambda+O(p^2)\bigr).      \tag{\(\mathsf{MR}_7\)}
\]
Its Teichmuller factor is a \(p\)-th power.  In
\(E_\lambda=E_\lambda^0(\mu_p)\), the absolute ramification index is
\(p-1\).  If \(A_\lambda\ne0\), the principal factor in
\((\mathsf{MR}_7)\) has nonzero class in the critical graded piece
\(U_{p-1}/U_p\), so it is not a \(p\)-th power.  If
\(A_\lambda=0\), it lies in \(U_{2p-2}\), which is the \(p\)-th power
of \(U_{p-1}\) by the \(p\)-adic logarithm.  This proves
\((\mathsf{MR}_2)\).  The critical Kummer pairing is perfect between
the displayed graded piece and \(U_1/U_2\), and is trivial on \(U_2\).
This gives the exact conductor dichotomy and the local formula
\((\mathsf{MR}_4)\); it is the standard explicit norm-residue formula
of Iwasawa~\cite{Iwasawa68}.  Since \(\rho\) is a global unit and
\(p\) is invertible away from the primes above \(p\), the Kummer
extension is unramified at every other finite place, proving
\((\mathsf{MR}_3)\).

At a place \(\mathfrak P\mid2\), bilinearity and the tame unit formula
give
\[
 (\rho,w)_{\mathfrak P,p}
 =\left(\frac\rho{\mathfrak P}\right)_p^{n_\mathfrak P}.
\]
At every place away from \(2p\), both entries are units and the tame
symbol is one.  The global product formula, followed by
\((\mathsf{MR}_4)\), proves \((\mathsf{MR}_5)\).

Finally \(\chi_N^j\), \(\chi_p\), and their product are all nontrivial
for \(1\leq j<N\), because \((N,p)=1\).  Thus \(J_j\) is integral and
the standard identity
\[
 J_j\overline{J_j}=Q=2^{r^ab}
\]
(with complex conjugation in this display) shows that its divisor is
supported only above two.  Also
\(J_j=-1+\pi C_{j,\lambda}+O(\pi^2)\).  Hence
\(-J_j=1-\pi C_{j,\lambda}+O(\pi^2)\), so
\(B_\lambda(-J_j)=-C_{j,\lambda}\).  The two minus signs in
\((\mathsf{MR}_5)\) cancel and give \((\mathsf{MR}_6)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the remaining ordinary ray coordinate]
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-mirimanoff-ray} makes the obstruction
conductor-sensitive and explicit, but does not prove its nonvanishing.
Stickelberger determines the valuations on the left of
\((\mathsf{MR}_6)\), not the \(p\)-adic unit coefficients on the
right.  Mixed Jacobi sums supply those coefficients, but their inverse
Fourier transform, at the compatible \(p\)-adic place and generator
used in \((\mathsf{OMJ})\), is exactly
\(\operatorname{ind}_p(1+x_a)\), the original selected coordinate.
If every \(A_\lambda\) vanishes, the Kummer extension is everywhere
unramified; it is then either trivial or, if nontrivial, defines a
\(p\)-class-group character.  If some \(A_\lambda\) is nonzero, reciprocity gives a
conductor-\(p^2\) ray equation but still requires the selected Jacobi
phase.  Rohrlich reduced the ramified Jacobi local component to an
explicit Hilbert-symbol coefficient, and Coleman--McCallum subsequently
computed the odd-prime local components and conductors
\cite{Rohrlich86,ColemanMcCallum88}.  Those formulas solve Rohrlich's
stated local-component problem, but do not evaluate the separate
Conway-selected regulator \((\mathsf{MR}_1)\): applying them here still
requires isolating the compatible prime above two, equivalently the
missing mixed ray-class coordinate.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Unconditional projective order bounds}

\begin{proposition}[unconditional nontrivial projective order]
\label{prop:singleton-projective-nontrivial}
Let
\[
 d_{r,a}=\ord(y_aF_{a-1}^\times)\mid\mathcal L_{r,a}.
\]
Then
\[
 d_{r,a}\notin\{1,r\},\qquad v_r(\mathcal L_{r,a})=1.   \tag{\(\mathsf{PO}_1\)}
\]
Consequently \(d_{r,a}\) has a prime divisor \(p\ne r\) at every
level.  This asserts only \(p\mid d_{r,a}\): when
\(v_p(\mathcal L_{r,a})>1\), it does not by itself give the full
\(p\)-primary equality in \((O)\).  If the chosen divisor satisfies
\(v_p(\mathcal L_{r,a})=1\), however, its instance of \((O)\) follows.
In particular, if \(r\ne3\) and \(\mathcal L_{r,a}/r\) is prime, then
the entire ordinary target \((O)\) at this layer holds.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
If \(d_{r,a}=1\), then \(y_a\), and hence \(x_a=y_a+1\), lies in
\(F_{a-1}\), contrary to \([F_a:F_{a-1}]=r\).  If \(d_{r,a}=r\), then
\(y_a^r=c\in F_{a-1}^\times\).  Since \(y_a\) has degree \(r\) over
\(F_{a-1}\), its minimal polynomial would be \(Y^r-c\).  On the other
hand \(x_a^r=x_{a-1}\) shows that its monic degree-\(r\) minimal
polynomial is
\[
 (Y+1)^r+x_{a-1}.
\]
The coefficient of \(Y^{r-1}\) in the latter is
\(r\equiv1\pmod2\), whereas it is zero in \(Y^r-c\), a contradiction.

Because \(r\mid2^b-1\), also \(q_0\equiv1\pmod r\).  LTE gives
\[
 v_r(\mathcal L_{r,a})
 =v_r(q_0^r-1)-v_r(q_0-1)=v_r(r)=1.
\]
Thus a divisor of \(\mathcal L_{r,a}\) other than \(1\) and \(r\)
has a prime divisor different from \(r\).  The final assertions follow
from \((\mathsf P_4)\), with the stated full-valuation caveat.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[absolute-Frobenius signed-ball order sieve]
\label{prop:singleton-conjugate-ball}
Retain the notation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:singleton-projective-spine}.  Put
\[
 t_r=\ord_r(2),\qquad
 \nu_{r,a}=\frac{r^ab}{t_r},\qquad
 R=\frac{r-1}{2},
\]
and define
\[
 B_{r,a}^{\mathrm{abs},\pm}
 =\sum_{u=0}^{R}\sum_{v=0}^{R}
   [X^uY^v]\!
   \left(1+\frac{X}{1-X}+\frac{Y}{1-Y}\right)^{\nu_{r,a}}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_0\)}
\]
Then
\[
 d_{r,a}=\ord(y_aF_{a-1}^{\times})
 \geq B_{r,a}^{\mathrm{abs},\pm}.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_1\)}
\]
Moreover \(\nu_{r,a}\geq r^a\).  Thus this contains the bound obtained
from the \(r\) relative conjugates, is strictly stronger at every
\(a>1\), and is also strictly stronger at \(a=1\) whenever \(b>t_r\).
More explicitly, if $B_r^{\pm}$ denotes the same coefficient sum as
\((\mathsf{CS}_0)\) with exponent $r$, then
\[
 B_{r,a}^{\mathrm{abs},\pm}
 \geq B_r^{\pm}+2(\nu_{r,a}-r),\qquad
 B_{r,a}^{\mathrm{abs},\pm}
 \geq\binom{\nu_{r,a}}R\binom{\nu_{r,a}-R}R.          \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_1'\)}
\]
The same construction gives a selected line-saturation statement.  Define
\[
 \mathcal C_{r,a}
 =\left\{x_{a-1}^{(2^{t_rj}-1)/r}:0\leq j<\nu_{r,a}\right\}
 \subset F_{a-1}^{\times}.
\]
Then $|\mathcal C_{r,a}|=\nu_{r,a}$, and failure at a primary factor
$p$ forces
\[
 1+cx_a\in(F_a^{\times})^p
 \qquad\text{for every }c\in\mathcal C_{r,a}.          \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_{\mathrm{sat}}\)}
\]

Consequently, if \(p\ne r\) divides \(\mathcal L_{r,a}\) and
\[
 p>\frac{\mathcal L_{r,a}}
          {B_{r,a}^{\mathrm{abs},\pm}},                  \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_2\)}
\]
then the Conway-selected \(p\)-th power-residue symbol is nontrivial and
\[
 v_p(d_{r,a})=v_p(\mathcal L_{r,a}).                     \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_3\)}
\]
Thus every prime factor above the sharper explicit complementary
threshold is proved in the ordinary arm, including its full primary
valuation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(D=r^ab=d(x_a)\), \(t=t_r\), and
\(\nu=\nu_{r,a}=D/t\).  Since \(r^a\mid\ord(x_a)\), one has
\(t\mid D\).  For \(0\leq j<\nu\), put
\[
 n_j=\frac{2^{tj}-1}{r},\qquad
 c_j=x_{a-1}^{n_j}\in F_{a-1}^{\times}.
\]
The equation \(x_a^r=x_{a-1}\) gives the
absolute-Frobenius linearization
\[
 y_a^{2^{tj}}
 =1+x_a^{2^{tj}}
 =1+c_jx_a.                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_4\)}
\]
These \(\nu\) coefficients are pairwise distinct.  Indeed, if
\(c_i=c_j\), then
\(x_a^{2^{ti}}=x_a^{2^{tj}}\).  The absolute Frobenius orbit of
\(x_a\) has exact length \(D\), so \(D\mid t(i-j)\).  Since
\(0\leq i,j<D/t\), this forces \(i=j\).
This proves the cardinality assertion for $\mathcal C_{r,a}$.  If
$y_a=z^p$, then \((\mathsf{CS}_4)\) gives
$1+c_jx_a=(z^{2^{tj}})^p$ for every $j$, proving
\((\mathsf{CS}_{\mathrm{sat}})\).

The relation \(r\mid2^b-1\) gives \(t_r\mid b\).  Hence
\[
 \nu_{r,a}=\frac{r^ab}{t_r}\geq r^a.                \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_5\)}
\]

For \(\mathbf e=(e_0,\ldots,e_{\nu-1})\in\mathbb Z^\nu\), write
\(e_j^+=\max(e_j,0)\) and \(e_j^-=\max(-e_j,0)\), and consider
\[
 \mathcal S_R
 =\left\{\mathbf e\in\mathbb Z^\nu:
          |\mathbf e^+|\leq R,\ |\mathbf e^-|\leq R\right\}.
\]
The single-coordinate generating series
\[
 1+X+X^2+\cdots+Y+Y^2+\cdots
 =1+\frac{X}{1-X}+\frac{Y}{1-Y}
\]
shows that
\(|\mathcal S_R|=B_{r,a}^{\mathrm{abs},\pm}\).

For \(\mathbf e\in\mathcal S_R\), form the nonzero signed product
\[
 Z_{\mathbf e}
 =\prod_{j=0}^{\nu-1}(1+c_jx_a)^{e_j}\in F_a^\times.
\]
Suppose \(Z_{\mathbf e}\) and \(Z_{\mathbf f}\) represent the same class
modulo \(F_{a-1}^{\times}\), and put
\(\mathbf g=\mathbf e-\mathbf f\).  Then
\[
 \prod_j(1+c_jx_a)^{g_j^+}
 =c\prod_j(1+c_jx_a)^{g_j^-}
 \quad\text{for some }c\in F_{a-1}^{\times}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_6\)}
\]
The two polynomial products in \((\mathsf{CS}_6)\) have degrees at most
\[
 |\mathbf g^+|
 \leq|\mathbf e^+|+|\mathbf f^-|
 \leq2R=r-1,
\]
and the same bound holds for \(|\mathbf g^-|\).  Their difference
therefore has degree less than \(r\), whereas \(x_a\) has degree \(r\)
over \(F_{a-1}\); it must be the zero polynomial.  Both constant terms
are one, so \(c=1\).  The normalized linear factors \(1+c_jX\) are
pairwise distinct.  Unique factorization gives
\(\mathbf g^+=\mathbf g^-\), hence \(\mathbf e=\mathbf f\).

Finally, \((\mathsf{CS}_4)\) gives
\[
 [Z_{\mathbf e}]
 =\prod_{j=0}^{\nu-1}[y_a]^{e_j2^{tj}}
 =[y_a]^{\sum_j e_j2^{tj}}.
\]
Thus the \(B_{r,a}^{\mathrm{abs},\pm}\) elements of \(\mathcal S_R\)
give distinct powers of the single class \([y_a]\), proving
\((\mathsf{CS}_1)\).  Embedding the old $r$-coordinate ball and then
adding the two signed unit vectors on each new coordinate proves the
first inequality in \((\mathsf{CS}_1')\).  Taking vectors with exactly
$R$ entries $+1$, exactly $R$ disjoint entries $-1$, and all
other entries zero proves the second.  In particular, strictness when
\(\nu_{r,a}>r\) follows at once, and the preceding inequality for
\(\nu_{r,a}\) gives the two stated sufficient conditions.

If the selected \(p\)-th residue symbol were trivial, then \(y_a\) would
be a \(p\)-th power in \(F_a\).  Since the full \(p\)-part of
\(|F_a^{\times}|\) lies in the projective quotient, this would give
\(d_{r,a}\mid\mathcal L_{r,a}/p\).  Under \((\mathsf{CS}_2)\) that
contradicts \((\mathsf{CS}_1)\).  Nontriviality is equivalent to the full
primary equality by Proposition~\ref{prop:singleton-cyclotomic-kummer},
which proves \((\mathsf{CS}_3)\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Limits of generic criteria}

Thus the singleton-one arm is not an unspecified root search.  It is the
uniform assertion that the explicitly defined transverse norm
$\Theta_{q,s}$ contains the full $p$-primary part for every prime
$p$ of exact order $qs$.  The $C_k$ norms below are the special case
$q=3^k$, $b_r=2$, $s=1$; the $m_{359}$ obstruction is the same square at
$q=179$, $b_r=19580$, $s=1$.
Lenstra's use of Carlitz's primitive-root distribution theorem gives an
effective bound at which \emph{some} finite translate works
\cite{Carlitz53,Lenstra77}; it does not evaluate this fixed first
transverse norm.

There is a sharp reason that this effective argument cannot be replaced,
for arbitrary finite-field elements, by any fixed initial translate
window.

\begin{proposition}[finite-window Kummer no-go]
\label{prop:translate-no-go}
Let $p$ be an odd prime and let

\[
 A=\{b_1,\ldots,b_r\}\subset\overline{\F}_2
\]

be a finite set of distinct elements.  Choose
$k_0=\F_{2^d}$ containing both $A$ and $\mu_p$.  There are
arbitrarily large integers $n$ and elements
$x_n\in\F_{2^{dn}}$ of exact degree $dn$ over $\F_2$ such that

\[
 x_n+b_i\in(\F_{2^{dn}}^\times)^p
 \qquad(1\leq i\leq r).
\]

Consequently no absolute truncation of Lenstra's translate-generation
lemma, nor any argument using only full degree, an order-$p$
character, and a fixed affine translate set, can prove an absolute
bound for arbitrary elements.  This does not decide the distinguished
elements \(\kappa_{f(p)}\): any uniform excess theorem must use their
special Conway-tower identities.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Over $k_0(X)$ form the Kummer extension

\[
 \mathcal K=k_0(X)(Y_1,\ldots,Y_r),
 \qquad Y_i^p=X+b_i.
\]

The classes of the $X+b_i$ are independent in
$\overline{k}_0(X)^\times/
 \overline{k}_0(X)^{\times p}$: in a relation
$\prod_i(X+b_i)^{e_i}=G^p$, $0\leq e_i<p$, the valuation at
$X=-b_i$ forces $e_i=0$.  Thus the smooth projective curve $C$
with function field \(\mathcal K\) is geometrically integral and

\[
 [\mathcal K:k_0(X)]=p^r,
 \qquad \operatorname{Gal}(\mathcal K/k_0(X))
       \cong(\mathbb Z/p\mathbb Z)^r.
\]

The cover $C\to\mathbb P^1_X$ is ramified at the $r$ points
$-b_i$ and at infinity, each with inertia order $p$.  Hence
Riemann--Hurwitz gives

\[
 2g(C)-2
 =-2p^r+(r+1)p^r\left(1-\frac1p\right).
\]

Put $Q=2^d$.  Hasse--Weil, followed by deletion of the finitely many
branch points, supplies

\[
 Q^n+O_{p,r}(Q^{n/2})
\]

unramified $\F_{Q^n}$-points.  Since the map to the $X$-line has
degree $p^r$, the number of resulting $X$-coordinates is at least

\[
 \frac{2^{dn}-O_{p,r}(2^{dn/2})}{p^r}.
\]

The union of all proper subfields of \(\F_{2^{dn}}\) has at most

\[
 \sum_{\substack{e\mid dn\\e<dn}}2^e
 \leq dn\,2^{dn/2}
\]

elements.  For all sufficiently large $n$ the former lower bound is
larger.  Choose an unramified $X$-coordinate $x_n$ outside that
union.  It has exact degree $dn$, and its lifted coordinates satisfy
$x_n+b_i=Y_i^p\ne0$ for every $i$, as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Kummer-eigenline translate no-go]
\label{prop:kummer-eigenline-no-go}
Let \(r\) and \(p\) be distinct odd primes, let \(q\) be a
prime power with
\[
 r\mid q-1,\qquad \ord_p(q)=r,
\]
and put \(K=\mathbb F_{q^r}\).  Fix
\(\zeta\in\mathbb F_q^\times\) of order \(r\), and let
\[
 L_\zeta=\{x\in K:x^q=\zeta x\}.
\]
Every nonzero \(x\in L_\zeta\) has degree \(r\) over \(\mathbb F_q\),
the element \(x^r\) lies in \(\mathbb F_q^\times\), and
\[
 X^r-x^r
\]
is irreducible over \(\mathbb F_q\).  Nevertheless, if
\[
 q-(p-1)(r-1)\sqrt q>p,                              \tag{\(\mathsf{KE}\)}
\]
there is a nonzero \(x\in L_\zeta\) such that
\[
 x+1\in(K^\times)^p.
\]
Thus irreducibility of the Kummer binomial, exact degree, and the
Frobenius eigenrelation still do not force the ordinary singleton
translate to have a nonzero \(p\)-Kummer class.

For every odd prime \(r\), such characteristic-two countermodels occur
over arbitrarily large base fields.  Indeed, choose a prime \(p\) with
\(\ord_p(2)=r\), put \(t=\ord_r(2)\), and take
\[
 q=2^{\,t(1+kr)}\qquad(k\geq0).
\]
Then the two displayed congruence conditions hold, and
\((\mathsf{KE})\) holds for all sufficiently large \(k\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By the normal-basis theorem, the \(q\)-Frobenius on \(K\), viewed as
an \(\mathbb F_q\)-linear operator, is the regular representation of
the cyclic group of order \(r\).  Since
\(\mu_r\subset\mathbb F_q\) and the characteristic does not divide
\(r\), its \(r\) eigenspaces are one-dimensional.  Hence
\(L_\zeta\) is a line.  A nonzero \(v\in L_\zeta\) does not lie in
\(\mathbb F_q\), and, as \(r\) is prime, it has degree \(r\).
Moreover
\[
 (v^r)^q=v^r.
\]
If \(v^r=a^r\) for some \(a\in\mathbb F_q^\times\), then
\(v/a\in\mu_r\subset\mathbb F_q\), a contradiction.  Kummer's
prime-degree criterion therefore makes \(X^r-v^r\) irreducible.
The same argument applies to \(x=cv\) for every
\(c\in\mathbb F_q^\times\).

Let \(\chi:K^\times\to\mu_p\) be a character of exact order \(p\);
its kernel is \((K^\times)^p\).  No element \(1+cv\), with
\(c\in\mathbb F_q\), vanishes.  Character orthogonality gives
\[
\begin{split}
  N&:=\#\{c\in\mathbb F_q:1+cv\in(K^\times)^p\}\\
  &=\frac1p\left(
      q+\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\sum_{c\in\mathbb F_q}
                     \chi^j(1+cv)\right).
\end{split}
\]
The element \(v^{-1}\) has degree \(r\), so Katz's affine-line
estimate~\cite{Katz89} gives, for \(1\leq j<p\),
\[
\left|\sum_{c\in\mathbb F_q}\chi^j(1+cv)\right|
 =\left|\chi^j(v)\sum_{c\in\mathbb F_q}
                         \chi^j(c+v^{-1})\right|
\leq(r-1)\sqrt q.
\]
Consequently
\[
 N\geq\frac{q-(p-1)(r-1)\sqrt q}{p}>1.
\]
The value \(c=0\) accounts for only one solution, so another has
\(c\ne0\); the corresponding \(x=cv\) proves the first assertion.

For the final assertion, Zsigmondy's theorem (whose exponent-six
exception is irrelevant for the odd prime \(r\)) supplies a prime divisor
\(p\) of \(2^r-1\) with \(\ord_p(2)=r\).  Since
\(t=\ord_r(2)\mid r-1\), the exponent \(t(1+kr)\) is divisible by
\(t\) and coprime to \(r\).  Hence \(r\mid q-1\) and
\(\ord_p(q)=r\).  Letting \(k\) grow proves
\((\mathsf{KE})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[tower-faithful common-section countermodels]
\label{prop:ordinary-tower-no-go}
Let \(r\) and \(p\) be distinct odd primes, let \(\nu\geq1\), put
\(R=r^\nu\), and let \(q\) be even with
\[
 r\mid q-1,\qquad \ord_p(q)=R.
\]
Choose any
\[
 A_*\in\F_q^\times\setminus(\F_q^\times)^r,
 \qquad \theta^R=A_*,
\]
in \(K=\F_{q^R}\).  Then \(X^R-A_*\) is irreducible over \(\F_q\).
If
\[
 \mathcal N
 =\#\{c\in\F_q:1+c\theta\in(K^\times)^p\},
\]
then
\[
 \mathcal N\geq
 \frac{q-(p-1)(R-1)\sqrt q}{p}.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_1\)}
\]

For every nonzero scalar counted by \(\mathcal N\), put
\[
 x_\nu=c\theta,\qquad
 x_i=x_\nu^{r^{\nu-i}}\ (0\leq i<\nu),\qquad
 \alpha_c=x_0=c^RA_*.
\]
Then
\[
 x_i^r=x_{i-1},\qquad
 [\F_q(x_i):\F_q]=r^i,                                \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_2\)}
\]
and every binomial \(X^{r^i}-\alpha_c\) is irreducible over \(\F_q\).
If \(M=\ord(\alpha_c)\), then
\[
 v_r(M)=v_r(q-1),\qquad
 \ord(x_i)=r^iM.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_3\)}
\]

Put
\[
 q_0=q^{R/r},\qquad
 L=\frac{q^R-1}{q_0-1},\qquad e=\frac Lp.
\]
The projective class of \(1+x_\nu\) in
\(K^\times/\F_{q_0}^\times\) misses the full \(p\)-primary part of
its order, with no assumption on \(v_p(L)\).  More precisely, for the
section polynomials of Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-binary-sections},
\[
 P_{\alpha_c}(Z)\mid R_{e,d}(Z)
 \quad(1\leq d<R,\ r\nmid d),                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_4\)}
\]
where \(P_{\alpha_c}\) is the minimal polynomial over \(\F_2\).

These countermodels occur with every Conway-like degree constraint.
Fix \(r,\nu\), let \(p\) be a primitive prime divisor of \(2^R-1\),
put \(\tau=\ord_r(2)\), and set
\[
 b=\tau2^j,\qquad q=2^b.
\]
For all sufficiently large \(j\), there is a choice above for which
\(\alpha_c\) has exact absolute degree \(b\).  Then
\[
 [\F_2(x_i):\F_2]=br^i,\qquad
 \ord_{r^iM}(2)=br^i,                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_5\)}
\]
\(r\nmid b\), and every prime divisor of \(b\) is smaller than \(r\).
Thus the full Kummer ancestry, smooth degree, binomial irreducibility,
primary order, Lucas sections, and exact \(p\)-primary bookkeeping all
admit failure.  The remaining distinction is the unscaled Conway
selector \(c=1\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because \(A_*\) is an \(r\)-nonresidue, its order contains the full
\(r\)-primary part of \(q-1\).  The finite-field binomial criterion
therefore makes \(X^R-A_*\) irreducible.  Let
\(\chi:K^\times\to\mu_p\) have exact order \(p\).
Character orthogonality and Katz's affine-line estimate, applied to
the degree-\(R\) element \(\theta^{-1}\), give
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathcal N
 &=\frac1p\left(
      q+\sum_{h=1}^{p-1}\sum_{c\in\F_q}
        \chi^h(1+c\theta)\right),\\
 \left|\sum_{c\in\F_q}\chi^h(1+c\theta)\right|
 &\leq(R-1)\sqrt q.
 \end{split}
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{TN}_1)\).

Multiplication by \(c^R\) does not change the \(r\)-power coset of
\(A_*\), so every \(\alpha_c\) is again an \(r\)-nonresidue with the
full \(r\)-part in its order.  Applying the same binomial criterion to
\(X^{r^i}-\alpha_c\) proves \((\mathsf{TN}_2)\); the usual order
induction proves \((\mathsf{TN}_3)\).

Since \(\ord_p(q_0)=r\), one has \(p\mid L\) and
\(p\nmid q_0-1\).  If \(1+x_\nu=z^p\), then in the cyclic group
\(K^\times/\F_{q_0}^\times\), of order \(L\),
\[
 [1+x_\nu]^e=[z]^L=1.
\]
Thus the full \(p\)-primary valuation of the projective order is
strictly deficient even when \(p^2\mid L\).

Let \(B=x_{\nu-1}\) and \(n=R/r\).  Expanding in the
\(\F_{q_0}\)-basis \(1,x_\nu,\ldots,x_\nu^{r-1}\) gives
\[
 S_{e,k}(B)=0\qquad(1\leq k<r).
\]
Now \(B^n=\alpha_c\), and \(1,B,\ldots,B^{n-1}\) is an \(\F_q\)-basis.
The ancestry expansion
\[
 S_{e,k}(B)
 =\sum_{u=0}^{n-1}B^uR_{e,k+ru}(\alpha_c)
\]
therefore proves every divisibility in \((\mathsf{TN}_4)\).

For the final family, Zsigmondy's theorem supplies \(p\) because
\(R\) is odd.  The choice \(b=\tau2^j\) gives
\(\gcd(b,R)=1\), \(r\mid q-1\), and \(\ord_p(q)=R\).
Take \(A_*\) primitive.  The union of the proper subfields of
\(\F_q\) has at most \(b\sqrt q\) elements, while the map
\(c\mapsto c^RA_*\) has fibres of size at most \(R\).  Hence a counted
nonzero \(c\) with \(\alpha_c\) of exact degree \(b\) exists whenever
\[
 \frac{q-(p-1)(R-1)\sqrt q}{p}>1+Rb\sqrt q,           \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_6\)}
\]
which holds for all sufficiently large \(j\).  Since
\(\F_2(x_i)\) contains \(\F_2(\alpha_c)=\F_q\),
\((\mathsf{TN}_2)\) gives the degree in \((\mathsf{TN}_5)\);
\((\mathsf{TN}_3)\) gives its order statement.  Finally
\(\tau\mid r-1\), so \(r\nmid b\) and every prime divisor of \(b\)
is smaller than \(r\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the common zero locus is large]
\label{rem:ordinary-common-sections}
In Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-tower-no-go}, define
\[
 D_e(Z)=
 \gcd_{\substack{1\leq d<R\\r\nmid d}}R_{e,d}(Z),
 \qquad g_R=\gcd(R,q-1).
\]
Every nonzero scalar counted by \(\mathcal N\) gives the common root
\(\alpha_c=c^RA_*\), and each such value has at most \(g_R\) preimages.
Thus
\[
 \deg D_e\geq
 \left\lceil
 \frac{q-(p-1)(R-1)\sqrt q-p}{pR}
 \right\rceil.                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{TZ}_1\)}
\]
In the binary family of the proposition, let \(M_b(D_e)\) denote the
number of distinct degree-\(b\) irreducible factors.  Excluding the
proper-subfield values in the same count gives
\[
 M_b(D_e)\geq
 \left\lceil
 \max\left\{0,\,
 \frac{q-\bigl((p-1)(R-1)+pRb\bigr)\sqrt q-p}{pRb}
 \right\}
 \right\rceil.                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{TZ}_2\)}
\]
This tends to infinity with \(j\).  Even the exact simultaneous
section pattern is therefore abundant among tower-faithful selectors;
only membership of the actual Conway value in this common zero locus
remains unresolved.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[a fixed-translate minimal-selector countermodel]
\label{prop:ordinary-fixed-translate-countermodel}
Let
\[
 f(T)=T^{20}+T^3+1,\qquad
 F=\F_2[T]/(f),\qquad t=T\bmod f,\qquad M=2^{20}-1.
\]
The polynomial \(f\) is irreducible and \(t\) is primitive in
\(F^\times\).  Let \(K=\F_{16}\subset F\), and put
\[
 A=t^{M/5},\qquad x=t^{M/25},\qquad y=1+x.              \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_1\)}
\]
Then
\[
 \ord(A)=5,\qquad \ord(x)=25,\qquad x^5=A,
 \qquad F=K(x),                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_2\)}
\]
so \(X^5-A\) is irreducible over \(K\).  Nevertheless the literal fixed
translate fails at \(p=41\):
\[
 y^{M/41}=y^{25575}=1.                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_3\)}
\]
Indeed
\[
 \Phi_5(16)=69905=5\cdot11\cdot31\cdot41,\qquad
 e=\frac{\Phi_5(16)}{41}=1705,
\]
and \(y^e\in K\).  Therefore the exact binary-section criterion gives
\[
 S_{1705,k}(A)=0\qquad(1\leq k<5).                     \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_4\)}
\]

The relative-unit and reciprocity data fail simultaneously.  The element
\[
 \rho=y^{15}
\]
has norm one from \(F\) to \(K\) and is a \(41\)-st power.  In the
cyclotomic lift of conductor \(25\), at the prime
\(\mathfrak P\) above two which sends \(\zeta_{25}\) to \(x\), one has
\[
 \left(\frac{1-\zeta_{25}}{\mathfrak P}\right)_{41}=1,
 \qquad
 \left(
  \frac{(1-\zeta_{25}^{16})/(1-\zeta_{25})}{\mathfrak P}
 \right)_{41}=1.                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_5\)}
\]

The model also has the ordinal-minimal selector property.  Let
\(s=t^{M/3}\), of order three.  The fifth powers in \(K\) are
\[
 \{0,1,s,s^2\},\qquad s^2=1+s.
\]
Extend \((1,s,A)\) to an ordered \(\F_2\)-basis of \(K\).  In the induced
binary labeling, the labels \(0,1,2,3\) are precisely the fifth powers
and \(A\) has label \(4\).  Thus \(A\) is literally the least
fifth-power nonresidue even though the fixed translate \(1+x\) fails.

This is not a counterexample to the Conway arm.  Its exact missing datum is
multiplicative ancestry:
\[
 \ord(A)=5,\qquad \ord(\alpha_5)=15.
\]
Accordingly the model has conductor \(25\), whereas the Conway-selected
Kummer step has conductor \(75\).  Hence fixed translate, exact Kummer
degree, simultaneous binary sections, norm-one Hilbert--90, reciprocity,
and ordinal-minimal nonresidue status do not by themselves prove the
ordinary arm; the proof must use the full multiplicative order of the lower
Conway ancestor and its selected prime above two.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
All finite assertions have short polynomial certificates.  In the bit
encoding of \(\F_2[T]\), \(f=\mathtt{0x100009}\).  Rabin's criterion gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 t^{2^{20}}&=t,\\
 R_{10}:=t^{2^{10}}+t&=\mathtt{0x46101},&
 \mathtt{0x237d4}\,f+\mathtt{0x81c75}\,R_{10}&=1,\\
 R_4:=t^{16}+t&=\mathtt{0x10002},&
 \mathtt{0xec7d}\,f+\mathtt{0xec7ca}\,R_4&=1.
 \end{aligned}                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_6\)}
\]
Since the prime divisors of \(20\) are two and five, these two gcd
certificates prove irreducibility.  Moreover
\[
 M=3\cdot5^2\cdot11\cdot31\cdot41,
\]
and the residues \(t^{M/p}\), for \(p=3,5,11,31,41\), are respectively
\[
 \mathtt{0xc5deb},\quad\mathtt{0xff200},\quad
 \mathtt{0x4a5df},\quad\mathtt{0x1e639},\quad
 \mathtt{0xa00ea},
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_7\)}
\]
none equal to one.  Thus \(t\) is primitive.

The orders in \((\mathsf{FT}_2)\) follow immediately.  The element \(A\)
has exact degree \(\ord_5(2)=4\), hence generates the unique \(K=\F_{16}\).
The element \(x\) has degree \(\ord_{25}(2)=20\), so \(F=K(x)\); its
degree-five minimal polynomial is \(X^5-A\).  Direct reduction gives
\[
 x=\mathtt{0xc2073},\qquad y=\mathtt{0xc2072},\qquad
 y^{25575}=1,\qquad y^{1705}=\mathtt{0x85029},
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{FT}_8\)}
\]
and the last element is fixed by the sixteenth-power Frobenius.  This
proves \((\mathsf{FT}_3)\), membership \(y^e\in K\), and hence
\((\mathsf{FT}_4)\) by \((\mathsf{OB}_6)\).

Since \(y\) is a \(41\)-st power, so is \(y^{15}\); telescoping its five
relative conjugates gives norm one.  Reduction of the two cyclotomic
units in \((\mathsf{FT}_5)\) gives \(y\) and \(y^{15}\), respectively,
so both residue symbols are trivial.

Finally \(K^\times\) has order fifteen, and its fifth-power image is the
order-three subgroup generated by \(s\).  The relation \(s^2+s+1=0\)
identifies its four affine elements with the first four binary labels.
Since \(A\) has order five, it does not lie in
\(\F_4=\langle1,s\rangle\); thus \((1,s,A)\) is independent and extends
to the asserted basis.
\end{proof}


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{The selected phase at \(p=359\)}{The selected phase at p=359}}
\label{sec:m359-obstruction}

Set
\[
  d=19{,}580,\qquad E=179d=3{,}504{,}820,\qquad
  B=\F_{2^d},\qquad F=\F_{2^E},\qquad L=\F_{2^{179}}.
\]
For an independent derivation of \(m_{359}=1\), \(f(359)=179\),
$\Qset(f(359))=\Qset(179)=\{179\}$, and the candidate element is
$\beta=\kappa_{179}+1$. To represent $\kappa_{179}$, the tower uses the
lower prime row $f(179)=178$, $\Qset(178)=\{89\}$, $m_{179}=1$.
Thus in the tower model $F=B(x)$ with $x=\kappa_{179}$ and
\[
  x^{179}=\kappa_{89}+1\in B,
\]
because the lower row $m_{179}=1$ has already been certified.

The cheap Kummer norm down the top tower step is not the
$359$-test norm. Indeed,
\[
  \Norm_{F/B}(x+1)=1+(\kappa_{89}+1)=\kappa_{89}
\]
(evaluate the monic polynomial $T^{179}+(\kappa_{89}+1)$ at $T=1$, in
characteristic $2$). But $359\nmid |B^\times|=2^{19580}-1$, since
$\ord_{359}(2)=179$ and $179\nmid19580$. Thus the structurally cheap
norm lands in exactly the field where the $359$-primary obstruction is
invisible.

The norm forced by Proposition~\ref{prop:norm} is the
transverse one
\[
  \Norm_{F/L}(\beta)=\prod_{i=0}^{19579}\Frob^{179i}(\beta)\in L.
\]
Here $\gcd(179,19580)=1$, so $B\cap L=\F_2$ and
$F=BL$. The Frobenius $\Frob^{179}$ preserves $B$ as a set but acts
nontrivially on it; it is not the relative Kummer action
$x\mapsto \zeta x$ over $B$. Consequently the known lower certificates
for $m_{89}$ and $m_{179}$ do not propagate to $m_{359}$ by taking the
easy top-step norm.

The Wieferich caveat is absent at the next two pressure
points: direct modular checks give
$2^{179}\not\equiv1\pmod{359^2}$ and
$2^{359}\not\equiv1\pmod{719^2}$. The order form of
Corollary~\ref{cor:order} is therefore equivalent to the full power
criterion at \(p=359\) and at the first row beyond the table, \(p=719\),
which is evaluated below.


\begin{proposition}[safe-prime trace detector]
\label{prop:safe-prime-trace-detector}
Let \(p=2r+1\) be prime, where \(r\) is an odd prime and
\(\ord_p(2)=r\).  For every \(A\in\mu_p(\F_{2^r})\),
\[
 A\ne1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{2^r}/\F_2}(A+A^{-1})=1.       \tag{\(\mathsf{SPH}_0\)}
\]
Thus every safe-prime instance of the ordinary arm reduces its selected
power-residue obstruction to one absolute trace bit; the proposition
detects that bit but does not evaluate it.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The subgroup \(H=\langle2\rangle\subset\F_p^\times\) has order \(r\),
so it is the quadratic-residue subgroup.  Since \(p=2r+1\equiv3\pmod4\),
the cosets \(H\) and \(-H\) partition \(\F_p^\times\).  If
\(A=\zeta^u\ne1\), its Frobenius exponents and those of \(A^{-1}\)
are respectively \(uH\) and \(-uH\).  Hence, in characteristic two,
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{2^r}/\F_2}(A+A^{-1})
 =\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\zeta^{uj}=1.
\]
For \(A=1\), the traced element is zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the selected phase at \(p=359\)]
\label{prop:m359-safe-phase}
Retain the notation above and put
\[
 \Theta=\Norm_{F/L}(x+1),\qquad
 c=\frac{2^{179}-1}{359},\qquad A=\Theta^c\in\mu_{359}(L).
\]
Then
\[
 2^{19580}\equiv3\pmod{359},\qquad
 v_{359}(2^{179}-1)=1,
\]
and the independent order condition has the exact binary form
\[
 m_{359}=1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad A\ne1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L/\F_2}(A+A^{-1})=1.          \tag{\(\mathsf{SPH}_1\)}
\]

There is also an intrinsic selected-phase resultant description.  Let
$a=x^{179}=\kappa_{89}+1$, and let
$P_a(T)\in\F_2[T]$ be its degree-$19580$ minimal polynomial.  Over
$L$ one has
\[
 P_a(X^{179})=\prod_{j=0}^{178}G_j(X),\qquad
 G_j(X)=\prod_{i=0}^{19579}
       \bigl(X-x^{2^{j+179i}}\bigr),                 \tag{\(\mathsf{SPH}_2\)}
\]
where the $G_j$ are the $179$ Frobenius-conjugate irreducible factors
of degree $19580$.  The common-embedding factor is $G_0$, and
\[
 G_0(1)=\Theta,\qquad
 \prod_{j=0}^{178}G_j(1)=P_a(1)=1.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{SPH}_3\)}
\]
Thus the full resultant symmetrizes the required class away; the only
remaining datum is the selected value in \((\mathsf{SPH}_1)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since $19580\equiv69\pmod{179}$, repeated squaring gives
$2^{69}\equiv3\pmod{359}$, proving the first congruence.  The same
calculation one power higher gives
\[
 2^{179}\equiv10412=1+29\cdot359\pmod{359^2},
\]
so the displayed valuation is one.  Proposition~\ref{prop:norm} now
identifies the $359$-power test on $x+1$ with the corresponding test on
$\Theta\in L^\times$.  Since the $359$-part of $|L^\times|$ has order
exactly $359$, that test is nontrivial exactly when $A\ne1$.

Proposition~\ref{prop:safe-prime-trace-detector}, with
\((p,r)=(359,179)\), proves the final equivalence in
\((\mathsf{SPH}_1)\).

The lower-row condition says that
$a=\kappa_{89}+1$ is not a $179$-th power in $B$, while
$\mu_{179}\subset B$.  The standard prime Kummer criterion therefore
makes $X^{179}-a$ irreducible over $B$; equivalently
$P_a(X^{179})$ is irreducible of degree $179\cdot19580$ over $\F_2$.
Over $L=\F_{2^{179}}$, its roots split
into the $179$ orbits in \((\mathsf{SPH}_2)\), each of length $19580$;
this proves the asserted factorization and irreducibility.  Evaluating
the common-embedding orbit at one gives
\[
 G_0(1)=\prod_{i=0}^{19579}(1+x^{2^{179i}})
       =\Norm_{F/L}(1+x)=\Theta.
\]
Finally, evaluation of the full factorization at one gives $P_a(1)$.
This is $\Norm_{B/\F_2}(1+a)$, up to the irrelevant characteristic-two
sign, and $1+a=\kappa_{89}\ne0$; its norm lies in
$\F_2^\times=\{1\}$.  Hence \((\mathsf{SPH}_3)\) follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the fixed full-conductor polynomial at \(p=359\)]
\label{prop:ordinary-359-full-conductor}
Define polynomials over \(\F_2\) by
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 F_5(Z)&=Z^4+Z+1,\\
 F_{11}(Z)&=F_5\bigl((Z+1)^5\bigr),\\
 F_{89}(Z)&=F_{11}\bigl((Z+1)^{11}\bigr),\\
 F_{179}(Z)&=F_{89}\bigl((Z+1)^{89}\bigr),\\
 \mathcal P_{359}(X)&=F_{179}(X^{179}).
 \end{aligned}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{OFC}_1\)}
\]
The actual Conway dependencies
\[
 \alpha_{179}=\kappa_{89}+1,\qquad
 \alpha_{89}=\kappa_{11}+1,\qquad
 \alpha_{11}=\kappa_5+1,\qquad
 \alpha_5=\kappa_4
\]
and their degree data identify these as the minimal polynomials of
\(\alpha_5,\alpha_{11},\alpha_{89},\alpha_{179},\kappa_{179}\),
respectively.  Their exact degree/term-count pairs are
\[
 (4,3),\quad(20,5),\quad(220,55),\quad(19580,3447),
 \quad(3504820,3447).                               \tag{\(\mathsf{OFC}_2\)}
\]
Thus the actual full-conductor polynomial \(\mathcal P_{359}\) is sparse,
but it retains both the complete ancestry and the mark
\(X\mapsto\kappa_{179}\).

Put \(G=2^{3504820}-1\).  The unresolved selected assertion has the
equivalent exact forms
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 m_{359}=1
 &\iff
 \gcd\!\left(\mathcal P_{359}(X),
              (1+X)^{G/359}+1\right)=1\\
 &\iff \mathcal P_{359}(T^{359}+1)
          \text{ is irreducible over }\F_2\\
 &\iff \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{2^{179}}/\F_2}
       (A+A^{-1})=1,
 \end{aligned}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{OFC}_3\)}
\]
where \(A\) is the selected phase of
Proposition~\ref{prop:m359-safe-phase}.  Under failure the gcd in
\((\mathsf{OFC}_3)\) is \(\mathcal P_{359}\), the composition is
reducible, and the trace is zero.

There is consequently no full-ancestry fixed-mark countermodel to this
test.  Any two roots of the irreducible polynomial \(\mathcal P_{359}\)
give isomorphic marked finite-field realizations, and a field isomorphism
preserves whether \(1+\kappa_{179}\) has a \(359\)-th root.  Once
\((\mathsf{OFC}_1)\) and the mark are retained, the displayed gcd or trace
must actually be evaluated; changing models cannot alter the answer.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-dependency-descent}, applied successively
with \(Q=5,11,89\), gives the first four minimal polynomials in
\((\mathsf{OFC}_1)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-full-conductor}
at the final \(179\)-Kummer step gives
\(\mathcal P_{359}(X)=F_{179}(X^{179})\) and its irreducibility.
The degrees follow multiplicatively.  The term counts are exact finite
polynomial arithmetic; the maintained certificate computes the canonical
little-endian coefficient-bit encoding of \(\mathcal P_{359}\), which has
438103 bytes and SHA--256
\[
 \texttt{7f1d6d5cb36c2c233b5b67282540ef1a238f0b2374b81171e9b726cc2c559978}.
\]

The first equivalence in \((\mathsf{OFC}_3)\) is the irreducible-factor
criterion applied to the selected root \(\kappa_{179}\).  Since
\(\mu_{359}\subset\F_{2^{3504820}}\), the prime Kummer--Capelli criterion
identifies nonvanishing of that Euler test with irreducibility of
\(T^{359}-(1+\kappa_{179})\); taking the norm of its coefficients to
\(\F_2\) gives the displayed composition.  The last equivalence is
Proposition~\ref{prop:m359-safe-phase}.  Finally a field isomorphism carries
\(y^{359}=1+\kappa_{179}\) to the same equation for the image of the marked
root, proving the no-countermodel assertion.

The exact sparse compositions and fingerprints are reproduced by
\path{experiments/ordinary_359_full_conductor.py}.  Lean checks the
first dependency composition and the invariance of marked power status under
a ring equivalence.  The full finite-field dependency chain, degree
identifications, and gcd/trace evaluation remain paper-level; in particular
this proposition packages but does not prove \(m_{359}=1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[a compact factor certificate at the fixed \(p=359\) mark]
\label{prop:ordinary-359-factor-certificate}
Let \(h(Z)\in\F_2[Z]\) be irreducible of degree \(179\), put
\(k=\F_2[Z]/(h)\), and regard \(\mathcal P_{359}\) as a polynomial in
\(k[X]\).  Suppose that a monic polynomial \(g(X)\in k[X]\) satisfies
\[
 \deg g=19580,\qquad g\mid\mathcal P_{359}.
 \tag{\(\mathsf{OFCert}_1\)}
\]
Put
\[
 \theta=g(1),\qquad c=\frac{2^{179}-1}{359}.
\]
Then \(g\) is one of the irreducible degree-\(19580\) factors of
\(\mathcal P_{359}\) over \(k\), and
\[
 m_{359}=1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \theta^c\ne1.
 \tag{\(\mathsf{OFCert}_2\)}
\]
Consequently, an explicit pair \((h,g)\) satisfying
\((\mathsf{OFCert}_1)\) and \(g(1)^c\ne1\) is a compact exact certificate
for the fixed-mark assertion.  Packed coefficient bits for \(g\) require
only \(19581\cdot179=3504999\) bits, or \(438125\) bytes, before framing.
One deterministic coefficient-field choice is
\[
 h(Z)=Z^{179}+Z^4+Z^2+Z+1;
\]
the Rabin equalities \(Z^{2^{179}}\equiv Z\pmod h\) and
\(\gcd(h,Z^2+Z)=1\) certify its irreducibility.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(r=179\), \(d=19580\), and \(F=\F_{2^{rd}}\).  Since
\(\mathcal P_{359}\) is irreducible of degree \(rd\) over \(\F_2\),
the standard finite-field base-change theorem factors it over
\(k=\F_{2^r}\) into exactly \(r\) irreducible factors, each of degree
\(d\).  Thus \((\mathsf{OFCert}_1)\) makes \(g\) one such factor.  If
\(y\) is a root of \(g\), then
\[
 g(1)=\Norm_{F/k}(1+y)
      =(1+y)^{(2^{rd}-1)/(2^r-1)}.
\]
Therefore
\[
 g(1)^c=(1+y)^{(2^{rd}-1)/359}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{OFCert}_3\)}
\]
Every root of \(\mathcal P_{359}\) is an absolute-Frobenius conjugate of
the Conway-marked root \(\kappa_{179}\).  Frobenius preserves the
identity or nonidentity of the right side of
\((\mathsf{OFCert}_3)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-full-conductor}
now identifies its nonidentity with \(m_{359}=1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[a Hilbert-root certificate at the fixed \(p=359\) mark]
\label{prop:ordinary-359-hilbert-certificate}
Put
\[
 h(Z)=Z^{179}+Z^4+Z^2+Z+1,\qquad
 k=\F_2[Z]/(h),
\]
let \(f(A)=F_{179}(A)\) be the degree-\(19580\) polynomial in
\((\mathsf{OFC}_1)\), and set \(E=k[A]/(f)\).  Suppose \(y\in E\)
satisfies
\[
 y^{179}=A,
\]
let \(\widetilde y(A)\in k[A]\) be its unique representative of degree
less than \(19580\), and put
\[
 \theta=\operatorname{Res}_A(f(A),1+\widetilde y(A))
        =\Norm_{E/k}(1+y).
                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{OHC}_1\)}
\]
Then the minimal polynomial \(g\) of \(y\) over \(k\) is an irreducible
degree-\(19580\) factor of
\[
 \mathcal P_{359}(X)=f(X^{179}),
\]
\(\theta=g(1)\), and
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 m_{359}=1
 \iff
 \theta^{(2^{179}-1)/359}\ne1.
 \quad}                                                \tag{\(\mathsf{OHC}_2\)}
\]

The checked-in v1 certificate encodes \(y\) as a \(438103\)-byte
little-endian bitstream.  Bit \(179i+j\) is the coefficient of \(Z^j\)
in the \(A^i\)-coefficient of \(\widetilde y\); its SHA-256 digest is
\[
 \texttt{c62433e428e6b0942c210b2df2543fcff6a9e444b835ceb5390db1c9e433bd9e}.
\]
In the polynomial basis \(1,Z,\ldots,Z^{178}\) of \(k\), exact computation
gives
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \theta
 &=\texttt{0x28494a54795638cf203b2404fe5133e2ae0e13c2ca4e6},\\
 \theta^{(2^{179}-1)/359}
 &=\texttt{0x7f81a92c0c3b04e42a5d3aa423e1bc4d0082a3522e87e}
 \ne1.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{OHC}_3\)}
\end{equation}
The latter element has order \(359\), and the safe-prime trace of it plus
its inverse is one.  Therefore the selected value is
\[
 \boxed{m_{359}=1.}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The polynomial \(h\) is irreducible by its two Rabin residues, which the
maintained verifier checks.  Since
\(\gcd(19580,179)=1\), the standard finite-field base-change theorem shows
that \(f\) remains irreducible over \(k\).  Hence
\([E:k]=19580\).  The identity \(A=y^{179}\) gives
\[
 k(A)\subseteq k(y)\subseteq E=k(A),
\]
so equality holds and \(y\) also has degree \(19580\) over \(k\).
Moreover \(f(y^{179})=f(A)=0\), so its minimal polynomial \(g\) is one
of the degree-\(19580\) factors of \(\mathcal P_{359}\) over \(k\).
The norm identity in \((\mathsf{OHC}_1)\) gives
\(\theta=g(1)\), and
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-factor-certificate} proves
\((\mathsf{OHC}_2)\).

The maintained verifier
\path{experiments/ordinary_359_hilbert_certificate.py} reconstructs \(f\)
from the full selected dependency chain, checks the certificate hash and
\(y^{179}=A\), and in authoritative \texttt{--full} mode recomputes the
\(19579\)-step Euclidean resultant in \((\mathsf{OHC}_1)\).  It then checks
both masks in \((\mathsf{OHC}_3)\), nonidentity, order \(359\), and the
safe-prime trace.  Its default quick mode checks the root and stored phase
arithmetic but explicitly does not recompute the norm, so only full mode
verifies the complete certificate.
\end{proof}

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Source-pinned fixed-mark computation audit.}\par
The independently maintained calculator of Peeters~\cite{Peeters}, at
commit
\texttt{427d0db3d40fdfaf4345}\allowbreak
\texttt{deb14b160a00cf5250a1}, performs this exact
fixed-mark test in the mixed-radix algebra with components
\[
 \{2,4,5,11,89,179\},\qquad
 (1,2,4,20,220,19580,3504820)
\]
as basis strides.  Its defining reductions are
\[
 u_2^2=u_2+1,\quad u_4^2=u_4+u_2,\quad u_5^5=u_4,
 \quad u_{11}^{11}=u_5+1,\quad
 u_{89}^{89}=u_{11}+1,\quad
 u_{179}^{179}=u_{89}+1.
\]
Thus its candidate indices \texttt{[0,19580]} are literally
\(1+\kappa_{179}\), with the complete ancestry of
\((\mathsf{OFC}_1)\).  The code computes the exact exponent
\((2^{3504820}-1)/359\), records \texttt{\{359,1\}}, and reports
\(177379\) seconds for that row.  Its timing control flow records this
nonzero duration only for an uncached calculation.  This pins an executed
exact computation, not merely the copied OEIS integer.

The audited current upstream commit has an empty calculation log, but the
repository history retains substantially stronger evidence.  At historical commit
\[
 \texttt{7a26543f11f5319d04da5402840beecc9e5b35fe}
\]
two separate complete runs start from one
result term and finish all \(3504812\) exponent bits with the same final
support of \(1743227\) terms.  Their stored elapsed times are \(177379\) and
\(177717\) seconds.  The maintained provenance checker
\path{experiments/ordinary_359_source_audit.py} reads those historical git
blobs, verifies their complete-file SHA-256 digests, and checks both endpoint
pairs and both result rows.  Since basis one has support size one, the logged
support size proves nonidentity inside the pinned exact sparse implementation;
the repeat run is a reproducibility check of that implementation, not an
independent backend.

The upstream history itself retains neither the final coefficient vector nor
a factor \(g\) as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-factor-certificate}.  Its component
comparator also ties the (2)- and (4)-components and therefore relies on the
published input order being preserved;
the recorded GNU/Linux run has the stated order, but arbitrary source replay
is not formally portable without pinning it.  This caveat does not affect the
checked-in Hilbert-root certificate of
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-hilbert-certificate}; the historical runs
now provide independent source-pinned corroboration of that local exact
computation.

\begin{proposition}[nested compression of the next carry \(p=719\)]
\label{prop:ordinary-719-nested-compression}
Put
\[
 d=19580,\qquad r=179,\qquad s=359,\qquad p=719,\qquad b=rd,
\]
and retain the selected fields
\[
 a_0=\kappa_{89}+1,\qquad
 B_0=\F_{2^d},\qquad B=B_0(x)=\F_{2^b},\qquad x^r=a_0.
\]
Let
\[
 K=\F_{2^s},\qquad
 C=B_0K=\F_{2^{ds}},\qquad
 E=BK=C(x)=\F_{2^{bs}}.
\]
The relevant degrees are
\[
 [B:B_0]=r,\quad [C:B_0]=s,\quad [E:C]=r,\quad
 [E:B]=s,\quad [C:K]=d.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_1\)}
\]

Write \(\beta=1+x=\alpha_{359}\).  The certified \(p=359\) phase is
\[
 \eta=\beta^{(2^b-1)/s}\ne1.
\]
Consequently \(T^s+\beta\) is irreducible over \(B\).  If
\(y^s=\beta\), then \(B(y)=E\), and every such \(y\) is a relative
Frobenius conjugate of the Conway-marked \(\kappa_{359}\).  Put
\[
 z=\Norm_{E/C}(1+y),\qquad
 \theta=\Norm_{C/K}(z)=\Norm_{E/K}(1+y).
\]
Then the next excess row has the exact tests
\[
 \boxed{
 m_{719}=1
 \iff \theta^{(2^s-1)/p}\ne1
 \iff z^{(2^{ds}-1)/p}\ne1.}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_2\)}
\]

The construction has two exact lower norm shadows.  If
\(w=\Norm_{E/C}(y)\), then
\[
 \boxed{
 w^s=\kappa_{89},\qquad
 w=\kappa_{89}^{e_0}\in B_0,\qquad
 e_0=\frac{r(2^d-1)+1}{s}.}                          \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_3\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \boxed{\Norm_{C/B_0}(z)=a_0=\kappa_{89}+1.}         \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_4\)}
\]
Neither shadow determines the target.  If \(G=2^{ds}-1\), then
\(\mu_p\subset K\subset C\), and for every \(\zeta\in\mu_p\),
\[
 \Norm_{C/B_0}(\zeta)=1,
 \qquad
 (z\zeta)^{G/p}=z^{G/p}\zeta^{G/p}.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_5\)}
\]
Here \(v_p(G)=1\), so \(\zeta\mapsto\zeta^{G/p}\) permutes
\(\mu_p\).  Thus, for every \(z\) in the fixed fibre
\(\Norm_{C/B_0}^{-1}(a_0)\), its \(\mu_p\)-twist orbit contains exactly
one element with trivial \(p\)-phase and \(p-1\) with nontrivial phase.
This is a no-go
for the scalar norm shadows only; it does not vary the selected Conway
factor.

There is nevertheless an exact finite certificate route.  The polynomial
\[
 \mathcal P_{719}^{\mathrm{nest}}(T)
 =(T^s+1)^r+a_0\in B_0[T]                            \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_6\)}
\]
is irreducible of degree \(rs=64261\) over \(B_0\).  Over \(C\) it is
the product of \(s\) irreducible factors of degree \(r\).  For any one
monic factor \(g\),
\[
 z_g=g(1)=\Norm_{C(y_g)/C}(1+y_g)
\]
and
\[
 \boxed{
 m_{719}=1
 \iff g(1)^{(2^{7029220}-1)/719}\ne1.}               \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_7\)}
\]
The factors are \(B_0\)-Galois conjugate, so identity or nonidentity is
independent of the chosen factor.  A naive packed table of all \(180\)
coefficients of \(g\) would occupy \(158157450\) bytes before framing
(the known monic coefficient may of course be omitted).

Thus, before the crossed certificate below is evaluated, the next unsupported
carry is reduced from a direct
degree-\(1258230380\) Euler test to one degree-\(179\) factor and one
Euler test in \(C=\F_{2^{7029220}}\).  The proposition supplies the
equivalence and a concrete exact algorithm, but this proposition alone does
not evaluate \(g(1)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-evaluation}
performs the equivalent smaller crossed-tower test exactly.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The gcds
\[
 \gcd(d,r)=\gcd(d,s)=\gcd(b,s)=1
\]
give \((\mathsf{O719}_1)\).  Since \(\ord_s(2)=r\), one has
\(\mu_s\subset B\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-hilbert-certificate}
identifies the displayed \(\eta\) with the certified nontrivial
\(s\)-residue of \(\beta\).  As \(s\) is prime, the Kummer criterion
makes \(T^s+\beta\) irreducible over \(B\), and gives all assertions
about \(y\).

For completeness, \(y\) also has a Hilbert--90 construction from the
certified phase.  Choose a normal element \(t\) of \(K/\F_2\), let
\(\sigma=\Frob^b\), and set
\[
 v=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}\eta^{-i}\sigma^i(t).
\]
Linear disjointness makes the conjugates of \(t\) \(B\)-linearly
independent, so \(v\ne0\), while \(\sigma(v)=\eta v\).  Hence
\(c=v^s\in B\).  With
\[
 M=\frac{2^b-1}{s},
\]
one has \(c^M=v^{2^b-1}=\eta=\beta^M\).  The direct check
\(2^{179}\not\equiv1\pmod{359^2}\), together with \(s\nmid d\), gives
\(\gcd(s,M)=1\).  If \(se\equiv1\pmod M\), then
\[
 y=(\beta/c)^e v
\]
satisfies \(y^s=\beta\).

The singleton transverse criterion at \(p=719\) gives the first
equivalence in \((\mathsf{O719}_2)\).  Norm transitivity gives
\[
 \theta^{(2^s-1)/p}
 =z^{(2^{ds}-1)/(2^s-1)\cdot(2^s-1)/p}
 =z^{(2^{ds}-1)/p},
\]
which gives the second.  The direct congruence
\[
 2^{359}\equiv1+696\cdot719\pmod{719^2}              \tag{\(\mathsf{O719}_8\)}
\]
shows that this Euler test also has the full \(719\)-primary strength.

For the first shadow,
\[
 w^s=\Norm_{E/C}(y^s)
     =\Norm_{E/C}(1+x)
     =1+a_0=\kappa_{89}.
\]
Because \(r\nmid ds\), \(s\)-powering is bijective on \(C^\times\);
it is also bijective on \(B_0^\times\).  The root is therefore unique
and belongs to \(B_0\).  The congruence \(2^d\equiv3\pmod s\) gives
\[
 se_0=r(2^d-1)+1,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{O719}_3)\).  In the other tower direction,
\[
 \Norm_{E/B}(1+y)=1+y^s=x,
\]
and therefore
\[
 \Norm_{C/B_0}(z)
 =\Norm_{E/B_0}(1+y)
 =\Norm_{B/B_0}(x)=a_0,
\]
proving \((\mathsf{O719}_4)\).

Since \(p\nmid2^d-1\) but \(p\mid2^{ds}-1\), the relative norm exponent
\((2^{ds}-1)/(2^d-1)\) is divisible by \(p\), proving the first identity
in \((\mathsf{O719}_5)\).  Applying the lifting-the-exponent formula to
\((\mathsf{O719}_8)\) gives
\[
 v_p(G)=v_p(2^s-1)+v_p(d)=1.
\]
Thus \(p\nmid G/p\), which proves the permutation and the exact
phase-blindness assertion.

Finally \(x=y^s+1\), so \(y\) is a root of
\(\mathcal P_{719}^{\mathrm{nest}}\).  Since
\([B_0(y):B_0]=rs\), that polynomial is its minimal polynomial.
The standard finite-field base-change theorem, with extension degree
\(s\), splits it over \(C\) into
\(\gcd(rs,s)=s\) irreducible factors of degree \(r\).  For a root
\(y_g\) of any factor \(g\), the monic evaluation formula gives
\[
 g(1)=\Norm_{C(y_g)/C}(1+y_g).
\]
All factors are Frobenius conjugate over \(B_0\), so Frobenius preserves
identity or nonidentity of the Euler phase.  This proves
\((\mathsf{O719}_7)\).

The finite-field factorization, norm shadows, and phase-blindness argument
remain paper-level.  The compact crossed-tower construction below supplies a
smaller exact certificate route, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-evaluation} instantiates its
concrete selected evaluation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[a compact crossed-tower certificate for the fixed
\(p=719\) mark]
\label{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-certificate}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-nested-compression}, and put
\[
 r=179,\quad d=19580,\quad s=359,\quad p=719,
 \quad D=\F_{2^r},\quad K=\F_{2^s}.
\]
Let \(B=D[A]/(f(A))\), where \(f=F_{179}\) has degree \(d\), and retain
the checked \(p=359\) root \(x\in B\) with \(x^r=A\).  Put
\(\beta=1+x\),
\[
 M=\frac{2^{rd}-1}{s},\qquad
 \eta=\beta^M\in\mu_s(D),
\]
where the certificate proves \(\eta\ne1\).  The exact congruences
\[
 M\equiv241\pmod s,\qquad 241^{-1}\equiv216\pmod s
\]
allow us to set \(c=\eta^{216}\).  Then \(c^M=\eta\),
\(c\notin(D^\times)^s\), and
\[
 H:=D[V]/(V^s+c)\cong\F_{2^{rs}}
\]
is a field; write \(v\) for the class of \(V\), and identify the unique
subfield of \(H\) of order \(2^s\) with \(K\).  With compatible embeddings
in an algebraic closure, this is the compositum \(H=DK\).

Suppose that \(a\in B\) and a monic \(f_a(T)\in D[T]\) satisfy
\[
 a^s=\beta/c,\qquad \deg f_a=d,\qquad f_a(a)=0.
                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{O719C}_1\)}
\]
Then \(D(a)=B\), so \(f_a\) is the minimal polynomial of \(a\) over
\(D\).  Put
\[
 W=v^d f_a(v^{-1})\in H.
\]
The fixed marked row has the exact compact test
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 m_{719}=1\iff W^{(2^{rs}-1)/p}\ne1.
 \quad}                                                \tag{\(\mathsf{O719C}_2\)}
\]
Thus an explicit pair \((a,f_a)\) satisfying
\((\mathsf{O719C}_1)\) and the nonidentity in
\((\mathsf{O719C}_2)\) is an exact certificate.  In the fixed polynomial
bases, omitting the known monic coefficient, each of \(a\) and \(f_a\)
occupies exactly \(rd=3504820\) bits, or \(438103\) bytes when separately
byte-aligned; two such files total \(876206\) bytes.  A single continuously
packed concatenation instead occupies \(876205\) bytes.  An optional stored
\(H\)-element occupies \(rs=64261\) bits, or \(8033\) bytes.  All four
payloads \(a,f_a,W\), and the final \(719\)-phase are instantiated below.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The direct checks modulo \(s^2\) give
\(v_s(2^r-1)=1\).  Hence a nontrivial element of \(\mu_s(D)\) is not an
\(s\)-th power, and the prime Kummer criterion gives the field \(H\).
The two displayed congruences give \(c^M=\eta=\beta^M\), hence
\((\beta/c)^M=1\).  Since \(\gcd(s,M)=1\), the \(s\)-th power image in
\(B^\times\) is exactly the subgroup of order \(M\), so an \(a\) in
\((\mathsf{O719C}_1)\) exists.

From \(x=ca^s+1\) and \(A=x^r\) one obtains
\[
 D(A)\subseteq D(a)\subseteq B.
\]
The polynomial \(f\) remains irreducible over \(D\), because its degree
\(d\) is coprime to \(r\).  Thus \([B:D]=d\), the inclusions are
equalities, and the asserted \(f_a\) is the minimal polynomial.

Put \(E=BH=B(DK)=BK\), the same field \(E\) as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-nested-compression}, and let \(y=av\).
Then \(y^s=\beta\), so \(y\) is one of the
marked equation's Frobenius-conjugate roots.  Since \(\gcd(d,s)=1\),
\(f_a\) remains irreducible over \(H\), and in characteristic two
\[
 \Norm_{E/H}(1+y)
 =\prod_{\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(B/D)}(1+v\sigma(a))
 =v^d f_a(v^{-1})=W.                                \tag{\(\mathsf{O719C}_3\)}
\]
Norm transitivity and
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-nested-compression} give
\[
 \Norm_{E/K}(1+y)
 =\Norm_{H/K}(W)
 =W^{(2^{rs}-1)/(2^s-1)}.
\]
Raising to \((2^s-1)/p\) proves \((\mathsf{O719C}_2)\).
Frobenius conjugation preserves identity or nonidentity, so the allowed
choice of \(a\) does not change the test.

Lean kernel-checks the scaled finite-product algebra underlying the last
equality in \((\mathsf{O719C}_3)\) and conditional nested exponent
multiplication.  The field identifications, Kummer construction,
minimal-polynomial and norm specializations, numerical exponent
factorization, and the deduction from the finite certificate remain
paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[checked first crossed payload for the fixed \(p=719\)
mark]
\label{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-root-certificate}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-certificate}.  In its
fixed polynomial bases there is an explicitly stored element \(a\in B\)
satisfying
\[
 a^{359}=\frac{1+x}{c},\qquad ca^{359}+1=x,
 \qquad (ca^{359}+1)^{179}=A.
\]
Its continuously packed little-endian coefficient payload has
\(3504820\) bits, hence \(438103\) bytes, and SHA-256
\[
\texttt{c7385164fedcbb971ff2bb239ef9e0a0d1ccb81b16edd81eee2a139030ba8758}.
\]
Consequently \(D(a)=B\), so the minimal polynomial of \(a\) over \(D\)
has degree \(19580\).  The maintained exact verifier reconstructs \(x\)
and \(c\) from the checked \(p=359\) artifact and verifies the displayed
equations.  Its full-upstream mode additionally recomputes
\(\Norm_{B/D}(1+x)\).

This constructs the first payload required by
\((\mathsf{O719C}_1)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-evaluation}
supplies the remaining payloads and evaluates the final phase.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The finite computation is exactly the maintained artifact verifier described
above.  From \(x=ca^{359}+1\) and \(A=x^{179}\) one gets
\(D(A)\subseteq D(a)\subseteq B\).  Since \(B=D(A)\), equality follows.
The finite computation is not kernel-checked in Lean.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact crossed evaluation at the fixed \(p=719\) mark]
\label{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-evaluation}
Retain Propositions~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-certificate} and
\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-root-certificate}.  There is an explicitly
stored monic polynomial \(f_a\in D[T]\) of degree \(19580\), represented by
its \(19580\) non-leading coefficients, such that \(f_a(a)=0\).  Its
separately byte-aligned coefficient payload has \(438103\) bytes and SHA-256
\[
\texttt{66a4aaa3a406d67bc9aba4ae56c9bc218f65d296d7c68725da6b1417854b92de}.
\]
The exact crossed norm
\[
 W=v^{19580}f_a(v^{-1})\in H
\]
has a \(8033\)-byte payload with SHA-256
\[
\texttt{fc00d40eabdba738d950bf17317e68418d0b8813ba3325c1ce35b1975f9569c6}.
\]
Finally
\[
 \Xi=W^{(2^{64261}-1)/719}\ne1,\qquad \Xi^{719}=1,
                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{O719E}_1\)}
\]
and the \(8033\)-byte payload for \(\Xi\) has SHA-256
\[
\texttt{da5858b2e53bfce7e83944a41e264a0fa01a3f0a1b030b889239afd383708a81}.
\]
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{m_{719}=1.}                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{O719E}_2\)}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The maintained exact verifier checks the hashes and canonical padding of all
four payloads, reconstructs the certified \(p=359\) root and phase, verifies
\(a^{359}=(1+x)/c\), and recomputes \(W\) from the displayed scaled
minimal-polynomial evaluation.  Its authoritative full mode also verifies
\(f_a(a)=0\) by dense modular composition.  It evaluates the Euler exponent
exactly in base \(2^{179}\), using the diagonal Frobenius action in the
Kummer basis, and obtains \((\mathsf{O719E}_1)\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-certificate} then gives
\((\mathsf{O719E}_2)\).

This is a locally replayable exact finite computation plus the paper-level
crossed-tower deduction.  The payload evaluation and finite-field
specialization are not kernel-checked in Lean; the Lean development checks
only the generic scaled-product and nested-exponent algebra used above.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the exact ordinary carry at \(p=727\)]
\label{prop:ordinary-727-certificate}
One has
\[
 \ord_{727}(2)=121=11^2,
 \qquad Q(121)=\{121\},
 \qquad \alpha_{727}=\kappa_{121}+1.
\]
More precisely, the actual selected full-conductor polynomial is
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 P_{\alpha_{11}}(X)
   &=X^{20}+X^{16}+X^5+X+1,\\
 P_{\kappa_{121}}(X)
   &=P_{\alpha_{11}}(X^{121})
     =X^{2420}+X^{1936}+X^{605}+X^{121}+1.
 \end{aligned}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{O727}_1\)}
\]
The second polynomial is irreducible over \(\F_2\).  In its quotient field,
with \(x\) the class of \(X\), exact binary arithmetic gives
\[
 x^{(2^{2420}-1)/727}=1,
 \qquad
 (x+1)^{(2^{2420}-1)/727}\ne1,
 \qquad
 \left((x+1)^{(2^{2420}-1)/727}\right)^{727}=1.
                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{O727}_2\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{m_{727}=1.}                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{O727}_3\)}
\]
The canonical fixed-width SHA--256 fingerprints of the modulus and the
nontrivial phase in the polynomial basis are, respectively,
\[
 \begin{split}
 &\texttt{a10b35b0caf07945af6d71c0f3fccb7b3e67c3f5ae3124e19b5482e960cec852},\\
 &\texttt{1b95861258bcf45a90c284011c3337561996e5cc70ee6e05374b121515103e57}.
 \end{split}
                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{O727}_4\)}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Direct modular arithmetic gives \(2^{121}=1\pmod{727}\) but
\(2^{11}\ne1\pmod{727}\), proving the order assertion.  The selected
dependency \(\alpha_{11}=\kappa_5+1\) and
\(P_{\alpha_5}(T)=T^4+T+1\) give
\[
 P_{\alpha_{11}}(X)
 =P_{\alpha_5}((X+1)^5)
 =X^{20}+X^{16}+X^5+X+1.
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-full-conductor} at the \(11^2\)-Kummer
step gives the second polynomial in \((\mathsf{O727}_1)\) and its
irreducibility.  Independently, the maintained checker verifies the complete
Rabin criterion: \(X^{2^{2420}}=X\) modulo the displayed polynomial and
\[
 \gcd\!\left(P_{\kappa_{121}},
             X^{2^{2420/r}}+X\right)=1
 \qquad(r=2,5,11).
\]
It then evaluates the two exact Euler powers in
\((\mathsf{O727}_2)\).  The first equality proves that translate zero still
has a \(727\)-th root; the nonidentity at translate one proves that translate
one does not.  Hence the least finite translate is one.

The stdlib verifier is
\path{experiments/ordinary_727_certificate.py}.  Lean kernel-checks that
727 is prime, the order arithmetic above, and the selected degree 2420.
The irreducibility and Euler evaluations are exact finite computation, not a
kernel theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[ordinary affine orbit and the marked scalar]
\label{prop:ordinary-affine-marked-scalar}
Let \(Q\) be a power of two, let \(r\) be an odd prime with
\(r\mid Q-1\), and let
\[
 k=\F_Q,\qquad K=\F_{Q^r},\qquad x^r=a\in k^\times,
\]
where \(X^r-a\) is irreducible over \(k\).  Put
\[
 \lambda=x^{Q-1}=a^{(Q-1)/r}\in\mu_r(k).
\]
Then \(\lambda\) has order \(r\), \(x^Q=\lambda x\), and the complete
affine translate orbit has the exact polynomial
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \prod_{c\in k}\bigl(Y+1+cx\bigr)
   =Y^Q+\lambda Y+(1+\lambda).\quad}                 \tag{\(\mathsf{OAM}_1\)}
\]

For every \(u\in k^\times\), the rescaled root \(x_u=ux\) has the same
Frobenius multiplier \(\lambda\), and
\[
 \{1+cx_u:c\in k\}=\{1+cx:c\in k\}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{OAM}_2\)}
\]
Let \(p\mid(Q^r-1)/(Q-1)\), assume \(p\nmid Q-1\), and let
\(\chi:K^\times\to\mu_p\) have exact order \(p\).  Then \(\chi\) is
trivial on \(k^\times\), so the complete multiset of labels
\[
 \{\chi(1+cx):c\in k\}
\]
and every symmetric invariant of it are unchanged by \(x\mapsto ux\).
Their product is one.  But the distinguished value transforms as
\[
 H(x)=\chi(1+x),\qquad H(x_u)=\chi(1+ux),             \tag{\(\mathsf{OAM}_3\)}
\]
so rescaling merely moves which nonzero scalar is called \(1\).

For the actual \((Q,r,p)=(2^{19580},179,359)\), the bound
\((\mathsf{KE})\) holds.  The locally certified equality \(m_{359}=1\) makes
\(H(x)\ne1\), whereas Proposition~\ref{prop:kummer-eigenline-no-go}
supplies a nonzero \(u\in k^\times\) with \(H(x_u)=1\).  By
\((\mathsf{OAM}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{OAM}_2)\), these two marked Kummer lines
have the same affine-orbit polynomial, labelled histogram, and every
symmetric translate statistic; by \((\mathsf{SPH}_0)\), their trace bits
are opposite.  Hence those aggregate data cannot evaluate the bit in
\((\mathsf{SPH}_1)\).  The actual lower multiplicative ancestry fixes the
mark \(c=1\); discarding it restores this rescaling freedom.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Irreducibility implies \(x\ne0\), and
\[
 x^{Q-1}=(x^r)^{(Q-1)/r}=a^{(Q-1)/r}\in\mu_r(k).
\]
Because \(r\) is prime, a smaller order would make \(\lambda=1\), hence
\(x^Q=x\), contrary to \([k(x):k]=r\).  The standard additive-polynomial identity
\(\prod_{c\in k}(Z+c)=Z^Q+Z\), applied to \(Z=(Y+1)/x\), gives
\[
 \prod_{c\in k}(Y+1+cx)
 =(Y+1)^Q+x^{Q-1}(Y+1),
\]
which is \((\mathsf{OAM}_1)\).  Multiplication by \(u\) permutes \(k\),
proving \((\mathsf{OAM}_2)\); it also leaves \(\lambda\) unchanged because
\(u^{Q-1}=1\).

The hypothesis \(p\nmid Q-1\) makes \(\chi\)
trivial on \(k^\times\).  Taking \(\chi\) of the constant term in
\((\mathsf{OAM}_1)\) gives product one because \(1+\lambda\in k^\times\).
The reindexing statement and \((\mathsf{OAM}_3)\) are immediate.  Lean
kernel-checks the denominator-cleared collapse from the two additive-polynomial
terms to the right side of \((\mathsf{OAM}_1)\); the finite-field product
and Kummer specialization remain paper-level.  In the \(359\)-instance,
\(\sqrt Q=2^{9790}>(358)(178)+359\), so \((\mathsf{KE})\) applies; the
certified nontrivial line and the trivial rescaled line then give the
claimed opposite trace bits.
\end{proof}

\emph{Certified datum.} The value \(m_{359}=1\) is present in A380496,
and Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-hilbert-certificate} now
independently evaluates the trace in \((\mathsf{SPH}_1)\) by exact finite
computation.
Quadratic half-orbit norms do not decide it:
$\sum_{h\in H}h=0$ in $\F_{359}$, so their symbol product is identically
one.  Proposition~\ref{prop:kummer-eigenline-no-go} likewise shows why
the safe-prime eigenline data alone cannot select the Conway phase.

\subsection{The ordinary-spine conjecture \texorpdfstring{\((O)\)}{(O)}}

For each odd component prime \(r\ne3\), prove that the selected relative
unit of Proposition~\ref{prop:singleton-relative-unit} has full primary
order at every divisor of \(\mathcal L_{r,a}\).  The signed
absolute-Frobenius signed-ball theorem proves the larger primary factors;
the remaining
smaller factors, including the independent \(p=359\) trace evaluation,
are the exact unresolved core.

\section{\texorpdfstring{The cubic \(3^k\) spine}{The cubic 3-power spine}}\label{sec:family}

Write $\zeta:=\kappa_{3^k}$ and $h:=3^k$ throughout this section.

\begin{conjecture}[\(C_k\): Gaussian-period primitivity]\label{conj:ck}
The period
\[
 \gamma_k=\zeta+\zeta^{-1}\in\F_{2^{3^k}}^\times
\]
is primitive for every \(k\geq1\).
\end{conjecture}

\subsection{Structure}

\begin{proposition}[cyclotomic recognition]\label{prop:cyclo}
$\zeta^{3^k}=\kappa_2$, which has order $3$; hence $\zeta$ is a
primitive $3^{k+1}$-st root of unity. Since $2$ is a primitive root
modulo $3^{k+1}$, the cyclotomic polynomial
$\Phi_{3^{k+1}}(x)=x^{2h}+x^h+1$ is irreducible over $\F_2$, the
component field is $\F_2(\zeta)=\F_{2^{2h}}$, and it carries the index-$2$
subfield $L=\F_{2^h}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[half-angle splitting]\label{prop:halfangle}
With $s=(3^{k+1}+1)/2$ (the inverse of $2$ modulo $3^{k+1}$),
\[
