  \kappa_{3^k}+1=\zeta^{s}\,\bigl(\zeta^{s}+\zeta^{-s}\bigr),
\]
where $\zeta^{s}$ lies in the norm-one circle $U$ (of order exactly
$3^{k+1}$ within the subgroup of order $2^h+1$) and
$(\zeta^{s}+\zeta^{-s})^2=\zeta+\zeta^{-1}=:\gamma_k\in L^{\times}$.
Since $\F_{2^{2h}}^{\times}=L^{\times}\times U$ with coprime orders,
\[
  \ord(\kappa_{3^k}+1)=3^{k+1}\cdot\ord(\gamma_k),
  \qquad
  \ord(\gamma_k)\mid 2^{3^k}-1.
\]
Machine-checked corollaries: $(\kappa+1)^{2^h-1}=\zeta^{-1}$ and
$\Norm_{F/L}(\kappa+1)=\gamma_k$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[translates $m=2,3$]\label{prop:translates}
$\kappa_{3^k}+2$ and $\kappa_{3^k}+3$ split as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:halfangle} with $\gamma_k$ replaced by a Galois
conjugate. Therefore
$\ord(\kappa_{3^k}+m)=3^{k+1}\cdot\ord(\gamma_k)$ for $m=1,2,3$, and
$\ord(\kappa_{3^k})=3^{k+1}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[the $2\cdot3^k$ exception, unconditionally]
\label{prop:exception}
Any prime $p$ with $f(p)=2\cdot3^k$ satisfies
$p\mid 2^{3^k}+1$, hence $p$ divides neither $3^{k+1}$ nor $2^{3^k}-1$,
hence $p$ never divides $\ord(\kappa_{3^k}+m)$ for $m\in\{0,1,2,3\}$. By
the order criterion, $m_p\geq4$. The full primitivity conjecture $C_k$
below is not needed; the splitting alone forces the bound. The argument
applies beyond the current tables, e.g.\ $p=87211$, $f=54$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[the $3$-power column]\label{prop:column}
For a prime $r$ with $f(r)=3^k$, the values $m\in\{0,2,3\}$ are
impossible: $m_r=0$ would require $r\mid\ord(\kappa_{3^k})=3^{k+1}$, a
power of $3$, while $r\equiv1\pmod{3^k}$ is a prime distinct from $3$;
and $m=2,3$ share $m=1$'s order class by
Proposition~\ref{prop:translates}. Any failure of $C_k$ therefore gives
$m_r\geq4$ directly.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[norm tower]\label{prop:tower}
$\gamma_{k-1}=\gamma_k^3+\gamma_k
=\Norm_{L_k/L_{k-1}}(\gamma_k)$ (minimal polynomial
$X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}$), so $\ord(\gamma_{k-1})\mid\ord(\gamma_k)$. With
lifting-the-exponent ($v_r(2^{3^k}-1)=v_r(2^{f(r)}-1)$ for old primes
$r\neq3$), full-order parts propagate upward, and conjecture $C_k$
reduces to $C_{k-1}$ plus primitivity at the new primes
$r\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[the norm-kernel seam]\label{prop:norm-kernel-seam}
Put $q=2^{3^{k-1}}$, so
$L_k=\F_{q^3}$ and $L_{k-1}=\F_q$.  There is a canonical direct product
\[
  L_k^{\times}=L_{k-1}^{\times}\times U_k,
  \qquad |U_k|=q^2+q+1=\Phi_{3^k}(2),
\]
where $U_k=\ker\Norm_{L_k/L_{k-1}}$.  If
$\gamma_k=a_kb_k$ in this product, then
\[
  \gamma_{k-1}=\Norm(\gamma_k)=a_k^3,
\]
and cubing is an automorphism of $L_{k-1}^{\times}$.  Consequently
\[
  C_k\iff C_{k-1}\ \text{and}\ b_k\ \text{generates }U_k.
\]
In particular, the norm recursion determines the old component $a_k$
exactly but supplies no order information about the new component $b_k$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The two displayed factor orders are coprime:
\[
 \gcd(q-1,q^2+q+1)=\gcd(q-1,3)=1,
\]
because $q=2^{\text{odd}}\equiv2\pmod3$.  The cyclic group
$L_k^{\times}$ therefore splits as asserted.  The relative norm is
$z\mapsto z^{1+q+q^2}$; it kills $U_k$ and restricts on
$L_{k-1}^{\times}$ to $a\mapsto a^3$, which is bijective because
$3\nmid q-1$.  The final equivalence follows from coprimality of the two
factor orders.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Equivalent algebraic and cyclotomic forms}

\begin{proposition}[primitive Dickson iterate]\label{prop:dickson-iterate}
Let $D_n(X,1)$ be the Dickson polynomial characterized by
$D_n(u+u^{-1},1)=u^n+u^{-n}$, and put $f(X)=X^3+X$.  Then
\[
 P_k(X):=D_{3^k}(X,1)+1=f^{\circ k}(X)+1
\]
is the minimal polynomial of $\gamma_k$ over $\F_2$.  In particular it
is irreducible of degree $3^k$, and
\[
 C_k\iff P_k(X)\text{ is a primitive polynomial over }\F_2.
\]
The polynomials also obey the coefficient recursion
\[
 P_{k+1}=P_k^3+P_k^2+1,
 \qquad P_1=X^3+X+1.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Dickson composition law gives
$D_{3^k}=D_3^{\circ k}=f^{\circ k}$ in characteristic two.  Since
$\zeta^{3^k}$ is a primitive cube root of unity,
\[
 D_{3^k}(\gamma_k,1)
 =\zeta^{3^k}+\zeta^{-3^k}=1.
\]
On the other hand, the Frobenius orbit of $\zeta+\zeta^{-1}$ has length
$3^k$: the least $j>0$ with $2^j\equiv\pm1\pmod{3^{k+1}}$ is $3^k$.
Thus the displayed monic polynomial, which has degree $3^k$, is the
minimal polynomial.  Its root is primitive precisely when the
polynomial is primitive.  Finally, writing $D_{3^k}=P_k+1$ and using
$D_3(Y)=Y^3+Y$ gives
$P_{k+1}=(P_k+1)^3+(P_k+1)+1=P_k^3+P_k^2+1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the coefficient recursion does not preserve primitivity]
\label{prop:cubic-recursion-no-go}
Over \(\mathbb F_2\), put
\[
 F=X^{10}+X^3+1,
 \qquad
 H=F^3+F^2+1
  =X^{30}+X^{23}+X^{16}+X^{10}+X^9+X^3+1.
\]
Then \(F\) is primitive and \(H\) is irreducible, but a root of \(H\)
has order
\[
 7161=3\cdot7\cdot11\cdot31
 <2^{30}-1.
\]
Thus neither irreducibility of the next polynomial nor primitivity of
the preceding polynomial turns the recursion in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dickson-iterate} into a formal induction.
Additional structure of the actual \(P_k\) sequence---for example its
power-of-three degrees and cyclotomic ancestry---is required.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Rabin's criterion is certified by
\[
 X^{2^{10}}\equiv X\pmod F,
 \qquad
 \gcd(F,X^{2^5}+X)=\gcd(F,X^{2^2}+X)=1.
\]
Moreover \(2^{10}-1=3\cdot11\cdot31\), and direct reduction gives
\[
 X^{1023}\equiv1\pmod F,
\]
while
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 X^{341}&\equiv X^7+X^6+X^5+X^3+X^2+1,\\
 X^{93}&\equiv X^9+X^7+X^6+X^3+1,\\
 X^{33}&\equiv X^9+X^6+X^5+X^3+X^2
 \end{aligned}
 \qquad(\bmod F).
\]
The last three residues are not one, so \(F\) is primitive.

For \(H\), the exact Rabin reductions are
\[
 X^{2^{30}}\equiv X\pmod H,
\]
\[
 \gcd(H,X^{2^{15}}+X)
 =\gcd(H,X^{2^{10}}+X)
 =\gcd(H,X^{2^6}+X)=1,
\]
which prove irreducibility.  Finally
\[
 X^{7161}\equiv1\pmod H,
\]
whereas
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 X^{2387}&\equiv
  X^{26}+X^{23}+X^{22}+X^{16}+X^{15}+X^7+X^6+X^3+1,\\
 X^{1023}&\equiv X^{20}+X^{10}+X^6+X^3+1,\\
 X^{651}&\equiv X^{23}+X^{21}+X^9+X^3,\\
 X^{231}&\equiv
  X^{23}+X^{22}+X^{21}+X^{12}+X^9+X^8+X^7+X^5+X^3+1
 \end{aligned}
 \qquad(\bmod H).
\]
These residues are not one and correspond to division of \(7161\) by its four
prime factors, so the displayed order is exact.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[shifted cyclotomic-unit form]\label{prop:shifted-unit}
Put $x_k=1+\zeta\in\F_{2^{2h}}^\times$, where $h=3^k$ and
$N=3^{k+1}$.  Then
\[
 x_k^{2^h-1}=\zeta^{-1},\qquad
 x_k^{2^h+1}=\gamma_k,\qquad
 \ord(x_k)=N\,\ord(\gamma_k).
\]
Consequently
\[
 C_k\iff \ord(1+\zeta)=3^{k+1}(2^{3^k}-1).
\]
Thus the cubic arm is equivalently maximal order for the reduction at
$2$ of the cyclotomic unit $1+\zeta_{3^{k+1}}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because $2^h\equiv-1\pmod N$,
$x_k^{2^h}=1+\zeta^{-1}=\zeta^{-1}x_k$.  Dividing and multiplying by
$x_k$ gives the first two identities.  The factors $2^h-1$ and
$2^h+1$ are coprime.  In the corresponding direct-product
decomposition of $\F_{2^{2h}}^\times$, the first displayed power has
exact order $N$ and the second has order $\ord(\gamma_k)$, so the order
of $x_k$ is their product.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[norm-coherent cyclotomic lift]\label{prop:unit-lift}
Let $\xi_k=\zeta_{3^{k+1}}$ in characteristic zero and
$u_k=1+\xi_k$.  Then $2$ is inert in $\Q(\xi_k)$ and
\[
 \Norm_{\Q(\xi_k)/\Q(\xi_{k-1})}(u_k)=u_{k-1}.
\]
The norm to the maximal real subfield is
\[
 \Norm_{\Q(\xi_k)/\Q(\xi_k+\xi_k^{-1})}(u_k)
 =2+\xi_k+\xi_k^{-1},
\]
whose reduction modulo the unique prime above $2$ is $\gamma_k$.
Thus Proposition~\ref{prop:shifted-unit} is the residue-field shadow of
a norm-coherent cyclotomic-unit system in the cyclotomic
$\mathbb Z_3$-tower.
Conditional on $C_{k-1}$, $C_k$ is exactly the assertion that, for every
prime $\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$, the reduction of $u_k$ is not an
$\ell$-th power.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The residue degree of $2$ in $\Q(\xi_k)$ is
$\ord_{3^{k+1}}(2)=2\cdot3^k=\varphi(3^{k+1})$, proving inertness.  The
three relative conjugates of $\xi_k$ are $\xi_k,\xi_k\omega$, and
$\xi_k\omega^2$, so
\[
 \prod_{j=0}^2(1+\xi_k\omega^j)=1+\xi_k^3=1+\xi_{k-1}.
\]
Multiplication by the complex-conjugate factor gives the real norm.
The final assertion follows from Proposition~\ref{prop:shifted-unit}:
in a cyclic group an element has the full $\ell$-primary part of the
group order precisely when it is not an $\ell$-th power.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The selected Kummer class and reciprocity boundary}

\begin{proposition}[circular-unit and ray-class form]
\label{prop:circular-ray}
Let
\[
 F_k=\Q(\xi_k+\xi_k^{-1}),\qquad
 \lambda_k=(1-\xi_k)(1-\xi_k^{-1}),
\]
let $\mathfrak p_k=(2)$ be the unique prime of $F_k$ above $2$, and
let $\mathcal C_k\subset\mathcal O_{F_k}^{\times}$ be the real
circular-unit group.  If $\sigma(\xi_k)=\xi_k^2$, then
\[
 c_k:=\frac{\sigma(\lambda_k)}{\lambda_k}
     =(1+\xi_k)(1+\xi_k^{-1})
     =2+\xi_k+\xi_k^{-1}
\]
is a circular unit, its Galois conjugates generate a subgroup of
$2$-power index in $\mathcal C_k$, and its reduction modulo
$\mathfrak p_k$ is $\gamma_k$.  Since the residue-field unit group has
odd order, that $2$-power index disappears on reduction.  Consequently
\[
 \overline{\mathcal C_k}=\langle\gamma_k\rangle
 \subset (\mathcal O_{F_k}/\mathfrak p_k)^{\times}
       \simeq\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times},                         \tag{\(\clubsuit\)}
\]
and hence
\[
 [\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times}:\langle\gamma_k\rangle]
 =[(\mathcal O_{F_k}/\mathfrak p_k)^{\times}:
     \overline{\mathcal C_k}].                              \tag{\(\spadesuit\)}
\]
In particular, $C_k$ is exactly surjectivity modulo $2$ of the real
circular units of conductor $3^{k+1}$.

More precisely, put $E_k=\mathcal O_{F_k}^{\times}$ and let
$\operatorname{Cl}_{\mathfrak p_k}(F_k)$ be the ray class group of finite modulus
$\mathfrak p_k$.  The ray class exact sequence gives
\[
 [\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times}:\langle\gamma_k\rangle]
 =\frac{|\operatorname{Cl}_{\mathfrak p_k}(F_k)|}
        {|\operatorname{Cl}(F_k)|}
   [\overline{E_k}:\overline{\mathcal C_k}],                \tag{\(\heartsuit\)}
\]
where
$[\overline{E_k}:\overline{\mathcal C_k}]\mid
 [E_k:\mathcal C_k]$.  The circular-unit index theorem identifies
the odd part of the latter index with the odd part of
$|\operatorname{Cl}(F_k)|$.
Thus, for an odd prime $\ell\nmid|\operatorname{Cl}(F_k)|$,
\[
 v_{\ell}\!\left(
 [\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times}:\langle\gamma_k\rangle]\right)
 =v_{\ell}\!\left(
 \frac{|\operatorname{Cl}_{\mathfrak p_k}(F_k)|}
      {|\operatorname{Cl}(F_k)|}\right).                    \tag{\(\diamondsuit\)}
\]
Equation~\((\heartsuit)\) names the obstruction but does not reduce its
difficulty: the ray class group is defined through this residue-unit
quotient, and the class-number exclusion in \((\diamondsuit)\) is itself
not known uniformly in $k$.
Finally, with $h=3^k$, $q=2^{h/3}$, and
$\tau=\sigma^{h/3}$, the relative circular unit
\[
 \varepsilon_k=\frac{\tau(c_k)}{c_k},\qquad
 \Norm_{F_k/F_{k-1}}(\varepsilon_k)=1,
\]
reduces to $\eta_k$.  Thus the exact character obstruction of
Proposition~\ref{prop:character-test} is the new relative part of the
same ray-class obstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The class of $2$ generates
$(\mathbb Z/3^{k+1}\mathbb Z)^{\times}/\{\pm1\}$, so $\sigma$ generates
$\operatorname{Gal}(F_k/\Q)$.  The squares of the standard real
circular units are generated, up to $-1$, by the quotients
$\sigma^j(\lambda_k)/\lambda_k$.  Telescoping gives
\[
 \frac{\sigma^j(\lambda_k)}{\lambda_k}
 =\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\sigma^i(c_k),
\]
so the conjugates of $c_k$ generate this square subgroup.  Squaring is
an automorphism of the odd-order residue-field unit group, so the full
circular-unit group and this subgroup have the same reduction image.
Reduction commutes with $\sigma$, which becomes Frobenius at the inert
prime $2$, and
\[
 \overline{c_k}
 =(1+\zeta)(1+\zeta^{-1})=\zeta+\zeta^{-1}=\gamma_k.
\]
The reductions of the conjugates are therefore
$\gamma_k,\gamma_k^2,\gamma_k^{2^2},\ldots$, and they generate the
single multiplicative subgroup $\langle\gamma_k\rangle$.  This proves
\((\clubsuit)\) and \((\spadesuit)\).

The exact sequence
\[
 E_k\longrightarrow
 (\mathcal O_{F_k}/\mathfrak p_k)^{\times}\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Cl}_{\mathfrak p_k}(F_k)
 \longrightarrow\operatorname{Cl}(F_k)
 \longrightarrow1
\]
proves \((\heartsuit)\).  The divisibility of reduction indices is
immediate from $\mathcal C_k\subset E_k$, and the circular-unit index
theorem gives \((\diamondsuit)\); see Washington~\cite[Ch.~8]{Washington97}.
Finally $\tau$ generates $\operatorname{Gal}(F_k/F_{k-1})$, so the norm of its
coboundary $\tau(c_k)/c_k$ telescopes to $1$.  Modulo $2$ it is
$\gamma_k^q/\gamma_k=\eta_k$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[cubic pure-radical reflection descent]
\label{prop:cubic-reflection-descent}
Put \(h=3^k\), let \(\xi=\zeta_{3^{k+1}}\), and retain
\[
 K=F_k=\Q(\xi+\xi^{-1}),\qquad
 c_k=(1+\xi)(1+\xi^{-1}).
\]
For a prime \(\ell\mid\Phi_h(2)\), put
\[
 B=K(\zeta_\ell+\zeta_\ell^{-1}),
 \qquad M=K(\zeta_\ell).
\]
The conductors are coprime, so
\(K\cap\Q(\zeta_\ell)=\Q\) and \(M/B\) is quadratic.  The element
\(c_k\) is a global unit fixed by the nontrivial involution
\(j\in\operatorname{Gal}(M/B)\), and its reduction at the unique prime
of \(K\) above two is \(\gamma_k\).

Assume first that \(c_k\notin(M^\times)^\ell\).  Choose
\(\theta^\ell=c_k\), extend \(j\) by \(j(\theta)=\theta\), and put
\[
 E_C=M(\theta),\qquad L_C=E_C^{\langle j\rangle}=B(\theta).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CRD}_1\)}
\]
Then \(E_C/B\) is dihedral of order \(2\ell\), while \(L_C/B\) has
degree \(\ell\).  The selected condition has the exact norm form
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \text{the \(\ell\)-primary condition in \(C_k\) fails}
 \iff
 2\in\Norm_{L_C/B}(L_C^\times).
 \quad}                                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{CRD}_2\)}
\]
Moreover \(L_C/B\) satisfies the Hasse norm principle, and its norm-one
torus satisfies weak approximation.  At every prime of \(B\) above two,
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 \text{\(\ell\)-primary failure}&
 L_C\otimes_BB_{\mathfrak r}\simeq B_{\mathfrak r}^{\,\ell},\\
 \text{\(\ell\)-primary success}&
 L_C\otimes_BB_{\mathfrak r}\text{ is the unramified degree-\(\ell\) field}.
 \end{array}                                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{CRD}_3\)}
\]
Every local norm condition for the element two away from the primes above
two is automatic.  Thus the reflection norm equation has no hidden
class-group, Tate--Shafarevich, or Brauer--Manin obstruction: it is exactly
the original selected residue condition.

If \(c_k\in(M^\times)^\ell\), then \(c_k\in(B^\times)^\ell\) as well,
the selected condition fails, and the construction degenerates to
\(L_C=B\); in this convention \((\mathsf{CRD}_2)\) remains true.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(\ord_\ell(2)=h\), one has \(\ell\nmid3^{k+1}\).  Coprimality of
cyclotomic conductors gives the field-intersection assertion.  Also
\(\Norm_{\Q(\xi)/\Q}(1+\xi)=\Phi_{3^{k+1}}(-1)=1\), so \(c_k\) is a
global unit.  The identity and reduction claim are
\[
 c_k=2+\xi+\xi^{-1},
 \qquad
 \overline{c_k}=\zeta+\zeta^{-1}=\gamma_k;
\]
the first algebraic identity is kernel-checked in
\texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.

Let \(\sigma(\theta)=\zeta_\ell\theta\).  Since \(j\) fixes \(\theta\)
and inverts \(\zeta_\ell\), one has
\(j\sigma j^{-1}=\sigma^{-1}\), proving the dihedral and fixed-field
assertions.  The completionwise reflection-norm equivalence
\((\mathsf{HD}_5)\) applies to this dihedral extension.

The unique completion \(K_{\mathfrak p}/\Q_2\) is unramified of degree
\(h\) and already contains \(\mu_\ell\), because
\(\ord_\ell(2)=h\).  Hence \(\mathfrak p\) splits completely in both
\(B/K\) and \(M/K\), and \(M/B\) splits at every prime above two.
At each such completion, local Kummer theory says that adjoining an
\(\ell\)-th root of \(c_k\) is split exactly when
\(\gamma_k\) is an \(\ell\)-th power; otherwise it is the unramified
degree-\(\ell\) field.  Its norm valuations are then divisible by
\(\ell\), whereas \(v_{\mathfrak r}(2)=1\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{CRD}_3)\) and the two-adic part of
\((\mathsf{CRD}_2)\).

At every finite place away from \(2\ell\), the entries \(c_k\) and two
are tame units.  At a place above \(\ell\), the totally ramified group
\(\operatorname{Gal}(M/K)\) fixes \(c_k\), two, and the place while
acting faithfully on \(\mu_\ell\); Hilbert-symbol covariance therefore
forces \((c_k,2)_{\lambda,\ell}=1\).  The archimedean places of \(M\)
are complex.  Cyclic Hasse for \(E_C/M\), followed by the
completionwise reflection equivalence, proves
\((\mathsf{CRD}_2)\) and the Hasse norm principle for \(L_C/B\).
Independently, the metacyclic norm-torus theorem of
Hoshi--Yamasaki gives both the Hasse norm principle and weak
approximation for this odd-dihedral reflection field
\cite[Theorem~3.2 and Example~4.6]{HoshiYamasaki25}.

Finally, if \(c_k=y^\ell\) in \(M\), then
\(c_k^2=\Norm_{M/B}(y)^\ell\).  Since \(2\) is invertible modulo the odd
prime \(\ell\), this implies \(c_k\in(B^\times)^\ell\).  Its residue is
an \(\ell\)-th power and the degenerate convention follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the exceptional residue character is the Kummer regulator]
\label{prop:cubic-ray-exceptional-character}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray}, and put
\[
 n=3^k,\qquad M=2^n-1,\qquad
 A=(\mathcal O_{F_k}/\mathfrak p_k)^\times,
 \qquad \Delta=\langle\sigma\rangle.
\]
Let \(\ell\mid\Phi_n(2)\), let \(\mathcal O\) be the ring of integers
of an unramified extension of \(\mathbb Q_\ell\) containing \(\mu_n\),
and let \(\lambda\) be its maximal ideal.  There is a unique
\(\vartheta\in\mu_n(\mathcal O)\) such that
\(\vartheta\equiv2\pmod\lambda\).  In additive exponent notation, the
corresponding character quotient of the residue module is
\[
 A_\vartheta
 :=\frac{A\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathcal O}
          {(\sigma-\vartheta)(A\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathcal O)}
 \simeq \frac{\mathcal O}{(2-\vartheta)}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_1\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 v_\lambda(2-\vartheta)
 =v_\ell(2^n-1)=v_\ell(\Phi_n(2)).                  \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_2\)}
\]
Thus the local ray determinant has a forced zero at every current
prime; it can never prove the cubic arm by being an \(\ell\)-adic unit.

Choose a generator \(g_0\) of \(A\), write
\(\gamma_k=g_0^{e_k}\), and let \(R_\vartheta\) be the class of
\(e_k\) in the right side of \((\mathsf{RC}_1)\).  Then
\[
 (A/\overline{\mathcal C_k})_\vartheta
 \simeq
 \frac{\mathcal O}{(2-\vartheta,R_\vartheta)},       \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_3\)}
\]
and, with the compatible generator used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-mixed-jacobi-top},
\[
 R_\vartheta\equiv L(1)\pmod\lambda.                \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_4\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \begin{split}
 \ell\nmid[A:\overline{\mathcal C_k}]
 &\iff R_\vartheta\not\equiv0\pmod\lambda\\
 &\iff L(1)\ne0
 \iff \gamma_k\notin A^\ell.
 \end{split}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_5\)}
\]
The exceptional ray-class regulator is therefore exactly the selected
Kummer coordinate, rather than an independent class-number factor.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The cyclotomic-divisor criterion gives
\(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=n\); in particular \(\ell\nmid n\).
The residue class of two is consequently a simple root of
\(X^n-1\) modulo \(\ell\), and Hensel lifting gives the unique
\(\vartheta\).  The exponent map identifies the
\(\mathbb Z[\Delta]\)-module \(A\) with
\(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z\), on which \(\sigma\) acts as multiplication
by two.  It follows immediately that
\[
 A_\vartheta\simeq\mathcal O/(M,2-\vartheta).
\]
In \(\mathcal O\) one has
\[
 M=2^n-1=\prod_{\zeta\in\mu_n}(2-\zeta).
\]
Every factor except \(2-\vartheta\) is a unit modulo \(\lambda\).
Hence \((M)=(2-\vartheta)\), which proves
\((\mathsf{RC}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{RC}_2)\); the last equality in
\((\mathsf{RC}_2)\) again uses
\(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=n\).

Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray} gives
\(\overline{\mathcal C_k}=\langle\gamma_k\rangle\).
Passing to the \(\vartheta\)-quotient therefore adds the single relation
\(e_k=0\), proving \((\mathsf{RC}_3)\).
For the final identification, use the generator \(g\) of
\(\mathbb F_{2^{2n}}^\times\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-mixed-jacobi-top} and take
\(g_0=g^{2^n+1}\).  If \(z=g^{(2^{2n}-1)/(3n)}\), then
\[
 \gamma_k=z^{-1}(1+z)^2.
\]
The \(3n\)-torsion factor has zero \(\ell\)-index, while
\(2^n+1\equiv2\pmod\ell\).  Comparing exponents relative to \(g\)
and \(g_0\) gives \(e_k\equiv L(1)\pmod\ell\), which is
\((\mathsf{RC}_4)\).  The equivalences in \((\mathsf{RC}_5)\) now
follow from cyclicity of \(A\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[what the ray-class Gras theorem does and does not say]
All's local presentation~\cite[Cor.~2.4]{All17}, specialized to the
inert rational prime two, is the group-ring form of
\((\mathsf{RC}_1)\).  His ray-class Gras theorem~\cite[Thm.~1.2]{All17}
applies here because \(\ell\nmid2|\Delta|\), but it compares the
Schmidt group of \(2\)-cyclotomic units congruent to one modulo two
with the corresponding nontrivial ray-class component.  It does not
identify that group with the reduction of the ordinary circular units
in Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray}, and it supplies no
nonvanishing of \(R_\vartheta\).  The elementary calculation above is
the required specialization: the known Euler factor vanishes, and its
remaining circular-unit regulator is precisely \(L(1)\).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[the circular-unit circulant is residue-invisible]
\label{prop:cubic-circulant-no-go}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray}, put
\[
 n=3^k,\qquad M=2^n-1,\qquad
 R=\mathbb Z[\Delta],\qquad
 \nu=1+\sigma+\cdots+\sigma^{n-1},
\]
and let \(\mathcal L_k\) be the multiplicative lattice generated by the
conjugates of \(c_k\).  The exponent map identifies
\[
 \mathcal L_k\simeq R/\mathbb Z\nu.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CL}_1\)}
\]
On this rank-\(n-1\) lattice the circulant difference operator
\(\delta=\sigma-1\) has
\[
 \left|\det(\delta\mid R/\mathbb Z\nu)\right|
   =\prod_{\substack{\chi^n=1\\\chi\ne1}}|\chi-1|
   =n=3^k.                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{CL}_2\)}
\]
This three-primary determinant does \emph{not} imply that the index of
the reduction of \(\mathcal L_k\) is three-primary.  Indeed, if
\(s=\ord(\gamma_k)\), reduction is exactly
\[
 \rho_s:R/\mathbb Z\nu\longrightarrow\mathbb Z/s\mathbb Z,
 \qquad [f]\longmapsto f(2)\pmod s,                   \tag{\(\mathsf{CL}_3\)}
\]
where the target records exponents of \(\gamma_k\).  Hence
\[
 [\mathbb F_{2^n}^{\times}:\overline{\mathcal L_k}]
   =M/s,
\]
while \(\delta\) acts on the target as multiplication by
\(2-1=1\).  Thus the determinant in \((\mathsf{CL}_2)\) is invisible to
every residue quotient relevant to the cubic arm.

More strongly, this failure is formal rather than merely a gap in the
determinant estimate.  For every divisor \(d\mid M\), let
\(G=\langle g\rangle\) be cyclic of order \(M\), with the
\(\Delta\)-action \(\sigma(g)=g^2\).  Then
\[
 \rho_d:R/\mathbb Z\nu\longrightarrow G,
 \qquad [f]\longmapsto g^{d f(2)}                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CL}_4\)}
\]
is a well-defined equivariant reduction model whose image has index
exactly \(d\).  It preserves the integral orbit lattice, the sole
characteristic-zero norm relation, Frobenius covariance, and the
circulant determinant.  If \(n=3h\), \(q=2^h\), and
\(d\mid q^2+q+1\), then \(\gcd(d,q-1)=1\) and the relative norm of
\(g^d\) has order \(q-1\); thus the model preserves maximal lower-field
norm as well.  If, in addition, \(M/d\) has a divisor on which two has
order \(n\), the Frobenius orbit of \(g^d\) still has length \(n\).
Consequently even the orbit degree and old-factor norm propagation can
coexist with a non-three-primary current index.  The models
\((\mathsf{CL}_4)\) are not finite-field counterexamples to \(C_k\):
they prove that an argument using only the conjugate exponent lattice
and its circulant regulator cannot evaluate the selected reduction of
the circular unit.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray} shows that the conjugates of
\(c_k\) generate a finite-index subgroup of the real circular units,
so \(\mathcal L_k\) has rank \(n-1\).  They are totally positive and
hence have no nontrivial torsion.  Their norm relation is
\(c_k^\nu=1\).  The kernel of the exponent map \(R\to\mathcal L_k\)
therefore has rank one and contains the primitive vector \(\nu\), so
it is exactly \(\mathbb Z\nu\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{CL}_1)\).

The characteristic polynomial of \(\sigma\) on
\(R/\mathbb Z\nu\) is \((X^n-1)/(X-1)\).  Evaluating it at one gives
\((\mathsf{CL}_2)\).  At the inert prime above two, \(\sigma\) reduces
to Frobenius and \(\sigma^i(c_k)\) reduces to
\(\gamma_k^{2^i}\), proving \((\mathsf{CL}_3)\).  In particular the
image has order \(s\), and \(\sigma-1\) evaluates to one.

For \((\mathsf{CL}_4)\), well-definedness follows from
\(\nu(2)=M\), and equivariance follows from
\(2^n\equiv1\pmod M\).  The image is \(\langle g^d\rangle\), of index
\(d\).  Finally put \(A=1+q+q^2=M/(q-1)\).  The relative norm is
\[
 \Norm(g^d)=(g^d)^A.
\]
Because \(q\equiv-1\pmod3\), one has \(\gcd(A,q-1)=1\); hence a
divisor \(d\mid A\) is coprime to \(q-1\), and the displayed element
has order \(q-1\).  The Frobenius-orbit assertion follows because the
orbit length of an element of order \(M/d\) is
\(\ord_{M/d}(2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exceptional eigenspace and Hilbert-reciprocity no-go]
\label{prop:cubic-reciprocity-no-go}
Put \(n=3^k\), \(m=3n\), let
\[
 F=\mathbb Q(\xi_m+\xi_m^{-1}),\qquad
 \mathfrak p=(2),
\]
and write \(\Delta=\operatorname{Gal}(F/\mathbb Q)=\langle\sigma\rangle\),
where \(\sigma(\xi_m)=\xi_m^2\).  Let
\(\ell\mid\Phi_n(2)\), and set
\[
 V_\ell=(\mathcal O_F/\mathfrak p)^\times/
          ((\mathcal O_F/\mathfrak p)^\times)^\ell .
\]
Then \(V_\ell\) is one-dimensional over \(\mathbb F_\ell\), and
\(\sigma\) acts on it as the scalar \(2\).  Let
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_2:\mathbb F_\ell[\Delta]\longrightarrow\mathbb F_\ell,
 \qquad \sum a_j\sigma^j\longmapsto\sum a_j2^j.
\]
The following reciprocity operators vanish on \(V_\ell\):
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_2(1+\sigma+\cdots+\sigma^{n-1})=0,
 \qquad \operatorname{ev}_2(2-\sigma)=0.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_1\)}
\]
If \(q=2^{n/3}\) and \(\tau=\sigma^{n/3}\), then
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_2(1+\tau+\tau^2)=1+q+q^2=0,
 \qquad \operatorname{ev}_2(\tau-1)=q-1\ne0.             \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_2\)}
\]
Moreover the plus projection of the classical Stickelberger element of
\(\mathbb Q(\xi_m)\) acts as zero on \(V_\ell\).  Its zero is simple:
for
\[
 \nu_n(X)=1+X+\cdots+X^{n-1}
\]
one has
\[
 \nu_n(2)=0,\qquad
 \nu_n'(2)=n2^{n-1}\ne0\pmod\ell.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_3\)}
\]

Let
\[
 c=(1+\xi_m)(1+\xi_m^{-1}),\qquad
 \gamma=\overline c\in\mathbb F_{2^n}^\times,\qquad
 \mathcal A_{k,\ell}=\gamma^{(2^n-1)/\ell}\in\mu_\ell.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_4\)}
\]
Then cubic failure at \(\ell\) is exactly
\(\mathcal A_{k,\ell}=1\).  Although
\(\mathcal A_{k,\ell}\) is a local Kummer/Artin symbol, global Hilbert
reciprocity imposes no condition on this one distinguished value.
More precisely, put \(E=F(\mu_\ell)\).  The prime \(\mathfrak p\)
splits into \(\ell-1\) primes in \(E/F\).  If
\(\mathfrak P_0\mid\mathfrak p\) is chosen so that
\[
 (c,2)_{\mathfrak P_0,\ell}=\mathcal A_{k,\ell}
\]
for the reciprocity convention sending the uniformizer \(2\) to
arithmetic Frobenius, then the \(\ell-1\) symbols above two are
\[
 (c,2)_{a\mathfrak P_0,\ell}=\mathcal A_{k,\ell}^{\,a},
 \qquad a\in\mathbb F_\ell^\times.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_5\)}
\]
Every local symbol away from two is one, and Hilbert reciprocity
reduces to
\[
 \prod_{a\in\mathbb F_\ell^\times}\mathcal A_{k,\ell}^{\,a}
 =\mathcal A_{k,\ell}^{\ell(\ell-1)/2}=1,                \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_6\)}
\]
which holds for every possible value of
\(\mathcal A_{k,\ell}\).  Thus norm coherence, the classical
Stickelberger ideal, and global Kummer reciprocity cannot distinguish
failure from nonfailure.  A completion must compute a first-order unit
regulator in the simple exceptional eigenspace, rather than another
ideal factorization or product formula.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The standard cyclotomic-divisor criterion gives
\(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=n\).  Since
\((\mathcal O_F/\mathfrak p)^\times\simeq\mathbb F_{2^n}^\times\),
its quotient by \(\ell\)-th powers has order \(\ell\), and Frobenius
acts by \([x]\mapsto[x^2]=2[x]\).  This proves the module assertion.
Equations \((\mathsf{ER}_1)\) follow from
\(2^n=1\pmod\ell\).  The element \(q=2^{n/3}\) has exact order three
modulo \(\ell\), proving \((\mathsf{ER}_2)\).

Let \(G=\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb Q(\xi_m)/\mathbb Q)\), let \(j\)
be complex conjugation, and write
\[
 \theta_m=\frac1m
 \sum_{\substack{1\le a<m\\(a,m)=1}}a\,\sigma_a^{-1}
\]
for the Stickelberger element.  Pairing \(a\) with \(m-a\) gives
\[
 (1+j)\theta_m=\sum_{g\in G}g.
\]
On an even representation, the right side is twice the norm element
of \(\Delta\), whose evaluation at two is zero by
\((\mathsf{ER}_1)\).  Since \(\ell\ne2,3\), division by two and by
\(m\) is valid modulo \(\ell\).  Hence \(\theta_m\), and therefore
every classical Stickelberger-ideal multiple controlling
Gauss--Jacobi-sum ideals, acts trivially on \(V_\ell\).  The
particularly relevant integrality factor \(2-\sigma\) vanishes
separately.  Differentiating
\[
 (X-1)\nu_n(X)=X^n-1
\]
at \(X=2\) gives \((\mathsf{ER}_3)\), since \(\ell\nmid2n\).

The reduction of \(c\) is \(\gamma\).  In the local field
\(F_{\mathfrak p}\), which contains \(\mu_\ell\), adjoining an
\(\ell\)-th root \(z\) of \(c\) is the unramified Kummer extension
selected by the residue of \(c\).  Arithmetic Frobenius satisfies
\[
 \frac{\operatorname{Frob}(z)}z
 =\overline z^{\,2^n-1}
 =\gamma^{(2^n-1)/\ell},
\]
proving \((\mathsf{ER}_4)\) and the failure criterion.  Also, for the
relative circular unit \(\varepsilon=\tau(c)/c\),
\[
 (\varepsilon,2)_{\mathfrak p,\ell}
 =\mathcal A_{k,\ell}^{q-1};
\]
because \(q-1\ne0\pmod\ell\), the relative coboundary carries exactly
the same unknown symbol and supplies no new relation.

It remains to audit global reciprocity.  The completion
\(F_{\mathfrak p}\) is the unramified degree-\(n\) extension of
\(\mathbb Q_2\).  Since
\(\mathbb Q_2(\mu_\ell)/\mathbb Q_2\) is also unramified of degree
\(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=n\), it is already contained in
\(F_{\mathfrak p}\).  The conductors \(m\) and \(\ell\) are coprime,
so \(F\cap\mathbb Q(\mu_\ell)=\mathbb Q\); consequently
\([E:F]=\ell-1\), and \(\mathfrak p\) splits completely in \(E/F\).
Galois functoriality of the Hilbert symbol gives
\((\mathsf{ER}_5)\).

We now check every place above \(\ell\), the only point at which wild
local reciprocity might have supplied extra information.  Let
\(K_0\) be the maximal unramified subfield of an \(\ell\)-adic
completion of \(E\).  That completion is
\(K=K_0(\mu_\ell)\), with absolute ramification index
\(e=\ell-1\), and both \(c\) and \(2\) belong to \(K_0^\times\).
For any unit \(a\in K_0^\times\), write
\[
 a=[\overline a]\,\langle a\rangle,\qquad
 \langle a\rangle\in1+\ell\mathcal O_{K_0}.
\]
The Teichm\"uller factor has order prime to \(\ell\), hence is an
\(\ell\)-th power.  If \(U_i=1+\mathfrak m_K^i\), then
\[
 1+\ell\mathcal O_{K_0}\subset U_{\ell-1}.
\]
The standard Hilbert-filtration orthogonality for a field containing
\(\mu_\ell\) is~\cite{Iwasawa68}
\[
 (U_i,U_j)_\ell=1\qquad\text{when}\qquad
 i+j>\frac{\ell e}{\ell-1}=\ell.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_7\)}
\]
Taking \(i=j=\ell-1\) proves
\((c,2)_{\lambda,\ell}=1\) at every \(\lambda\mid\ell\).
At every finite place away from \(2\ell\), both entries are units and
the tame Hilbert-symbol formula is one; archimedean places contribute
nothing because \(\ell\) is odd.  The global product formula is
therefore precisely \((\mathsf{ER}_6)\).  Since
\(\sum_{a=1}^{\ell-1}a\equiv0\pmod\ell\), this relation is
tautological and leaves the single symbol at \(\mathfrak P_0\)
arbitrary.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the simple exceptional zero does not evaluate its Bockstein]
\label{prop:cubic-bockstein-no-go}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-reciprocity-no-go}.  Put
\[
 \mathcal A=\mathbb Z_\ell[\Delta],\qquad
 \mathfrak m=(\ell,\sigma-2),\qquad
 \nu=1+\sigma+\cdots+\sigma^{n-1},
\]
and let \(u=(2^n-1)/\ell\in\mathbb Z_\ell\).  Then
\[
 \nu\equiv
 \ell u+n2^{n-1}(\sigma-2)\pmod{\mathfrak m^2}.       \tag{\(\mathsf{BZ}_1\)}
\]
The coefficient \(n2^{n-1}\) is a unit modulo \(\ell\), but this says
only that the first-order exceptional coordinate is one-dimensional;
it does not determine the cyclotomic unit's value in that coordinate.
Indeed \((\sigma-1)\nu=\sigma^n-1=0\), while \(\sigma-1\) is a unit
modulo \(\mathfrak m^2\).  Thus \((\mathsf{BZ}_1)\) is the cotangent
relation
\[
 0=\ell u+n2^{n-1}(\sigma-2)\quad\text{in }\mathcal A/\mathfrak m^2,
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{BZ}_1'\)}
\]
not a nonzero value of the sought unit class.

More sharply, let \(K/\mathbb Q_2\) be the unramified extension of
degree \(n\), and let
\[
 \lambda=(1-\xi_m)(1-\xi_m^{-1})=t v,
 \qquad
 t\in\mu_{2^n-1},\quad v\in1+2\mathcal O_K
\]
be its Teichm\"uller/principal-unit decomposition at two.  One has
\(\Norm_{K/\mathbb Q_2}(t)=1\) and
\(\Norm_{K/\mathbb Q_2}(v)=3\).  Conversely, for every
\(t_0\in\mu_{2^n-1}\) there is \(v_0\in1+2\mathcal O_K\) such that
\[
 \lambda_0=t_0v_0,\qquad
 \Norm_{K/\mathbb Q_2}(\lambda_0)=3,\qquad
 \overline{\lambda_0}=\overline{t_0}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{BZ}_2\)}
\]
The residue on the right may have any \(\ell\)-power class.  Hence the
norm-three identity, Frobenius action, and the nonzero derivative in
\((\mathsf{BZ}_1)\) cannot by themselves prove cubic nonfailure.  The
missing datum is the distinguished cyclotomic unit's actual coordinate
--- for example through a derived local-unit regulator --- not the formal
existence of the Bockstein coordinate.  This is a local countermodel to
those data only; it is not a global cyclotomic counterexample.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(\epsilon=\sigma-2\).  Modulo
\((\ell^2,\ell\epsilon,\epsilon^2)=\mathfrak m^2\), binomial expansion
gives
\[
 \sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(2+\epsilon)^j
 \equiv(2^n-1)+\nu_n'(2)\epsilon
 \equiv\ell u+n2^{n-1}\epsilon.
\]
Here the discarded part of
\(\nu_n'(2)=n2^{n-1}-(2^n-1)\) lies in
\(\ell\epsilon\).  Since \(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=n\), one has
\(n\mid\ell-1\), so \(n2^{n-1}\not\equiv0\pmod\ell\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{BZ}_1)\).  The identity
\((\sigma-1)\nu=0\) and
\(\sigma-1=1+\epsilon\in(\mathcal A/\mathfrak m^2)^\times\) prove
\((\mathsf{BZ}_1')\); its one nonzero linear coefficient leaves a
one-dimensional cotangent space.

The real cyclotomic norm of \(\lambda\) is three.  The norm of every
Teichm\"uller unit is its residue-field norm, so
\(\Norm(t)=t^{2^n-1}=1\), leaving \(\Norm(v)=3\).  Conversely the norm
map from the principal units of an unramified local extension onto the
base principal units is surjective.  Given \(t_0\), choose \(v_0\) with
norm three; then \((\mathsf{BZ}_2)\) follows.  In addition
\(\sigma(t_0v_0)/(t_0v_0)\) reduces to \(t_0^2/t_0=t_0\), so even the
relative-coboundary form carries the same arbitrary residue class.
Choosing \(t_0\) inside or outside the subgroup of \(\ell\)-th powers
proves the asserted neutrality.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the derivative regulator is the original Kummer class]
\label{prop:cubic-derivative-regulator}
Retain the notation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray}, put \(n=3^k\), and write

\[
 \nu_n(X)=1+X+\cdots+X^{n-1},\qquad
 \mathcal R_k^{\partial}
   :=\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}\sigma^j(c_k)^j
    =c_k^{\,\sigma\nu_n'(\sigma)}.
\]
Then the first-order circular-unit regulator singled out by the simple
exceptional zero is explicit:
\[
 \mathcal R_k^{\partial}=\frac{\lambda_k^n}{3},
 \qquad
 \overline{\mathcal R_k^{\partial}}=\gamma_k^n.       \tag{\(\mathsf{DR}_1\)}
\]
Consequently, for every prime
\(\ell\mid\Phi_n(2)\), its reduction is an \(\ell\)-th power if and only
if \(\gamma_k\) is.  Thus the derivative regulator carries exactly the
unknown Kummer class in \(C_k\): the nonzero derivative identifies the
coordinate but does not evaluate it.

For \(k\geq2\) this identification is norm coherent in the sharp form
\[
 \Norm_{F_k/F_{k-1}}(\mathcal R_k^{\partial})
       =(\mathcal R_{k-1}^{\partial})^3.              \tag{\(\mathsf{DR}_2\)}
\]
Hence
\[
 \mathcal E_k^{\partial}
   :=\frac{\mathcal R_k^{\partial}}
           {\mathcal R_{k-1}^{\partial}}
    =\left(\frac{\lambda_k^3}{\lambda_{k-1}}\right)^{n/3}
\]
has relative norm one.  At every current
\(\ell\mid\Phi_n(2)\), the lower-field factor is automatically an
\(\ell\)-th power, so
\(\overline{\mathcal E_k^{\partial}}\) again carries precisely the
Kummer class of \(\gamma_k\).  In particular, passing to a derived
relative unit does not create an inductive relation capable of proving
\(C_k\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(c_k=\sigma(\lambda_k)/\lambda_k\), the weighted product
telescopes cyclically:
\[
 \prod_{j=0}^{n-1}
 \left(\frac{\sigma^{j+1}(\lambda_k)}
            {\sigma^j(\lambda_k)}\right)^j
 =\frac{\lambda_k^{n-1}}
        {\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\sigma^i(\lambda_k)}
 =\frac{\lambda_k^n}{\Norm_{F_k/\mathbb Q}(\lambda_k)}.
\]
The real cyclotomic norm of \(\lambda_k\) is \(3\), proving the first
identity in \((\mathsf{DR}_1)\).  Modulo the prime above two, both
\(\lambda_k\) and \(c_k\) reduce to \(\gamma_k\), while \(3\) reduces to
one; hence the second identity follows.  Because
\(\operatorname{ord}_{\ell}(2)=n\), one has
\(\ell\nmid n\), so raising the one-dimensional \(\ell\)-power quotient
to the \(n\)-th power is an automorphism.  This proves the equivalence of
Kummer classes.

Finally,
\(\Norm_{F_k/F_{k-1}}(\lambda_k)=\lambda_{k-1}\).  Taking the relative
norm of \(\lambda_k^n/3\), and remembering that the relative degree is
three, gives
\[
 \Norm_{F_k/F_{k-1}}(\mathcal R_k^{\partial})
   =\frac{\lambda_{k-1}^n}{3^3}
   =\left(\frac{\lambda_{k-1}^{n/3}}3\right)^3,
\]
which is \((\mathsf{DR}_2)\).  The displayed formula and norm-one
property of \(\mathcal E_k^{\partial}\) follow immediately.  Since
\(\ell\nmid 2^{n/3}-1\), exponentiation by \(\ell\) is an automorphism
of \(\F_{2^{n/3}}^\times\); every lower-field factor is therefore an
\(\ell\)-th power.  The relative unit has the same current Kummer class,
as claimed.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[weighted Frobenius words only erase or reparameterize]
\label{prop:cubic-weighted-orbit-dichotomy}
Let \(x\in\F_{2^n}^\times\), let the prime
\(\ell\mid 2^n-1\), and put

\[
 A=x^{(2^n-1)/\ell}\in\mu_\ell,
 \qquad \sigma(x)=x^2.
\]
For a finite group-ring word \(w=\sum_i a_i\sigma^i\), with
\(a_i\in\mathbb Z\), define

\[
 x^w=\prod_i\sigma^i(x)^{a_i},
 \qquad
 \operatorname{ev}_2(w)=\sum_i a_i2^i\pmod\ell.
\]
Then

\[
 (x^w)^{(2^n-1)/\ell}=A^{\operatorname{ev}_2(w)}.       \tag{\(\mathsf{WO}_1\)}
\]
Consequently a weighted Frobenius-orbit construction has only two
possibilities.  If \(\operatorname{ev}_2(w)=0\), it is identically
\(\ell\)-power flat.  If
\(\operatorname{ev}_2(w)\ne0\), and in particular if that value is a
unit modulo \(\ell\), its nontriviality is exactly the nontriviality of
the original selected class \(A\), merely transported by an automorphism
of \(\mu_\ell\).

For the cubic arm, take \(n=3^k\), a current prime
\(\ell\mid\Phi_n(2)\), and
\(\nu_n=1+\sigma+\cdots+\sigma^{n-1}\).  Then

\[
 \operatorname{ev}_2(\nu_n)=0,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{ev}_2\bigl(\sigma\nu_n'(\sigma)\bigr)
   =2\nu_n'(2)\equiv n\pmod\ell.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{WO}_2\)}
\]
Thus the norm word is automatically flat, whereas the derivative
regulator of Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-derivative-regulator} has
Euler phase \(A^n\).  Its nonzero derivative does not evaluate the
selected class; it is an invertible reparameterization of it.

Nor can the discrete coefficient \(L(1)\) itself have a
normalization-independent formula in terms of a fixed integer Euler or
Fermat quotient.  In the notation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-mixed-jacobi-top}, put
\(Q=2^{2n}\), \(m=3n\), and
\(z=g^{(Q-1)/m}\).  Replacing the compatible primitive generator \(g\)
by \(g^a\), where

\[
 a\equiv1\pmod m,
 \qquad \gcd(a,Q-1)=1,
\]
keeps \(z\) fixed but changes

\[
 L(1)\longmapsto a^{-1}L(1)\pmod\ell.              \tag{\(\mathsf{WO}_3\)}
\]
The Chinese remainder theorem permits every nonzero value of
\(a\pmod\ell\) while retaining these conditions.  Hence only the
vanishing or nonvanishing of \(L(1)\) is intrinsic; a literal equality
with a fixed scalar Euler quotient is not even well normalized.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put \(E=(2^n-1)/\ell\).  Frobenius commutes with taking the \(E\)-th
power, so

\[
 (x^w)^E
 =\prod_i(x^{2^i})^{a_iE}
 =\prod_i(x^E)^{a_i2^i}
 =A^{\sum_i a_i2^i},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{WO}_1)\).  The two alternatives follow because
\(A\in\mu_\ell\).

The first congruence in \((\mathsf{WO}_2)\) is
\(\nu_n(2)=2^n-1\equiv0\pmod\ell\).  Differentiating
\((X-1)\nu_n(X)=X^n-1\) and evaluating at \(X=2\) gives

\[
 \nu_n(2)+\nu_n'(2)=n2^{n-1}.
\]
Therefore \(2\nu_n'(2)\equiv n2^n\equiv n\pmod\ell\).
Since \(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=n\), one has \(n\mid\ell-1\), so
\(\ell\nmid n\).

Finally, \(a\equiv1\pmod m\) gives

\[
 (g^a)^{(Q-1)/m}=g^{(Q-1)/m}=z.
\]
If \(1-z=g^v=(g^a)^{v'}\), then \(v'\equiv a^{-1}v\pmod\ell\), proving
\((\mathsf{WO}_3)\).  To realize a prescribed nonzero residue modulo
\(\ell\), impose it together with \(a\equiv1\pmod m\), and impose
\(a\equiv1\) modulo every other prime divisor of \(Q-1\); the Chinese
remainder theorem applies because \(\ell\nmid m\).  The resulting \(a\)
is coprime to \(Q-1\), as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the Coleman specialization has a forced exceptional zero]
\label{prop:cubic-coleman-exceptional-zero}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray}, put \(n=3^k\) and
\(m=3n\), and let \(\ell\mid\Phi_n(2)\) be prime.  The norm-coherent
real circular unit \(\mathbf c=(c_k)_k\) has, at the prime three, the
Coleman power series
\[
 f_{\mathbf c}(T)=\frac{(2+T)^2}{1+T},\qquad
 (1+T)\frac{d}{dT}\log f_{\mathbf c}(T)=\frac{T}{2+T}.       \tag{\(\mathsf{CO}_1\)}
\]
Write \(\zeta_3^{\mathrm{KL}}\) for the Kubota--Leopoldt three-adic
zeta pseudo-measure.  With the Coleman-map normalization
of~\cite[Sections~7.2--7.4]{RJW25},
its image on the even Iwasawa quotient is
\[
 \operatorname{Col}(\mathbf c)
   =2\bigl([2]-[1]\bigr)\zeta_3^{\mathrm{KL}}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{CO}_2\)}
\]

Let \(\widetilde\lambda\) be the characteristic-zero lift of the
primitive even character in \((\mathsf{MJ}_2)\): choose a primitive
\(n\)-th root \(\vartheta\) and set
\[
 \widetilde\lambda(\mathord\pm2^i)=\vartheta^i
 \quad(0\leq i<n).
\]
There is a prime \(\mathfrak L\mid\ell\) of its coefficient field for
which \(\vartheta\equiv2\pmod{\mathfrak L}\).  For every positive even
integer \(r\), Coleman interpolation gives the algebraic value
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_{\widetilde\lambda x^r}
       \operatorname{Col}(\mathbf c)
 =2\bigl(\widetilde\lambda(2)2^r-1\bigr)
       L(\widetilde\lambda,1-r)
 =-\frac2r\bigl(\widetilde\lambda(2)2^r-1\bigr)
       B_{r,\widetilde\lambda}.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CO}_3\)}
\]
At the least positive even weight for which the smoothing factor
vanishes after reduction, namely \(r=n-1\), this value is
forced to vanish modulo \(\mathfrak L\):
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_{\widetilde\lambda x^{n-1}}
       \operatorname{Col}(\mathbf c)
 \equiv0\pmod{\mathfrak L}.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{CO}_4\)}
\]
Indeed its smoothing factor reduces to \(2^n-1\).  Dividing that
forced factor before reduction leaves only
\[
 2L(\widetilde\lambda,2-n)
   =-\frac{2}{n-1}B_{n-1,\widetilde\lambda}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{CO}_5\)}
\]
The Coleman identity controls this quotient three-adically; it gives no
\(\mathfrak L\)-unit theorem and no identification of
\((\mathsf{CO}_5)\) with the selected residue index
\(L(1)=\operatorname{ind}_\ell(1-z)\).  At weight zero the corresponding
specialization is a noncritical three-adic \(L\)-value and has no
canonical reduction at \(\mathfrak L\).

Thus the standard Coleman/\(p\)-adic-\(L\)-function specialization cannot
prove \(\mathcal R_\lambda\not\equiv1\pmod{(\zeta_\ell-1)^2}\): its
algebraic specialization meets a forced exceptional zero, while its
noncritical specialization lives at the wrong prime.  This is a
route-exclusion theorem, not a failure of \(C_k\).  The separation is
already exact at \(k=2\): Proposition~\ref{prop:prime-step} proves
\(C_2\), but for \(n=9\), \(\ell=73\), and \(r=8\), the right side of
\((\mathsf{CO}_3)\) is zero modulo \(\mathfrak L\) because
\(2^9-1=7\cdot73\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write
\[
 c(2)_k=\frac{\xi_k^2-1}{\xi_k-1}=1+\xi_k.
\]
The standard cyclotomic-unit Coleman series is
\(((1+T)^2-1)/T=2+T\).  Complex conjugation substitutes
\((1+T)^{-1}-1\) for \(T\), so its conjugate has series
\((2+T)/(1+T)\).  Their product is \(f_{\mathbf c}\), and direct
logarithmic differentiation proves \((\mathsf{CO}_1)\).

The cyclotomic-unit formula
\(\operatorname{Col}(c(2))=([2]-[1])\zeta_3^{\mathrm{KL}}\) and
equivariance under
complex conjugation give \((\mathsf{CO}_2)\) after passage to the even
quotient.  Evaluating at the even character
\(\widetilde\lambda x^r\) and using the Kubota--Leopoldt interpolation
formula gives the first equality in \((\mathsf{CO}_3)\); the standard
identity \(L(\chi,1-r)=-B_{r,\chi}/r\) gives the second.

Since \(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=n\), reduction at a suitable prime
above \(\ell\) sends \(\vartheta\) to \(2\).  Also \(n\mid\ell-1\)
and in fact \(n<\ell-1\), so \(r=n-1\) is positive, even, and smaller
than \(\ell-1\).  The generalized Bernoulli quotient in
\((\mathsf{CO}_3)\) is therefore \(\mathfrak L\)-integral: its
conductor and \(r\) are prime to \(\ell\), and no ordinary Bernoulli
denominator occurring below weight \(\ell-1\) contains \(\ell\).
Finally,
\[
 \widetilde\lambda(2)2^{n-1}-1
 \equiv2^n-1\equiv0\pmod{\mathfrak L},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{CO}_4)\).  Removing this displayed factor gives
\((\mathsf{CO}_5)\).  The final assertions follow from the places at
which the two interpolation values live and from the exact
\(k=2\) prime-step theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[semidirect ray extension forced by cubic failure]
\label{prop:cubic-ray-extension}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray}, put
\(n=3^k\), and let \(\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)\).  Assume
\(\ell\nmid|\operatorname{Cl}(F_k)|\).  Then
\[
 \gamma_k\in(\F_{2^n}^\times)^\ell                  \tag{\(\mathsf{RF}\)}
\]
if and only if there exists a cyclic extension \(M/F_k\) of degree
\(\ell\), conductor exactly \(\mathfrak p_k=(2)\), totally tamely
ramified there and unramified at every other finite prime.  This
extension is Galois over \(\Q\), with
\[
 \operatorname{Gal}(M/\Q)\cong
 C_\ell\rtimes_2 C_n,
 \qquad \sigma\tau\sigma^{-1}=\tau^2.               \tag{\(\mathsf{RF}_1\)}
\]
Moreover, the unique prime of \(F_k\) above three splits completely in
\(M/F_k\).  Both prescribed local behaviors are admissible: at two this
is the standard tame extension with inertia \(C_\ell\), residue degree
\(n\), and Frobenius action \(\tau\mapsto\tau^2\); at three the
\(C_\ell\)-layer is split.  Thus secant saturation and circular-unit
reciprocity give no local contradiction.  Without the class-number
hypothesis, cubic failure yields the sharp alternative
\[
 \ell\mid|\operatorname{Cl}(F_k)|
 \quad\hbox{or the extension in \((\mathsf{RF}_1)\) exists}.  \tag{\(\mathsf{RF}_2\)}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(E_k=\mathcal O_{F_k}^\times\) and
\(\mathcal C_k\) for the circular units.  Proposition~\ref{prop:circular-ray}
identifies the reduction of \(\mathcal C_k\) with
\(\langle\gamma_k\rangle\).  If \((\mathsf{RF})\) holds, then
\(\mathcal C_k\) maps trivially to
\[
 V:=\F_{2^n}^\times/(\F_{2^n}^\times)^\ell\cong C_\ell.
\]
The odd part of \([E_k:\mathcal C_k]\) is the odd part of the class
number.  Under the stated hypothesis its \(\ell\)-part vanishes, so
\(E_k\) also maps trivially to \(V\).  The ray sequence then supplies
an order-\(\ell\) quotient of
\(\operatorname{Cl}_{\mathfrak p_k}(F_k)\).  Because the ordinary class
group has no \(\ell\)-part, the quotient lies entirely in tame inertia
at \(\mathfrak p_k\).  Class field theory gives the asserted
\(M/F_k\).

The quotient is Galois-stable.  The generator \(\sigma\) of
\(\operatorname{Gal}(F_k/\Q)\) acts on the residue field as
\(x\mapsto x^2\), hence on \(V\) by exponent two.  Since
\(\ord_\ell(2)=n\), this action is faithful; Schur--Zassenhaus, or the
coprimality of \(n\) and \(\ell\), gives \((\mathsf{RF}_1)\).
Conversely, tame inertia in any such extension gives the unique quotient
\(\F_{2^n}^\times\twoheadrightarrow C_\ell\).  Global units lie in its
kernel, so in particular \(\gamma_k\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.

Finally let
\[
 \lambda_k=(1-\xi_k)(1-\xi_k^{-1}),\qquad
 c_k=(1+\xi_k)(1+\xi_k^{-1}).
\]
The element \(\lambda_k\) generates the unique prime
\(\mathfrak r_k\) above three, and
\(\lambda_k/c_k\equiv1\pmod{\mathfrak p_k}\), because both reduce to
\((1+\zeta)(1+\zeta^{-1})=\gamma_k\).  Hence
\(\mathfrak r_k=(\lambda_k/c_k)\) is trivial in the full ray class
group and splits in \(M/F_k\).  The local statements now follow from
\(\ell\mid2^n-1\) and the displayed semidirect action.  If the
class-number hypothesis fails, the first alternative in
\((\mathsf{RF}_2)\) holds; otherwise the preceding construction proves
the second.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Kernel tower and quantitative order bounds}

\begin{proposition}[explicit cubic kernel tower]\label{prop:eta-tower}
Define $\eta_0=0$.  For $k\geq1$ put
\[
 q_k=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad
 \eta_k=\gamma_k^{q_k-1},\qquad
 U_k=\ker\!\left(\Norm_{\F_{q_k^3}/\F_{q_k}}\right).
\]
Then $\eta_k\in U_k$, $|U_k|=q_k^2+q_k+1=\Phi_{3^k}(2)$, and, with
$\omega=\zeta^{3^k}\in\F_4$,
\[
 q_k\equiv3^k-1\pmod{3^{k+1}},\qquad
 \eta_k=\frac{\omega+\omega^2\zeta^2}{1+\zeta^2},\qquad
 \zeta^2=\frac{\eta_k+\omega}{\eta_k+\omega^2}.
\]
Moreover $\eta_k$ has the irreducible cubic equation over
$\F_{q_k}=\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}$
\[
 X^3+\eta_{k-1}X^2+(\eta_{k-1}+1)X+1=0.       \tag{\(*\)}
\]
Equivalently,
\[
 \Norm(\eta_k)=1,\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(\eta_k)=\eta_{k-1},\qquad
 e_2(\eta_k)=\eta_{k-1}+1.
\]
It also satisfies
\[
 \eta_k^{q_k+1}+\eta_k+1=0,
 \qquad \eta_k+1=\eta_k^{q_k+1},
 \qquad \ord(\eta_k+1)=\ord(\eta_k).          \tag{\(\dagger\)}
\]
Thus $\eta_k$ lies in the planar Singer difference set
\[
 \mathcal H_k=\{y\in U_k:y^{q_k+1}+y+1=0\}
\]
of size $q_k+1$ inside $U_k$~\cite{MRS20}.
Finally,
\[
 C_k\iff C_{k-1}\ \text{and}\
 \ord(\eta_k)=q_k^2+q_k+1.                    \tag{\(**\)}
\]
(Here $C_0$ is the tautological assertion that $1$ generates
$\F_2^\times$.)
Thus the new-prime obstruction is the single assertion that the
distinguished root of \((*)\) is a generator of the cubic norm-one
torus (equivalently, multiplication by it is a Singer cycle).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The congruence starts with $2\equiv3-1\pmod9$ and lifts by cubing:
if $a\equiv-1+3^k\pmod{3^{k+1}}$, then
$a^3\equiv-1+3^{k+1}\pmod{3^{k+2}}$.  Hence
$\zeta^{q_k}=\omega\zeta^{-1}$.  Dividing
$\gamma_k^{q_k}$ by $\gamma_k$ now gives the stated M\"obius formula;
solving it gives the inverse formula.

Put $t=\zeta^2$ and
$M(T)=(\omega+\omega^2T)/(1+T)$.  The same formula one level down says
$\eta_{k-1}=M(t^3)$ (also for $k=1$, when it gives $\eta_0=0$).  If
$a=\eta_{k-1}$ and $X=M(t)$, then
\[
 \left(\frac{X+\omega}{X+\omega^2}\right)^3
 =\frac{a+\omega}{a+\omega^2}.
\]
Cross-multiplication and $\omega^2+\omega+1=0$ reduce exactly to
\((*)\).  Also
$\eta_k^{1+q_k+q_k^2}=\gamma_k^{q_k^3-1}=1$, so $\eta_k\in U_k$.
It cannot lie in $\F_{q_k}$: otherwise its norm-one condition would give
$\eta_k^3=1$, hence $\eta_k=1$ because $3\nmid q_k-1$; the inverse
M\"obius formula would then give $t=\omega$, contradicting
$\ord(t)=3^{k+1}>3$.  Thus \((*)\) is irreducible, and its coefficients
give the three symmetric identities.

The earlier minimal polynomial
$X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}$ gives
$\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q_k^3}/\F_{q_k}}(\gamma_k)=0$.  Dividing
$\gamma_k+\gamma_k^{q_k}+\gamma_k^{q_k^2}=0$ by $\gamma_k$ yields
$1+\eta_k+\eta_k^{q_k+1}=0$, proving the first two parts of
\((\dagger)\).  Since
$\gcd(q_k+1,q_k^2+q_k+1)=1$, exponentiation by $q_k+1$ is an
automorphism of $U_k$, proving the equality of orders.  The cited
difference-set identification is the norm-one realization of the planar
Singer set.

In the direct product of Proposition~\ref{prop:norm-kernel-seam},
raising to $q_k-1$ kills the old-field component and is an automorphism
on $U_k$, since $\gcd(q_k-1,q_k^2+q_k+1)=1$.  Therefore the kernel
component of $\gamma_k$ generates $U_k$ exactly when $\eta_k$ does;
Proposition~\ref{prop:norm-kernel-seam} gives \((**)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the normal Singer translate]
\label{prop:singer-normal-translate}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}, put
\[
 \varepsilon_0=1,\qquad \varepsilon_k=\eta_k+1\quad(k\geq1),
\]
and write \(q=q_k\), \(N=q^2+q+1\).  Then
\(\varepsilon_k\in U_k\) has order \(\ord(\eta_k)\) and has the
irreducible relative cubic
\[
 X^3+\varepsilon_{k-1}X^2
   +(\varepsilon_{k-1}+1)X+1.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{SN}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_{k-1}}(\varepsilon_k)=\varepsilon_{k-1},\qquad
 e_2(\varepsilon_k)=\varepsilon_{k-1}+1,\qquad
 \Norm_{L_k/L_{k-1}}(\varepsilon_k)=1.              \tag{\(\mathsf{SN}_2\)}
\]
Moreover \(\varepsilon_k\) is normal over \(\F_2\).  More exactly, if
\(\sigma(x)=x^2\) and
\[
 T_j=\sum_{r=0}^{3^{k-j}-1}\sigma^{r3^j}\quad(0\leq j\leq k),
 \qquad E_0=T_0,\qquad E_j=T_j+T_{j-1}\quad(j\geq1),
\]
then the \(E_j\) are the cyclotomic Frobenius projectors and
\[
 T_j(\varepsilon_k)=\varepsilon_j,\qquad
 E_0(\varepsilon_k)=1,\qquad
 E_j(\varepsilon_k)=\eta_j+\eta_{j-1}\ne0\quad(j\geq1). \tag{\(\mathsf{SN}_3\)}
\]
Thus the absolute Frobenius orbit of \(\varepsilon_k\) is an
\(\F_2\)-basis of \(L_k\).  In the same decomposition
\[
 E_0(\eta_k)=0,\qquad E_j(\eta_k)=E_j(\varepsilon_k)\ne0\quad(j\geq1),
\]
so the orbit of \(\eta_k\) spans exactly the absolute trace-zero
hyperplane and has rank \(3^k-1\).

For the immediate extension put
\(x=\varepsilon_k\), \(y=x^q\), \(z=x^{q^2}\), and
\(b=\varepsilon_{k-1}\).  The trace Gram and cyclic Moore determinants
are
\[
 G=(\operatorname{Tr}(x^{q^i}x^{q^j}))_{0\leq i,j<3}
  =\begin{pmatrix}
     b^2&b+1&b+1\\ b+1&b^2&b+1\\ b+1&b+1&b^2
    \end{pmatrix},
\]
\[
 \det G=b^2(b^2+b+1)^2,
 \qquad
 \det\!\begin{pmatrix}x&y&z\\y&z&x\\z&x&y\end{pmatrix}
   =b(b^2+b+1).                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{SN}_4\)}
\]
Both determinants are nonzero.  Finally
\[
 C_k\iff C_{k-1}\ \text{and}\
       \varepsilon_k\ \text{generates }U_k.          \tag{\(\mathsf{SN}_5\)}
\]
Thus the current Singer coordinate has a normal translate of exactly the
same multiplicative order.  This proves absolute and immediate normality;
it does not assert that \(\varepsilon_k\) is completely normal.  It is
also distinct from the later trace-zero coordinate
\(h_k=1+\gamma_k^{-1}\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In characteristic two, translating the cubic for \(\eta_k\) by
\(X\mapsto X+1\) gives \((\mathsf{SN}_1)\); translation preserves
irreducibility, and its symmetric coefficients give
\((\mathsf{SN}_2)\).  The Singer identity gives
\[
 \varepsilon_k=\eta_k^{q+1},\qquad
 \varepsilon_k^q=\eta_k^{-1}.
\]
Since \(N=q(q+1)+1\), one has \(\gcd(q+1,N)=1\); exponentiation by
\(q+1\) is an automorphism of \(U_k\), proving equality of orders and
\((\mathsf{SN}_5)\) from Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}.

The operator \(T_j\) is \(\operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_j}\) and, since all relative
degrees are odd, is the idempotent projector onto \(L_j\).  Trace
transitivity and \((\mathsf{SN}_2)\) give
\(T_j(\varepsilon_k)=\varepsilon_j\), hence \((\mathsf{SN}_3)\).
Its last value is nonzero: equality \(\eta_j=\eta_{j-1}\) would put
\(\eta_j\) in \(L_{j-1}\), contrary to the irreducibility of its
relative cubic.  Now
\(\ord_{3^j}(2)=2\cdot3^{j-1}=\varphi(3^j)\), so
\(\Phi_{3^j}\) is irreducible over \(\F_2\).  Each nonzero
\(E_j\)-component therefore generates its whole simple Frobenius block.
All blocks occur in \(\varepsilon_k\), proving normality; omitting only
the \(E_0\)-block gives the assertion for \(\eta_k\).

Equation \((\mathsf{SN}_1)\) gives
\(e_1=b\), \(e_2=b+1\), \(e_3=1\).  Hence the diagonal Gram entries are
\(\operatorname{Tr}(x^2)=b^2\) and the off-diagonal entries are
\(\operatorname{Tr}(xy)=xy+yz+zx=b+1\), giving the first determinant in
\((\mathsf{SN}_4)\).  In characteristic two the cyclic Moore determinant
is
\[
 x^3+y^3+z^3+xyz=e_1^3+e_1e_2=b(b^2+b+1),
\]
whose square is \(\det G\).  Here \(b\ne0\), and
\(b^2+b+1=0\) would give an element of order three in
\(L_{k-1}^{\times}\); this is impossible because
\(3\nmid2^{3^{k-1}}-1\).  The translated-cubic, Moore, Gram, and
coprime-order algebra used here is kernel-checked in
\texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}; the Frobenius-projector argument is paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[reciprocal cubic Kummer descent]
\label{prop:cubic-kummer-descent}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}, write
\[
 K=\F_q,\qquad L=\F_{q^3},\qquad
 U=\ker\!\left(\Norm_{L/K}\right),\qquad
 a=\eta_{k-1},
\]
and let the prime \(\ell\mid q^2+q+1\).  Define
\(S_r(C,D)\in\F_2[C,D]\) by
\[
 S_0=1,\qquad S_1=C,\qquad S_2=C^2,\qquad
 S_r=CS_{r-1}+DS_{r-2}+S_{r-3}\quad(r\geq3).
 \tag{\(\mathsf{CK}_1\)}
\]
Then failure at \(\ell\), namely \(\eta_k\in U^\ell\), is equivalent
to the existence of \(c,d\in K\) satisfying
\((\mathsf{CK}_3)\) below.  Every such pair automatically has
\[
 X^3+cX^2+dX+1
 \quad\hbox{irreducible over }K,                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CK}_2\)}
\]
and the two equations are
\[
 S_\ell(c,d)=a,\qquad S_\ell(d,c)=a+1.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CK}_3\)}
\]
Given a witness \((c,d)\), choose a root \(b\) of
\((\mathsf{CK}_2)\) and Frobenius-conjugate it so that
\(b^\ell=\eta_k\).  Then \(c=\operatorname{Tr}(b)\) and
\(d=\operatorname{Tr}(b^{-1})\).  For a fixed primitive
\(\rho\in\mu_\ell\subset L\) and
\(j\in\mathbb Z/\ell\mathbb Z\), put
\[
 \begin{split}
 c_j={}&\rho^jb+\rho^{jq}b^q+\rho^{jq^2}b^{q^2},\\
 d_j={}&\rho^{j(1+q)}b^{1+q}
       +\rho^{j(q+q^2)}b^{q+q^2}
       +\rho^{j(q^2+1)}b^{q^2+1}.
 \end{split}                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CK}_4\)}
\]
Every \(c_j,d_j\) lies in \(K\), every displayed cubic below is
irreducible over \(K\), and one has the exact factorization
\[
 X^{3\ell}+aX^{2\ell}+(a+1)X^\ell+1
 =\prod_{j\bmod\ell}
   \left(X^3+c_jX^2+d_jX+1\right).                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CK}_5\)}
\]
The reciprocal cubic \(X^3+dX^2+cX+1\) contains the conjugates of
\(b^{-1}\); equivalently, \(b^{q+1}=b^{-q^2}\) is an
\(\ell\)-th root of \(\eta_k+1\).  Finally
\[
 \F_2(c,d)=K,\qquad
 \sum_jc_j=\sum_jd_j=0,\qquad
 \prod_j(c_j+d_j)=1.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{CK}_6\)}
\]
Thus cubic failure is a selected pair of reciprocal degree-three
Dickson equations over the preceding field, rather than merely a factor
of the degree-\(3\ell\) Capelli composition.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because \(\ell\mid q^2+q+1\) and \(3\nmid q^2+q+1\), one has
\(\ord_\ell(q)=3\).  Suppose first that \(b\in U\) and
\(b^\ell=\eta_k\).  The element \(b\) cannot lie in \(K\), since
its \(\ell\)-th power does not.  Hence its conjugates have the
irreducible minimal polynomial
\[
 X^3+cX^2+dX+1,\qquad
 c=\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(b),\qquad
 d=\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(b^{-1}).
\]
The recurrence \((\mathsf{CK}_1)\) is the power-sum recurrence for
these three roots.  It gives
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(b^\ell)=S_\ell(c,d)=a,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(b^{-\ell})=S_\ell(d,c)=a+1,
\]
where the second equality uses the reciprocal cubic and the symmetric
coefficients of the polynomial in Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}.

Conversely, let \(c,d\in K\) satisfy \((\mathsf{CK}_3)\), and let
\(b_1,b_2,b_3\) be the roots of the corresponding cubic in an
algebraic closure.  Their product is one.  The first elementary
symmetric function of their \(\ell\)-th powers is
\(S_\ell(c,d)=a\), and the second is
\[
 b_1^{-\ell}+b_2^{-\ell}+b_3^{-\ell}=S_\ell(d,c)=a+1,
\]
and the third is one.  Those powers are therefore the three distinct
roots of the separable polynomial \(X^3+aX^2+(a+1)X+1\).  A
reducible cubic over the finite field \(K\) would have a root
\(b_i\in K\), making \(b_i^\ell\) a \(K\)-root of that irreducible
target polynomial.  This is impossible, so \((\mathsf{CK}_2)\) is
automatic.  We may therefore write the roots as
\(b,b^q,b^{q^2}\); their product puts \(b\) in \(U\).  A
Frobenius conjugate of \(b\) is consequently an \(\ell\)-th root of
\(\eta_k\) in \(U\); choose this conjugate for the rest of the proof.

For the factorization, Frobenius cycles
\[
 \rho^jb,\quad \rho^{jq}b^q,\quad
 \rho^{jq^2}b^{q^2}
\]
because \(q^3\equiv1\pmod\ell\).  Their product is one because
\(1+q+q^2\equiv0\pmod\ell\), and their first two symmetric
coefficients are \((\mathsf{CK}_4)\).  No orbit has size one, since
its \(\ell\)-th power would put \(\eta_k\) in \(K\).  If a member of
the triple indexed by \(j\) equalled a member indexed by \(j'\), then
raising to the \(\ell\)-th power and using irreducibility of the
polynomial for \(\eta_k\) would first force the same Frobenius position;
primitivity of \(\rho\) would then force \(j=j'\).  Thus the triples
are disjoint.  These are all roots of the
left side of \((\mathsf{CK}_5)\), proving the factorization and
irreducibility.  The reciprocal assertion follows from
\(b^{1+q+q^2}=1\) and
\((b^{q+1})^\ell=\eta_k^{q+1}=\eta_k+1\).

Since \(a=S_\ell(c,d)\) generates \(K/\F_2\), one has
\(\F_2(c,d)=K\).  Comparing the coefficients of
\(X^{3\ell-1}\) and \(X\) in \((\mathsf{CK}_5)\) gives the two sums in
\((\mathsf{CK}_6)\); evaluation at \(X=1\) gives the product.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Dickson Jacobian and the selected cubic fibre]
\label{prop:cubic-dickson-jacobian}
Let \(F\) have characteristic two and let \(r\geq1\) be odd.  With the
recurrence \((\mathsf{CK}_1)\), put
\[
 A_r=S_r(C,D),\qquad B_r=S_r(D,C),\qquad
 J_r=\det\frac{\partial(A_r,B_r)}{\partial(C,D)}.
\]
Then the following polynomial identity holds in \(F[C,D]\):
\[
 \boxed{(CD+1)J_r=A_rB_r+1.}                              \tag{\(\mathsf{CJ}_1\)}
\]
If \(X^3+AX^2+BX+1\) is separable, the geometric fibre
\((A_r,B_r)=(A,B)\) consists of exactly \(r^2\) reduced points and
is everywhere \(\acute{e}\)tale.

Now specialize to Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-kummer-descent}.  The
geometric \((\mathsf{CK}_3)\)-fibre has \(\ell^2\) reduced points, and
its \(K\)-rational locus has cardinality
\[
 \#\{(c,d)\in K^2:(\mathsf{CK}_3)\}
 =\begin{cases}
   \ell,&\eta_k\in U^\ell,\\
   0,&\eta_k\notin U^\ell.
  \end{cases}                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{CJ}_2\)}
\]
At every rational point,
\[
 J_\ell(c,d)=\frac{a^2+a+1}{cd+1}\ne0.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{CJ}_3\)}
\]
Since \(\gcd(\ell,q-1)=1\), all nonzero Jacobian values, the source and
target discriminants
\[
 (cd+1)^2,\qquad(a^2+a+1)^2,
\]
and their ratios are automatically \(\ell\)-th powers in \(K\).

There is likewise only one Kummer obstruction behind the reciprocal pair.
For a geometric \(b\) with \(b^\ell=\eta_k\), set
\[
 \theta=b^{q^2+q+1}\in\mu_\ell.                          \tag{\(\mathsf{CJ}_4\)}
\]
It is independent of the choice \(b\mapsto\rho^jb\), and
\[
 \eta_k\in U^\ell\iff\theta=1.
\]
The companion root \(b^{q+1}\) satisfies
\((b^{q+1})^\ell=\eta_k+1\), and its norm obstruction is
\(\theta^{q+1}=\theta^{-q^2}\).  Since
\(\gcd(q+1,\ell)=1\), it vanishes exactly when \(\theta\) does.  Thus
the ordered second equation in \((\mathsf{CK}_3)\) selects the target
instead of its reciprocal, but supplies no independent residue class.

Under failure, put \(z_{k-1}:=a^2+a+1\).  The \(\ell\) pairs in
\((\mathsf{CK}_4)\) also obey the selected norm product
\[
 \boxed{
 \prod_{j\bmod\ell}(c_j^2+c_jd_j+d_j^2)
 =a^2+a+1=z_{k-1}.}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{CJ}_5\)}
\]
Every factor is nonzero.  This recursion again has no \(\ell\)-residue
content, because raising to the \(\ell\)-th power is an automorphism of
\(K^\times\).

Finally, the symmetric Berlekamp representative of a separable cubic
\(X^3+CX^2+DX+1\) is
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathcal B(C,D)
 &=\frac{C^3+D^3+CD+1}{(CD+1)^2}\\
 &=\frac{(C+D+1)(C^2+CD+D^2+C+D+1)}{(CD+1)^2}.
 \end{split}                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CJ}_6\)}
\]
Its absolute trace is zero for every irreducible cubic, while the selected
target has the displayed representative
\(\mathcal B(a,a+1)=0\).  Therefore ramification, discriminant, inversion,
and Berlekamp-trace invariants all leave precisely the original selected
class \(\theta\); none excludes the rational fibre in
\((\mathsf{CJ}_2)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Work first in the Laurent ring with independent \(x,y\) and
\(z=(xy)^{-1}\).  Put
\[
 C=x+y+z,\qquad D=xy+yz+zx.
\]
The Newton recurrence gives
\[
 (A_r,B_r)
 =(x^r+y^r+z^r,x^{-r}+y^{-r}+z^{-r}).
\]
For logarithmic differentials,
\[
 \det\frac{\partial(C,D)}{\partial(\log x,\log y)}
 =(x+y)(y+z)(z+x)=CD+1.
\]
At the powered point, the corresponding determinant is
\(A_rB_r+1\), while the power map
\((x,y)\mapsto(x^r,y^r)\) has logarithmic determinant \(r^2=1\) in
characteristic two.  The chain rule therefore gives explicitly
\[
 J_r(CD+1)=r^2(A_rB_r+1)=A_rB_r+1.
\]
Since \(C,D\) are
algebraically independent, it is an identity in \(F[C,D]\).

Label the three distinct target roots.  Choose independently an \(r\)-th
root of each of the first two and make the third the inverse of their
product.  This gives \(r^2\) source cubics.  Two choices cannot give the
same unordered source roots: raising to the \(r\)-th power would permute
distinct labeled target roots, forcing the permutation and then both
choices to agree.  This accounts for the full geometric fibre.
On it \(A_rB_r+1\ne0\); \((\mathsf{CJ}_1)\) forces both \(CD+1\) and
\(J_r\) to be nonzero, proving reducedness and \(\acute{e}\)taleness.

The automatic irreducibility proved in
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-kummer-descent} identifies every rational point
with a root \(b\) whose \(\ell\)-th power is one of the three selected
target roots.  After fixing \(b^\ell=\eta_k\), the \(\ell\) possible roots
are exactly \(\rho^jb\), and each cubic has exactly one root over
\(\eta_k\).  Thus \((\mathsf{CK}_5)\) gives all rational points under
failure, while the equivalence in that proposition gives none under
success.  This proves \((\mathsf{CJ}_2)\), and
\((\mathsf{CJ}_1)\) proves \((\mathsf{CJ}_3)\).  The power-residue and
discriminant statements follow from \(\gcd(\ell,q-1)=1\) and the
characteristic-two discriminant formula
\(\operatorname{disc}(X^3+CX^2+DX+1)=(CD+1)^2\).

For \((\mathsf{CJ}_4)\), raising \(\theta\) to the \(\ell\)-th power
uses \(\Norm(\eta_k)=1\), while changing \(b\) by \(\rho^j\) changes
\(\theta\) by \(\rho^{j(q^2+q+1)}=1\).  Membership of \(b\) in \(U\)
is exactly \(\theta=1\), and
\[
 \Norm_{L/K}(b^{q+1})
 =\theta^{q+1}=\theta^{-q^2}
\]
proves the reciprocal claim.

Because \(\ord_\ell(q)=3\), one has \(\ell\equiv1\pmod3\).  Evaluate
\((\mathsf{CK}_5)\) at \(\omega\) and \(\omega^2\), where
\(\omega^2+\omega+1=0\).  The two values on the left are respectively
\(a+\omega\) and \(a+\omega^2\), while the product of the corresponding
two values of the \(j\)-th cubic is
\(c_j^2+c_jd_j+d_j^2\).  This proves \((\mathsf{CJ}_5)\).  A zero factor
would either give \(c_j=d_j=0\), contradicting irreducibility, or put
\(c_j/d_j\in\F_4\setminus\F_2\).  But \(K\) has odd degree over
\(\F_2\), so \(K\cap\F_4=\F_2\).

For ordered roots \(x,y,z\), direct simplification gives
\[
 \beta=\frac{x}{x+y}+\frac{y}{y+z}+\frac{z}{z+x},
 \qquad \beta^2+\beta=\mathcal B(C,D).
\]
If the cubic is irreducible and the cyclic ordering is Frobenius ordering,
then \(\beta\in K\), so the absolute trace of \(\mathcal B(C,D)\) is
zero.  Reversing the cyclic order changes \(\beta\) by one: \(\beta\)
itself depends on the orientation, while \(\beta^2+\beta\) is invariant.
Substitution \(C=a,D=a+1\) makes
the numerator in \((\mathsf{CJ}_6)\) zero.  This proves the final audit.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[absolute-trace obstruction to normal-period arguments]
\label{prop:cubic-trace-zero}
For every $k\geq1$ one has
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/\F_2}(\gamma_k)=0,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/\F_2}(\eta_k)=0.
\]
Consequently neither $\gamma_k$ nor $\eta_k$ is normal over $\F_2$.
In particular, a theorem whose hypothesis is that the selected Gaussian
period is normal cannot prove the cubic arm for this family.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The relative minimal polynomial of $\gamma_k$ over $L_{k-1}$ is
$X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}$.  Its quadratic coefficient is zero, so
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_{k-1}}(\gamma_k)=0.
\]
Trace transitivity gives the first equality.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}, the relative minimal polynomial of
$\eta_k$ has quadratic coefficient $\eta_{k-1}$; in characteristic two,
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_{k-1}}(\eta_k)=\eta_{k-1}.
\]
Iterating trace transitivity reduces the absolute trace to that of
$\eta_0=0$, proving the second equality.

If either element were normal, its full absolute-Frobenius orbit would be
an $\F_2$-basis of $L_k$.  The sum of that orbit is the absolute trace, so
trace zero would give the nontrivial all-ones linear relation among the
basis vectors.  This is impossible.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the inverse selector is completely normal]
\label{prop:cubic-inverse-completely-normal}
Put \(\gamma_0=\beta_0=1\) and, for \(k\geq1\),
\[
 \beta_k=\gamma_k^{-1}\in L_k=\F_{2^{3^k}}.
\]
Then \(\beta_k\) is completely normal over \(\F_2\): for every
\(0\leq s<k\), its \(2^{3^s}\)-Frobenius orbit is an
\(L_s\)-basis of \(L_k\).  In particular,
\[
 \dim_{\F_2}\langle\beta_k^{2^i}:0\leq i<3^k\rangle=3^k.       \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_1\)}
\]
By contrast, the exact Frobenius-orbit ranks of the original coordinate
and its translate are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \dim_{\F_2}\langle\gamma_k^{2^i}:0\leq i<3^k\rangle
   &=2\cdot3^{k-1},\\
 \dim_{\F_2}\langle(\gamma_k+1)^{2^i}:0\leq i<3^k\rangle
   &=2\cdot3^{k-1}+1.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_2\)}
\]
The inverse selector is also the derivative coordinate:
\[
 X P_k'(X)=P_k(X)+1,\qquad P_k'(\gamma_k)=\beta_k.      \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_{2a}\)}
\]
Thus complete additive normality is unconditional after inversion.
It supplies no criterion for multiplicative primitivity in \(C_k\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The relation
\(\gamma_k^3+\gamma_k+\gamma_{k-1}=0\) gives, on inversion,
\[
 \beta_k^3+\beta_{k-1}\beta_k^2+\beta_{k-1}=0.          \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_3\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_{k-1}}(\beta_k)=\beta_{k-1},
\]
and transitivity gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_j}(\beta_k)=\beta_j\) for every \(j<k\).
The denominator-cleared algebra in \((\mathsf{CN}_3)\) is
kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.

The base case of \((\mathsf{CN}_{2a})\) is immediate from
\(P_1=X^3+X+1\).  Differentiating
\(P_{k+1}=P_k^3+P_k^2+1\) in characteristic two shows that
\(XP_{k+1}'=P_{k+1}+1\) whenever \(XP_k'=P_k+1\).  Evaluation at
\(\gamma_k\) gives the second identity.  The generic derivative induction
step is also kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.

For the absolute rank calculation, let \(\sigma(x)=x^2\), and define the
trace projectors
\[
 T_j=\sum_{r=0}^{3^{k-j}-1}\sigma^{r3^j}
     =\operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_j},
 \qquad
 E_0=T_0,\quad E_j=T_j+T_{j-1}\quad(1\leq j\leq k).
\]
Because
\[
 X^{3^k}-1=\prod_{j=0}^k\Phi_{3^j}(X)
\]
and
\(\ord_{3^j}(2)=\varphi(3^j)\), every factor
\(\Phi_{3^j}\) is irreducible over \(\F_2\).  Hence \(E_j\) is the
projector onto one simple Frobenius block.  Now
\[
 E_0(\beta_k)=1,\qquad
 E_j(\beta_k)=\beta_j+\beta_{j-1}\ne0.
\]
Thus every simple block occurs, proving \((\mathsf{CN}_1)\) and absolute
normality.

For \(\gamma_k\), the missing quadratic coefficient in
\(X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}\) gives
\(T_j(\gamma_k)=0\) for every \(j<k\).  It therefore occupies exactly the
top \(\Phi_{3^k}\)-block, of dimension
\(\varphi(3^k)=2\cdot3^{k-1}\).  Adding one supplies exactly the
one-dimensional invariant block as well, proving
\((\mathsf{CN}_2)\).

Finally, Scheerhorn's completely-normal Dickson criterion applies
directly to every intermediate extension: \(3^k\) is a power of the odd
prime dividing \(2+1\), and
\[
 P_k(X)=D_{3^k}(X,1)+1
\]
is irreducible by Proposition~\ref{prop:dickson-iterate}.  The reciprocal
polynomial, whose root is \(\beta_k\), is therefore completely normal
\cite[Corollary~1]{Scheerhorn96}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[canonical half-circulant collapse of the two normal coordinates]
\label{prop:cubic-two-normal-bases}
Retain Propositions~\ref{prop:singer-normal-translate} and
\ref{prop:cubic-inverse-completely-normal}.  Put \(n=3^k\),
\(\sigma(x)=x^2\), and regard \(\F_2[C_n]\) as
\(\F_2[X]/(X^n-1)\) acting through \(X=\sigma\).  Define
\[
 D(X)=X+X^2+\cdots+X^{n-2}
\]
and
\[
 C_k(X)=
 \begin{cases}
  1+X+X^3+\cdots+X^{n-2},&k\ \text{even},\\
  X^2+X^4+\cdots+X^{n-1},&k\ \text{odd}.
 \end{cases}
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\ \beta_k=D(\sigma)\varepsilon_k,\qquad
 \varepsilon_k=C_k(\sigma)\beta_k,\qquad C_kD=1\ }
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NB}_1\)}
\]
in \(\F_2[C_n]\).  At an \(n\)-th root \(\xi\), the two circulants
have spectral values
\[
 D(1)=C_k(1)=1,
 \qquad
 D(\xi)=\frac{\xi+1}{\xi},\quad
 C_k(\xi)=\frac{\xi}{\xi+1}\quad(\xi\ne1).          \tag{\(\mathsf{NB}_2\)}
\]
Consequently their regular-representation determinants are one.  The
absolute normal bases generated by \(\beta_k\) and \(\varepsilon_k\)
differ by this determinant-one Frobenius circulant and have the same
full-field subspace polynomial \(Z^{2^n}+Z\).

Let \(\ell\mid q^2+q+1\), let \(\chi:U_k\to\mu_\ell\) have exact
order \(\ell\), and let
\(\widetilde\chi:L_k^\times\to\mu_\ell\) be its unique extension
trivial on \(L_{k-1}^\times\).  Then
\[
 \eta_k\in U_k^\ell
 \iff \widetilde\chi(\beta_k)=1
 \iff \widetilde\chi(\varepsilon_k)=1.             \tag{\(\mathsf{NB}_3\)}
\]
Under failure both full absolute Frobenius orbits in
\((\mathsf{NB}_1)\) lie in \(\ker\widetilde\chi\); conversely,
membership of either orbit already is the original failure.  Thus the
coordinate axes, the cyclic half-block sums supplied by \(C_k\), their
\((n-2)\)-term complements supplied by \(D\), and every determinant or
subspace-polynomial invariant of this change of normal basis provide no
second Kummer condition.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(\varepsilon=\eta+1\) and
\(\varepsilon^{1/2}=\sigma^{-1}(\varepsilon)\).  The Singer identity and
the selected identity
\(h_k=1+\beta_k=\eta_k^{q/2+1}\) give
\[
 (\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{1/2})^2
 =\varepsilon^2+\varepsilon
 =\eta^2+\eta=\eta^{q+2}=h_k^2.
\]
Squaring is injective, so
\[
 \beta_k=1+\varepsilon+\sigma^{-1}(\varepsilon).
\]
Since \(\operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/\F_2}(\varepsilon)=1\), this is
\(\beta_k=\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}\sigma^i(\varepsilon)=D(\sigma)\varepsilon\).

It remains to invert \(D\).  Its support in \(\mathbf Z/n\mathbf Z\) is
every residue except \(0,-1\).  If \(S\) is the support of \(C_k\), the
coefficient of \(X^r\) in \(C_kD\) is
\[
 |S|+\mathbf1_S(r)+\mathbf1_S(r+1)\pmod2.
\]
For \(n\equiv1\pmod4\),
\(S=\{0\}\cup\{1,3,\ldots,n-2\}\); for \(n\equiv3\pmod4\),
\(S=\{2,4,\ldots,n-1\}\).  In both cases \(|S|\) is odd, and the
displayed coefficient is one precisely at \(r=0\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{NB}_1)\); summing the geometric progression at \(\xi\)
proves \((\mathsf{NB}_2)\).  An invertible matrix over \(\F_2\) has
determinant one.

Finally,
\[
 \beta_k^{q-1}=\eta_k^{-1},\qquad
 \varepsilon_k=\eta_k^{q+1},
\]
and both \(q-1\) and \(q+1\) are units modulo \(\ell\).  Applying
\(\widetilde\chi\) proves \((\mathsf{NB}_3)\), and Frobenius stability
of its kernel proves the orbit formulation.  Lean kernel-checks the
three-term square-root bridge and the Singer exponent identity; the
universal cyclic support count and finite-field character interpretation
remain paper-level.

This exhausts linear Frobenius-equivariant normal-basis invariants.  It does
not rule out a nonlinear additive--multiplicative correlation tailored to
the selected half-block incidence, although current character bounds allow
the \(n\) prescribed orbit points.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[an actual-degree two-normal-basis countermodel]
\label{prop:cubic-two-normal-countermodel}
The complete incidence package in
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-two-normal-bases} is compatible with current
Kummer failure, even at an actual cubic-tower degree and an actual current
prime.

Use the polynomial-bit convention in which bit \(i\) is the coefficient of
\(X^i\), put
\[
 p(X)=\mathtt{0x329341f8b47dd7938af63},\qquad
 E=\F_2[X]/(p),\qquad q=2^{27},
\]
\[
 N=q^2+q+1=18014398643699713,\qquad \ell=2593,
\]
and in the polynomial basis set
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \eta&=1629469875507523981620540,\\
 \varepsilon&=1629469875507523981620541,\\
 \beta&=2293671573472151973449566.
 \end{aligned}
\]
Then \(p\) is irreducible of degree \(81\), \(\ell\) is a prime divisor
of \(N\), and
\[
 \eta^{q+1}=\eta+1,\qquad \eta^N=1,\qquad
 \eta^{N/\ell}=1.                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{NBC}_1\)}
\]
Moreover,
\[
 \varepsilon=\eta+1=\eta^{q+1},\qquad
 \beta^{q-1}=\eta^{-1},\qquad
 \beta=1+\eta^{q/2+1}
      =\sum_{i=1}^{79}\varepsilon^{2^i}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{NBC}_2\)}
\]
Thus the exact Singer, normalized inverse, and half-circulant identities
all hold, while \(\eta,\varepsilon,\beta\) lie in the
index-\(2593\) Kummer kernel of \(E^\times\).

The normality data are equally strong.  The absolute Moore determinant of
\(\varepsilon\) is one.  The relative Moore determinants of \(\beta\)
over the subfields of degrees \(1,3,9,27\) are respectively
\[
 1,\quad
 996154457819232351242539,\quad
 1913091018644430508445588,\quad
 1836908266524337706399502,                           \tag{\(\mathsf{NBC}_3\)}
\]
all nonzero.  Hence \(\varepsilon\) is normal over \(\F_2\) and
\(\beta\) is completely normal.

The missing datum is exactly the selected lower recursion.  If
\[
 a=\operatorname{Tr}_{E/\F_{2^{27}}}(\eta)
   =\eta+\eta^q+\eta^{q^2},
\]
then
\[
 a^{2^9+1}+a+1\ne0.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{NBC}_4\)}
\]
Thus the model exits the Conway-selected trace ancestry immediately below
the top step.  It proves that the two full Frobenius orbits, their nonlinear
kernel incidence, all Moore/subspace determinants, and the canonical
half-circulant relation add no obstruction independent of that ancestry.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The exact maintained verifier in \path{experiments/},
\path{cubic_two_normal_countermodel.py}, performs carryless
polynomial arithmetic modulo \(p\).  Rabin's criterion is certified by
\[
 X^{2^{81}}\equiv X\pmod p,\qquad
 \gcd(p,X^{2^{27}}-X)=1,
\]
which suffices because the only prime divisor of \(81\) is three.  It then
checks \((\mathsf{NBC}_1)\), \((\mathsf{NBC}_2)\), the three full-field
\(2593\)-power identities, the Moore determinants
\((\mathsf{NBC}_3)\), and the strict inequality
\((\mathsf{NBC}_4)\).

Lean independently kernel-checks the Rabin residues, the Singer, norm,
