For \(u=1\), one has \(\Delta_1=1\), recovering the prime-step
closure.  For composite \(N\), the displayed resultant remains a
selected nonvanishing assertion.  Primitivity of \(s_k\) alone cannot
force it: primitive base coefficients with nonprimitive norm-one roots
of \(X^3+sX^2+1\) already occur in finite fields.  Any induction must
use the full recursion \((\mathsf{NS}_5)\), not only the sparse cubic.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because \(3\nmid q-1\), cube roots are unique in \(K^\times\).
Substitute \(z_k=d_k\tau_k\) and \(z_{k-1}=d_k^3\) into
\((\mathsf{RS}_2)\), then divide by \(d_k^3\).  This gives
\((\mathsf{NS}_2)\); irreducibility follows because \(z_k\) and
\(\tau_k\) differ by a base scalar.  Its coefficients prove
\((\mathsf{NS}_3)\).  A base scalar is killed by \(q-1\), so
\((\mathsf{RS}_5)\) gives the first identity in
\((\mathsf{NS}_4)\).  On the norm-one group, both \(q-1\) and two
are coprime to \(N\); hence this power preserves order.

Taking norms in \(d_k^3=z_{k-1}\), using
\(\Norm(z_{k-1})=z_{k-2}\), and invoking uniqueness of cube roots
proves the first identity in \((\mathsf{NS}_5)\); squaring proves the
second.  Since \(z_{k-1}=d_{k-1}\tau_{k-1}\),
\[
 s_k^3=z_{k-1}^2=s_{k-1}\tau_{k-1}^2,
\]
which proves the recursion.  The equality \(s_k^3=z_{k-1}^2\) also
shows that \(s_k\) and \(z_{k-1}\) generate the same field: inverse
Frobenius recovers \(z_{k-1}\) from \(z_{k-1}^2\), while the unique
cube root in \(K^\times\) is a power and recovers \(s_k\) from
\(z_{k-1}^2\).  Under \(C_{k-1}\),
Corollary~\ref{cor:cubic-reciprocal-selector} makes \(z_{k-1}\)
primitive.  Squaring and cubing are automorphisms of the multiplicative
group \(K^\times\), so \(s_k\) is primitive as well.

For the rigidity assertion, the cubic at level one is irreducible, so its
three roots are one absolute-Frobenius orbit.  Inductively suppose the chosen
pair at level \(j-1\) is a compatible Frobenius conjugate of the selected
pair.  The recursion determines \(s_j\) uniquely, because cubing is an
automorphism of \(L_{j-1}^\times\), and gives the corresponding conjugate of
the selected coefficient.  The three roots of
\(X^3+s_jX^2+1\) are its relative-Frobenius orbit, hence exactly the three
extensions of that lower embedding to \(L_j\).  Choosing any of them extends
the Frobenius-compatible chain and proves the induction.

The two identities \(c^3=r\) and \(r^\ell=z_{k-1}=d_k^3\) give
\((c^\ell)^3=d_k^3\).  Cube-root uniqueness proves \(c^\ell=d_k\),
and substitution in \((\mathsf{KT}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{KT}_4)\)
proves \((\mathsf{NS}_6)\).
Finally \((\mathsf{RS}_5)\), together with
\(\ord(\tau_k)=\ord(\eta_k)\), proves the displayed equivalence
between \(C_k\) and the lower-level assertion plus \(\ord(\tau_k)=N\).

The conjugates of \(\tau_k^u\) have polynomial
\[
 X^3+A_u(s_k)X^2+B_u(s_k)X+1.
\]
Its characteristic-two discriminant is \(\Delta_u(s_k)^2\), and the
odd-index form of \((\mathsf{IF}_3)\) identifies \(\Delta_u(s_k)\) with the Jacobian
of \(\Psi_{u,1}\) at \((s_k,0)\).  It vanishes exactly when two
conjugates collide.  In that event \(\tau_k^u\in K^\times\), while
also \(\tau_k^u\) has norm one; since
\(K^\times\cap R^1_{L/K}\mathbb G_m=\{1\}\), this is precisely
\(\Omega_{k,\ell}=1\).  The converse is immediate, proving
\((\mathsf{NS}_8)\).

Put \(\delta=\tau_k^{q-1}=\eta_k^{-2}\).  The product of the three
pairwise differences of the conjugates of \(\tau_k^u\) is
\[
 \Norm_{L/K}(1+\delta^u).
\]
This is \((\mathsf{NS}_9)\).  Equivalently its denominator relative
to \(u=1\) is one, since
\[
 \Norm(1+\eta_k^{-2})
 =\Norm(1+\eta_k)^2=1;
\]
the last equality follows by evaluating the selected reciprocal cubic
of \(\eta_k\) at one.  Finally a nonzero element of the finite field
generated by \(s_k\) has absolute norm one, while zero has norm zero.
The product formula for the resultant proves
\((\mathsf{NS}_{10})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[trace-recursive reparameterization of the Singer class]
\label{cor:cubic-trace-recursive-singer}
Set \(\gamma_0=1\), \(h_0=0\), and, for \(k\geq1\),
\[
 h_k=1+\gamma_k^{-1}.
\]
Then \(h_k\) has the irreducible relative cubic
\[
 \boxed{X^3+h_{k-1}X^2+X+1}                        \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_1\)}
\]
over \(L_{k-1}\).  Consequently
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(h_k)=h_{k-1},\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(h_k^{-1})=1,\qquad
 \Norm(h_k)=1.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_2\)}
\]
In the notation of Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-normalized-singer},
\[
 \boxed{
 \tau_k^3=h_k^4,\qquad
 h_k=\frac{1+\zeta_k^3}{(1+\zeta_k)^3},\qquad
 h_k^2=\eta_k^{q+2},\qquad
 h_k=\eta_k^{q/2+1}.}                               \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_3\)}
\]
All exponents in the last line are invertible on the norm-one group:
\[
 \gcd(3,N)=\gcd(4,N)=\gcd(q/2+1,N)=1.
\]
Hence
\[
 \boxed{\ord(\tau_k)=\ord(h_k)=\ord(\eta_k).}        \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_4\)}
\]
Thus \(h_k\) gives a particularly sparse trace-recursive tower, but it is an
invertible-power reparameterization of the same selected order coordinate,
not an inductive order estimate.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Substitute \(\gamma_k=(h_k+1)^{-1}\) into
\(\gamma_k^3+\gamma_k+\gamma_{k-1}=0\), clear \((h_k+1)^3\), and use
\(\gamma_{k-1}=(h_{k-1}+1)^{-1}\).  This gives
\((\mathsf{HTR}_1)\).  The fractional-linear substitution preserves the
relative degree, so the cubic is irreducible; its coefficients give
\((\mathsf{HTR}_2)\).

The identities \(\gamma_{k-1}=\gamma_k(\gamma_k+1)^2\) and
\(z_j=\gamma_j^{-2}\) give
\[
 \tau_k^3=\frac{z_k^3}{z_{k-1}}
 =\left(1+\gamma_k^{-1}\right)^4=h_k^4.
\]
The displayed formula in \(\zeta_k\) is a direct simplification of
\(1+\gamma_k^{-1}\).  Using
\(\eta_k=\gamma_k^{q-1}\) and
\(\gamma_k^N=\gamma_{k-1}\) gives \(h_k^2=\eta_k^{q+2}\); injectivity of
squaring gives the last formula.  The gcds are immediate except possibly the
last: a common divisor of \(q/2+1\) and \(N=q^2+q+1\) divides three, while
\(q/2+1\not\equiv0\pmod3\).  The order equalities now follow from
\((\mathsf{HTR}_3)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-branch norm descent of the cubic critical value]
\label{prop:cubic-critical-norm-descent}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-normalized-singer}.  For every positive
odd \(u\), the critical polynomial in \((\mathsf{NS}_7)\) has a unique
compression
\[
 \boxed{\Delta_u(S)=\widehat\Delta_u(S^3)},\qquad
 \widehat\Delta_u(V)\in\F_2[V].                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_1\)}
\]
For independent indeterminates \(V,Y\), define an operator
\(\mathcal C:\F_2[V]\to\F_2[V]\) by
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C(F)(V)
 =\operatorname{Res}_Y\!\left(
   Y^3+VY^2+V,F(Y)
  \right).}                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_2\)}
\]
At every actual normalized edge \(j\geq2\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C(F)(s_{j-1}^3)
 =\Norm_{L_{j-1}/L_{j-2}}F(s_j^3).}                \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_3\)}
\]

Now let \(u=N/\ell\) be a current value at level \(k\).  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C^{\circ(k-1)}(\widehat\Delta_u)(1)
 =\Norm_{L_{k-1}/\F_2}\Delta_u(s_k).}              \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_4\)}
\]
For \(k=1\), the left side means zero iterations and \(s_1=1\).  Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 \Omega_{k,\ell}=1
 \iff
 \mathcal C^{\circ(k-1)}(\widehat\Delta_u)(1)=0,}  \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_5\)}
\]
and desired nonfailure is exactly the terminal value one.

This is stricter than the unstructured resultant in
\((\mathsf{NS}_{10})\): one fixed cubic norm operator carries the selected
critical value through every actual normalized ancestry edge.  It is still a
lossless descent, not a universal nonvanishing theorem.  The terminal bit is
the same selected order coordinate, and its degree is not forced below the
minimal polynomial by the recursion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Give \(C,D,E\) the weights \(1,2,3\).  The recurrence
\((\mathsf{IF}_2)\) makes \(T_n(C,D,E)\) homogeneous of weight \(n\).
Therefore every exponent of \(S\) in \(A_u(S)\) is congruent to \(u\)
modulo three, while every exponent in \(B_u(S)\) is congruent to \(2u\).
Every exponent in \(A_uB_u+1\) is consequently divisible by three, proving
the existence and uniqueness in \((\mathsf{CND}_1)\).

Put \(U_j=s_j^3=z_{j-1}^2\).  Squaring the selected irreducible cubic
\((\mathsf{RS}_2)\) for \(z_{j-1}\) shows that, over \(L_{j-2}\),
the element \(U_j\) has the irreducible polynomial
\[
 Y^3+U_{j-1}Y^2+U_{j-1}.
\]
The monic root-product formula for \((\mathsf{CND}_2)\) therefore gives
\((\mathsf{CND}_3)\) with no sign or leading factor.  Iteration and transitivity of
norms give \((\mathsf{CND}_4)\), using \(s_1=1\).  Finally
\((\mathsf{NS}_8)\) says that \(\Omega_{k,\ell}=1\) exactly when
\(\Delta_u(s_k)=0\); a finite-field norm vanishes exactly with its argument,
and every nonzero element of \(\F_2\) is one.  This proves
\((\mathsf{CND}_5)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[cyclotomic factorization of the cubic critical value]
\label{prop:cubic-critical-cyclotomic-factorization}
Let \(u\geq1\) be odd and \(d=(u-1)/2\).  There is a unique monic
\(H_u\in\F_2[X]\) such that
\[
 \mathcal D_u(X,1)=XH_u(X)^2,\qquad
 \deg H_u=d,\qquad H_u(0)=1.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_1\)}
\]
Its roots are the distinct nonzero elements
\[
 x_\rho=\rho+\rho^{-1},
 \qquad
 \{\rho,\rho^{-1}\}\subset\mu_u\setminus\{1\},   \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_2\)}
\]
where all nonidentity \(u\)-th roots, not only primitive roots, occur.  The
critical compression in Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-critical-norm-descent}
has the exact factorization, with multiplicity,
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \widehat\Delta_u(V)
 &=\operatorname{Res}_X\!\left(
   H_u(X),VX+(X+1)^3
  \right)\\
 &=\prod_{\{\rho,\rho^{-1}\}}
   \left(x_\rho V+(x_\rho+1)^3\right)\\
 &=\prod_{\{\rho,\rho^{-1}\}}
   \left(V+\frac{(x_\rho+1)^3}{x_\rho}\right).
 \end{aligned}}                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_3\)}
\]
The last equality uses \(\prod x_\rho=H_u(0)=1\).  The displayed critical
values need not be distinct; for example
\(\widehat\Delta_7(V)=(V+1)^3\).

The selected specialization remembers the actual Conway cubic exactly.  For
arbitrary \(z,Y\) in characteristic two, put
\[
 C_V(X)=X^3+X^2+(V+1)X+1.
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 C_{z^2}(1+zY^2)
 =z^3(Y^3+Y)^2+z^2.}                                \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_4\)}
\]
At level \(k\), take
\(z=z_{k-1}=\gamma_{k-1}^{-2}\) and
\(V=s_k^3=z_{k-1}^2\).  Because \(z\gamma_{k-1}^2=1\), this becomes
\[
 C_V(1+zY^2)=z^3(Y^3+Y+\gamma_{k-1})^2.             \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_5\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 \widehat\Delta_u(s_k^3)
 =\Norm_{L_k/L_{k-1}}
   H_u\!\left(1+z_{k-1}\gamma_k^2\right).}          \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_6\)}
\]
For \(u=N/\ell\), failure is equivalently that the selected cubic
\(C_{s_k^3}\) shares a root with \(H_u\).  This reconstructs the exact
cyclotomic collision behind the terminal bit; it does not force the resultant
nonzero when \(u\) is composite.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The standard Dickson factorization
\(\mathcal D_u(\rho+\rho^{-1},1)=\rho^u+\rho^{-u}\), with reciprocal
pairs grouped together, gives \((\mathsf{CCF}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{CCF}_2)\).
Let \(t_1,t_2,t_3\) be the roots of
\(T^3+ST^2+1\), so \(e_1=S,e_2=0,e_3=1\).  By the definitions in
\((\mathsf{NS}_7)\),
\[
 \Delta_u(S)
 =\prod_{i<j}(t_i^u+t_j^u).
\]
Factor each power difference over \(\mu_u\), remove the \(\rho=1\) layer,
and pair \(\rho\) with \(\rho^{-1}\).  For
\(x=\rho+\rho^{-1}\), the resulting symmetric product is
\[
 \prod_{i<j}(t_i^2+xt_it_j+t_j^2)=xS^3+(x+1)^3.
\]
Indeed \(e_2=0\) gives \(t_it_j=t_k(t_i+t_j)\); each factor is then
\((S+t_k)(S+(x+1)t_k)\), and multiplication over \(k\) gives the displayed
identity.  Taking the product over the roots of the monic \(H_u\) proves the
resultant and both products in \((\mathsf{CCF}_3)\), including multiplicity.

Expanding \(C_{z^2}(1+zY^2)\) proves \((\mathsf{CCF}_4)\).  The selected
relation \(z\gamma_{k-1}^2=1\) gives \((\mathsf{CCF}_5)\).  The roots of the
right-hand Conway cubic are the three relative conjugates of \(\gamma_k\),
so its transported roots are precisely the roots of the monic \(C_V\).
The root-product formula for the resultant now yields
\((\mathsf{CCF}_6)\).  Finally \((\mathsf{NS}_8)\) identifies its vanishing
with the original Euler obstruction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the full ancestral Jacobian line collapses]
\label{prop:cubic-ancestral-jacobian-line}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dickson-iterate}, put
\(f(X)=X^3+X\), and extend its notation by
\(P_0(X)=X+1\), so that
\(P_i(X)=f^{\circ i}(X)+1\) for every \(i\geq0\).
For \(m\geq0\), the Dickson square root from
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-critical-cyclotomic-factorization} has the
exact factorization
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{gathered}
 f^{\circ m}(X)=XH_{3^m}(X)^2,
 \qquad H_{3^m}(X)=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}P_i(X),\\
 (f^{\circ m})'(X)=H_{3^m}(X)^2.
 \end{gathered}}                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_1\)}
\]

Put \(\gamma_0=1\).  For \(1\leq m\leq k\), define
\[
 R_{k,m}:=H_{3^m}(\gamma_k),
 \qquad J_{k,m}:=(f^{\circ m})'(\gamma_k).
\]
Then the complete selected ancestry gives
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 R_{k,m}
   &=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}(\gamma_{k-i}+1)
     =(\gamma_k+1)c_{k,m},\\
 c_{k,m}
   &:=\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(\gamma_{k-i}+1)
     \in L_{k-1}^{\times},\\
 J_{k,m}=R_{k,m}^2
   &=\frac{\gamma_{k-m}}{\gamma_k}.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_2\)}
\]
In particular, all nonempty ancestral square roots span the one-dimensional
\(L_{k-1}\)-space \((\gamma_k+1)L_{k-1}\), and all partial Jacobians span
\((\gamma_k+1)^2L_{k-1}\).  At the full depth,
\[
 R_{k,k}=\prod_{j=1}^{k}(\gamma_j+1),
 \qquad J_{k,k}=\gamma_k^{-1}=\beta_k.              \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_3\)}
\]

Now let \(\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)\) be prime, put
\(q=2^{3^{k-1}}\), and define
\[
 \mathcal E_\ell(x)
 :=x^{(2^{3^k}-1)/\ell}\in\mu_\ell.
\]
For every \(1\leq m\leq k\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal E_\ell(J_{k,m})
   =\mathcal E_\ell(\gamma_k)^{-1},
 \qquad
 \mathcal E_\ell(R_{k,m})^2
   =\mathcal E_\ell(\gamma_k)^{-1}.}                \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_4\)}
\]
Since \(\ell\) is odd and
\(\eta_k=\gamma_k^{q-1}\), with
\(\gcd(q-1,\ell)=1\), this gives the exact equivalence
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal E_\ell(R_{k,m})=1
 \iff \mathcal E_\ell(J_{k,m})=1
 \iff \mathcal E_\ell(\eta_k)=1.}                 \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_5\)}
\]

More generally, let
\(F\in L_{k-1}[T_1,\ldots,T_k]\) be homogeneous of total degree \(d\).
Whenever the displayed evaluations are nonzero, homogeneity and
\((\mathsf{AJ}_2)\) give
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal E_\ell\bigl(F(R_{k,1},\ldots,R_{k,k})\bigr)
   &=\mathcal E_\ell(\gamma_k+1)^d,\\
 \mathcal E_\ell\bigl(F(J_{k,1},\ldots,J_{k,k})\bigr)
   &=\mathcal E_\ell(\gamma_k)^{-d}.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_6\)}
\]
Thus if \(\ell\nmid d\), either residue test is one exactly on the
original current failure; if \(\ell\mid d\), it is identically one.
The coefficients of \(F\) may depend nonsymmetrically on every separate
lower selected ancestor.  Therefore partial iterated Jacobians, their
canonical Dickson square roots, lower-ancestry linear combinations, and
homogeneous determinants supply no second current Kummer coordinate.

This is a homogeneous residue-class collapse only.  It does not evaluate
whether a selected homogeneous expression is zero, does not cover sums of
different total degrees, and does not exclude a correlation using other
top-point data.  In particular it does not prove the required
\(\mathcal E_\ell(\eta_k)\ne1\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In characteristic two,
\[
 f(Y)=Y^3+Y=Y(Y+1)^2,
 \qquad f'(Y)=Y^2+1=(Y+1)^2.
\]
Starting with \(f^{\circ0}(X)=X\), induction therefore gives
\[
 f^{\circ(m+1)}(X)
 =f^{\circ m}(X)\bigl(f^{\circ m}(X)+1\bigr)^2
 =X\left(\prod_{i=0}^{m}P_i(X)\right)^2.
\]
The product is monic with constant term one, so uniqueness in
\((\mathsf{CCF}_1)\) identifies it with \(H_{3^{m+1}}\).
Differentiating \(XH_{3^m}^2\) in characteristic two proves the last
identity in \((\mathsf{AJ}_1)\).

The selected recursion says
\(f^{\circ i}(\gamma_k)=\gamma_{k-i}\) for \(0\leq i\leq k\).
Evaluation of \((\mathsf{AJ}_1)\) gives the product formulas in
\((\mathsf{AJ}_2)\); division of
\(\gamma_{k-m}=\gamma_kR_{k,m}^2\) gives its last equality.
All factors are nonzero.  Separating the \(i=0\) factor puts every remaining
factor in \(L_{k-1}^{\times}\), proving the line assertions and
\((\mathsf{AJ}_3)\).

For a current prime, \(\ord_\ell(2)=3^k\).  Hence
\(\ell\nmid|L_{k-1}^{\times}|\), so every nonzero lower-field coefficient
is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(L_k\) and has \(\mathcal E_\ell\)-value one.
Applying \(\mathcal E_\ell\) to \((\mathsf{AJ}_2)\) proves
\((\mathsf{AJ}_4)\).  The square map on \(\mu_\ell\) is injective, and
\[
 \mathcal E_\ell(\eta_k)
 =\mathcal E_\ell(\gamma_k)^{q-1}
\]
with \(q-1\) invertible modulo \(\ell\), proving
\((\mathsf{AJ}_5)\).

Finally, \((\mathsf{AJ}_2)\) and homogeneity give the exact factorizations
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 F(R_{k,1},\ldots,R_{k,k})
   &=(\gamma_k+1)^dF(1,c_{k,2},\ldots,c_{k,k}),\\
 F(J_{k,1},\ldots,J_{k,k})
   &=(\gamma_k+1)^{2d}
     F(1,c_{k,2}^2,\ldots,c_{k,k}^2).
 \end{aligned}
\]
The remaining factors lie in \(L_{k-1}\).  Applying
\(\mathcal E_\ell\), and using
\(\mathcal E_\ell(\gamma_k+1)^2
 =\mathcal E_\ell(\gamma_k)^{-1}\), proves
\((\mathsf{AJ}_6)\) and the stated dichotomy.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the mixed ancestral algebra is exactly the selected cubic field]
\label{prop:cubic-mixed-ancestral-presentation}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-ancestral-jacobian-line}, put
\[
 K=L_{k-1},\qquad z=R_{k,1}=\gamma_k+1,
\]
and extend the notation there by \(c_{k,1}=1\).  Then every
\(c_{k,m}\) lies in \(K^\times\), and
\[
 R_{k,m}=c_{k,m}z,\qquad J_{k,m}=R_{k,m}^2
 \quad(1\leq m\leq k).                              \tag{\(\mathsf{AMA}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 K[R_{k,1},\ldots,R_{k,k},J_{k,1},\ldots,J_{k,k}]
 =K[z]=L_k.                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{AMA}_2\)}
\]
This equality has the following exact formal presentation.  Let
\[
 \operatorname{ev}:K[T_1,\ldots,T_k,U_1,\ldots,U_k]\longrightarrow L_k,
 \qquad T_m\longmapsto R_{k,m},\quad U_m\longmapsto J_{k,m}.
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\ker(\operatorname{ev})=
 \bigl(T_m-c_{k,m}T_1\ (2\leq m\leq k),
       U_m-T_m^2\ (1\leq m\leq k),
       T_1^3+T_1^2+\gamma_{k-1}\bigr).}             \tag{\(\mathsf{AMA}_3\)}
\]
Thus every formal mixed-degree ancestral polynomial has, modulo this
kernel, a unique representative
\[
 A+BT_1+CT_1^2,\qquad A,B,C\in K,                   \tag{\(\mathsf{AMA}_4\)}
\]
and its selected evaluation in \(L_k\) vanishes exactly when
\(A=B=C=0\).  The restriction to the selected evaluation is essential:
the nonzero formal polynomial
\[
 T_1^3+T_1^2+\gamma_{k-1}
\]
already vanishes there.

Moreover the first ancestral root recovers the original selected endpoint:
\[
 \gamma_k=z+1,
 \qquad
 \mathcal E_\ell(\eta_k)
 =\bigl((z+1)^{q-1}\bigr)^{(q^3-1)/\ell},
 \qquad q=2^{3^{k-1}}.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{AMA}_5\)}
\]
Hence allowing zeros and arbitrary mixtures of ancestral degrees supplies
neither a larger coordinate algebra nor an independent Kummer coordinate:
it recovers the whole selected cubic field, including the original Euler
endpoint itself.  This is a presentation theorem, not a nonvanishing theorem.
A mixed-degree proof could still succeed by proving that a specific quadratic
normal remainder in \((\mathsf{AMA}_4)\) is nonzero; the proposition says
precisely that such a step is new selected arithmetic, not a consequence of
the ancestral generators or their formal relations.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation \((\mathsf{AMA}_1)\) is \((\mathsf{AJ}_2)\), with
\(c_{k,1}=1\); its displayed product also proves
\(c_{k,m}\in K^\times\).  Since \(T_1\) maps to \(z\), this proves
\((\mathsf{AMA}_2)\).

The minimal polynomial \(X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}\) of \(\gamma_k\) over
\(K\), translated by \(X\mapsto X+1\), is
\[
 X^3+X^2+\gamma_{k-1},
\]
which is again irreducible.  Therefore every listed generator in
\((\mathsf{AMA}_3)\) lies in \(\ker(\operatorname{ev})\).  Conversely,
after quotienting by the listed ideal, the linear relations eliminate
\(T_2,\ldots,T_k\), the quadratic relations eliminate every \(U_m\), and
the last relation leaves exactly
\[
 K[T_1]/(T_1^3+T_1^2+\gamma_{k-1}).
\]
The induced map from this quotient to \(K[z]=L_k\) is the standard
minimal-polynomial isomorphism.  This proves the reverse ideal containment,
hence \((\mathsf{AMA}_3)\), and Euclidean division by the monic cubic gives
\((\mathsf{AMA}_4)\).  Its uniqueness follows equally from the
\(K\)-linear independence of \(1,z,z^2\).  Finally \(z=\gamma_k+1\) and
\(q^3=2^{3^k}\), together with \(\eta_k=\gamma_k^{q-1}\), give
\((\mathsf{AMA}_5)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the selected Singer trace is the whole remaining phase]
\label{prop:cubic-prescribed-trace}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-wendt-singer}.  For \(a\in\F_q\)
put
\[
 D_a=\{x\in D:\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(x)=a\}.
\]
Each \(D_a\) is empty or one three-element relative-Frobenius orbit.
Let \(\chi:U\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order \(\ell\), where
\(\ord_\ell(q)=3\), and put \(H=\ker\chi\).  If \(D_a\ne\varnothing\),
define
\[
 \Theta_\chi(a)=\sum_{x\in D_a}\chi(x).
\]
Then
\[
 D_a\subset H\iff\Theta_\chi(a)=3.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{ST}_1\)}
\]
In particular, current failure is exactly
\[
 \Theta_\chi(\eta_{j-1})=3.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{ST}_2\)}
\]
For any nontrivial additive character \(\psi\) of \(\F_q\), Fourier
inversion gives
\[
 \Theta_\chi(a)
 =\frac1q\sum_{b\in\F_q}\psi(-ba)
   \sum_{x\in D}\chi(x)
      \psi\!\left(b\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(x)\right).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ST}_3\)}
\]
Whenever the moment criterion \((\mathsf{SW}_4)\) forces
\(D\cap H\ne\varnothing\), some trace fibre actually attains the
extremal value \(3\).  Hence a uniform character bound cannot exclude
\((\mathsf{ST}_2)\); one must distinguish the selected Conway fibre.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The trace-\(a\) elements of \(D\) are the roots of
\[
 T^3+aT^2+(a+1)T+1.
\]
Relative Frobenius has no fixed point on \(D\): an element of
\(D\cap\F_q\) would lie in \(\F_q^\times\cap U=\{1\}\), while
\(1\notin D\).  Thus every nonempty fibre is one orbit of length
three.

For \(x\in D_a\), put \(\theta=\chi(x)\).  Then
\[
 \Theta_\chi(a)=\theta+\theta^q+\theta^{q^2}.
\]
The fibre lies in \(H\) exactly when \(\theta=1\).  Conversely, equality
to \(3\) in the displayed sum forces all three unit complex numbers to
equal one, by equality in the triangle inequality.  This proves
\((\mathsf{ST}_1)\).  The distinguished fibre is the relative orbit of
\(\eta_j\), whose trace is \(\eta_{j-1}\), proving
\((\mathsf{ST}_2)\).  Character orthogonality on the additive group of
\(\F_q\) gives \((\mathsf{ST}_3)\).  Finally, if \(x\in D\cap H\), its
whole relative orbit lies in \(H\), so its trace fibre has
\(\Theta_\chi=3\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the recursive trace--Fourier flag collapses at the top edge]
\label{prop:cubic-trace-fourier-flag-collapse}
Retain Propositions~\ref{prop:eta-tower},
\ref{prop:cubic-wendt-factor}, and
\ref{prop:cubic-prescribed-trace}.  Put
\[
 L_j=\F_{2^{3^j}},\qquad q=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad
 N=q^2+q+1,
\]
and write
\[
 D_k=\{x\in L_k^\times:x^{1+q+q^2}=1,\ x^{q+1}+x+1=0\}.
\]
For \(0\leq j<k\), let
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{k/j}=\operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/L_j}\) and define the
complete lower trace flag
\[
 \mathfrak T_k(x)=\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}_{k/j}(x)\bigr)_{0\leq j<k}.
\]
Trace transitivity gives the exact factorization
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak T_k(x)
 =\Bigl(
   \operatorname{Tr}_{L_{k-1}/L_j}
     \bigl(\operatorname{Tr}_{k/(k-1)}(x)\bigr)
   \Bigr)_{0\leq j<k}.}                              \tag{\(\mathsf{RTF}_1\)}
\]
Consequently the literal full-ancestry fibre is already the immediate
trace fibre:
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal F_k
 &:=\{x\in D_k:\operatorname{Tr}_{k/j}(x)=\eta_j
                  \text{ for every }0\leq j<k\}\\
 &=\{x\in D_k:\operatorname{Tr}_{k/(k-1)}(x)=\eta_{k-1}\}\\
 &=\{\eta_k,\eta_k^q,\eta_k^{q^2}\}.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{RTF}_2\)}
\]

This collapse holds before choosing a multiplicative character and is exact
for every weighting which depends only on the iterated trace tuple
\(\mathfrak T_k(x)\).  Its additive Fourier form is especially sharp.  Put
\[
 \psi_j(y)=(-1)^{\operatorname{Tr}_{L_j/\F_2}(y)}.
\]
For arbitrary \(b_j\in L_j\), embedded in \(L_{k-1}\), one has
\[
 \boxed{
 \prod_{j=0}^{k-1}
 \psi_j\!\left(b_j\operatorname{Tr}_{k/j}(x)\right)
 =\psi_{k-1}\!\left(
   \left(\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}b_j\right)
   \operatorname{Tr}_{k/(k-1)}(x)
 \right).}                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{RTF}_3\)}
\]
Thus stacking additive Fourier modes from every lower trace level creates
no mode beyond the single immediate-edge additive character in
\((\mathsf{ST}_3)\).

Let \(\ell\mid N\) be prime, put \(u=N/\ell\), let
\(\chi:U_k\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order \(\ell\), and write
\(\theta=\chi(\eta_k)\).  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{x\in\mathcal F_k}\chi(x)
 =\theta+\theta^q+\theta^{q^2}
 =\Theta_\chi(\eta_{k-1}).}                          \tag{\(\mathsf{RTF}_4\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 C\text{-failure at }(k,\ell)
 &\iff \theta=1
 \iff \mathcal F_k\subset\ker\chi\\
 &\iff \sum_{x\in\mathcal F_k}\chi(x)=3
 \iff P_{\eta,k}\mid G_u.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{RTF}_5\)}
\]
Hence the complete literal lower trace ancestry, its arbitrary additive
multi-Fourier projectors, and the distinguished Wendt incidence all return
to the same selected three-point phase orbit.  This does not cover a weight
which depends on the top point through data other than its iterated traces,
a nonsymmetric chosen-root expression, or a genuinely mixed
additive--multiplicative correlation between the top point and separate
lower ancestors.  Nor does it evaluate
\(\Theta_\chi(\eta_{k-1})\); the selected nonvanishing remains open.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(j<k\), trace transitivity is
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{k/j}
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{L_{k-1}/L_j}
    \circ\operatorname{Tr}_{k/(k-1)},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{RTF}_1)\).  The selected tower satisfies
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{L_i/L_{i-1}}(\eta_i)=\eta_{i-1}\) at every edge.
Therefore the immediate equality
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{k/(k-1)}(x)=\eta_{k-1}\) forces every lower
equality in the first line of \((\mathsf{RTF}_2)\), and the converse is
immediate.

For \(x\in D_k\) with relative trace \(a\), its relative orbit has cubic
\[
 X^3+aX^2+(a+1)X+1.
\]
At \(a=\eta_{k-1}\), this is the selected irreducible cubic of
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}; its roots are
\(\eta_k,\eta_k^q,\eta_k^{q^2}\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{RTF}_2)\).

For the Fourier assertion, trace transitivity and \(L_j\)-linearity give
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L_j/\F_2}\!\left(
   b_j\operatorname{Tr}_{k/j}(x)\right)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{L_k/\F_2}(b_jx)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{L_{k-1}/\F_2}\!\left(
   b_j\operatorname{Tr}_{k/(k-1)}(x)
 \right).
\]
Summing over \(j\) and exponentiating by \(-1\) proves
\((\mathsf{RTF}_3)\).

Finally multiplicativity gives
\(\chi(\eta_k^{q^i})=\theta^{q^i}\), proving
\((\mathsf{RTF}_4)\).  Since
\(\ker\chi=U_k^\ell\), failure is \(\theta=1\).  Equality of the
three-term sum with three forces the three complex unit summands to be one,
by equality in the triangle inequality.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-wendt-factor}
identifies the same failure with \(P_{\eta,k}\mid G_u\), proving
\((\mathsf{RTF}_5)\).

Lean kernel-checks the abstract compatible-fibre equality, factorization of
a full flag weight through its immediate coordinate, and summation of linear
Fourier functionals after composition with one top map.  Finite-field trace
transitivity, the Singer-fibre specialization, and the character/Wendt
identifications remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the selected trace hyperplane is self-polar]
\label{prop:cubic-self-polar-hyperplane}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}.  Put
\[
 F=L_{k-1}=\F_q,\qquad E=L_k=\F_{q^3},\qquad
 q=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad \gamma=\gamma_k,
\]
and let \(\rho:E^\times\to\mu_\ell\subset\mathbb C^\times\) be the exact
current character of Proposition~\ref{prop:character-test}.  Thus \(\rho\)
is nontrivial and \(\rho|_{F^\times}=1\).  For \(a\in E^\times\), put
\[
 H_a=\{x\in E:\operatorname{Tr}_{E/F}(ax)=0\},\qquad
 \Sigma_a(\rho)=\sum_{[x]\in H_a^\times/F^\times}\rho(x).
 \tag{\(\mathsf{CPH}_1\)}
\]
The summand is independent of the representative because \(\rho\) is
trivial on \(F^\times\).  If \(\psi:F\to\mathbb C^\times\) is any nontrivial
additive character and
\[
 G_\psi(\rho)=\sum_{x\in E^\times}\rho(x)
       \psi\!\left(\operatorname{Tr}_{E/F}(x)\right),
\]
then
\[
 \boxed{\Sigma_a(\rho)=q^{-1}\rho(a)^{-1}G_\psi(\rho),
 \qquad |\Sigma_a(\rho)|=\sqrt q.}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{CPH}_2\)}
\]
In particular the orientation is inverse in \(a\).

The selected depressed cubic implies
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E/F}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}_{E/F}(\gamma^2)=0,
 \qquad
 \boxed{H_\gamma=F+F\gamma.}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{CPH}_3\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 \frac{\Sigma_\gamma(\rho)}{\Sigma_1(\rho)}=\rho(\gamma)^{-1},
 \qquad
 C\text{-failure at }(k,\ell)
 \iff \Sigma_\gamma(\rho)=\Sigma_1(\rho).}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{CPH}_4\)}
\]
Moreover \(F+F(\gamma+c)=F+F\gamma\) for every \(c\in F\).  Hence every
projective-line correlation obtained by translating the selected top
coordinate by an arbitrary lower-ancestry scalar gives the same
\(\Sigma_\gamma\).  The mixed additive--multiplicative hyperplane method
therefore recovers the selected Euler phase exactly, but does not evaluate it.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since every \(F^\times\)-orbit in \(H_a^\times\) has size \(q-1\),
\[
 \Sigma_a(\rho)=\frac1{q-1}\sum_{x\in E^\times}
 \rho(x)\,\mathbf1_{\operatorname{Tr}(ax)=0}.
\]
Additive orthogonality gives
\[
 \mathbf1_{\operatorname{Tr}(ax)=0}=\frac1q\sum_{u\in F}
 \psi\!\left(u\operatorname{Tr}(ax)\right).
\]
The \(u=0\) term vanishes because \(\rho\) is nontrivial.  For
\(u\in F^\times\), substituting \(y=uax\) and using \(\rho(u)=1\) gives
\[
 \sum_{x\in E^\times}\rho(x)\psi(\operatorname{Tr}(uax))
 =\rho(a)^{-1}G_\psi(\rho).
\]
There are \(q-1\) such values of \(u\), proving the first identity in
\((\mathsf{CPH}_2)\).  The lifted additive character
\(x\mapsto\psi(\operatorname{Tr}(x))\) is nontrivial; the standard finite-field Gauss-sum
identity therefore gives \(|G_\psi(\rho)|=|E|^{1/2}=q^{3/2}\), proving the
absolute value.

The relative minimal polynomial of \(\gamma\) is
\(X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}\).  Its zero quadratic coefficient gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma)=0\), and
\(\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma^2)=\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma)^2=0\).  Thus
\(F+F\gamma\subseteq H_\gamma\).  The left side has \(F\)-dimension two
because \(\gamma\notin F\); the right side has dimension two because the
finite-field trace pairing is nondegenerate and \(\gamma\ne0\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{CPH}_3)\).  Dividing the two nonzero instances of
\((\mathsf{CPH}_2)\), with \(a=\gamma,1\), gives
\((\mathsf{CPH}_4)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:character-test} identifies
\(\rho(\gamma)=1\) with current failure.  Finally
\(F+F(\gamma+c)=F+F\gamma\) is immediate.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact one-step lifting of the previous Singer set]
\label{prop:cubic-previous-singer-lift}
Let \(j\geq2\), put \(r=2^{3^{j-2}}\), \(q=r^3\), and fix
\(\omega\in\F_4\setminus\F_2\).  For \(s\in\{r,q\}\), write
\[
 U_s=\ker(\Norm_{\F_{s^3}/\F_s}),\qquad
 D_s=\{x\in U_s:x^{s+1}+x+1=0\}.
\]
In a common algebraic closure put
\[
 M(T)=\frac{\omega+\omega^2T}{1+T}.
\]
Then \(M\) gives a bijection
\[
 \{t:t^{s+1}=\omega^2\}\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}D_s,
\]
and, for \(t^{q+1}=\omega^2\),
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(M(t))=M(t^3).   \tag{\(\mathsf{SL}_1\)}
\]
Consequently the restricted trace map
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}:\operatorname{Tr}^{-1}(D_r)\cap D_q\longrightarrow D_r
\]
is surjective with every fibre of cardinality exactly three, and
\[
 |\operatorname{Tr}^{-1}(D_r)\cap D_q|=3(r+1).      \tag{\(\mathsf{SL}_2\)}
\]
Thus precisely \(r+1\) of the \((q+1)/3\) nonempty current Singer
trace fibres lie over the previous Singer set.  In particular, the
selected coefficient's membership in \(D_r\) does not distinguish its
current fibre: every previous Singer point has a current three-point
fibre satisfying the same norm-one, Singer, and cubic-trace identities.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(x\in D_s\), put
\[
 t=\frac{x+\omega}{x+\omega^2}.
\]
Since \(s\) is a power of two with odd exponent,
\(\omega^s=\omega^2\).  Using
\(x^s=(x+1)/x\) gives \(t^s=\omega^2/t\), and hence
\(t^{s+1}=\omega^2\) and \(x=M(t)\).  Conversely,
\(t^{s+1}=\omega^2\) gives
\[
 M(t)^s=\frac{M(t)+1}{M(t)}.
\]
Iterating this order-three Möbius map shows that \(M(t)\) belongs to
\(\F_{s^3}\), has norm one, and lies in \(D_s\).  This proves the
bijection.

For \(x=M(t)\in D_q\), put
\(a=\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(x)\).  The relative orbit has cubic
\[
 X^3+aX^2+(a+1)X+1.
\]
Substitution and cross-multiplication give
\[
 \left(\frac{x+\omega}{x+\omega^2}\right)^3
 =\frac{a+\omega}{a+\omega^2}.
\]
Thus \(t^3=M^{-1}(a)\), proving \((\mathsf{SL}_1)\).

Now let \(a=M(u)\in D_r\), so \(u^{r+1}=\omega^2\), and choose any
of the three roots \(t^3=u\).  Write
\[
 C=\frac{r^2-r+1}{3},\qquad q+1=3(r+1)C.
\]
For \(j=2\), one has \(r=2\) and \(C=1\).  For \(j\geq3\),
\(r\equiv-1\pmod9\), so
\(r^2-r+1\equiv3\pmod9\).  In either case \(C\equiv1\pmod3\), and
\[
 t^{q+1}
 =(t^3)^{(r+1)C}
 =(\omega^2)^C
 =\omega^2.
\]
All three roots therefore give distinct points of \(D_q\) with trace
\(a\).  Conversely, \((\mathsf{SL}_1)\) shows that every point above
\(D_r\) arises this way, proving \((\mathsf{SL}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the complete cubic recursion is Frobenius-rigid]
Suppose \(\xi_0=0\) and, for every \(i\geq1\), the element \(\xi_i\)
is a root of
\[
 X^3+\xi_{i-1}X^2+(\xi_{i-1}+1)X+1.
\]
Then \(\xi_i=\eta_i^{2^{e_i}}\) for some \(e_i\), with
\[
 e_i\equiv e_{i-1}\pmod{3^{i-1}}.
\]
Indeed, after
\(\xi_{i-1}=\eta_{i-1}^{2^e}\), the stage-\(i\) cubic is the
\(2^e\)-Frobenius twist of the selected cubic.  Its three roots are
\[
 \eta_i^{\,2^{e+t3^{i-1}}},\qquad t=0,1,2.
\]
Induction proves the assertion.  Thus imposing the complete recursive
chain determines one absolute Frobenius orbit, whose members all have
the same multiplicative order.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-previous-singer-lift}
therefore rules out a one-step membership argument; it does not create
a false recursive tower.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[the natural secant Kummer cover is rational]
Over \(\F_4\), the Möbius function

\[
 M(T)=\frac{\omega+\omega^2T}{1+T}
\]

which expresses the cyclotomic secant has Kummer cover

\[
 Z^\ell=M(T),\qquad
 T=\frac{Z^\ell+\omega}{Z^\ell+\omega^2}.
\]

Its function field is therefore \(\F_4(Z)\), of genus zero.  A
positive-genus Weil estimate on this cover cannot rule out the
distinguished torsion fibre; the remaining information is its actual
Artin value.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Capelli form of the surviving lemma]
\label{prop:capelli}
Put
\[
 A(X)=X^3+X+1,\qquad B(X)=X^2+X,\qquad
 R(X)=\frac{A(X)}{B(X)}.
\]
Let $Q_0(X)=X$ and, for $k\geq1$, define the monic binary polynomial
\[
 Q_k(X)=B(X)^{3^{k-1}}
        Q_{k-1}\!\left(R(X)\right).                         \tag{\(\star\)}
\]
Then $Q_k$ is the minimal polynomial of $\eta_k$ over $\F_2$ and is
irreducible of degree $3^k$.  Its first four values are
\begin{align*}
 Q_1(X)&=X^3+X+1,\\
 Q_2(X)&=X^9+X+1,\\
 Q_3(X)&=X^{27}+X^{25}+X^{24}+X^{19}+X^{18}+X^{16}
          +X^{10}+X^9+X^3+X+1,\\
 Q_4(X)&=X^{81}+X^{64}+X^{16}+X+1.
\end{align*}
For every prime $\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $\chi_{k,\ell}(\eta_k)\neq1$;
\item $\eta_k$ is not an $\ell$-th power in
      $\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times}$;
\item $X^{\ell}-\eta_k$ is irreducible over $\F_{2^{3^k}}$;
\item $Q_k(X^{\ell})$ is irreducible over $\F_2$.
\end{enumerate}
Thus the surviving character lemma is equivalently the assertion that
all the explicitly recursive compositions $Q_k(X^{\ell})$, for
$\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$, are irreducible.  At the first composite
level $k=4$, the three targets are the monomial compositions of the
displayed pentanomial $Q_4$ by
$\ell\in\{2593,71119,97685839\}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower} gives $R(\eta_k)=\eta_{k-1}$ and an
irreducible cubic for $\eta_k$ over $\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}$.  Since
$\eta_{k-1}\in\F_2(\eta_k)$, induction gives
$[\F_2(\eta_k):\F_2]=3^k$.  Clearing the denominator in the minimal
polynomial of $\eta_{k-1}$ gives \((\star)\), a monic polynomial of
degree $3^k$ having $\eta_k$ as a root: its leading term is
$A(X)^{3^{k-1}}=X^{3^k}+\cdots$.  It is therefore the minimal
polynomial.  Direct substitution in \((\star)\) gives the four displayed
polynomials.

Here $\Phi_{3^k}(2)=q_k^2+q_k+1\equiv1\pmod3$, so a prime
$\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$ is not $3$.  The cyclotomic-divisor criterion
therefore gives $\ord_{\ell}(2)=3^k$, and hence
$\mu_{\ell}\subset\F_{2^{3^k}}$.  Since $\ell$ is prime, Kummer theory
says that $X^{\ell}-\eta_k$ either splits off a linear factor or is
irreducible, according as $\eta_k$ is or is not an $\ell$-th power.
This proves the equivalence of (ii) and (iii), while
Capelli's lemma gives the equivalence of (iii) and (iv).  The direct
product
$\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times}\simeq
 \F_{q_k}^{\times}\times U_k$ and
$\ell\nmid(q_k-1)$ show that being an $\ell$-th power in the full
field is equivalent, for $\eta_k\in U_k$, to lying in $U_k^{\ell}$.
This is the equivalence with (i).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Singer membership is not primitivity]
\label{rem:singer-not-primitive}
The difference-set identification cannot replace the character test.
Indeed, exponentiation
\[
 s_k:U_k\longrightarrow U_k,\qquad s_k(y)=y^{q_k+1},
\]
is an automorphism of order $6$: one has
$\gcd(q_k+1,|U_k|)=1$ and
$(q_k+1)^3\equiv-1\pmod{|U_k|}$, while
$(q_k+1)^2\equiv q_k\not\equiv1$.  The Singer equation is simply
$s_k(y)=y+1$.  Thus its consequence
$\ord(y+1)=\ord(y)$ is automatic order preservation by an
automorphism and supplies no order divisor by itself.
For example, take $q=32$ and let $y$ be a root of
$X^3+X^2+1$ over $\F_2$.  Then $y$ has order $7$ and
$q+1\equiv5\pmod7$; the defining cubic gives
$y^5+y+1=0$.  Hence $y\in\{x:x^{q+1}+x+1=0\}$, but $y$ does not
generate the norm-one group of order
$q^2+q+1=7\cdot151$.  What must be used in the Conway tower is the
recursive coefficient $\eta_{k-1}$ in \((*)\), not Singer membership
alone.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[prime-step closure]\label{prop:prime-step}
If $\Phi_{3^k}(2)$ is prime, then
\[
 C_k\iff C_{k-1}.
\]
Consequently $C_1,C_2,C_3$ hold analytically: the three new kernel
orders are respectively
\[
 7,\qquad73,\qquad262657,
\]
and all are prime.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower} proves that $\eta_k$ has degree $3$
over $\F_{q_k}$, so in particular $\eta_k\neq1$.  A nonidentity element
of a group of prime order generates it, and \((**)\) gives the first
claim.  The primality of $7$ and $73$ is immediate.  For completeness,
$262657-1=2^9\cdot3^3\cdot19$, and base $5$ gives the Pocklington
certificate
\[
 5^{262656}\equiv1\pmod{262657},\qquad
 \gcd\!\left(5^{262656/r}-1,262657\right)=1
 \quad(r=2,3,19),
\]
which proves the third primality assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[cubic-arm equivalence]\label{prop:gauss-arm}
For primes $r$ with $f(r)\mid 3^k$ a $3$-power:
\[
  C_k
  \iff
  m_r=1\ \text{for every prime $r$ with } f(r)\in\{3,9,\dots,3^k\}.
\]
The family formula is the $0/1/4$ candidate of
Section~\ref{sec:introduction}
restricted to the $3$-power column.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[cyclotomic-unit residue reduction]
\label{prop:unit-residue}
Let $h=3^k$ and let $\ell$ be any prime divisor of
$2^h-1$.  Then
\[
 \gamma_k=\zeta^{-1}(1+\zeta)^2
\]
is an $\ell$-th power in $\F_{2^h}$ if and only if $1+\zeta$ is an
$\ell$-th power in $\F_{2^{2h}}$.  The same equivalence holds at every
power $\ell^a\mid2^h-1$; non-$\ell$-power already implies that
$\gamma_k$ has the full $\ell$-primary part of $2^h-1$ in its order.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because $\ell\mid2^h-1$, one has $\ell\nmid2^h+1$, so extension from
$\F_{2^h}^{\times}$ to $\F_{2^{2h}}^{\times}$ preserves the
$\ell$-primary quotient.  Also $\ell\neq3$, hence $\zeta^{-1}$ is an
$\ell$-th power in the $3^{k+1}$-group generated by $\zeta$; squaring
is an automorphism of every odd $\ell$-primary quotient.  The displayed
factorization proves both assertions.
\end{proof}

General multiplicative-order bounds for Gaussian periods do not imply
maximal order~\cite{ASV10}.  Here the norm recursion
$\gamma_k^3+\gamma_k=\gamma_{k-1}$ propagates the old factors, but a
proof must still show that each new prime-power factor of
$\Phi_{3^k}(2)$ divides $\ord(\gamma_k)$.  Irreducibility of the
corresponding Dickson polynomial is likewise weaker than primitivity
\cite{MM05}.  Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower} supplies the appropriate
cubic replacement and places the root on a Singer difference set, but
Remark~\ref{rem:singer-not-primitive} shows that Singer membership
alone cannot prove generation.

\subsection{The cubic-spine conjecture \texorpdfstring{\((C)\)}{(C)}}

The universal cubic arm is Conjecture~\ref{conj:ck}.  The prime-step
argument proves \(C_k\) analytically for \(k\leq3\), and
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-partitions} proves the required primary
order above an explicit partition threshold at every level.  The
remaining task is the selected Kummer-class nonvanishing at the smaller
new primary factors.  Propositions~\ref{prop:cubic-ray-exceptional-character}
and~\ref{prop:cubic-real-resultant} show respectively that the current
ray Euler factor has a forced simple zero whose circular-unit regulator is
exactly \(L(1)\), and that the real norm criterion has no viable size
bound.  Proposition~\ref{prop:parity-code-no-go} rules out a generic
cyclic-code or difference-set contradiction.  Finite order checks, their factorization
dependencies, and the distinction between local certificates and
external primality records are isolated in
Appendix~\ref{sec:cubic-evidence}.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-coleman-exceptional-zero}
also closes the natural three-adic Coleman route: the circular unit has
the explicit series \((2+T)^2/(1+T)\), but its algebraic
\(\widetilde\lambda x^{3^k-1}\)-specialization is forced to vanish
modulo every current \(\ell\), even at the proved level
\((k,\ell)=(2,73)\).  After division by that exceptional factor one
is left with a generalized Bernoulli value for which Coleman theory
supplies no \(\ell\)-adic unit
statement or cross-prime identification with the selected Kummer index.
\section{The singleton-even Conway--Fermat arm}\label{sec:fermat}

The candidate contains strong explicit maximal-order assertions even
before the non-cyclotomic singleton chains are reached.  This prevents a
proof from following from field degree or irreducibility alone.

\subsection{Maximal projective order}

\begin{proposition}[the singleton-even arm is Conway--Fermat maximality]
\label{prop:fermat-arm}
For $n\geq0$, put
\[
 h_n=2^{n+1},\qquad c_n=\kappa_{h_n}=2^{2^n},\qquad
 E_n=\F_{2^{h_n}},\qquad F_n=2^{2^n}+1.
\]
With $E_{-1}=\F_2$, let
\[
 \delta_n=\ord\bigl(c_nE_{n-1}^{\times}\bigr)
 \quad\text{in}\quad E_n^{\times}/E_{n-1}^{\times}.
\]
Then $1<\delta_n\mid F_n$, and the $m=0$ arm of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:rule} for every prime $p\mid F_n$ is equivalent to
\[
  \delta_n=F_n.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Conway finite-nimber tower satisfies
\[
 c_n^2+c_n=c_{n-1}c_{n-2}\cdots c_0
\]
(with the empty product $1$).  Thus $E_n/E_{n-1}$ is quadratic and
$c_n\notin E_{n-1}$, so the coset order is nontrivial.  Its ambient
quotient has order
\[
 \frac{2^{2^{n+1}}-1}{2^{2^n}-1}=F_n,
\]
which proves $1<\delta_n\mid F_n$; equivalently the relative norm is
$c_n^{F_n}=c_{n-1}\cdots c_0$.

If $p\mid F_n$, then $2^{2^n}\equiv-1\pmod p$, hence
$f(p)=2^{n+1}=h_n$.  Fermat numbers are pairwise coprime, so the entire
$p$-primary part of $|E_n^{\times}|$ lies in the quotient by
$E_{n-1}^{\times}$.  The full power criterion therefore gives
$m_p=0$ iff
$v_p(\delta_n)=v_p(F_n)$.  Requiring this for every prime divisor of
$F_n$ is exactly $\delta_n=F_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[exact primitive-product equivalence]
\label{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence}
Put \(a_n=\prod_{j=0}^n c_j\).  For every \(n\geq2\),
\[
 \ord(c_n)=\ord(a_n)=\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j.             \tag{\(\mathsf{PP}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 c_0c_n\text{ is primitive in }E_n
 \iff
 \delta_j=F_j\quad(1\leq j\leq n).                    \tag{\(\mathsf{PP}_2\)}
\]
Thus the all-level Conway--Fermat arm is exactly the all-level
primitive-product problem studied by Popovych~\cite{Popovych18}, in both
directions, rather than merely an analogous maximal-order question.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Write \(q=2^{2^n}\).  Since
\(\gcd(q-1,q+1)=1\), the cyclic group \(E_n^\times\) is the direct
product of \(E_{n-1}^\times\), of order \(q-1\), and its subgroup of
order \(F_n=q+1\).  Write accordingly
\(c_n=g_nu_n\), where \(u_n\in E_{n-1}^\times\) and
\(\ord(g_n)=\delta_n\).  The tower norm gives
\[
 a_{n-1}=c_n^{q+1}=u_n^2,
\]
so \(\ord(u_n)=\ord(a_{n-1})\).  The two factors have coprime orders,
and therefore
\[
 \ord(c_n)=\delta_n\ord(a_{n-1}),
 \qquad
 a_n=a_{n-1}c_n=g_nu_n^3.
\]
At \(n=1\), the quotient has prime order \(F_1=5\), so
\(\delta_1=5\); the same decomposition gives \(u_1^3=1\) and
therefore \(a_1=g_1\), of order \(5\).  Inductively
\(\ord(u_n)=\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\delta_j\), which is prime to three
because every \(\delta_j\mid F_j\) for \(j\geq1\).  Cubing therefore
preserves the order of \(u_n\), proving \((\mathsf{PP}_1)\).

The element \(c_0\) has order three, coprime to \(\ord(c_n)\), while
\[
 |E_n^\times|=3\prod_{j=1}^nF_j.
\]
Hence \(c_0c_n\) is primitive exactly when
\(\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j=\prod_{j=1}^nF_j\).  Since the Fermat numbers
are pairwise coprime and \(\delta_j\mid F_j\), this is equivalent to
all the displayed equalities in \((\mathsf{PP}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the Conway and Wiedemann norm-one drivers are not Frobenius-identical]
\label{prop:fermat-wiedemann-separation}
Put \(q=|E_{n-1}|=2^{2^n}\) and
\[
 w_n=c_n^{q-1}=\frac{c_n+1}{c_n}.
\]
The canonical isomorphism
\[
 E_n^\times/E_{n-1}^\times
   \longrightarrow
 \ker\!\left(\Norm_{E_n/E_{n-1}}\right),
 \qquad zE_{n-1}^\times\longmapsto z^{q-1},
\]
preserves order, and therefore
\[
 \ord(w_n)=\delta_n,\qquad
 w_n+w_n^{-1}=a_{n-1}^{-1}.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{WW}_1\)}
\]
If \(d_n=a_{n-1}^{-1}\), then the Conway drivers themselves obey
\[
 d_{n+1}=d_n(w_n+1)=\frac{(w_n+1)^3}{w_n}.           \tag{\(\mathsf{WW}_2\)}
\]
This has the same norm-one shape as Wiedemann's iterated quadratic
tower~\cite{Wiedemann88}, whose selected elements satisfy
\[
 x_n+x_n^{-1}=x_{n-1},
 \qquad \ord(x_n)\mid F_n,
\]
but it is not the same recursion.  For every \(n\geq3\), no
\(\F_2\)-automorphism of an algebraic closure can send \(x_n\) to
\(w_n\).  Thus Wiedemann maximality cannot be transferred to the
Conway--Fermat arm by a Frobenius identification of the selected
elements.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The displayed quotient map is injective because its kernel is
\(E_{n-1}^\times\), and both groups have order \(q+1=F_n\), proving the
order assertion.  The Conway relation gives
\(c_n(c_n+1)=a_{n-1}\), whence
\[
 w_n+w_n^{-1}
 =\frac{(c_n+1)^2+c_n^2}{c_n(c_n+1)}
 =a_{n-1}^{-1}
\]
in characteristic two.
Also \(a_n=a_{n-1}c_n\) and
\(c_n=(w_n+1)^{-1}\), so
\[
 d_{n+1}=d_n(w_n+1)
 =(w_n+w_n^{-1})(w_n+1)
 =\frac{(w_n+1)^3}{w_n},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{WW}_2)\).

If an \(\F_2\)-automorphism sent \(x_n\) to \(w_n\), it would send
\(x_{n-1}=x_n+x_n^{-1}\) to \(a_{n-1}^{-1}\), and hence preserve their
multiplicative orders.  But
\(\ord(x_{n-1})\mid F_{n-1}\), whereas
\[
 \ord(a_{n-1}^{-1})
 =\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\delta_j
\]
by \((\mathsf{PP}_1)\), and this order is divisible by
\(\delta_1=F_1=5\).  Fermat numbers are pairwise coprime, so
\(5\nmid F_{n-1}\) for \(n\geq3\), a contradiction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the classical \(Q\)-transform stops at irreducibility]
\label{prop:fermat-q-transform}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-arm}, put \(m=2^n\), let
\(A_{n-1}\) be the minimal polynomial of \(a=a_{n-1}\), and set

\[
 f(X)=X^mA_{n-1}(X^{-1}),\qquad
 u=\frac{c_n+1}{c_n}.
\]

Then \(f\) is the minimal polynomial of \(a^{-1}\), and the minimal
polynomial of \(u\) is exactly the classical binary \(Q\)-transform
\[
 f^Q(U)=U^m f(U+U^{-1}).                              \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_1\)}
\]
It is irreducible of degree \(2m\).  If \(e=\ord(u)\), the complete
self-reciprocal order information supplied by this transformation is
\[
 e\mid F_n,\qquad
 \min\{j>0:2^j\equiv\pm1\pmod e\}=m.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_2\)}
\]
Every nontrivial divisor \(e\mid F_n\) satisfies
\((\mathsf{QT}_2)\).  Thus the full \(Q\)-transform and
self-reciprocal irreducibility theorems do not distinguish
\(e=F_n\) from any proper fibotomic stratum.

Nor is the Conway tower obtained by iterating the \(Q\)-transform.  After
the quadratic lift one has
\[
 a_n=\frac{u}{(u+1)^3},\qquad
 a_n^{-1}=\frac{(u+1)^3}{u},                      \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_3\)}
\]
so the next step is a cubic pushforward followed by another quadratic
preimage, not a second application of \(U\mapsto U+U^{-1}\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The identity \((\mathsf{WW}_1)\) gives
\[
 u+u^{-1}=a^{-1},
\]
so \(u\) is a root of \((\mathsf{QT}_1)\).  The constant and
linear coefficients of \(A_{n-1}\) are one by
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-reciprocal-trace}.  More specifically,
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion} gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}(a)=1\), so the coefficient of \(X^{m-1}\) in
\(A_{n-1}\), equivalently \(f'(0)\), is one.  The
Varshamov--Garakov criterion in the form of
Meyn and G\"otz~\cite{MeynGotz89} therefore proves that \(f^Q\) is
irreducible of degree \(2m\).

The order classification of self-reciprocal irreducibles
\cite{YucasMullen04} gives \((\mathsf{QT}_2)\).  Conversely, if
\(1<e\mid2^m+1\), then \(2^m\equiv-1\pmod e\).  The order of two
modulo \(e\) divides \(2m\), does not divide \(m\), and \(m\) is a power
of two; hence it is \(2m\), and the first exponent giving either sign is
\(m\).  This proves the exact limitation.  Finally
\(a=u/(u+1)^2\) and \(c_n=(u+1)^{-1}\), which gives
\((\mathsf{QT}_3)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[two-step quartic and exact Kummer dichotomy]
\label{prop:fermat-two-step-quartic}
Let \(n\geq2\), put \(B=a_{n-2}\), \(K_0=E_{n-2}\), and
\(c=c_n\).  Then the minimal polynomial of \(c\) over \(K_0\) is
\[
 f_n(X)=X^4+(B+1)X^2+BX+B^3.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_1\)}
\]
For every prime \(\ell\mid F_n\), the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item the order \(\delta_n\) contains the full \(\ell\)-primary part
      of \(F_n\);
\item \(c\) is not an \(\ell\)-th power in \(E_n^\times\);
\item \(f_n(X^\ell)\) is irreducible over \(K_0\).
\end{enumerate}
If these conditions fail, then \(f_n(X^\ell)\) is a product of exactly
\(\ell\) distinct irreducible quartics over \(K_0\).  Thus a
minimal-counterexample argument may be descended across two quadratic
levels without discarding the selected coefficient \(B\), but failure
remains a complete quartic splitting pattern rather than a degree
contradiction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(a=a_{n-1}=Bc_{n-1}\).  The two consecutive Conway relations
give
\[
 c_{n-1}^2+c_{n-1}=B,\qquad c^2+c=a.
\]
Multiplying the first by \(B^2\) and using \(a=Bc_{n-1}\) gives
\[
 a^2+Ba+B^3=0.
\]
Eliminating \(a=c^2+c\) now gives \(f_n(c)=0\).
Moreover \(B\ne0\), so \(c_{n-1}=a/B\) belongs to \(K_0(c)\).
Consequently
\[
 K_0(c)\supset K_0(c_{n-1})=E_{n-1},
\]
and adjoining \(c\) gives \(E_n\).  Since
\([E_n:K_0]=4\), the displayed quartic is the minimal polynomial.

Fix \(\ell\mid F_n\).  The entire \(\ell\)-primary part of
\(E_n^\times\) lies in
\(E_n^\times/E_{n-1}^\times\), so (i) and (ii) are the exact cyclic
power criterion.  Also \(\mu_\ell\subset E_n\).  Capelli's lemma gives
\[
 f_n(X^\ell)\text{ irreducible over }K_0
 \iff X^\ell-c\text{ irreducible over }E_n,
\]
and Kummer theory over \(E_n\) identifies the latter condition with
\(c\notin E_n^{\times\ell}\).  This proves the equivalence.

Under failure write \(c=t^\ell\) with \(t\in E_n\).  Every root of
\(f_n(X^\ell)\) lies in \(E_n\), since \(\mu_\ell\subset E_n\).
No such root lies in the unique intermediate field \(E_{n-1}\):
otherwise its \(\ell\)-th power, one of the four \(K_0\)-conjugates of
\(c\), would lie there, forcing \(c\) itself into \(E_{n-1}\).
Hence every root has degree four over \(K_0\).  The polynomial is
separable and has \(4\ell\) roots, so it factors into exactly
\(\ell\) distinct irreducible quartics.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[coefficient conic of the bad quartics]
\label{prop:fermat-quartic-conic}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-two-step-quartic}, put
\(Q=|K_0|\), \(L=E_{n-1}\), and suppose that \(c=t^\ell\) in
\(E_n\).  Set
\[
 r=\Norm_{E_n/L}(t),\qquad e=r+r^Q,\qquad d=rr^Q.
\]
Then
\[
 r^\ell=a_{n-1},\qquad e\ne0,\qquad d^\ell=B^3.       \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_2\)}
\]
For \(\zeta\in\mu_\ell\), put
\[
 y_\zeta=t\zeta,\qquad s_\zeta=y_\zeta+y_\zeta^{Q^2}.
\]
Writing \(\mathcal D_\ell(S,P)\) for the Dickson power sum of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-root-ancestry}, one has
\[
 \mathcal D_\ell(s_\zeta,r)=1.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_3\)}
\]
The \(\ell\) irreducible quartic factors of \(f_n(X^\ell)\) are
exactly
\[
 \begin{split}
 g_{s_\zeta}(X)
 &=\Norm_{L/K_0}(X^2+s_\zeta X+r)\\
 &=X^4+\alpha_\zeta X^3+\beta_\zeta X^2
       +\gamma_\zeta X+d,
 \end{split}
\]
where
\[
 \alpha_\zeta=s_\zeta+s_\zeta^Q,\qquad
 \beta_\zeta=e+s_\zeta s_\zeta^Q,\qquad
 \gamma_\zeta=s_\zeta r^Q+s_\zeta^Q r.
\]
Every coefficient triple lies on the conic
\[
 \gamma_\zeta^2+\alpha_\zeta e\gamma_\zeta
 +\alpha_\zeta^2d=e^2(\beta_\zeta+e).                \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_4\)}
\]
Conversely, \(\alpha_\zeta\) and \(\gamma_\zeta\) recover
\[
 s_\zeta=\frac{\gamma_\zeta+\alpha_\zeta r}{e},
\]
and \(\beta_\zeta\) is then forced.  Thus the coefficient conic is
a lossless change of coordinates on the same Dickson fibre
\(\mathcal D_\ell(S,r)=1\); it supplies no independent obstruction
to the bad factorization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Taking the norm of \(t^\ell=c\) from \(E_n\) to \(L\) gives
\(r^\ell=a_{n-1}\).  If \(r\) belonged to \(K_0\), then so would
\(a_{n-1}=Bc_{n-1}\); since \(B\ne0\) and
\(c_{n-1}\notin K_0\), this is impossible.  Hence \(e\ne0\).  A second
norm and the quadratic equation for \(a_{n-1}\) give
\[
 d^\ell=\Norm_{L/K_0}(a_{n-1})=B^3.
\]

Because \(\ell\mid Q^2+1\), every \(\zeta\in\mu_\ell\) has norm one
from \(E_n\) to \(L\).  Thus \(y_\zeta\) has relative trace
\(s_\zeta\) and norm \(r\).  The Dickson identity gives
\[
 \mathcal D_\ell(s_\zeta,r)
 =y_\zeta^\ell+(y_\zeta^{Q^2})^\ell
 =c+c^{Q^2}=1.
\]
The traces \(s_\zeta\) are distinct: equality would identify the two
unordered root pairs of the corresponding quadratic; the crossed
identification has \(\ell\)-th powers \(c\) and \(c+1\), which is
impossible.  Taking the \(Q\)-conjugate norm of these quadratics gives
all \(\ell\) quartics and the displayed coefficient formulas; their
irreducibility was proved in Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-two-step-quartic}.

Finally
\[
 \gamma_\zeta+\alpha_\zeta r=e s_\zeta.
\]
Taking the norm from \(L\) to \(K_0\) and expanding both sides gives
\((\mathsf{TQ}_4)\).  Since \(e\ne0\), the same identity recovers
\(s_\zeta\), after which
\(\beta_\zeta=e+s_\zeta s_\zeta^Q\) is forced.  This proves the
lossless assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fibonacci-polynomial criterion]
\label{prop:fermat-fibotomic}
Fix $n\geq0$, put $m=2^n$, $q=2^m$, and write
$K=E_{n-1}=\F_q$, $L=E_n=\F_{q^2}$ and
$a=a_{n-1}=\prod_{j<n}c_j$.  If
\[
 S_0(X)=0,\qquad S_1(X)=1,\qquad
 S_{r+2}(X)=S_{r+1}(X)+XS_r(X),
\]
then
\[
 \delta_n=\min\{r>0:S_r(a)=0\},
\]
and hence
\[
 \delta_n=F_n
 \iff
 S_{F_n/\ell}(a)\ne0
 \quad\hbox{for every prime }\ell\mid F_n.       \tag{\(\mathsf F_n\)}
\]

More precisely, for odd $d>1$ define the fibotomic polynomial
\[
 \mathfrak F_d(X)=
 \prod_{\substack{\{v,v^{-1}\}\\ \ord(v)=d}}
 \left(X-\frac{v}{(v+1)^2}\right),
\]
where the product takes one representative of each inverse-pair of
primitive $d$-th roots.  Then $\mathfrak F_d\in\F_2[X]$,
\[
 S_r(X)=\prod_{\substack{d\mid r\\d>1}}\mathfrak F_d(X)
 \quad(r\text{ odd}),                                      \tag{\(\mathsf B_r\)}
\]
and, if $A_{n-1}$ is the minimal polynomial of $a_{n-1}$ over $\F_2$,
\[
 \delta_n=d\iff A_{n-1}\mid\mathfrak F_d.
\]
Every irreducible factor of every $\mathfrak F_d$ with
$1<d\mid F_n$ has the same degree $m$.  Thus the Conway--Fermat
conjecture is exactly the assertion
$A_{n-1}\mid\mathfrak F_{F_n}$; field degree cannot distinguish that
stratum from any proper-divisor stratum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put $c=c_n$ and $u=(c+1)/c$.  Since $c^2+c=a$ and
$c^q=c+1$, the element $u$ lies in the norm-one group of order
$q+1=F_n$, and $\delta_n=\ord(u)$.  The two roots $c,c+1$ of
$T^2+T+a$ give the Binet identity
\[
 S_r(a)=c^r+(c+1)^r=\frac{1+u^r}{(1+u)^r}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf B\)}
\]
This proves the first criterion.  In a cyclic group of order $F_n$ an
element has full order precisely when its $F_n/\ell$-th power is
nontrivial for every prime $\ell\mid F_n$, proving
\((\mathsf F_n)\).

For odd $r$, the nontrivial $r$-th roots $v$ occur in inverse pairs,
and the common value $v/(v+1)^2$ of a pair is, by
\((\mathsf B)\), a root of $S_r$.  Frobenius permutes the pairs, so
the displayed factors have coefficients in $\F_2$.  Both sides of
\((\mathsf B_r)\) are monic of degree $(r-1)/2$, which proves the
factorization and the divisibility criterion.

Finally $2^m\equiv-1\pmod d$ for every nontrivial $d\mid F_n$.
Because $m$ is a power of two, $\ord_d(2)=2m$, and the least $j>0$
with $2^j\equiv\pm1\pmod d$ is $m$.  This is exactly the Frobenius
orbit length of $v/(v+1)^2$: indeed
\[
 \frac{v}{(v+1)^2}=\frac{w}{(w+1)^2}
 \iff (v+w)(vw+1)=0,
\]
so only the inverse pair $\{v,v^{-1}\}$ has the same image.  This
proves the equal-degree assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[trailing-zero compression and its exact circularity]
\label{prop:fermat-trailing-zero}
Let \(K\) be a characteristic-two field, let \(a\in K\), and put
\[
 T_t(a)=\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}a^{2^j}.
\]
For the Fibonacci values of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic}, one has, for all \(t,h\geq0\),
\[
 \boxed{
 S_{2^th+1}(a)
 =S_{h+1}(a)^{2^t}+T_t(a)S_h(a)^{2^t}.}              \tag{\(\mathsf{TZ}_1\)}
\]
If \(S_h(a)\ne0\), this is equivalently
\[
 \boxed{
 S_{2^th+1}(a)=0
 \iff
 \left(\frac{S_{h+1}(a)}{S_h(a)}\right)^{2^t}=T_t(a).}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{TZ}_2\)}
\]

Now retain the Conway--Fermat notation, assume \(n\geq2\), and put
\(m=2^n\).  Let \(d>1\) divide \(F_n\), write
\[
 d=1+2^th,
 \qquad 0<t\leq m,
\]
where \(t\) is a chosen power-of-two divisor of \(d-1\), not necessarily
its exact $2$-adic valuation, so \(h\) need not be odd.  Suppose
\(\delta_n\mid d\) and \(\delta_n>2^t\).  Then
\[
 S_h(a)\ne0,\qquad S_{h+1}(a)\ne0.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{TZ}_3\)}
\]
In particular, for a failed prime-factor test
\(d=F_n/\ell>1\), one may take \(t=n+2\): every nontrivial divisor of
\(F_n\) is \(1\pmod {2^{n+2}}\), and so is larger than \(2^{n+2}\).
Writing
\[
 R_h=\frac{S_{h+1}(a)}{S_h(a)},
\]
the hypothetical failure then has the choice-free form
\[
 \boxed{R_h=1+T_{m-t}(a).}                            \tag{\(\mathsf{TZ}_4\)}
\]

This sharper formula is nevertheless automatic on every relevant
proper-order stratum.  Indeed, if \(u=(c_n+1)/c_n\) has order
\(\delta_n\mid d\), and \(k=2^{m-t}\), then
\[
 2^th\equiv-1\pmod {\delta_n},\qquad
 2^m\equiv-1\pmod {\delta_n}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 h\equiv k\pmod {\delta_n}.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{TZ}_5\)}
\]
Thus the continued-fraction, Cassini, trace, and Frobenius identities
obtained functorially from \((\mathsf{TZ}_4)\) hold on every corresponding
order-\(\delta_n\) stratum.  By themselves they do not distinguish the
selected Conway factor from a proper fibotomic factor; new full-ancestry
information could still make such an identity useful.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In characteristic two the doubling identities are
\[
 S_{2r}=S_r^2,
 \qquad
 S_{2r+1}=S_{r+1}^2+aS_r^2.
\]
Induction on \(t\), using \(T_{t+1}=T_t^2+a\), proves
\((\mathsf{TZ}_1)\); division by \(S_h^{2^t}\) proves
\((\mathsf{TZ}_2)\).

By Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic}, the zero indices are exactly
the multiples of \(\delta_n\).  If \(\delta_n\mid h\), then
\(\delta_n\mid 2^th\) and \(\delta_n\mid 2^th+1=d\), forcing
\(\delta_n\mid1\).  If \(\delta_n\mid h+1\), then
\[
 \delta_n\mid 2^t(h+1)-d=2^t-1,
\]
contrary to \(\delta_n>2^t\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{TZ}_3)\).  The standard prime-divisor congruence for \(F_n\)
gives the stated choice \(t=n+2\), and \(n+2\leq2^n=m\) for \(n\geq2\).

The absolute trace of \(a=a_{n-1}\) is one, so
\[
 1=T_m(a)=T_t(a)+T_{m-t}(a)^{2^t}.
\]
Under failure, \((\mathsf{TZ}_2)\) therefore gives
\[
 R_h^{2^t}=(1+T_{m-t}(a))^{2^t}.
\]
Frobenius is injective, proving \((\mathsf{TZ}_4)\).

Finally multiply \(2^th\equiv-1\pmod{\delta_n}\) by
\(2^{m-t}\) and use \(2^m\equiv-1\pmod{\delta_n}\) to obtain
\((\mathsf{TZ}_5)\).  Hence \(u^h=u^k\).  The Binet identity and
\(S_k(a)=c_n^k+(c_n+1)^k=1\) give the explicit calculation
\[
 \begin{split}
 T_{m-t}(a)&=c_n^k+c_n,\\
 R_h
 &=c_n\frac{1+u^{h+1}}{1+u^h}
  =c_n\frac{1+u^{k+1}}{1+u^k}
  =c_n^k+c_n+1.
 \end{split}
\]
Thus \((\mathsf{TZ}_4)\) is automatic on the whole
order-\(\delta_n\) stratum; the identities derived from it alone cannot
select the Conway ancestry.  Conversely
\((\mathsf{TZ}_2)\) returns the original zero \(S_d(a)=0\).  The
compression is exact but supplies no independent nonvanishing invariant.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[full-ancestry trace descent is the lower tautology]
\label{prop:fermat-compressed-trace-descent}
For \(i\geq0\), put \(d=2^i\),
\(K_0=E_{i-1}=\F_{2^d}\), and
\(K=E_i=\F_{2^{2d}}\).  Write \(A=a_{i-1}\in K_0\),
\(a=a_i\in K\), and suppose \(0\leq t\leq d\).  Thus
\[
 a^{2^d}=a+A,\qquad T_d(A)=1.
\]
For every integer \(D\geq t\), put
\[
 C_{D,t}(x)=1+T_{D-t}(x).
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K/K_0}\bigl(C_{2d,t}(a)\bigr)
   =C_{d,t}(A).}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{CT}_1\)}
\]
The value on the right is exactly the lower full-Fermat continued
fraction:
\[
 C_{d,t}(A)
 =\frac{S_{2^{d-t}+1}(A)}{S_{2^{d-t}}(A)}.            \tag{\(\mathsf{CT}_2\)}
\]
Consequently, when the hypothetical ratio in
\((\mathsf{TZ}_4)\) is descended through the actual quadratic
ancestry, every eligible relative trace lands on the lower level's
unconditional full-Fermat zero identity.  Relative trace therefore
preserves all ancestry but produces no contradiction to a proper top
order.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Partial Frobenius traces are additive and commute with Frobenius
powering.  Hence \(a^{2^d}=a+A\) gives
\[
\begin{split}
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K/K_0}\bigl(1+T_{2d-t}(a)\bigr)
 &=T_{2d-t}(A)\\
 &=T_d(A)+T_{d-t}(A)
  =1+T_{d-t}(A),
\end{split}
\]
which is \((\mathsf{CT}_1)\).  The doubling formulas used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-trailing-zero} give
\[
 S_{2^r}(A)=1,\qquad S_{2^r+1}(A)=1+T_r(A).
\]
Taking \(r=d-t\) proves \((\mathsf{CT}_2)\).  The formal
identities---additivity, Frobenius commutation, index splitting, and
the first displayed relative-trace calculation---are independently
kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The selected factor and the limits of one-step induction}

\begin{proposition}[coarse top-step data cannot force maximality]
\label{prop:fermat-countermodels}
For every divisor $d>1$ of $F_n$ there are elements
$a_d\in\F_q$ and $c_d\in\F_{q^2}$ such that
\[
 [\F_2(a_d):\F_2]=m,\qquad
 [\F_2(c_d):\F_2]=2m,\qquad
 c_d^q=c_d+1,\qquad c_d^2+c_d=a_d,
\]
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(a_d)=1,\qquad
 \Norm_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}(c_d)=a_d,
\]
but the order of $c_d\F_q^\times$ is exactly $d$.  In particular all
proper-divisor strata obey the same degree, trace, Artin--Schreier and
relative-norm data as the Conway top step.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose $v$ of order $d$ in the norm-one subgroup of
$\F_{q^2}^{\times}$ and put
\[
 c_d=\frac1{v+1},\qquad a_d=\frac{v}{(v+1)^2}.
\]
Then $v^q=v^{-1}$, so $c_d^q=c_d+1$; the remaining displayed
identities follow directly.  The orbit calculation in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic} gives the two field degrees.
Consequently $T^2+T+a_d$ is irreducible over $\F_q$, which is
equivalent to the stated absolute trace, and
$(c_d+1)/c_d=v$ gives quotient order $d$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the exact recursively selected factor]
\label{prop:fermat-resultant}
Set $a_{-1}=1$, $a_i=\prod_{j\leq i}c_j$, and let $A_i$ be
its minimal polynomial over $\F_2$, with $A_{-1}(Y)=Y+1$.  Then
\[
 a_i^2+a_{i-1}a_i+a_{i-1}^3=0
\]
and
\[
 A_i(X)=\operatorname{Res}_Y
 \bigl(A_{i-1}(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3\bigr).                       \tag{\(\mathsf R_i\)}
\]
Hence the unresolved Conway--Fermat lemma is the precise recursive
claim that the factor selected by \((\mathsf R_i)\) lies in the
full-order fibotomic stratum, rather than in any equal-degree
$\mathfrak F_d$ with $d\mid F_{i+1}$ and $d<F_{i+1}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Multiplying $c_i^2+c_i=a_{i-1}$ by $a_{i-1}^2$ gives the first
identity.  We prove the degree assertion and the resultant formula
inductively from $a_{-1}=1$.  The resultant in \((\mathsf R_i)\) is monic of degree
$2\deg A_{i-1}$ and vanishes at $a_i$.  Since
$c_i=a_i/a_{i-1}\notin E_{i-1}$, one has $a_i\notin E_{i-1}$.
But $E_i/\F_2$ has degree $2^{i+1}$, and every proper subfield of
$E_i$ is contained in its unique index-two subfield $E_{i-1}$.
Therefore $a_i$ has full degree $2^{i+1}=2\deg A_{i-1}$, so the
resultant is its minimal polynomial.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[rational normalization, unique parents, and finite-field collapse]
\label{prop:fermat-resultant-dynamics}
For a monic polynomial \(A\in\F_2[Y]\), write
\[
 \mathcal T(A)(X)=\operatorname{Res}_Y
   \bigl(A(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3\bigr).
\]
The correspondence \(X^2+YX+Y^3=0\) has rational normalization
\(\mathbf P^1_c\), with
\[
 Y=\alpha(c):=c^2+c,
 \qquad
 X=\beta(c):=c^3+c^2.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{RDy}_1\)}
\]
Consequently, after base change to \(\overline{\F}_2\), its transform
on effective zero-cycles on the \(X\)-line is
\[
 \operatorname{div}_0\mathcal T(A)
 =\beta_*\alpha^*\operatorname{div}_0(A).           \tag{\(\mathsf{RDy}_2\)}
\]

If \(A\) is irreducible of degree \(m\) and a root of \(A\) has
absolute trace one, then \(\mathcal T(A)\) is irreducible of degree
\(2m\), and its roots again have trace one.  On this locus
\(\mathcal T\) is injective.  More precisely, if
\(B=\mathcal T(A)\), \(x\) is a root of \(B\), and \(q=2^m\), then
the parent root and hence its minimal polynomial are recovered by
\[
 y=x^q+x.                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{RDy}_3\)}
\]

Conversely, let \(B\) be an irreducible trace-one polynomial of degree
\(2m\), let \(x\) be one of its roots, and put \(y=x^{2^m}+x\).
Then \(B\) has a parent under \(\mathcal T\) if and only if
\[
 \boxed{x^{2^m+1}=y^3.}                             \tag{\(\mathsf{RDy}_4\)}
\]
When this holds, the parent is unique.

The rational dynamics adds no shorter selector.  If
\(H\in\overline{\F}_2(t)\) satisfies
\[
 H\circ\alpha=H\circ\beta,
\]
then \(H\) is constant; neither \(\alpha\) nor \(\beta\) factors
rationally through the other.  Their critical portrait is likewise
universal: \(\alpha\) branches only at infinity, \(\beta\) has critical
values \(0,\infty\), and that two-point set is invariant.  Every
trace-one orbit avoids it.  Thus all trace-one seeds of the same degree
\(m\) have iterates irreducible of degrees \(2^rm\), with common
splitting fields \(\F_{2^{2^rm}}\).

In the norm-one coordinates
\[
 \Phi(v)=\frac{v}{(v+1)^2},\qquad
 \Psi(v)=\frac{v}{(v+1)^3},
\]
the transition is \(\Phi(w)=\Psi(v)\).  There is no rational selection
\(w=R(v)\), since it would force \(2\deg R=3\).  Hence critical-orbit,
splitting-field, degree, and rational-first-integral data do not select
the full Fermat stratum.  The actual \(A_i\) is instead the unique node
whose repeated parent chain ends at \(A_{-1}=X+1\); applying
\((\mathsf{RDy}_4)\) along that chain is exactly the existing full
trace--norm ancestry, not a new nonvanishing invariant.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In the function field of the correspondence put \(c=X/Y\).  Then
\(c^2+c+Y=0\), which gives \((\mathsf{RDy}_1)\); the defining property
of the resultant gives \((\mathsf{RDy}_2)\).

Let \(y\) be a trace-one root of \(A\), choose
\(c^2+c=y\), and put \(x=yc\).  The Artin--Schreier criterion makes
\(Z^2+Z+y\) irreducible over \(\F_{2^m}\), so
\[
 c^{2^m}=c+1,\qquad x^{2^m}=x+y.
\]
Therefore \(y=x^{2^m}+x\) and \(c=x/y\) both belong to
\(\F_2(x)\), whence \(x\) has degree \(2m\).  The monic degree-
\(2m\) resultant is its minimal polynomial, and trace transitivity
gives \(\operatorname{Tr}_{2m/1}(x)=\operatorname{Tr}_{m/1}(y)=1\).
This also proves parent recovery and injectivity.

For a child, the two relative conjugates are \(x,x+y\), so a parent
necessarily gives
\[
 x^{2^m+1}=x(x+y)=y^3.
\]
Conversely assume \((\mathsf{RDy}_4)\) and put \(c=x/y\).  Then
\[
 c^2+c=\frac{x(x+y)}{y^2}=y.
\]
The element \(y\) cannot lie in a proper subfield of \(\F_{2^m}\):
otherwise \(\F_2(y,c)\), and hence \(\F_2(x)\), would have degree
strictly less than \(2m\).  Thus its minimal polynomial has degree
\(m\).  Moreover trace transitivity and the trace-one hypothesis on
\(B\) give
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{m/1}(y)=\operatorname{Tr}_{2m/1}(x)=1\).
The preceding construction therefore shows that its transform is \(B\).
This proves \((\mathsf{RDy}_4)\) and uniqueness.

Degrees of nonconstant rational maps multiply.  Hence
\(H\circ\alpha=H\circ\beta\) would give
\(2\deg H=3\deg H\), while a factorization of either projection
through the other would give \(2=3\deg R\) or \(3=2\deg R\).
The derivative and pole calculations for \(c^2+c\) and
\(c^2(c+1)\) give the stated critical portrait.  Iterating the first
part proves the common finite-field tower.  Finally
\(\Phi(R(v))=\Psi(v)\) has the same impossible degree equation, and
the unique-parent conclusion follows from \((\mathsf{RDy}_3)\).
Lean independently checks \(\beta=c\alpha\),
\(\alpha(c+1)=\alpha(c)\),
\(\beta(c+1)=\beta(c)+\alpha(c)\), the curve equation, and
\(\beta(c)\beta(c+1)=\alpha(c)^3\) in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the full ancestry chain is an étale zero--unit dichotomy]
\label{prop:fermat-full-chain-etale}
For $n\geq1$, put
\[
 \begin{split}
 I_n=(&X_0^2+X_0+1,\\
     &X_i^2+X_{i-1}X_i+X_{i-1}^3\ :\ 1\leq i<n)
 \subseteq\F_2[X_0,\ldots,X_{n-1}].
 \end{split}
\]
Evaluation at the complete Conway ancestry is an isomorphism
\[
 \F_2[X_0,\ldots,X_{n-1}]/I_n
   \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } E_{n-1},
 \qquad X_i\longmapsto a_i.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 I_n\cap\F_2[X_{n-1}]=(A_{n-1}(X_{n-1})),
\]
and, for every $R\in\F_2[X]$,
\[
 I_n+(R(X_{n-1}))=
 \begin{cases}
   I_n,&A_{n-1}\mid R,\\
   (1),&A_{n-1}\nmid R.
 \end{cases}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_2\)}
\]
The quotient in \((\mathsf{FE}_1)\) is finite étale of degree $2^n$;
geometrically its points form one free Frobenius orbit.  Moreover
\[
 \det\bigl(m_{R(a_{n-1})}:E_{n-1}\to E_{n-1}\bigr)
 =\Norm_{E_{n-1}/\F_2}(R(a_{n-1}))
 =\operatorname{Res}(A_{n-1},R).                  \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_3\)}
\]
Thus for $R=S_d$, adjoining the complete ancestry equations and the
hypothetical proper-order equation produces no intermediate intersection:
it gives the whole selected orbit or the empty scheme.  Proving the second
case is exactly $A_{n-1}\nmid S_d$, the original Conway--Fermat target.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Successively adjoining the variables in their displayed monic quadratic
equations makes the source of \((\mathsf{FE}_1)\) a vector space of dimension
at most $2^n$.  Evaluation is onto: its image contains all the $a_i$, and
the top one generates $E_{n-1}$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}.  Since
$[E_{n-1}:\F_2]=2^n$, evaluation is an isomorphism.  The elimination and
zero--unit assertions are then just divisibility by the minimal polynomial
and the fact that the quotient is a field.

The Jacobian of the displayed generators is lower triangular with diagonal
$1,X_0,\ldots,X_{n-2}$.  Every $a_i$ is nonzero, so it is nonsingular at
every geometric point; Frobenius transitivity follows from the full degree of
$a_{n-1}$.  Finally, the determinant of multiplication by an element is its
field norm, and the norm of $R(a_{n-1})$ is the displayed monic resultant.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[odd fibotomic intersections are reduced]
\label{prop:fermat-fibonacci-squarefree}
For every odd $d>1$, the Fibonacci polynomial $S_d(X)$ is squarefree over
$\F_2$.  More explicitly, if $u^d=1$, $u\ne1$, and
$\Phi(u)=u/(1+u)^2$, then
\[
 S_d(\Phi(u))=0,
 \qquad
 S_d'(\Phi(u))=\frac1{u(1+u)^{d-2}}\ne0.           \tag{\(\mathsf{FS}_1\)}
\]
Equivalently, in characteristic two,
\[
 S'_{2r}(X)=0,
 \qquad
 S'_{2r+1}(X)=S_r(X)^2.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{FS}_2\)}
\]
Hence if a selected factor $A_{n-1}$ divides $S_d$, it occurs with
multiplicity one.  Writing $S_d=A_{n-1}Q$, its higher Hasse derivatives at
$a=a_{n-1}$ merely give
\[
 S_d^{[j]}(a)=
   \sum_{s=1}^{j}A_{n-1}^{[s]}(a)Q^{[j-s]}(a),      \tag{\(\mathsf{FS}_3\)}
\]
which recursively determines the quotient jets because
$A_{n-1}'(a)\ne0$.  Tangent multiplicity and local jets therefore do not
strengthen the selected nondivisibility problem by themselves.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Binet parametrization gives
\[
 S_d(\Phi(u))=\frac{1+u^d}{(1+u)^d}.
\]
Differentiate at a nontrivial $d$-th root $u$.  Since $d$ is odd and the
characteristic is two, the derivative of the right side is
$u^{-1}(1+u)^{-d}$, while
$\Phi'(u)=(1+u)^{-2}$.  This proves \((\mathsf{FS}_1)\).
The inverse-pair parametrization $u\sim u^{-1}$ supplies all
$(d-1)/2=\deg S_d$ roots, so $S_d$ is squarefree.

Alternatively, differentiating the Fibonacci recurrence and using the
doubling identities proves \((\mathsf{FS}_2)\) by induction.  This derivative
identity and its nonvanishing consequence are independently kernel-checked in
\texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.  Formula \((\mathsf{FS}_3)\) is the Hasse product rule
after evaluating at a root of $A_{n-1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the natural ancestry filtration loses Frobenius]
\label{prop:fermat-groebner-filtration}
Retain the full-chain ideal $I_n$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-full-chain-etale} and give $X_i$ weight
$2^i$.  For every monomial order refining this weight, the displayed
generators of $I_n$ are a Gr\"obner basis with initial ideal
\[
 (X_0^2,\ldots,X_{n-1}^2).
\]
Consequently the associated graded algebra is
\[
 \operatorname{gr}E_{n-1}
 \simeq
 \F_2[x_0,\ldots,x_{n-1}]/(x_0^2,\ldots,x_{n-1}^2), \tag{\(\mathsf{GF}_1\)}
\]
and Frobenius annihilates every positive-degree element of this graded
algebra.  Thus a max-weight, Newton-polygon, or unique-leading-monomial
argument cannot propagate a nonzero leading class through the squaring
steps in Fibonacci fast doubling; each such step necessarily falls to
uncontrolled lower filtration.

The exact loss at one Conway edge can be written without division.  If
\[
 x^2=Ax+A^3,
 \qquad
 S_r(x)=U_r+xV_r,
\]
then
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 U_{2r}&=U_r^2+A^3V_r^2,&
 V_{2r}&=AV_r^2,\\
 U_{2r+1}&=U_{r+1}^2+A^3V_{r+1}^2+A^4V_r^2,&
 V_{2r+1}&=AV_{r+1}^2+U_r^2+(A^3+A^2)V_r^2.
 \end{aligned}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{GF}_2\)}
\]
The three summands in either odd update may cancel in every leading grade.
Moreover every ordered multiplicative valuation on the finite field
$E_{n-1}$ is trivial.  Hence replacing the weight filtration by a genuine
field valuation cannot repair the loss.  This rules out a leading-term proof
by itself; it does not rule out an induction that controls the complete lower
normal form by new ancestry-sensitive information.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The leading term of the first chain equation is $X_0^2$.  For $i>0$,
the three terms have weights
\[
 2^{i+1},\qquad 3\cdot2^{i-1},\qquad 3\cdot2^{i-1},
\]
so the unique leading term is $X_i^2$.  These leading monomials are
pairwise coprime; Buchberger's coprime criterion proves the Gr\"obner-basis
claim and \((\mathsf{GF}_1)\).  In characteristic two, the square of a sum
of squarefree positive-degree monomials is the sum of their squares, each of
which contains some $x_i^2$, so it vanishes in the associated graded.

The characteristic-two doubling identities are
\[
 S_{2r}=S_r^2,
 \qquad
 S_{2r+1}=S_{r+1}^2+xS_r^2.
\]
Substitute $S_r=U_r+xV_r$, reduce every $x^2$ by $Ax+A^3$, and collect
the constant and $x$-coefficients to obtain \((\mathsf{GF}_2)\).  Both
algebraic reductions are independently kernel-checked in the Lean companion.
Finally, if $v$ is an ordered multiplicative
valuation and $z\ne0$, then $z^{|E_{n-1}^\times|}=1$, so
$|E_{n-1}^\times|v(z)=0$ and hence $v(z)=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the top-bit block is the same projective-order problem]
\label{prop:fermat-top-bit-companion}
Fix one Conway edge, put
\[
 A=a_{i-1},\qquad c=c_i,\qquad a_i=Ac,\qquad c^2=c+A,
\]
and let \(M\) be the regular multiplication matrix of \(A\) on
\(E_{i-1}\).  In the \(E_{i-1}\)-decomposition
\(E_i=E_{i-1}\oplus cE_{i-1}\), multiplication by the literal top
Conway bit \(a_i\) has block matrix
\[
 M_i=\begin{pmatrix}0&M^2\\ M&M\end{pmatrix}.          \tag{\(\mathsf{CB}_1\)}
\]
Put
\[
 D=\operatorname{diag}(M,M),\qquad
 C_M=\begin{pmatrix}0&M\\ I&I\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Then \(M_i=DC_M=C_MD\), and for \(r\geq1\),
\[
 C_M^r=
 \begin{pmatrix}
  MS_{r-1}(M)&MS_r(M)\\
  S_r(M)&S_{r+1}(M)
 \end{pmatrix}.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{CB}_2\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 C_M^r\text{ is a lower-field scalar}
 \iff S_r(A)=0,
\]
where a lower-field scalar means
\(\operatorname{diag}(N_b,N_b)\), with \(N_b\) regular multiplication
by some \(b\in E_{i-1}\).  Equivalently, this is a scalar in
\(\operatorname{GL}_2(E_{i-1})\) after restriction of scalars.  The
first return of either \(C_M\) or \(M_i\) in
\(\operatorname{PGL}_2(E_{i-1})\) is exactly \(\delta_i\).  Moreover
\[
\begin{split}
 \det(XI-M_i)
 &=\det(X^2I+XM+M^3)\\
 &=\operatorname{Res}_Y
   \bigl(A_{i-1}(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3\bigr).
\end{split}                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CB}_3\)}
\]
Thus the nested multiplication matrix retains the complete Conway
ancestry, but its characteristic polynomial is the existing selected
resultant and its Singer/projective order is the existing Fibonacci
first zero.  A conjugacy to a Singer generator would already prove the
Conway--Fermat conjecture; it is not an independent route to it.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(x_0,x_1\in E_{i-1}\),
\[
 (Ac)(x_0+cx_1)
 =A^2x_1+c(Ax_0+Ax_1),
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{CB}_1)\).  The factorization by \(D\) is
immediate, and its factors commute because every block is a polynomial
in \(M\).  Induction using
