\(S_{r+2}=S_{r+1}+XS_r\) proves \((\mathsf{CB}_2)\).
Its off-diagonal blocks vanish exactly when \(S_r(M)=0\); evaluating
regular multiplication at \(A\) identifies this with \(S_r(A)=0\).
The Fibonacci criterion now gives the projective order.

Finally the commuting-block determinant formula gives the first line
of \((\mathsf{CB}_3)\), and regular multiplication turns that
determinant into the displayed field norm and resultant.  The
coordinate identity, companion-power formula, and scalar equivalence
are independently kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the first-failure cube character is automatic]
\label{prop:fermat-cube-no-go}
Assume \(n\geq2\), put
\[
 q=2^{2^n},\qquad Q=\sqrt q,
\]
and let \(v=u_{n-1}\) be the previous norm-one driver.  Exact Conway
ancestry gives
\[
 a_{n-1}=\frac{v}{(v+1)^3}.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CU}_1\)}
\]
Since \(v\) lies in the torus of order \(Q+1\) and
\(3\nmid Q+1\), choose \(\rho\) in that torus with \(\rho^3=v\).
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 a_{n-1}=
 \left(\frac{\rho}{v+1}\right)^3.}                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CU}_2\)}
\]
Thus the old coefficient is automatically a cube before its
multiplicative order is used.  If \(n\) is a first failed level, the
earlier maximalities give
\[
 \ord(a_{n-1})=\frac{q-1}{3};
\]
this says only that the already cubic element generates the cube
subgroup.

The fibotomic coordinate has an exact compatible transport.  Put
\(\Phi(x)=x/(x+1)^2\).  Whenever the denominators are nonzero,
\[
 \boxed{
 \Phi(x^3)
 =\frac{\Phi(x)^3}{(1+\Phi(x))^2}
 =(1+\Phi(x))
   \left(\frac{\Phi(x)}{1+\Phi(x)}\right)^3.}          \tag{\(\mathsf{CU}_3\)}
\]
For every \(e>1\) dividing \(q+1=F_n\), \(3\nmid e\), so cubing
permutes the exact-order-\(e\) norm-one elements.  Consequently, for
every cubic character \(\chi:\F_q^\times\to\mu_3\),
\[
 \sum_{\ord(u)=e}\chi(\Phi(u))
 =\sum_{\ord(x)=e}\chi(1+\Phi(x)).                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CU}_4\)}
\]
The cube character therefore transports bijectively to a translate-one
character on the same fibotomic stratum.  By itself this excludes no
proper stratum: it supplies no nonvanishing or emptiness theorem for the
translate-one character.  Old maximal order and cubic-subgroup
membership supply no first-failure contradiction by themselves beyond
the selected prime-factor divisibilities
\(A_{n-1}\mid S_{F_n/\ell}\), for primes \(\ell\mid F_n\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation \((\mathsf{CU}_1)\) is the lower expression for \(a_{n-1}\)
in \((\mathsf E)\).  The group of order \(Q+1\) has no three-torsion,
so cubing is bijective and \((\mathsf{CU}_2)\) follows directly.
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence} gives the displayed
first-failure order, and a cyclic group of order \(q-1\) has a cube
subgroup of order \((q-1)/3\).

In characteristic two,
\[
 x^3+1=(x+1)(x^2+x+1),\qquad
 1+\Phi(x)=\frac{x^2+x+1}{(x+1)^2}.
\]
Substitution proves both forms in \((\mathsf{CU}_3)\).  If \(x\) has
exact order \(e\), the excluded denominators would force order one or
three, which cannot divide a Fermat number.  Substitute \(u=x^3\) in
the left side of \((\mathsf{CU}_4)\); the last factor in
\((\mathsf{CU}_3)\) is a cube and disappears under \(\chi\).
The three rational identities used here are independently
kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact quadratic remainder descent]
\label{prop:fermat-remainder-descent}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}.  For
\(R(X)\in\F_2[X]\), divide \(R(X)\), now regarded as an element of
\(\F_2[Y][X]\), by the monic quadratic
\[
 Q_Y(X)=X^2+YX+Y^3.
\]
There are unique \(U_R,V_R\in\F_2[Y]\) such that
\[
 R(X)\equiv U_R(Y)+XV_R(Y)\pmod{Q_Y(X)}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{QR}_1\)}
\]
Then, for every \(i\geq0\),
\[
 \boxed{
 A_i(X)\mid R(X)
 \iff
 A_{i-1}(Y)\mid U_R(Y)
 \qquad\text{and}\qquad
 A_{i-1}(Y)\mid V_R(Y).}                             \tag{\(\mathsf{QR}_2\)}
\]
Consequently the selected divisibility
\(A_{n-1}\mid S_{F_n/\ell}\) can be pushed, without loss, through
every preceding Conway quadratic: iterating \((\mathsf{QR}_1)\) gives
an exact binary dependency tree of divisibilities at \(A_{-1}=Y+1\).
This criterion uses the complete deterministic ancestry, so a one-step
countermodel with the wrong predecessor does not certify the selected
divisibility.  Its leaf
conditions are nevertheless an exact re-expression of the original
selected Fibonacci value; degree alone does not make any leaf
coprimality automatic.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Euclidean division by the monic polynomial \(Q_Y\) proves existence
and uniqueness in \((\mathsf{QR}_1)\).  Substitute
\(Y=a_{i-1}\) and \(X=a_i\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}
gives \(Q_{a_{i-1}}(a_i)=0\), so
\[
 R(a_i)=U_R(a_{i-1})+a_iV_R(a_{i-1}).                 \tag{\(\mathsf{QR}_3\)}
\]
The extension
\(\F_2(a_i)/\F_2(a_{i-1})\) has degree two; hence
\(1,a_i\) is a basis over the lower field.  Thus the right side of
\((\mathsf{QR}_3)\) vanishes exactly when both lower coefficients
vanish.  The minimal-polynomial criterion gives
\((\mathsf{QR}_2)\), and iteration proves the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[complete remainder coordinates and Euclidean-tail exhaustion]
\label{prop:fermat-remainder-coordinate-exhaustion}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-remainder-descent}.  For \(i\geq-1\),
write
\[
 \mathcal R_i=\{R\in\F_2[X]:\deg R<2^{i+1}\},
 \qquad \mathcal R_{-1}=\F_2.
\]
For \(i\geq0\), let
\[
 R(X)\equiv U_R(Y)+XV_R(Y)\pmod{X^2+YX+Y^3}
\]
and define
\[
 \sigma_i:\mathcal R_i\longrightarrow
 \mathcal R_{i-1}\times\mathcal R_{i-1},\qquad
 \sigma_i(R)=
 \bigl(U_R\bmod A_{i-1},V_R\bmod A_{i-1}\bigr),     \tag{\(\mathsf{RCE}_1\)}
\]
using the unique reduced representatives.  Then every \(\sigma_i\) is an
\(\F_2\)-linear isomorphism.  Iteration gives a canonical isomorphism
\[
 \boxed{\mathbf C_n:\mathcal R_{n-1}
   \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\F_2^{\,2^n}.}              \tag{\(\mathsf{RCE}_2\)}
\]
For every \(R\in\F_2[X]\), the vector
\(\mathbf C_n(R\bmod A_{n-1})\) is exactly the ordered leaf vector of
the complete binary quadratic-remainder descent.  Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 A_{n-1}\mid R
 \iff R\bmod A_{n-1}=0
 \iff \mathbf C_n(R\bmod A_{n-1})=0.}                \tag{\(\mathsf{RCE}_3\)}
\]
Every binary leaf pattern occurs for exactly one reduced polynomial.

Write the first monic division as
\[
 R=Q_RA_{n-1}+\rho_R,\qquad \deg\rho_R<2^n.
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 R\longmapsto\bigl(Q_R,\mathbf C_n(\rho_R)\bigr)}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{RCE}_4\)}
\]
is a bijection
\[
 \F_2[X]\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
 \F_2[X]\times\F_2^{\,2^n},
 \qquad
 (Q,\mathbf c)\longmapsto
 A_{n-1}Q+\mathbf C_n^{-1}(\mathbf c).               \tag{\(\mathsf{RCE}_5\)}
\]
Thus the raw first quotient and the complete ancestry-coordinate vector are
independent for unrestricted input polynomials, even with the literal Conway
chain held fixed.

After the first division, the monic Euclidean remainder sequence for
\((R,A_{n-1})\) is the sequence for \((A_{n-1},\rho_R)\).  Its entire tail,
and every subresultant polynomial remainder sequence derived from that tail,
is a deterministic function of \(\rho_R\); conversely the first tail entry
recovers \(\rho_R\).  For \(R=S_d\),
\[
 \gcd(A_{n-1},S_d)=
 \begin{cases}A_{n-1},&\rho_{S_d}=0,\\1,&\rho_{S_d}\ne0,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Res}(A_{n-1},S_d)=
 \begin{cases}0,&\rho_{S_d}=0,\\1,&\rho_{S_d}\ne0.
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{RCE}_6\)}
\]
Therefore the complete intermediate remainder tree and the complete
Euclidean/subresultant tail are information-equivalent to the original
selected residue \(S_d\bmod A_{n-1}\); they are not an additional ancestry
invariant.  The independence in \((\mathsf{RCE}_4)\) does not require its
free input to be a Fibonacci polynomial.  Hence it does not exclude a special
coefficient identity relating the actual quotient \(Q_{S_d}\) to
\(\rho_{S_d}\), nor a direct proof that one coordinate of \(\rho_{S_d}\)
is nonzero.  Those are the surviving coefficient-sensitive routes.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Evaluation identifies \(\mathcal R_i\) with \(E_i\), since \(A_i\) is the
degree-\(2^{i+1}\) minimal polynomial of \(a_i\).  Under these
identifications, \(\sigma_i\) is the coordinate map
\[
 E_i\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }E_{i-1}\times E_{i-1},
 \qquad u+a_iv\longmapsto(u,v),
\]
by \((\mathsf{QR}_3)\) and the basis \(1,a_i\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{RCE}_2)\)--\((\mathsf{RCE}_3)\), including unique realization
of every leaf vector.  Monic division gives unique \(Q_R,\rho_R\), and
combining that bijection with \(\mathbf C_n\) proves
\((\mathsf{RCE}_4)\)--\((\mathsf{RCE}_5)\).

The Euclidean algorithm discards \(Q_R\) after its first step and continues
from \((A_{n-1},\rho_R)\).  Finally \(A_{n-1}\) is irreducible.  A nonzero
\(\rho_R\) has smaller degree and is therefore coprime to \(A_{n-1}\);
the resultant lies in \(\F_2\), so it is one in that case and zero otherwise.
This proves \((\mathsf{RCE}_6)\).  Lean kernel-checks the monic
quotient--remainder uniqueness and free-realization core, together with zero
reflection and product independence for an abstract additive coordinate
equivalence.  Its identification with the actual Conway tree and the
Euclidean/subresultant interpretation remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fibonacci quotient defects recover the endpoint]
\label{prop:fermat-quotient-parity-defects}
Fix \(n\geq1\), put
\[
 A=A_{n-1},\qquad m=\deg A=2^n,
\]
and let \(d>1\) be odd.  Write the actual monic Euclidean division
\[
 S_d=AQ_d+\rho_d,\qquad \deg\rho_d<m.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_1\)}
\]
Define the two special quotient defects
\[
 \mathcal O_d=S_d'+A'Q_d,\qquad
 \mathcal E_d=S_{d+1}+XA'Q_d.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_2\)}
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal E_d+X\mathcal O_d=S_d.}              \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_3\)}
\]
More precisely,
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal O_d
   &=AQ_d'+\rho_d',\\
 \mathcal E_d
   &=A(Q_d+XQ_d')+\rho_d+X\rho_d'.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_4\)}
\]
Consequently the reduced defects are
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal O_d\bmod A=\rho_d',
 \qquad
 \mathcal E_d\bmod A=\rho_d+X\rho_d'.}                \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_5\)}
\]
If
\[
 \rho_d(X)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}r_jX^j,
\]
then
\[
 \rho_d'(X)=\sum_j r_{2j+1}X^{2j},
 \qquad
 \rho_d(X)+X\rho_d'(X)=\sum_j r_{2j}X^{2j}.           \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_6\)}
\]
Thus the two defects are exactly the shifted odd-coordinate block and the
even-coordinate block of the selected remainder.  In particular
\[
 \boxed{
 \rho_d=
   (\mathcal E_d\bmod A)
   +X(\mathcal O_d\bmod A),}                          \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_7\)}
\]
and hence
\[
 \boxed{
 A\mid S_d
 \iff A\mid\mathcal O_d\ \text{and}\ A\mid\mathcal E_d.}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_8\)}
\]
Thus the reduced defect pair is information-equivalent to the original
selected endpoint, rather than an independent obstruction.  This
derivative/next-value coupling does not control higher \(A\)-adic data or
global coefficient relations in the unreduced quotient \(Q_d\).  A surviving
coefficient-sensitive proof must use such data or directly prove that one of
the two parity blocks in \((\mathsf{FQD}_5)\) is nonzero.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(d=2h+1\).  The characteristic-two derivative and doubling
identities give
\[
 S_d'=S_h^2=S_{2h}=S_{d-1}.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_{11}\)}
\]
The Fibonacci recurrence therefore becomes
\[
 S_{d+1}=S_d+XS_{d-1}=S_d+XS_d'.                     \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_{12}\)}
\]
Substituting this in the definitions of the defects makes both copies of
\(XA'Q_d\), and both copies of \(XS_d'\), cancel in characteristic two,
proving \((\mathsf{FQD}_3)\).

Differentiate \((\mathsf{FQD}_1)\):
\[
 S_d'=A'Q_d+AQ_d'+\rho_d'.
\]
Adding \(A'Q_d\) proves the first identity in
\((\mathsf{FQD}_4)\).  Substituting
\((\mathsf{FQD}_1)\) and this differentiated identity into
\[
 \mathcal E_d=S_d+XS_d'+XA'Q_d
\]
proves the second.  Reduction modulo \(A\) gives
\((\mathsf{FQD}_5)\).  Since differentiation kills even powers and takes
\(X^{2j+1}\) to \(X^{2j}\), formula \((\mathsf{FQD}_6)\) follows.
The two parity supports are disjoint, and
\[
 (\rho_d+X\rho_d')+X\rho_d'=\rho_d,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{FQD}_7)\).  The forward implication in
\((\mathsf{FQD}_8)\) follows from \((\mathsf{FQD}_4)\); the reverse
implication follows from \((\mathsf{FQD}_3)\).

The abstract defect reconstruction, odd/even remainder decomposition, and
exact-factor divisibility formulas are kernel-checked in
\texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.  Their specialization to formal polynomial
derivatives, the selected quotient, and the finite-field unit statements
remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-branch norm descent for the Fermat arm]
\label{prop:fermat-norm-descent}
For \(R(X)\in\F_2[X]\), retain the remainder
\[
 R(X)\equiv U_R(Y)+XV_R(Y)\pmod{X^2+YX+Y^3}
\]
from Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-remainder-descent}, and define
\[
 \mathcal N(R)(Y)
 =\operatorname{Res}_X\!\left(X^2+YX+Y^3,R(X)\right).
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal N(R)
 =U_R^2+YU_RV_R+Y^3V_R^2,}                           \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_1\)}
\]
and, for every \(i\geq0\), every actual Conway quadratic edge satisfies
\[
 \boxed{A_i\mid R\iff A_{i-1}\mid\mathcal N(R).}   \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_2\)}
\]
After identifying \(\F_2[Y]\) with \(\F_2[X]\) by \(Y\mapsto X\),
regard \(\mathcal N\) as an endomorphism before iterating it.
Consequently, for every \(n\geq0\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal N^{\circ n}(R)(1)
 =\Norm_{E_{n-1}/\F_2}\!\left(R(a_{n-1})\right).}   \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_3\)}
\]
The right side is one exactly when \(R(a_{n-1})\ne0\).

The Fibonacci family itself has a closed first norm layer.  Put
\(W_d=\mathcal N(S_d)\).  Then, for every \(d\geq0\),
\[
 W_0=0,\qquad W_1=W_2=1,\qquad W_3=1+Y+Y^3,
\]
and
\[
 \boxed{
 W_{d+4}=W_{d+3}+YW_{d+2}+Y^3W_{d+1}+Y^6W_d.}       \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_4\)}
\]
For every \(n\geq0\), prime \(\ell\mid F_n\), and current test
\(d=F_n/\ell\), however,
\[
 \mathcal N^{\circ n}(S_d)(1)
 =\begin{cases}
   0,&\delta_n\mid d,\\
   1,&\delta_n\nmid d.
  \end{cases}                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_5\)}
\]
Thus the single terminal bit compresses the complete binary remainder
tree, but its required value one is exactly the original selected
Conway--Fermat assertion.  The recurrence exposes rather than removes
the remaining obstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let \(x,x'\) be the two roots of \(X^2+YX+Y^3\).  Their sum is \(Y\)
and their product is \(Y^3\), so
\[
 (U_R+xV_R)(U_R+x'V_R)
 =U_R^2+YU_RV_R+Y^3V_R^2.
\]
This is the product formula for the resultant and proves
\((\mathsf{QN}_1)\).  At \(Y=a_{i-1}\), the two roots are the
relative conjugates of \(a_i\); hence
\[
 \mathcal N(R)(a_{i-1})
 =\Norm_{E_i/E_{i-1}}R(a_i).
\]
The field norm and its argument vanish together, proving
\((\mathsf{QN}_2)\).  Iterate, use transitivity of norms, descend to
\(A_{-1}=Y+1\), and use
\(a_{-1}=1\) to obtain \((\mathsf{QN}_3)\).

For \((\mathsf{QN}_4)\), let \(\lambda_1,\lambda_2\) and
\(\mu_1,\mu_2\) be the roots of
\[
 T^2+T+x,\qquad T^2+T+x'.
\]
The four products \(\lambda_i\mu_j\) have characteristic polynomial
\[
 T^4+T^3+YT^2+Y^3T+Y^6.
\]
The Binet formula gives
\[
 W_d=S_d(x)S_d(x')
 =\sum_{i,j}(\lambda_i\mu_j)^d,
\]
so their power-sum recurrence is exactly
\((\mathsf{QN}_4)\), with the displayed initial values obtained
directly.  Finally \(S_d(a_{n-1})=0\) exactly when
\(\delta_n\mid d\), by Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic};
\((\mathsf{QN}_3)\) now proves \((\mathsf{QN}_5)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the Fermat terminal word is the order word]
\label{cor:fermat-terminal-order-word}
For every \(n\geq0\) and \(d\geq0\), put
\[
 T_n(d)=\mathcal N^{\circ n}(S_d)(1).
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{T_n(d)=\epsilon_{\delta_n}(d).}              \tag{\(\mathsf{ZOW}_1\)}
\]
Consequently \(T_n\) has least period \(\delta_n\), exact linear complexity
\(\delta_n-1\), and exactly \(\delta_n\) states in both its \(2\)-kernel and
minimal binary DFAO.  Since \(\delta_n\mid F_n\), for every prime
\(\ell\mid F_n\),
\[
 \boxed{
 T_n(F_n/\ell)=1
 \iff v_\ell(\delta_n)=v_\ell(F_n).}                \tag{\(\mathsf{ZOW}_2\)}
\]
Thus exact quadratic ancestry compression does not reduce the state
complexity below the unknown Conway--Fermat quotient order.  It presents that
order itself as the terminal binary word.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-norm-descent} gives
\(T_n(d)=0\) exactly when \(S_d(a_{n-1})=0\), while
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic} identifies the latter condition with
\(\delta_n\mid d\).  This proves \((\mathsf{ZOW}_1)\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:order-indicator-word} supplies the period, complexity,
and automaton assertions.  Finally, for divisors
\(\delta_n\mid F_n\), the failure of \(\delta_n\mid F_n/\ell\) is equivalent
to equality of the two \(\ell\)-adic valuations, proving
\((\mathsf{ZOW}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the complementary selected resultant is the opposite bit]
\label{prop:fermat-complementary-resultant}
Fix \(n\geq1\), put
\[
 m=2^n,\qquad q=2^m,\qquad N=q+1=F_n,
 \qquad a=a_{n-1},\qquad A=A_{n-1},
\]
and let \(d\mid N\).  Fibonacci divisibility defines the cofactor
\[
 J_{N,d}(X)=\frac{S_N(X)}{S_d(X)}\in\F_2[X].
\]
Then
\[
 S_N(X)=1+\sum_{r=0}^{m-1}X^{2^r},\qquad S_N'(X)=1,
\]
and hence
\[
 1=S_d'(X)J_{N,d}(X)+S_d(X)J_{N,d}'(X).
                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{QNC}_1\)}
\]
At the complete actual Conway ancestry one has
\[
 S_d(a)J_{N,d}(a)=0,
 \qquad
 S_d'(a)J_{N,d}(a)+S_d(a)J_{N,d}'(a)=1.               \tag{\(\mathsf{QNC}_2\)}
\]
Consequently exactly one of \(S_d(a)\) and \(J_{N,d}(a)\) vanishes.  More
precisely,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 S_d(a)=0&\Longrightarrow S_d'(a)J_{N,d}(a)=1,\\
 J_{N,d}(a)=0&\Longrightarrow S_d(a)J_{N,d}'(a)=1.
 \end{aligned}                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{QNC}_3\)}
\]
The two selected resultants are therefore complementary bits:
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \operatorname{Res}(A,S_d)+\operatorname{Res}(A,J_{N,d})=1
 \quad\text{in }\F_2.\quad}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{QNC}_4\)}
\]
Equivalently, for the exact one-branch Conway norm operator of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-norm-descent},
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \mathcal N^{\circ n}(S_d)(1)
 +\mathcal N^{\circ n}(J_{N,d})(1)=1.\quad}          \tag{\(\mathsf{QNC}_5\)}
\]
For \(d=N/\ell\), the first summand is the desired primary success bit;
the cofactor, its derivative, and its complete ancestry descent supply exactly
the opposite bit, not a second obstruction.  More generally, in the complete
fibotomic factorization of \(S_N\), exactly one selected resultant vanishes,
and its position is the unknown order \(\delta_n\).  Thus factor--cofactor
splitting determines no preferred position.  This proposition does not rule
out a new ancestry-sensitive identity involving a polynomial outside this
factorization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The standard strong divisibility of Fibonacci polynomials gives
\(S_d\mid S_N\).  Fast doubling gives
\[
 S_N(X)=S_{2^m+1}(X)=1+\sum_{r=0}^{m-1}X^{2^r},
\]
whose formal derivative is one.  Differentiating
\(S_N=S_dJ_{N,d}\) proves \((\mathsf{QNC}_1)\).

The selected element has absolute trace one, so
\(S_N(a)=1+\operatorname{Tr}_{E_{n-1}/\F_2}(a)=0\).
Evaluation of the factorization and of \((\mathsf{QNC}_1)\) gives
\((\mathsf{QNC}_2)\).  The product identity says that at least one factor
vanishes, while the differentiated identity says they cannot both vanish;
it also gives the inverse identities \((\mathsf{QNC}_3)\).

Because \(A\) is monic,
\[
 \operatorname{Res}(A,R)
 =\Norm_{E_{n-1}/\F_2}(R(a))\in\F_2.
\]
A finite-field norm vanishes exactly with its argument, and the only nonzero
element of \(\F_2\) is one.  Thus the two resultants are \(0,1\) in some
order, proving \((\mathsf{QNC}_4)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-norm-descent}
identifies the two resultants with the two iterated terminal norms in
\((\mathsf{QNC}_5)\).  The fibotomic statement follows from the squarefree
factorization of \(S_N\).

Lean kernel-checks the power-of-two-plus-one Fibonacci derivative and the
abstract product-zero/differentiated-B\'ezout-one implication, including the
complementary-bit conclusion over \(\F_2\), in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.  The
Fibonacci divisibility, selected finite-field specialization, and resultant
identification remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[selected CRT projectors and ancestry trace annihilation]
\label{prop:fermat-selected-crt-projectors}
Retain the notation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-complementary-resultant}, and put
\[
 \mathcal T_N=\F_2[X]/(S_N),\qquad
 r=\deg S_d=\frac{d-1}{2},\qquad
 s=\deg J_{N,d}=\frac{N-d}{2}.
\]
In \(\mathcal T_N\), define
\[
 e_{\mathrm{fail}}=[S_d'J_{N,d}],
 \qquad
 e_{\mathrm{succ}}=[S_dJ_{N,d}'].
\]
Then
\[
 e_{\mathrm{fail}}+e_{\mathrm{succ}}=1,\qquad
 e_{\mathrm{fail}}e_{\mathrm{succ}}=0,\qquad
 e_{\mathrm{fail}}^2=e_{\mathrm{fail}},\qquad
 e_{\mathrm{succ}}^2=e_{\mathrm{succ}}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_1\)}
\]
Under the canonical Chinese-remainder isomorphism
\[
 \mathcal T_N
 \simeq
 \F_2[X]/(S_d)\times\F_2[X]/(J_{N,d}),
\]
their orientations are
\[
 e_{\mathrm{fail}}=(1,0),\qquad
 e_{\mathrm{succ}}=(0,1).                             \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_2\)}
\]
Moreover, every B\'ezout certificate
\[
 U S_d+VJ_{N,d}=1
\]
produces the same projectors:
\[
 [VJ_{N,d}]=e_{\mathrm{fail}},\qquad
 [US_d]=e_{\mathrm{succ}}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_3\)}
\]
Thus the terminal support information returned by the Euclidean algorithm,
or by any normalized final subresultant certificate, is canonical.

Evaluation at the actual selected Conway element \(a=a_{n-1}\) gives
\[
 \bigl(e_{\mathrm{fail}}(a),e_{\mathrm{succ}}(a)\bigr)
 =
 \begin{cases}
  (1,0),&S_d(a)=0,\\
  (0,1),&J_{N,d}(a)=0.
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_4\)}
\]
In particular the orientation relative to the desired resultants is
\[
 \boxed{
 e_{\mathrm{succ}}(a)=\operatorname{Res}(A,S_d),\qquad
 e_{\mathrm{fail}}(a)=\operatorname{Res}(A,J_{N,d}).} \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_5\)}
\]

This orientation is destroyed by every proper additive descent through the
literal Conway ancestry.  For \(-1\leq j\leq n-2\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_{n-1}/E_j}
   \bigl(e_{\mathrm{fail}}(a)\bigr)
 =
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_{n-1}/E_j}
   \bigl(e_{\mathrm{succ}}(a)\bigr)
 =0,}                                                \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_6\)}
\]
whereas
\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{Norm}_{E_{n-1}/E_j}
   \bigl(e_\star(a)\bigr)=e_\star(a)
 \quad(\star\in\{\mathrm{fail},\mathrm{succ}\}).}      \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_7\)}
\]
Thus norm descent retains exactly the original endpoint bit and trace descent
retains none of it.

The ambient multiplication characteristic polynomials are
\[
 \chi_{\mu_{e_{\mathrm{fail}}}}(T)
   =T^s(T+1)^r,\qquad
 \chi_{\mu_{e_{\mathrm{succ}}}}(T)
   =T^r(T+1)^s.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_8\)}
\]
For \(d=N/\ell\), with \(\ell\mid N\) prime, one has
\(N\equiv d\equiv1\pmod4\), so \(r\) and \(s\) are even and both ambient
traces vanish.  On the selected quotient
\(E_{n-1}=\F_2(a)\), whose degree is \(m=2^n\), multiplication by either
selected projector has
\[
 \chi_{\mu_{e_\star(a)}}(T)
   =(T+e_\star(a))^m
   =T^m+e_\star(a).                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{QNP}_9\)}
\]
Hence for \(n\geq1\) its trace and multiplication-characteristic-polynomial
discriminant vanish in both branches, while its determinant is
\(e_\star(a)\), again exactly the original selected resultant.

Consequently terminal Euclidean quotients only through their final support
projector, final B\'ezout or subresultant certificates, proper additive
ancestry traces, and multiplication trace or discriminant cannot decide which
fibotomic factor vanishes.  This does not rule out an identity involving
intermediate Euclidean quotient polynomials before support collapse, a
polynomial outside the factorization of \(S_N\), or any identity that directly
evaluates \(e_{\mathrm{succ}}(a)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-complementary-resultant} gives
\[
 S_dJ_{N,d}=S_N=0\quad\text{in }\mathcal T_N
\]
and
\[
 S_d'J_{N,d}+S_dJ_{N,d}'=S_N'=1.
\]
Their two summands therefore add to one and have product
\(S_d'S_dJ_{N,d}J_{N,d}'=0\).  Multiplying their sum by either
summand proves idempotence, giving \((\mathsf{QNP}_1)\).
Reduction modulo the two coprime factors proves \((\mathsf{QNP}_2)\).
The same reductions send \(VJ_{N,d}\) to \((1,0)\) and \(US_d\) to
\((0,1)\), proving the uniqueness assertion \((\mathsf{QNP}_3)\).

Since \(S_N(a)=0\), evaluation defines a map
\(\mathcal T_N\to E_{n-1}\).  The differentiated identity and the fact that
exactly one of \(S_d(a)\) and \(J_{N,d}(a)\) vanishes give
\((\mathsf{QNP}_4)\).  Both projectors evaluate in \(\F_2\).  A selected
resultant is the norm of the corresponding selected value to \(\F_2\), hence
is zero exactly when that value is zero and otherwise equals one.  This proves
the orientation in \((\mathsf{QNP}_5)\).

For \(-1\leq j\leq n-2\),
\[
 [E_{n-1}:E_j]=2^{\,n-1-j}
\]
is even.  The relative trace of either scalar bit is therefore zero in
characteristic two, while its relative norm is that bit raised to a positive
power and hence is unchanged.  This proves
\((\mathsf{QNP}_6)\)--\((\mathsf{QNP}_7)\).

The CRT decomposition makes multiplication by \(e_{\mathrm{fail}}\) the
identity on the degree-\(r\) factor and zero on the degree-\(s\) factor;
the roles reverse for \(e_{\mathrm{succ}}\).  This gives
\((\mathsf{QNP}_8)\).  A prime divisor \(\ell\) of
\(2^{2^n}+1\) has \(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=2^{n+1}\), so for \(n\geq1\)
one has \(\ell\equiv1\pmod4\), and consequently
\(d=N/\ell\equiv1\pmod4\).  Finally, on the degree-\(m\) selected field,
multiplication by \(e_\star(a)\in\F_2\) is scalar.  Since \(m\) is a power
of two,
\[
 (T+e_\star(a))^m=T^m+e_\star(a).
\]
For \(m>1\) this has zero trace and zero discriminant, while its constant
term, equivalently its determinant in characteristic two, is
\(e_\star(a)\).

The abstract complementary-idempotent identities, selected field branches,
uniqueness of B\'ezout support projectors, and the quadratic trace/norm core
are kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.  The polynomial CRT
specialization, resultant orientation, and characteristic-polynomial
interpretation remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-step maximality is not an inductive invariant]
\label{prop:fermat-one-step-no-go}
Let

\[
 C(Y)=Y^{16}+Y^{15}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^8+1
\]

and apply the exact Conway resultant operator of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}:

\[
\begin{split}
 B(X)&=\operatorname{Res}_Y(C(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3)\\
 &=X^{32}+X^{31}+X^{30}+X^{27}+X^{26}+X^{24}
   +X^{23}+X^{22}\\
 &\qquad{}+X^{19}+X^{15}+X^{14}+X^{11}+X^8+X^7+1.
\end{split}                                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_1\)}
\]

Then \(C\) is an irreducible factor of the full-order fibotomic
stratum \(\mathfrak F_{F_4}\), where \(F_4=65537\), whereas \(B\) is
an irreducible factor of the proper-order stratum

\[
 \mathfrak F_{6700417},\qquad
 6700417=F_5/641.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_2\)}
\]

Moreover, if \(y\) is a root of \(C\), then
\[
 \ord(y)=21845=\frac{2^{16}-1}{3}=5\cdot17\cdot257.    \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_{2a}\)}
\]
If \(x\) is a root of \(B\), then in fact
\[
 \ord(x)=\frac{2^{32}-1}{3}
        =F_1F_2F_3F_4.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_{2b}\)}
\]
Thus the countermodel also satisfies the exact multiplicative-order
consequence that all preceding Conway--Fermat maximalities impose on
both its degree-sixteen element and its successor.  In
particular, the successor contains every preceding Fermat factor with
full multiplicity even though its next norm-one component loses the
factor \(641\).  Even that strengthened predecessor/successor order
data, together with the exact resultant transition, does not force
target maximality.

Thus the exact one-step resultant can send a full-order predecessor
factor to a proper-order target factor, already at the first composite
Fermat level.  In particular, predecessor maximality plus the transition
formula alone is not an induction theorem.  This is not a counterexample
to the distinguished Conway path: \(C\) is an alternative factor at the
\(A_3\) level and need not have the earlier ancestry of the actual
\(A_3\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
First, exact modular squaring gives

\[
 Y^{2^{16}}\equiv Y\pmod C,
\]

and

\[
 Y^{256}+Y\equiv
 R:=Y^{15}+Y^{14}+Y^{13}+Y^9+Y^8+Y^2+Y\pmod C.
\]

The Bézout identity \(UC+VR=1\) holds for

\[
\begin{split}
 U={}&Y^{14}+Y^{13}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^7+Y^6+Y^5+Y^4\\
    &\quad{}+Y^3+Y+1,\\
 V={}&Y^{15}+Y^{14}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^9+Y^8+Y^5+Y^3+Y+1.
\end{split}
\]

Rabin's criterion, for which two is the only prime divisor of sixteen,
therefore proves that \(C\) is irreducible.

The Fibonacci polynomials satisfy, in characteristic two,

\[
 S_{2r}=S_r^2,\qquad
 S_{2r+1}=S_{r+1}^2+YS_r^2,
\]

and hence

\[
 S_{2^m+1}(Y)=1+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Y^{2^j}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_3\)}
\]

For a root \(y\) of \(C\), the coefficient of \(Y^{15}\) gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{2^{16}}/\F_2}(y)=1\).  Equation
\((\mathsf{NI}_3)\) therefore gives
\(S_{65537}(y)=0\).  Since \(65537\) is prime, \(y\) belongs to the
full-order stratum \(\mathfrak F_{65537}\).

The predecessor's own multiplicative order is certified independently
by the exact reductions
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 Y^{21845}&\equiv1,\\
 Y^{4369}&\equiv\mathtt{7576},&
 Y^{1285}&\equiv\mathtt{1653},&
 Y^{85}&\equiv\mathtt{2464}
 \pmod C,
 \end{aligned}
\]
where hexadecimal bit \(j\) is the coefficient of \(Y^j\).  The last
three residues are not one.  Since
\(21845=5\cdot17\cdot257\), these identities prove
\((\mathsf{NI}_{2a})\).  Under
\(\delta_j=F_j\) for \(1\leq j\leq3\),
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence} gives the same order
for \(a_3\).

Now choose \(z\) with \(z^2+z=y\) and put \(x=yz\).
The trace just computed makes \(Z^2+Z+y\) irreducible over
\(\F_{2^{16}}\).  Thus \(x\notin\F_{2^{16}}\); because every proper
subfield of \(\F_{2^{32}}\) lies in \(\F_{2^{16}}\), \(x\) has degree
thirty-two over \(\F_2\).  Moreover

\[
 x^2+yx+y^3=y^2(z^2+z+y)=0.                          \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_4\)}
\]

Modulo the relation in \((\mathsf{NI}_4)\), direct reduction gives

\[
 B(X)=C(Y)\bigl(Q_1(Y)X+Q_0(Y)\bigr),
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_5\)}
\]

where

\[
\begin{split}
 Q_1={}&Y^{29}+Y^{28}+Y^{25}+Y^{24}+Y^{21}+Y^{18}
       +Y^{17}+Y^{16}\\
      &\quad{}+Y^{15}+Y^{14}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^9+Y^6,\\
 Q_0={}&Y^{32}+Y^{31}+Y^{29}+Y^{28}+Y^{27}+Y^{26}
       +Y^{25}+Y^{23}\\
      &\quad{}+Y^{20}+Y^{14}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^9+1.
\end{split}
\]

Thus \(B(x)=0\).  Its displayed degree is thirty-two, so it is the
minimal polynomial of \(x\), and consequently the stated resultant.

The multiplicative order of this successor can also be read exactly,
without sampling.  Put \(q=2^{16}\).  Since \(y^q=y\) and the two roots
of \(Z^2+Z+y\) are \(z,z+1\), one has \(z^q=z+1\).  Therefore, with
\(v=(z+1)/z\),
\[
 x^{q+1}=y^3,
 \qquad
 x^{q-1}=v.                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_{5a}\)}
\]
The element \(v\) is a nonidentity element of the norm-one group of
order \(q+1=65537\); that order is prime, so \(\ord(v)=q+1\).  On the
other hand, \((\mathsf{NI}_{2a})\) and
\(\gcd(3,21845)=1\) give
\(\ord(y^3)=(q-1)/3\).

Now
\[
 \F_{q^2}^{\times}
 =\F_q^{\times}\times
   \ker\!\left(\Norm_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}\right),
\]
because the two factor orders \(q-1\) and \(q+1\) are coprime.  Write
the corresponding components of \(x\) as \(x_-\) and \(x_+\).
Equation \((\mathsf{NI}_{5a})\) has orders
\(\ord(x_-^2)=\ord(x_-)\) and
\(\ord(x_+^{-2})=\ord(x_+)\), since both factor groups have odd order.
It follows that
\[
 \ord(x_-)=\frac{q-1}{3},\qquad
 \ord(x_+)=q+1,
\]
and their coprime product proves \((\mathsf{NI}_{2b})\).  Notice that
the loss of \(641\) below concerns the following quotient of order
\(2^{32}+1=F_5\), not either component in this decomposition; keeping
these two adjacent quadratic levels separate is essential.

It remains to identify the target stratum.  From a pair
\((f,g)=(S_r,S_{r+1})\), put

\[
 A=f^2,\qquad D=g^2+Xf^2.
\]

Reading one further binary digit sends the pair to \((A,D)\) for a
zero and to \((D,g^2)\) for a one.  Repeated reduction modulo \(B\)
along the binary word

\[
 6700417=(11001100011110110000001)_2
\]

ends at the exact residue pair

\[
 \bigl(S_{6700417},S_{6700418}\bigr)
 \equiv
 \bigl(\mathtt{00000000},\mathtt{1875a343}\bigr)
 \pmod B,                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_6\)}
\]

where a hexadecimal bit in position \(j\) is the coefficient of \(X^j\).
This is an \(O(\log 6700417)\) polynomial-identity certificate, not
field sampling.  Hence \(S_{6700417}(x)=0\).  The integer \(6700417\)
is prime, so \(x\) belongs to the exact order-\(6700417\) fibotomic
stratum.  Finally, if \(v=(z+1)/z\), then

\[
 y=\frac{v}{(v+1)^2},\qquad
 x=yz=\frac{v}{(v+1)^3},
\]

showing directly that this is the same fibotomic/elliptic transition as
the Conway resultant.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Ancestry refinements and exact no-go results}

\begin{proposition}[reciprocal-trace ancestry and a trace-refined no-go]
\label{prop:fermat-reciprocal-trace}
Every distinguished Conway factor satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/\mathbb F_2}(a_i^{-1})=1.       \tag{\(\mathsf{RT}_1\)}
\]
Equivalently, the constant and linear coefficients of every \(A_i\)
are both one.  More generally, this reciprocal-trace bit is preserved
by every full-degree transition of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}.

This extra ancestry invariant still does not make one-step maximality
inductive.  Put
\[
 C_1(Y)=Y^{16}+Y^{15}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^6+Y+1.
\]
Then \(C_1\) is an irreducible full-order factor of
\(\mathfrak F_{65537}\), has constant and linear coefficients one, and
its exact Conway resultant is
\[
\begin{split}
 B_1(X)={}&X^{32}+X^{31}+X^{30}+X^{28}+X^{27}+X^{26}
 +X^{24}+X^{23}+X^{22}\\
 &+X^{20}+X^{19}+X^{18}+X^{16}+X^{15}+X^{14}
 +X^{13}+X^{12}+X^{11}\\
 &+X^9+X^8+X^7+X^5+X^2+X+1.
\end{split}                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{RT}_2\)}
\]
The polynomial \(B_1\) is irreducible but belongs to the proper-order
stratum
\[
 \mathfrak F_{6700417},\qquad 6700417=F_5/641.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{RT}_3\)}
\]
Thus predecessor maximality, the exact transition, and the first
reciprocal coefficient together still do not force target maximality.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The two \(E_i/E_{i-1}\)-conjugates of \(a_i^{-1}\) have sum
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/E_{i-1}}(a_i^{-1})
 =\frac{a_{i-1}}{a_{i-1}^3}=a_{i-1}^{-2}.
\]
Taking absolute traces and using compatibility with squaring gives
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(a_i^{-1})
 =\operatorname{Tr}(a_{i-1}^{-1})^2.
\]
Starting with \(a_{-1}=1\) proves \((\mathsf{RT}_1)\).  The absolute
norms are also one, so the constant coefficient is one and the linear
coefficient is norm times reciprocal trace.

For the more general preservation statement, let \(A\) be a monic
irreducible polynomial with constant coefficient one and root \(t\).
Its transition satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Res}_Y(A(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3)
 =\operatorname{Norm}_{\mathbb F_2(t)/\mathbb F_2}
   (1+t^{-2}X+t^{-3}X^2).
\]
The coefficient of \(X\) is therefore
\(\operatorname{Tr}(t^{-2})=\operatorname{Tr}(t^{-1})^2\), the same
bit as the linear coefficient of \(A\).

We now certify the countermodel.  Modulo \(C_1\),
\[
 Y^{2^{16}}=Y,
 \qquad
 Y^{256}+Y=R:=Y^{13}+Y^9+Y^7+Y^4+Y,
\]
and the exact B\'ezout identity \(UC_1+VR=1\) holds for
\[
\begin{split}
 U&=Y^{10}+Y^9+Y^7+Y^6+Y^5+Y^4+Y^3+1,\\
 V&=Y^{13}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^7+Y^3+Y^2+1.
\end{split}
\]
Rabin's criterion proves \(C_1\) irreducible.  If \(y\) is a root,
its trace is one, and the identity
\[
 S_{2^m+1}(Y)=1+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Y^{2^j}
\]
gives \(S_{65537}(y)=0\).  Since \(65537\) is prime, this is the
full-order stratum.

Direct expansion of the resultant gives \((\mathsf{RT}_2)\).  If
\(z^2+z=y\) and \(x=yz\), then \(x^2+yx+y^3=0\).  The trace-one
condition makes \(Z^2+Z+y\) irreducible over \(\mathbb F_{2^{16}}\),
so \(x\) has degree thirty-two.  Hence the displayed degree-thirty-two
resultant is irreducible.

Finally, repeated use of
\[
 S_{2r}=S_r^2,
 \qquad
 S_{2r+1}=S_{r+1}^2+XS_r^2
\]
along
\(6700417=(11001100011110110000001)_2\) gives the exact residues
\[
 (S_{6700417},S_{6700418})
 \equiv(0,\mathtt{3407b7e4})\pmod{B_1},
\]
where hexadecimal bit \(j\) is the coefficient of \(X^j\).  The
second residue is nonzero, and \(6700417\) is prime.  The fibotomic
criterion proves \((\mathsf{RT}_3)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[nine absolute moments do not force one-step maximality]
\label{prop:fermat-nine-moment-no-go}
The distinguished degree-sixteen factor is
\[
 A_3=X^{16}+X^{15}+X^{11}+X^{10}+X^8+X^7+X^6
      +X^3+X^2+X+1.
\]
Put
\[
 C=X^{16}+X^{15}+X^{11}+X^8+X^5+X^3+X^2+X+1.
\]
Then \(A_3+C=X^{10}+X^7+X^6+X^5\).  If \(a\) and \(y\)
are roots of \(A_3\) and \(C\), respectively, then
\[
 \bigl(\operatorname{Tr}(a^k)\bigr)_{k=1}^5
 =\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}(y^k)\bigr)_{k=1}^5
 =(1,1,1,1,0),
\]
and
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(a^{-k})=\operatorname{Tr}(y^{-k})=1
 \qquad(1\leq k\leq4).
\]
The factor \(C\) is irreducible and lies in the full-order stratum
\(\mathfrak F_{65537}\).  Nevertheless its exact Conway resultant is
\[
\begin{split}
 B={}&X^{32}+X^{31}+X^{30}+X^{20}+X^{19}+X^{16}+X^{15}
       +X^{14}\\
    &{}+X^{11}+X^8+X^5+X+1,
\end{split}
\]
which is irreducible and lies in the proper stratum
\(\mathfrak F_{6700417}\), where \(6700417=F_5/641\).
Thus predecessor maximality, the exact transition, the first five
forward power traces, and the first four inverse-power traces still do
not force target maximality.  These are absolute \(\mathbb F_2\)-moment
data only: \(C\) does not have the distinguished relative trace--norm
ancestry of \(A_3\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a monic polynomial
\(P=X^{16}+e_1X^{15}+\cdots+e_{16}\), Newton's identities in
characteristic two read
\[
 p_k+e_1p_{k-1}+\cdots+e_{k-1}p_1+(k\bmod2)e_k=0,
 \qquad p_k=\sum_{P(r)=0}r^k.
\]
Although the final term vanishes for even \(k\), equality of
\(e_1,\ldots,e_s\) still inductively forces equality of
\(p_1,\ldots,p_s\).  The leading five coefficient tuples of \(A_3\)
and \(C\) are both \((1,0,0,0,1)\), giving
\((p_1,\ldots,p_5)=(1,1,1,1,0)\).  Apply the same identities to
\(X^{16}P(X^{-1})/P(0)\): the first four coefficient tuples are both
\((1,1,1,0)\), giving the asserted inverse traces.

We give exact polynomial certificates for the remaining claims.  In the
following hexadecimal strings, bit \(j\) is the coefficient of degree
\(j\).  Modulo \(C=\mathtt{1892f}\),
\[
 Y^{2^{16}}=Y,
 \qquad R_C:=Y^{2^8}+Y=\mathtt{3877},
\]
and
\[
 U_C=\mathtt{cca},\qquad V_C=\mathtt{73b1},\qquad
 U_CC+V_CR_C=1.
\]
Rabin's criterion proves that \(C\) is irreducible.  Its
\(Y^{15}\)-coefficient gives \(\operatorname{Tr}(y)=1\), and hence
\[
 S_{65537}(y)=1+\sum_{j=0}^{15}y^{2^j}=0.
\]
Since \(65537\) is prime, \(C\) lies in the full-order stratum.

In the quotient by \(X^2+YX+Y^3\), exact reduction gives
\[
 B(X)=C(Y)\bigl(Q_1(Y)X+Q_0(Y)\bigr),
 \quad Q_1=\mathtt{31180083},\quad Q_0=\mathtt{1a928cc53}.
\]
If \(z^2+z=y\) and \(x=yz\), then \(x^2+yx+y^3=0\).
The trace-one condition makes \(Z^2+Z+y\) irreducible over
\(\mathbb F_{2^{16}}\), so \(x\) has degree thirty-two.  Hence the
monic degree-thirty-two polynomial \(B\) is the exact resultant and is
irreducible.  Independently, its Rabin certificate is
\[
 X^{2^{32}}=X\pmod B,
 \qquad R_B:=X^{2^{16}}+X=\mathtt{e55f1c7a},
\]
\[
 U_B=\mathtt{3295d0c3},\qquad
 V_B=\mathtt{6748328a},\qquad U_BB+V_BR_B=1.
\]
Finally the exact Fibonacci residues are
\[
 (S_{6700417},S_{6700418})
 \equiv(0,\mathtt{bda50b33})\pmod B,
 \qquad S_{641}\equiv\mathtt{7acf6362}\pmod B.
\]
As \(6700417\) is prime, the fibotomic criterion places \(B\) in the
claimed exact proper-order stratum.  If \(C\) had the distinguished
relative trace--norm ancestry,
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion} would force it to be a
Frobenius conjugate of \(a_3\), contrary to \(C\ne A_3\); this proves
the final scope statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[higher reciprocal moments]
The full inverse-power trace sequence propagates exactly, but quickly
becomes deeper ancestry rather than a short invariant.  If \(t=a_{i-1}\)
and
\[
 D_0=0,\qquad D_1=t^{-2},\qquad
 D_j=t^{-2}D_{j-1}+t^{-3}D_{j-2},
\]
then
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/\mathbb F_2}(a_i^{-j})
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{E_{i-1}/\mathbb F_2}(D_j(a_{i-1})).
\]
For any fixed factor, sufficiently many moments recover its minimal
polynomial.  What is not known is any uniformly bounded initial segment
which forces the full fibotomic stratum throughout the tower.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[complete ancestry and exact trace-fibre exhaustion]
\label{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion}
For \(i\geq0\) and \(-1\leq j<i\), the distinguished
elements satisfy
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/E_j}(a_i)=a_j,\qquad
 \operatorname{Norm}_{E_i/E_j}(a_i)=a_j^{3^{i-j}}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_1\)}
\]
Conversely, fix \(i\geq0\) and \(x_i\in E_i\).  Put
\[
 x_j=\operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/E_j}(x_i)\qquad(-1\leq j<i).
\]
If \(x_{-1}=1\) and
\[
 \operatorname{Norm}_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(x_j)=x_{j-1}^3
 \qquad(0\leq j\leq i),                                \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_2\)}
\]
then \(x_i\) is an \(\mathbb F_2\)-Frobenius conjugate of \(a_i\).
Thus \((\mathsf{AN}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{AN}_2)\) are a
necessary-and-sufficient ancestry invariant for the actual resultant
factor.

Now fix \(n\geq1\), put
\[
 F=E_{n-1}=\mathbb F_q,\qquad K=E_n=\mathbb F_{q^2},
\]
and let \(\ell\mid q+1\).  Set
\[
 H=(K^\times)^\ell,\qquad
 U=\ker\operatorname{Norm}_{K/F}.
\]
For every \(t\in F^\times\),
\[
 \#\{h\in H:\operatorname{Tr}_{K/F}(h)=t\}
   =\frac{q+1}{\ell}-1.                                \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_3\)}
\]
If \(t=a_{n-1}\), every element counted in
\((\mathsf{AN}_3)\) has absolute trace one and is a normal, hence
full-degree, element of \(K/\mathbb F_2\).  Thus, whenever \(q+1\)
is composite, full degree, normality, the complete Frobenius-orbit
length, and the actual relative-trace value all occur among
\(\ell\)-th powers.

The normalized relative norms of the elements in
\((\mathsf{AN}_3)\) form exactly
\[
 \mathcal B_\ell=
 \left\{\frac{u}{(u+1)^2}:u\in U^\ell,\ u\ne1\right\},
 \qquad
 |\mathcal B_\ell|
 =\frac12\left(\frac{q+1}{\ell}-1\right).               \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_4\)}
\]
More precisely, an element \(h\) of relative trace \(t\) is an
\(\ell\)-th power and has relative norm \(t^3\) if and only if
\(t\in\mathcal B_\ell\).  For \(t=a_{n-1}\) this is equivalent to
\[
 S_{(q+1)/\ell}(a_{n-1})=0.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_5\)}
\]
Finally \([a_n]=[c_n]\) in \(K^\times/K^{\times\ell}\).
Consequently the entire earlier ancestry is recoverable exactly, but
after the last quadratic step its power-residue content is precisely
the original fibotomic membership \((\mathsf{AN}_5)\).  Trace,
separate norm, normality, and Frobenius-orbit invariants give no
additional obstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The relation
\[
 a_i^2+a_{i-1}a_i+a_{i-1}^3=0
\]
is the minimal polynomial of \(a_i\) over \(E_{i-1}\).  It gives
immediate trace \(a_{i-1}\) and norm \(a_{i-1}^3\); transitivity gives
\((\mathsf{AN}_1)\).

For the converse, transitivity of trace gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(x_j)=x_{j-1}\).
Together with \((\mathsf{AN}_2)\) this says that \(x_j\) is a root of
\[
 X^2+x_{j-1}X+x_{j-1}^3.
\]
Starting from \(x_{-1}=a_{-1}=1\), induct on \(j\).  If
\(x_{j-1}\) is a Frobenius conjugate of \(a_{j-1}\), the displayed
polynomial is the corresponding Frobenius conjugate of the polynomial
for \(a_j\); its two roots are the two lifts of that conjugacy to
\(E_j\).  Hence \(x_j\), and finally \(x_i\), is a Frobenius conjugate
of the distinguished element.

Since \(\gcd(\ell,q-1)=1\), one has \(F^\times\subset H\).
Multiplication by any \(t\in F^\times\) bijects the trace-one and
trace-\(t\) fibres in \(H\).  In characteristic two,
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{K/F}(h)=0\) is equivalent to \(h^q=h\), so the
nonzero trace-zero elements are exactly \(F^\times\), all of which lie
in \(H\).  If the common nonzero-fibre size is \(M\), then
\[
 \frac{q^2-1}{\ell}=|H|=(q-1)+(q-1)M,
\]
which gives \((\mathsf{AN}_3)\).

The distinguished \(a_{n-1}\) has absolute trace one by
\((\mathsf{AN}_1)\).  Thus every \(h\) in its relative-trace fibre
also has absolute trace one.  To justify normality, let
\(N=[K:\mathbb F_2]\), a power of two, and let \(\rho\) generate its
cyclic Galois group.  After choosing one normal generator, \(K\) is
the regular module over
\[
 \mathbb F_2[\langle\rho\rangle]
 \simeq\mathbb F_2[X]/((X+1)^N),
\]
a local ring.  An element is a normal generator exactly when its
group-ring coordinate is a unit, equivalently when multiplication by
\((\rho+1)^{N-1}\) does not kill it.  Since
\[
 (\rho+1)^{N-1}=1+\rho+\cdots+\rho^{N-1},
\]
this last value is its absolute trace.  Hence absolute trace one is
equivalent to normality.

Write \(h=tz\) in a nonzero trace fibre.  Then
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{K/F}(z)=1\), so \(z^q=z+1\) and
\[
 \operatorname{Norm}_{K/F}(h)=t^2z(z+1).
\]
Put \(u=(z+1)/z\in U\); then \(z=1/(u+1)\) and
\[
 z(z+1)=\frac{u}{(u+1)^2}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_6\)}
\]
We claim \(z\in H\) if and only if \(u\in U^\ell\).  The forward
implication follows from \(u=z^{q-1}\).  Conversely, if
\(u\in U^\ell\), then \(u\in H\).  The right side of
\((\mathsf{AN}_6)\) lies in \(F^\times\subset H\), so in
\(K^\times/H\) one has
\[
 [u]=2[u+1].
\]
Because \(\ell\) is odd, multiplication by two is invertible; hence
\([u]=0\) forces \([u+1]=0\), and
\(z=(u+1)^{-1}\in H\).  The map
\(u\mapsto u/(u+1)^2\) identifies precisely the inverse pair
\(\{u,u^{-1}\}\), proving \((\mathsf{AN}_4)\).

The equality \(\operatorname{Norm}(h)=t^3\) is, after division by
\(t^2\), exactly \(t=z(z+1)\).  This proves the first assertion
following \((\mathsf{AN}_4)\).  The set \(U^\ell\) has order
\((q+1)/\ell\), and the Fibonacci-polynomial factorization identifies
its nontrivial inverse pairs with the roots of
\(S_{(q+1)/\ell}\), proving \((\mathsf{AN}_5)\).  Finally
\(a_n=a_{n-1}c_n\) and \(a_{n-1}\in F^\times\subset H\), so
\([a_n]=[c_n]\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[a completely normal primitive candidate]
\label{cor:fermat-completely-normal}
For every \(n\geq0\), the elements \(a_n\) and \(c_0a_n\) are normal
over every intermediate field \(E_j\), \(-1\leq j<n\).  In particular
they are completely normal over \(\mathbb F_2\).  Moreover

\[
 \ord(c_0a_n)=3\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j,                 \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_1\)}
\]

so

\[
 c_0a_n\text{ is primitive in }E_n
 \iff \delta_j=F_j\quad(1\leq j\leq n).              \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_2\)}
\]

Thus the singleton-even conjecture may equivalently be stated as
primitivity of one explicit completely normal element.

By contrast, the Popovych product \(c_0c_n\) is normal only over the
immediately preceding field when \(n\geq2\):

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_{n-1}}(c_0c_n)=c_0,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_j}(c_0c_n)=0
 \quad(-1\leq j\leq n-2).                             \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_3\)}
\]

Hence complete normality does not supply the missing primary order,
and no absolute normal-basis theorem applies directly to \(c_0c_n\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
For a cyclic extension \(L/K\) of characteristic two and degree a power
of two, an element \(x\in L\) is normal over \(K\) if and only if
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(x)\ne0\).  Indeed, after choosing a normal
generator, the regular module is

\[
 K[C_{2^r}]\simeq K[X]/((X+1)^{2^r}),
\]

a local ring.  The coordinate of \(x\) is a unit exactly when its
augmentation is nonzero, and that augmentation is nonzero exactly when
the relative trace of \(x\) is nonzero.

Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion} gives

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_j}(a_n)=a_j\ne0
 \qquad(-1\leq j<n),
\]

so \(a_n\) is normal over every \(E_j\).  For \(j\geq0\), the scalar
\(c_0\) lies in \(E_j\), and hence

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_j}(c_0a_n)=c_0a_j\ne0.
\]

At the bottom,

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/\mathbb F_2}(c_0a_n)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{E_0/\mathbb F_2}(c_0a_0)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{E_0/\mathbb F_2}(c_0^2)=1.
\]

This proves complete normality.  Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence}
gives \(\ord(a_n)=\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j\), which is prime to three;
since \(c_0\) has order three, \((\mathsf{CN}_1)\) follows.  The identity

\[
 |E_n^\times|=3\prod_{j=1}^nF_j
\]

and the pairwise coprimality of the Fermat numbers prove
\((\mathsf{CN}_2)\).

Finally the quadratic equation for \(c_n\) has trace one, so the first
trace in \((\mathsf{CN}_3)\) is \(c_0\).  Every further relative
trace takes the lower-field element \(c_0\) through a positive even
extension degree and is therefore zero in characteristic two.  The
normality criterion above completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\(\ell\)-th-root ancestry and the exact Dickson obstruction]
\label{prop:fermat-root-ancestry}
For variables \(S,P\), define the characteristic-two Dickson power
sums by
\[
 \mathcal D_0(S,P)=0,\qquad
 \mathcal D_1(S,P)=S,\qquad
 \mathcal D_{r+2}(S,P)
   =S\mathcal D_{r+1}(S,P)+P\mathcal D_r(S,P).
\]
Thus, whenever \(x+y=S\) and \(xy=P\),
\[
 \mathcal D_r(S,P)=x^r+y^r.
\]

Fix \(n\geq2\) and a prime \(\ell\mid F_n\).  Put \(r_{-1}=1\), and
for \(0\leq j<n\) let \(r_j\in E_j^\times\) be the unique element
such that
\[
 r_j^\ell=a_j.
\]
Then, for every \(0\leq j<n\),
\[
 \Norm_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(r_j)=r_{j-1}^3,
 \qquad
 \mathcal D_\ell\!\left(
   \operatorname{Tr}_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(r_j),r_{j-1}^3
 \right)=r_{j-1}^\ell.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{RA}\)}
\]

Set \(b=r_{n-1}\).  Failure of the \(\ell\)-primary
Conway--Fermat condition at the top step has the exact descended form
\[
 c_n\in(E_n^\times)^\ell
 \iff
 \text{there exists }s\in E_{n-1}
 \text{ such that }\mathcal D_\ell(s,b)=1.            \tag{\(\mathsf{RD}\)}
\]
If additionally \(\delta_j=F_j\) for every \(1\leq j<n\), then, with
\(q=|E_{n-1}|=2^{2^n}\),
\[
 \ord(b)=\ord(a_{n-1})=\frac{q-1}{3}.
\]
Consequently all exact lower trace, norm, and multiplicative-order
data are automatically compatible with a hypothetical top failure;
the single selected equation \((\mathsf{RD})\) is the remaining
obstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because \(\ell\mid F_n\), it is coprime to \(|E_j^\times|\) for every
\(j<n\).  Hence the \(\ell\)-power map is an automorphism on every
lower multiplicative group and the elements \(r_j\) are unique.
Applying the \(\ell\)-power map to the norm and using
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion} gives
\[
 \Norm(r_j)^\ell
 =\Norm(a_j)
 =a_{j-1}^3
 =(r_{j-1}^3)^\ell.
\]
Injectivity of the \(\ell\)-power map on \(E_{j-1}^\times\) proves
the first identity in \((\mathsf{RA})\).  If \(r_j'\) is the relative
conjugate of \(r_j\), then
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal D_\ell\!\left(r_j+r_j',r_jr_j'\right)
 &=r_j^\ell+(r_j')^\ell\\
 &=\operatorname{Tr}_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(a_j)
 =a_{j-1}=r_{j-1}^\ell,
 \end{aligned}
\]
which proves the second.

The group \(E_{n-1}^\times\) is uniquely \(\ell\)-divisible, while
\(E_n^\times/E_{n-1}^\times\) is cyclic.  Hence the coset of \(c_n\)
misses the full \(\ell\)-primary part of the quotient if and only if
it is an \(\ell\)-th power there, equivalently if and only if
\(c_n\in(E_n^\times)^\ell\).

Suppose first that \(c_n=t^\ell\).  Since
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_{n-1}}(c_n)=1\) and
\(\Norm_{E_n/E_{n-1}}(c_n)=a_{n-1}\), injectivity below gives
\[
 \Norm(t)=b.
\]
For \(s=\operatorname{Tr}(t)\), the power-sum identity therefore gives
\[
 \mathcal D_\ell(s,b)=\operatorname{Tr}(t^\ell)=1.
\]

Conversely, suppose \(\mathcal D_\ell(s,b)=1\), and let \(t,t'\) be
the roots of
\[
 X^2+sX+b.
\]
They lie in the unique extension of \(E_{n-1}\) of degree at most two,
hence in \(E_n\).  Their \(\ell\)-th powers satisfy
\[
 t^\ell+(t')^\ell=1,\qquad
 t^\ell(t')^\ell=b^\ell=a_{n-1}.
\]
Thus \(t^\ell,(t')^\ell\) are the two roots of
\[
 X^2+X+a_{n-1},
\]
namely \(c_n,c_n+1\).  In particular \(c_n\) is an \(\ell\)-th
power, proving \((\mathsf{RD})\).

Finally, prior maximality, Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence},
and the \(n=1\) base case established in its proof give
\[
 \ord(a_{n-1})
 =\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}F_j
 =\frac{q-1}{3}.
\]
Taking the unique \(\ell\)-th root preserves this order because
\(\ell\) is coprime to \(q-1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[choice-free lower root]
\label{cor:fermat-explicit-lower-root}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-root-ancestry}, and put
\[
 q=|E_{n-1}|,\qquad d=\frac{q+1}{\ell},\qquad Q=\frac q2,
 \qquad a=a_{n-1},\qquad b=r_{n-1}.
\]
Then the unique lower \(\ell\)-th root occurring in the Dickson
obstruction is determined without any root choice:
\[
 b^2=a^d,
 \qquad
 b=(a^d)^Q.                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{RE}\)}
\]
Consequently the exact failure criterion \((\mathsf{RD})\) is
\[
 c_n\in(E_n^\times)^\ell
 \iff
 \text{there exists }s\in E_{n-1}\text{ such that }
 \mathcal D_\ell\!\left(s,(a^d)^Q\right)=1.           \tag{\(\mathsf{RD}'\)}
\]
Thus the descent contains no auxiliary choice of a compatible lower root.
It still asks whether one selected Dickson value vanishes, so
\((\mathsf{RE})\) is a lossless normalization rather than a
nonvanishing theorem.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Since \(b\in\mathbb F_q\), one has \(b^q=b\).  The identities
\(b^\ell=a\) and \(\ell d=q+1\) give
\[
 b^2=b^{q+1}=b^{\ell d}=a^d.
\]
As \(q=2Q\), Frobenius fixity then gives
\[
 b=b^q=(b^2)^Q=(a^d)^Q.
\]
Substitution in \((\mathsf{RD})\) proves \((\mathsf{RD}')\).
The two displayed identities are formalized in the companion Lean file.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[hypothetical-failure Fibonacci blocks collapse]
\label{prop:fermat-failure-block-collapse}
Retain Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-explicit-lower-root}.  Thus
\[
 q=2^{2^n},\qquad N=q+1,\qquad
 d=\frac{N}{\ell},\qquad Q=\frac q2,\qquad
 a=a_{n-1}.
\]
Assume the hypothetical selected failure
\[
 S_d(a)=0,
\]
and put \(b_F=S_{d+1}(a)\).  Then every shifted Fibonacci value is
already determined by the initial length-\(d\) block and this one scalar:
\[
 \boxed{
 S_{kd+r}(a)=b_F^{\,k}S_r(a)
 \qquad(k,r\geq0).}                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{FBC}_1\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \boxed{
 b_F^2=a^d,\qquad b_F^\ell=a,\qquad
 b_F=(a^d)^Q.}                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{FBC}_2\)}
\]
Consequently \(b_F\) is exactly the choice-free lower
\(\ell\)-th root \(b=r_{n-1}\) of
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-explicit-lower-root}; the adjacent Fibonacci
value does not supply a second Kummer coordinate.

There is a universal polynomial form.  In \(\F_2[X]\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 S_{kd+r}(X)
   &\equiv S_{d+1}(X)^kS_r(X) \pmod{S_d(X)},\\
 S_{d+1}(X)^2
   &\equiv X^d \pmod{S_d(X)},\\
 S_{d+1}(X)^\ell
   &\equiv X \pmod{S_d(X)}.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{FBC}_3\)}
\]
Thus every selected consequence depending only on the residues of
consecutive or \(d\)-shifted Fibonacci values modulo the hypothetical
failure factor---including ring identities obtained from their continued
fractions, Cassini relations, or Euclidean reductions after passage to that
quotient---reduces to the initial block together with the already-known root
\((a^d)^Q\).  It cannot create a new nonvanishing constraint.

This does not determine whether \(A_{n-1}\mid S_d\).  In particular it
does not eliminate coefficient- or degree-sensitive information in the
intermediate Euclidean quotient polynomials before reduction modulo
\(S_d\), nor arbitrary subresultants of \(A_{n-1}\) and \(S_d\).
Those remain possible ancestry-sensitive inputs.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For fixed \(d\), both sequences
\[
 r\longmapsto S_{d+r}(a),
 \qquad
 r\longmapsto S_{d+1}(a)S_r(a)
\]
satisfy \(T_{r+2}=T_{r+1}+aT_r\).  Under \(S_d(a)=0\), their initial
values are respectively \((0,S_{d+1}(a))\) and
\((0,S_{d+1}(a))\).  They are therefore equal.  Iterating this
one-block identity proves \((\mathsf{FBC}_1)\).

Cassini's identity in characteristic two is
\[
 S_{r+1}(X)^2+S_r(X)S_{r+2}(X)=X^r.
\]
At \(r=d\), the failure hypothesis gives \(b_F^2=a^d\).  Taking
\(k=\ell,r=0\) in \((\mathsf{FBC}_1)\) gives \(S_N(a)=0\), while
\(S_q(X)=1\) because \(q\) is a power of two.  The Fibonacci recurrence
therefore gives
\[
 S_{N+1}(a)=S_{q+2}(a)
 =S_{q+1}(a)+aS_q(a)=a.
\]
Taking \(k=\ell,r=1\) in \((\mathsf{FBC}_1)\) now yields
\[
 b_F^\ell=S_{\ell d+1}(a)=S_{N+1}(a)=a.
\]
Finally \(b_F\in\F_q\), so \(b_F^q=b_F\).  Since \(q=2Q\),
\[
 b_F=(b_F^2)^Q=(a^d)^Q.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{FBC}_2)\) and identifies \(b_F\) with the unique
lower \(\ell\)-th root.

Apply the same recurrence argument in the quotient ring
\(\F_2[X]/(S_d)\).  There \(S_d=0\), so the block identity and Cassini
give the first two congruences of \((\mathsf{FBC}_3)\).  Taking
\(k=\ell,r=1\), using \(\ell d=q+1\), \(S_q=1\), and
\[
 S_{q+2}=S_{q+1}+XS_q,
\]
gives the third congruence.

Lean kernel-checks the recurrence block identity, its iteration, Cassini
in characteristic two, and the forced \(\ell\)-th-root identity.  Their
specialization to the selected Conway field and the polynomial-quotient
interpretation remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the first Fibonacci quotient digit under failure]
\label{cor:fermat-first-quotient-digit}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-failure-block-collapse}, and write
\[
 S_d=AQ_d+\rho_d,\qquad A=A_{n-1}.
\]
Under the hypothetical failure \(A\mid S_d\), the first \(A\)-adic
quotient digit is
\[
 \boxed{
 Q_d\equiv (A')^{-1}S_d'
      \equiv (XA')^{-1}S_{d+1}\pmod A.}              \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_9\)}
\]
Here \(X\) and \(A'\) are units modulo \(A\): one has \(A(0)=1\), while
the irreducible finite-field polynomial \(A\) is separable.  With
\[
 a=a_{n-1},\qquad
 b_F=S_{d+1}(a)=(a^d)^{q/2},
\]
one has
\[
 \boxed{
 Q_d(a)=\frac{b_F}{aA'(a)}.}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{FQD}_{10}\)}
\]
Thus the class of \(Q_d\) in
\(\F_2[X]/(A)\simeq\F_q\), and hence every Conway-coordinate descent,
norm, resultant, or trace of that first digit, is already determined by
the selected minimal polynomial and the existing choice-free Kummer root.
This does not control higher \(A\)-adic digits, equivalently
\(S_d\bmod A^j\) for \(j\geq3\), or global coefficient relations in the
unreduced quotient.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Under failure, \(\rho_d=0\).  The first identity in
\((\mathsf{FQD}_4)\) gives
\[
 S_d'+A'Q_d\equiv0\pmod A.
\]
Invertibility of \(A'\) proves the first congruence in
\((\mathsf{FQD}_9)\).  The relation
\(S_{d+1}\equiv XS_d'\pmod A\) proves the second.

Evaluation at \(a\) gives
\[
 S_{d+1}(a)=aS_d'(a),
\]
because \(S_d(a)=0\).  Hence \(S_d'(a)=b_F/a\), and evaluation of
\((\mathsf{FQD}_9)\) gives \((\mathsf{FQD}_{10})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[local uniformizers and quotient endpoint windows collapse]
\label{prop:fermat-higher-quotient-window-collapse}
Fix \(n\geq2\), and put
\[
 M=2^n,\qquad q=2^M,\qquad N=q+1,\qquad A=A_{n-1}.
\]
Let \(\ell\) be a proper prime divisor of \(N\), put \(d=N/\ell>1\),
and assume the hypothetical failure \(A\mid S_d\).  Write
\[
 Q_d=\frac{S_d}{A},\qquad
 Q_N^{\mathrm{full}}=\frac{S_N}{A}.
\]
The second quotient is unconditional, since
\(S_N(a_{n-1})=1+\operatorname{Tr}(a_{n-1})=0\).

For a nonzero polynomial \(P\), write
\[
 P^\vee(X)=X^{\deg P}P(X^{-1}).
\]
Then the two endpoint coefficient windows satisfy
\[
 \boxed{
 Q_d\equiv Q_N^{\mathrm{full}}\pmod{X^{4M}},\qquad
 Q_d^\vee\equiv (Q_N^{\mathrm{full}})^\vee\pmod{X^M}.}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{QW}_1\)}
\]
Thus the first \(4M\) low coefficients and the first \(M\) leading
coefficients of the hypothetical quotient are exactly those of the
unconditional full-Fermat cofactor; neither window depends on \(\ell\).

There is also an all-order local statement.  Let
\[
 \widehat{\mathcal O}_A
 =\widehat{\F_2[X]_{(A)}}
\]
be the \(A\)-adic completion.  The factorization \(S_d=AQ_d\) has
\(Q_d\in\widehat{\mathcal O}_A^\times\), and for every \(r\geq1\),
\[
 \boxed{(S_d^r)=(A^r)\quad\text{in }\widehat{\mathcal O}_A.}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{QW}_2\)}
\]
Choose a coefficient-field identification
\(\widehat{\mathcal O}_A\simeq\F_q[[T]]\), with \(A\mapsto T\).  Then
\[
 S_d\longmapsto T\,U_d(T),\qquad
 U_d(0)=Q_d(a_{n-1})
       =\frac{b_F}{a_{n-1}A'(a_{n-1})}\ne0,             \tag{\(\mathsf{QW}_3\)}
\]
and substitution \(T\mapsto T\,U_d(T)\) is a continuous
\(\F_q\)-algebra automorphism.

Consequently no argument depending only on the unmarked completed local
ring, its principal-ideal filtration, or the two endpoint windows in
\((\mathsf{QW}_1)\) can exclude failure.  This does not erase the higher
digits in the fixed \(A\)-coordinate: a relation coupling them to the global
coordinate \(X\), to middle coefficients of \(Q_d\), or to lower Conway
generators is not invariant under this reparametrization and remains a
possible ancestry-sensitive input.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Euler--Lucas congruence gives
\[
 \ell\equiv1\pmod{2^{n+2}}.
\]
Since \(N\equiv1\pmod{2^{n+2}}\), also
\[
 d=1+4Mh
\]
for an integer \(h\geq1\).  The trailing-zero identity gives
\[
 S_d
 =S_{h+1}^{\,4M}+T_{n+2}(X)S_h^{\,4M}.
\]
Every positive-index Fibonacci polynomial has constant coefficient one.
Hence, modulo \(X^{4M}\), both displayed Frobenius powers are one, and
\[
 S_d\equiv1+T_{n+2}(X)\pmod{X^{4M}}.
\]
On the other hand
\[
 S_N=1+T_M(X)
 \equiv1+T_{n+2}(X)\pmod{X^{4M}},
\]
because \(M\geq n+2\).  Therefore
\(A(Q_d-Q_N^{\mathrm{full}})\) is divisible by \(X^{4M}\).  Since \(A(0)=1\),
\(A\) is a unit modulo \(X^{4M}\), proving the first congruence.

Put \(D=\deg S_d=(d-1)/2=2Mh\).  The standard coefficient formula is
\[
 [X^{D-j}]S_d=\binom{D+j}{2j}\pmod2.
\]
For \(0<j<M\), the low \(n+1\) binary digits of \(D+j\) are those of
\(j\), while the highest nonzero digit of \(2j\) is one position above
the highest nonzero digit of \(j\).  Lucas's theorem therefore makes the
displayed coefficient zero.  Hence
\(S_d^\vee\equiv1\pmod{X^M}\).  The sparse formula for \(S_N\) likewise
gives \(S_N^\vee\equiv1\pmod{X^M}\).  Reciprocal factorization yields
\[
 S_d^\vee=A^\vee Q_d^\vee,\qquad
 S_N^\vee=A^\vee(Q_N^{\mathrm{full}})^\vee.
\]
Since \(A^\vee(0)=1\), cancellation modulo \(X^M\) proves the second
congruence.

Finally \(S_d\) is squarefree, so \(A\nmid Q_d\); hence \(Q_d\) is a
unit in the \(A\)-adic completion, proving \((\mathsf{QW}_2)\).
The residue in \((\mathsf{QW}_3)\) is
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-first-quotient-digit}.  A formal series
\(T\,U_d(T)\) with nonzero linear coefficient has a unique compositional
inverse, which proves the automorphism assertion.

Lean kernel-checks the unit-rescaling statement for every ideal power,
quotient-window cancellation by a common unit, and the nilpotent
trailing-zero identity.  The Fibonacci coefficient formula, Lucas window,
completed-local-ring identification, and selected specialization remain
paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the factor-sensitive endpoint boundary is exact]
\label{cor:fermat-factor-sensitive-window}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-higher-quotient-window-collapse}, and put
\[
 s=v_2(\ell-1),\qquad R=2^s.
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{v_2(d-1)=s,\qquad R\geq4M,\qquad s<M.}        \tag{\(\mathsf{QW}_4\)}
\]
Under the hypothetical failure \(A\mid S_d\), the first coefficients beyond
the factor-sensitive endpoint windows are exact:
\[
 \boxed{
 Q_d+Q_N^{\mathrm{full}}
   \equiv X^R\pmod{X^{R+1}},\qquad
 Q_d^\vee+(Q_N^{\mathrm{full}})^\vee
   \equiv X^{R/4}\pmod{X^{R/4+1}}.}                 \tag{\(\mathsf{QW}_5\)}
\]
Thus the quotients agree in their first \(R\) low coefficients and their
first \(R/4\) leading coefficients, and differ by one at the next coefficient
on each side.  Since \(R\geq4M\), this strictly sharpens
\((\mathsf{QW}_1)\).

Consequently even the first coefficient beyond either uniform window is not
a new selected obstruction: its location is determined by
\(v_2(\ell-1)\), and its defect is always one.  Relations involving later
coefficients, the middle of the quotient, both endpoints simultaneously, or
the fixed \(A\)-adic coordinate remain outside the conclusion.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Since \(2^M\equiv-1\pmod\ell\) and \(M=2^n\), one has
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2M\).  For \(n\geq2\), this first gives
\(\ell\equiv1\pmod8\), so two is a quadratic residue modulo \(\ell\).
Euler's criterion then shows that \(2M\mid(\ell-1)/2\), whence
\[
 4M\mid\ell-1.
\]
Therefore \(R\geq4M\).

Properness gives \(\ell<N=2^M+1\), hence
\[
 2^s=R\leq\ell-1<2^M,
\]
so \(s<M\).  Write \(\ell-1=Ra\), with \(a\) odd.  From \(\ell d=N\),
\[
 \ell(d-1)
 =N-\ell
 =2^M-(\ell-1)
 =R\bigl(2^{M-s}-a\bigr).
\]
The factor in parentheses is positive and odd, while \(\ell\) is odd.
Thus \(v_2(d-1)=s\).  Write
\[
 d=1+Rg,\qquad g\ \text{odd}.
\]

The trailing-zero identity at the exact extracted power \(R=2^s\) gives
\[
 S_d=S_{g+1}^{\,R}+T_s(X)S_g^{\,R}.
\]
Both Fibonacci polynomials have constant coefficient one, so
\[
 S_d\equiv1+T_s(X)\pmod{X^R}.
\]
Moreover the second summand contributes nothing at degree \(R\), while
\[
 [X^R]S_{g+1}^{\,R}
 =[X]S_{g+1}
 =\binom{g-1}{1}=0
 \quad\text{in }\F_2.
\]
On the other hand,
\[
 S_N=1+T_M(X)
 =1+T_s(X)+T_{M-s}(X)^R.
\]
Since \(s<M\), the last term has coefficient one at \(X^R\).  Hence
\[
 S_d+S_N\equiv X^R\pmod{X^{R+1}}.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{QW}_6\)}
\]

For the leading boundary put
\[
 D=\deg S_d=\frac{d-1}{2}=\frac R2g.
\]
The Fibonacci coefficient formula gives
\[
 [X^{D-j}]S_d=\binom{D+j}{2j}\pmod2.
\]
If \(0<j<R/4\), the low \(s-1\) binary digits of \(D+j\) are those of
\(j\), whereas the highest nonzero digit of \(2j\) lies one place above
the highest digit of \(j\) and still below the \(R/2\)-place.  Lucas's
theorem therefore makes the coefficient zero.  At \(j=R/4\), removing
the common \(2^{s-2}\)-shift in Lucas's product gives
\[
 \binom{D+R/4}{R/2}
 \equiv\binom{2g+1}{2}
 =g(2g+1)
 \equiv1\pmod2.
\]
Thus
\[
 S_d^\vee\equiv1+X^{R/4}\pmod{X^{R/4+1}}.
\]
The leading exponent of \(S_N\) is \(2^{M-1}\), and its next exponent is
\(2^{M-2}\); hence the first reciprocal gap is \(2^{M-2}\).  Since
\(s<M\),
\[
 R/4=2^{s-2}<2^{M-2},
\]
and therefore
\[
 S_N^\vee\equiv1\pmod{X^{R/4+1}}.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{QW}_7\)}
\]

Finally,
\[
 S_d=AQ_d,\qquad S_N=AQ_N^{\mathrm{full}},
\]
with \(A(0)=1\).  In \(\F_2[X]/(X^{R+1})\), multiplication by \(A\)
fixes \(X^R\), so cancellation in \((\mathsf{QW}_6)\) gives the first
congruence of \((\mathsf{QW}_5)\).  Reciprocal factorization uses the
common factor \(A^\vee\), whose constant coefficient is one because \(A\)
is monic.  The same argument applied to \((\mathsf{QW}_7)\) gives the
second congruence.

Lean kernel-checks the abstract common-factor boundary-defect transport.
The Fermat-divisor valuation, Fibonacci boundary coefficients, and Lucas
calculation remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[two fixed-coordinate quotient-jet blocks collapse]
\label{prop:fermat-fixed-adic-jet-blocks}
Retain Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-factor-sensitive-window}.  Thus
\[
 d=1+Rg,\qquad R=2^s,\qquad g\ \text{odd},\qquad
 T=T_s(X)=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}X^{2^i}.
\]
For a polynomial \(P\), write \(P^{[j]}\) for its \(j\)-th Hasse
derivative.  Put
\[
 r=\frac{g-1}{2},\qquad B=S_g,\qquad C=S_{g+1},\qquad
 H=S_r^{\,2R}.
\]
Then, as identities in \(\F_2[X]\),
\[
 \boxed{
 S_d^{[j]}=
 \begin{cases}
   S_d',&j=2^i,\quad 0\leq i<s,\\
   TH,&j=R,\\
   H,&j=R+2^i,\quad 0\leq i<s,\\
   0,&1\leq j<2R\ \text{otherwise},
 \end{cases}}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_1\)}
\]
and the apparent new scalar \(H\) satisfies the exact global identity
\[
 \boxed{X^R H=S_d+(1+T)S_d'.}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_2\)}
\]

Assume now the hypothetical failure \(A\mid S_d\), and write the unique
finite \(A\)-adic expansion, extended by zero digits, as
\[
 Q_d=\frac{S_d}{A}=\sum_{\nu\geq0}A^\nu q_\nu,
 \qquad \deg q_\nu<M.
\]
Then every fixed-coordinate digit
\[
 \boxed{q_0,q_1,\ldots,q_{2R-2}}                    \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_3\)}
\]
is determined recursively by \(A\), the fixed arithmetic parameter \(s\),
and the already-known first digit
\(q_0\equiv(A')^{-1}S_d'\pmod A\).  The second digit is explicitly
\[
 \boxed{
 (A')^2q_1\equiv(A'+A^{[2]})q_0+A'q_0'\pmod A.}       \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_4\)}
\]

The first Hasse jet not eliminated by these identities occurs at order
\(2R\).  With \(U=S_{r+1}\) and \(V=S_r\), it is
\[
 \boxed{
 S_d^{[2R]}=(1+T)(U')^{2R}+TX^R(V')^{2R}.}            \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_5\)}
\]
Equivalently, if \(r=2t\) this is
\((1+T)S_t^{4R}\), while if \(r=2t+1\) it is
\(TX^RS_t^{4R}\).  At the selected root \(a=a_{n-1}\), both displayed
prefactors are nonzero, and therefore
\[
 \boxed{
 S_d^{[2R]}(a)=0
 \iff S_{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}(a)=0.}                 \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_6\)}
\]
If \(r=2t\) is even, this value is automatically nonzero:
\[
 \boxed{r\equiv0\pmod2\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
        S_d^{[2R]}(a)\ne0.}                          \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_7\)}
\]
Thus the first term not reduced to the old quotient residue is exactly a
smaller selected Fibonacci value, and only the odd-\(r\) branch can retain a
zero there.  The proposition does not prove that remaining value nonzero, and
hence does not prove the Conway--Fermat maximal-order assertion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(R=2^s\), the Hasse--Frobenius rule is
\[
 (F^R)^{[j]}=0\quad(R\nmid j),\qquad
 (F^R)^{[Rm]}=(F^{[m]})^R.                            \tag{\(\ast\)}
\]
Moreover \(T^{[j]}=1\) when \(j=2^i\), \(0\leq i<s\), and is zero
for every other \(0<j<R\).  The trailing-zero compression and the
characteristic-two doubling identities give
\[
 S_d=C^R+TB^R,\qquad
 C=S_{2r+2}=S_{r+1}^2,\qquad
 B=S_{2r+1}=S_{r+1}^2+XS_r^2.
\]
In particular \(S_d'=B^R\), \(C'=0\), and \(B'=S_r^2\).  Applying
\((\ast)\) and the Hasse product rule gives \((\mathsf{QJ}_1)\).
Also
\[
 X^RH=(XS_r^2)^R=(B+C)^R=B^R+C^R
             =S_d+(1+T)S_d',
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{QJ}_2)\).

Let \(a=a_{n-1}\).  Since \(A\) is separable, \(A'(a)\ne0\).  For every
\(m\geq0\), repeated Hasse product expansion gives a triangular identity
\[
 Q_d^{[m]}(a)
  =(A'(a))^m q_m(a)
   +\Phi_m\bigl(A;q_0,\ldots,q_{m-1}\bigr)(a),        \tag{\(\dagger\)}
\]
where \(\Phi_m\) is explicit: a term from \(A^\nu q_\nu\) can survive at
\(a\) only if \(\nu\leq m\), and for \(\nu=m\) every one of the \(m\)
copies of \(A\) must receive Hasse order one.  Likewise \(S_d=AQ_d\)
gives
\[
 S_d^{[m+1]}(a)
 =A'(a)Q_d^{[m]}(a)
  +\sum_{i=2}^{m+1}A^{[i]}(a)Q_d^{[m+1-i]}(a).        \tag{\(\ddagger\)}
\]
Thus the coefficient of \(q_m(a)\) in \((\ddagger)\) is
\((A'(a))^{m+1}\ne0\).  Reduction of \((\mathsf{QJ}_2)\) modulo \(A\),
together with \(A(0)=1\), determines \(H\bmod A\) from
\(S_d'\bmod A\).  Hence \((\mathsf{QJ}_1)\), \((\dagger)\), and
\((\ddagger)\) determine \(q_m(a)\), and therefore the unique degree-
\(<M\) polynomial \(q_m\), successively for \(0\leq m\leq2R-2\).

For \(m=1\), \(S_d^{[2]}=S_d'\) because \(2<R\), and reduction modulo
\(A\) gives
\[
 A^{[2]}q_0+A'(q_0'+A'q_1)\equiv A'q_0,
\]
which is \((\mathsf{QJ}_4)\) in characteristic two.

Finally, \((\ast)\) at order \(2R\) gives
\[
 S_d^{[2R]}=(C^{[2]})^R+T(B^{[2]})^R.
\]
Writing \(U=S_{r+1}\), \(V=S_r\), the doubling formulas give
\(C^{[2]}=(U')^2\) and
\(B^{[2]}=(U')^2+X(V')^2\), proving \((\mathsf{QJ}_5)\).
The two parity specializations follow from
\(S_{2t}'=0\) and \(S_{2t+1}'=S_t^2\).  Finally
\[
 T_s(a)^2+T_s(a)=a^{2^s}+a.
\]
If \(T_s(a)\) were zero or one, then \(a^{2^s}=a\).  The exact degree
\([\F_2(a):\F_2]=M\) would force \(M\mid s\), contrary to
\(0<s<M\) in \((\mathsf{QW}_4)\).  Thus both \(T_s(a)\) and
\(1+T_s(a)\) are nonzero; also \(a\ne0\) because \(A(0)=1\).
This proves \((\mathsf{QJ}_6)\).

For \((\mathsf{QJ}_7)\), suppose \(r=2t\) and the displayed value
vanishes.  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic} then gives
\(\delta_n\mid t\).  The hypothetical failure gives \(\delta_n\mid d\),
while \(g=4t+1\), so
\[
 \delta_n\mid d-4Rt=R+1.
\]
Choose a prime \(p\mid\delta_n\).  Since also
\(p\mid N=2^M+1\), and \(M\) is a power of two, one has
\(\ord_p(2)=2M\).  But \(p\mid R+1=2^s+1\) also gives
\(\ord_p(2)\mid2s\), hence \(M\mid s\), contrary to \(0<s<M\) in
\((\mathsf{QW}_4)\).  This proves \((\mathsf{QJ}_7)\).

Lean kernel-checks the second-digit rearrangement, the exact identity
\((\mathsf{QJ}_2)\) and its quotient specialization, and the ring
normalization in \((\mathsf{QJ}_5)\).  The Hasse block bookkeeping and
the specialization to the selected Conway polynomial remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the odd fixed jet is an exact cofactor--Pierpont collision]
\label{prop:fermat-odd-jet-collision}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fixed-adic-jet-blocks}, assume the
hypothetical failure \(A\mid S_d\), and suppose
\[
 r=2t+1.
\]
Put
\[
 \Gamma_{n,\ell}
 :=\gcd(d,3R+1)
 =\gcd\bigl(d,2^{M-s}-3\bigr).
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 S_d^{[2R]}(a)=0
 &\iff S_t(a)=0
 \iff \delta_n\mid t\\
 &\iff \delta_n\mid\Gamma_{n,\ell}.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_8\)}
\]
In particular, \(\Gamma_{n,\ell}=1\) is a sufficient arithmetic
certificate that the odd order-\(2R\) jet is nonzero.

Every prime \(p\mid\Gamma_{n,\ell}\) satisfies
\[
 \boxed{
 \ord_p(2)=2M,\qquad
 2^{M-s}\equiv3\pmod p.}                              \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_9\)}
\]
If \(v=v_2(s)\), then \(v<n\) and
\[
 \boxed{
 \ord_p(3)=2^{n+1-v},\qquad
 p\mid3^{2^{n-v}}+1,\qquad
 p\equiv1\pmod{12}.}                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_{10}\)}
\]
Thus a surviving prime is simultaneously a primitive Fermat divisor for
base two and a primitive generalized-Fermat divisor for base three, with
the additional oriented discrete-log condition in
\((\mathsf{QJ}_9)\).

There is a sharp small-window dichotomy.  Every prime in
\((\mathsf{QJ}_{10})\) is one modulo both \(4M\) and \(12\), hence one
modulo \(12M\).  If
\[
 3R+1<(12M+1)^2,
\]
then
\[
 \boxed{\Gamma_{n,\ell}\in\{1,3R+1\}.}                \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_{11}\)}
\]
In the nontrivial case \(3R+1\) is prime.  Consequently, in this window,
\[
 S_d^{[2R]}(a)=0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \Gamma_{n,\ell}=\delta_n=3R+1.
\]
In particular this applies whenever \(s\leq2n+5\).

Using the cofactor \(d=F_n/\ell\) gives the sharp factor-sensitive screen.
The weaker full-Fermat gcd can retain the selected factor \(\ell\) itself
and therefore fail to certify nonvanishing when \(\Gamma_{n,\ell}=1\).
The proposition does not prove \(\Gamma_{n,\ell}=1\), nor does a nontrivial
\(\Gamma_{n,\ell}\) determine the selected order \(\delta_n\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first equivalence in \((\mathsf{QJ}_8)\) is
\((\mathsf{QJ}_6)\), and the Fibonacci zero-set criterion gives
\[
 S_t(a)=0\iff\delta_n\mid t.
\]
Under hypothetical failure one also has \(\delta_n\mid d\).  Since
\(g=4t+3\),
\[
 d=1+R(4t+3)=(3R+1)+4Rt.
\]
Thus \(\delta_n\mid t\) implies \(\delta_n\mid3R+1\).
Conversely, if \(\delta_n\mid3R+1\), then
\(\delta_n\mid4Rt\); and
\(\gcd(\delta_n,4R)=1\), because \(\delta_n\mid F_n\) is odd.
Hence \(\delta_n\mid t\).  This proves \((\mathsf{QJ}_8)\).

Since \(d\mid N=2^M+1\), the identities
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 2^{M-s}(3R+1)-3N&=2^{M-s}-3,\\
 N-R(2^{M-s}-3)&=3R+1
 \end{aligned}
\]
prove the two gcd presentations of \(\Gamma_{n,\ell}\).

Let \(p\mid\Gamma_{n,\ell}\) be prime.  Then \(p\mid F_n\), so
\(2^M\equiv-1\pmod p\).  Because \(M=2^n\), this forces
\(\ord_p(2)=2M\).  The second gcd presentation gives
\(2^{M-s}\equiv3\pmod p\).  Since \(0<s<M\),
\[
 \gcd(2M,M-s)=2^{v_2(s)}.
\]
Therefore
\[
 \ord_p(3)
 =\frac{2M}{\gcd(2M,M-s)}
 =2^{n+1-v_2(s)},
\]
which proves the primitive base-three assertion.  Also \(p\equiv1\pmod8\),
so two is a quadratic residue modulo \(p\); hence so is three.
Quadratic reciprocity then gives \(p\equiv1\pmod{12}\).

Finally every prime divisor of \(\Gamma_{n,\ell}\) is congruent to one
modulo both \(4M\) and \(12\), hence modulo \(12M\).  Therefore every
divisor of \(\Gamma_{n,\ell}\) is one modulo \(12M\), while \(R\geq4M\)
gives \(3R+1\equiv1\pmod{12M}\).  If
\(1<\Gamma_{n,\ell}<3R+1\), then
\(\Gamma_{n,\ell}\equiv1\pmod{12M}\); from
\(3R+1=\Gamma_{n,\ell}((3R+1)/\Gamma_{n,\ell})\), the complementary
quotient is also congruent to one modulo \(12M\).
They are therefore at least \(12M+1\), contradicting
\(3R+1<(12M+1)^2\).  Hence
\(\Gamma_{n,\ell}\) is either one or \(3R+1\).  If the latter were
composite, the same argument applied to a proper factor and its
complement would give the same contradiction.  This proves
\((\mathsf{QJ}_{11})\).  Finally \(s\leq2n+5\) gives
\(3R+1\leq96M^2+1<(12M+1)^2\).

Lean kernel-checks the quotient-ring collision equivalence, the linear
combination relating the two collision forms, and the cancellation by the
power-of-two unit.  The Fibonacci zero-set, finite-field order calculations,
and small-window specialization remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the cofactor collision has an isolated recovered jet]
\label{prop:fermat-odd-jet-recovery}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-odd-jet-collision}, and put
\(B=3R+1\).  At the opposite end of the factor range one has the
unconditional exclusion
\[
 \boxed{M-s\leq n+3\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
        \Gamma_{n,\ell}=1.}                           \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_{12}\)}
\]
Combined with \((\mathsf{QJ}_{11})\), a proper nontrivial collision
\(1<\Gamma_{n,\ell}<B\) can occur only when
\[
 2n+6\leq s\leq M-n-4.                                \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_{13}\)}
\]

There is also an exact next-jet statement.  Suppose the odd branch
\(r=2t+1\) has \(S_d^{[2R]}(a)=0\), and first let \(t>0\).  Write
\[
 t=2^u h,\qquad h\ \hbox{odd},\qquad W=2^{u+2}R.
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 S_d^{[2R+W]}(a)
 =T_s(a)a^R\bigl(S_h'(a)\bigr)^W
 =T_s(a)a^R
   S_{(h-1)/2}(a)^{2W}\ne0.}                          \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_{14}\)}
\]
The omitted case is exact: if \(t=0\), then \(d=B\) and
\[
 S_d=1+T_s(1+X^R),\qquad \deg S_d=3R/2<2R,             \tag{\(\mathsf{QJ}_{15}\)}
\]
so every Hasse jet of order at least \(2R\) vanishes.  Hence a vanished
odd order-\(2R\) jet cannot remain Hasse-flat unless the selected cofactor
is exactly \(d=3R+1\).

This is not a maximal-order proof: the recovered nonzero jet is compatible
with \(A\mid S_d\) and determines a later fixed-\(A\) quotient digit rather
than contradicting hypothetical failure.  Nor do
\((\mathsf{QJ}_{11})\)--\((\mathsf{QJ}_{13})\) exclude the endpoint
possibility \(\Gamma_{n,\ell}=B\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \((\mathsf{QJ}_{12})\), suppose that
\(p\mid\Gamma_{n,\ell}\) is prime and put \(x=M-s\).  Then
\((\mathsf{QJ}_{9})\)--\((\mathsf{QJ}_{10})\) give
\(p\geq12M+1\) and \(p\mid2^x-3\).  If \(x=1\), this says
\(p\mid-1\), which is impossible.  If \(2\leq x\leq n+3\), then
\[
