 G_{\chi^a\rho,w_n}
 =H^{-a}G_{\chi^a\rho,1}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{SG}_6\)}
\]
Thus arbitrary lower multiplicative characters do not repair the loss:
Gauss--Jacobi quotients of total \(\chi\)-weight zero erase the phase,
whereas nonzero total weight retains exactly the unknown \(H\).  This
statement covers lower characters factoring through the norm, not arbitrary
nonlinear ancestry-dependent sums.  Lean kernel-checks the abstract
nontrivial line sum, multiplicative change of variables, exceptional-line
aggregation, denominator-cleared period values, and label-blind symmetric
sums in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}; the finite-extension trace-line
identification remains paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the complete Conway bit basis has one Kummer phase]
\label{prop:fermat-bit-kummer-phase}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-semiprimitive-label}.  Thus
\(m=2^n\), \(q=2^m\), \(E_{n-1}=\F_q\), and
\(E_n=\F_{q^2}\).  Write
\[
 c=c_n=2^m,\qquad e_i=2^i\quad(0\leq i<2m),
 \qquad C=\chi(c),
\]
where the \(e_i\) are the literal Conway bit basis.  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \chi(e_i)=1\quad(0\leq i<m),\qquad
 \chi(e_{m+i})=C\quad(0\leq i<m),\qquad
 H=C^{-2}.}                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_1\)}
\]
In particular,
\[
 H=1
 \iff C=1
 \iff e_i\in(E_n^\times)^\ell\quad(0\leq i<2m).    \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_2\)}
\]
More generally, for finite subsets \(S,T\subset\{0,\ldots,m-1\}\),
\[
 \chi\!\left(
   \prod_{i\in S}e_i\prod_{j\in T}e_{m+j}
 \right)=C^{|T|}.                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_3\)}
\]
Thus every multiplicative-character identity formed from the complete
\emph{actual} bit ancestry has only one current coordinate: upper-block
weight zero is tautologically invisible, while nonzero weight modulo
\(\ell\) is an invertible power of the original selected phase.  In the
Gauss table of Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-semiprimitive-label}, all
lower-bit twists equal \(q\), and all upper-bit twists equal the one value
\(qC^{-a}\).  Evaluating every bit therefore supplies no phase independent
of \(H\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(i<m\), one has \(e_i\in E_{n-1}^\times\), and
\[
 \frac{q^2-1}{\ell}=(q-1)\frac{q+1}{\ell},
\]
so \(\chi(e_i)=1\).  The disjoint-Fermat-support rule for nim
multiplication is literal on these basis elements:
\[
 e_{m+i}=c e_i\qquad(0\leq i<m).
\]
Multiplicativity proves the second assertion and \((\mathsf{CBK}_3)\).
The Conway quadratic gives \(c^q=c+1\).  Since \(q\equiv-1\pmod\ell\),
\[
 H=\chi\!\left(\frac{c+1}{c}\right)
   =\frac{\chi(c)^q}{\chi(c)}=C^{-2}.
\]
Squaring is an automorphism of the odd-order group \(\mu_\ell\), and
\(\ker\chi=(E_n^\times)^\ell\), proving
\((\mathsf{CBK}_2)\).  The Gauss-sum statement follows by substituting
these values into \((\mathsf{SG}_1)\).

Lean kernel-checks the abstract multiplicative phase, monomial-weight, and
odd-torsion inverse-square cores in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.  The literal
Conway bit multiplication and finite-field Kummer interpretation remain
paper-level.  The proposition rules out multiplicative monomial arguments;
it does not rule out nonlinear additive combinations or a new identity
coupling \(\chi(x+y)\) to the selected ancestry.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the labelled first upper block]
\label{cor:fermat-upper-block-factorization}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-bit-kummer-phase}, put
\[
 a=a_{n-1}=c(c+1),\qquad d=\frac{q+1}{\ell},
\]
and, for \(\xi\in\mu_\ell\), define
\[
 B_\xi(X)=(X+c+1)^d+\xi(X+c)^d\in E_n[X].
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\quad X^q+X=\prod_{\xi\in\mu_\ell}B_\xi(X).\quad}       \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_4\)}
\]
For \(x\in E_{n-1}\), one has
\[
 B_\xi(x)=0\iff\chi(c+x)=\xi.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_5\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \#\{x\in E_{n-1}:\chi(c+x)=\xi\}=
 \begin{cases}
  d-1,&\xi=1,\\
  d,&\xi\ne1,
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_6\)}
\]
and, for every nonzero \(r\in\mathbf Z/\ell\mathbf Z\),
\[
 \sum_{x\in E_{n-1}}\chi(c+x)^r=-1.
\]
The trivial-label factor is already defined over the lower field and is
exactly
\[
 B_1(X)=S_d(X^2+X+a),\qquad B_1(0)=S_d(a).             \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_7\)}
\]
In particular,
\[
 H=1\iff\chi(c)=1\iff B_1(0)=0\iff S_d(a)=0.          \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_8\)}
\]
Thus the product factorization, factor degrees, and every unmarked histogram
or label power moment of the literal ordinal interval
\(c+E_{n-1}=\{q,\ldots,2q-1\}\) are fixed independently of the target.
The individual labelled factor retains one missing incidence: whether its
marked first point \(c\) lies on the trivial-labelled factor.  This is
precisely the original selected Fibonacci zero.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Put \(A=(X+c+1)^d\) and \(B=(X+c)^d\).  The cyclotomic product in
characteristic two gives
\[
 \prod_{\xi\in\mu_\ell}(A+\xi B)=A^\ell+B^\ell.
\]
Since \(c^q=c+1\), expanding the right-hand side gives
\[
 (X+c+1)^{q+1}+(X+c)^{q+1}=X^q+X,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{CBK}_4)\).  For \(x\in E_{n-1}\),
\[
 \chi(c+x)
 =\left(\frac{c+x+1}{c+x}\right)^d,
\]
so \((\mathsf{CBK}_5)\) follows.  Projectivizing identifies
\(x\mapsto(c+x)E_{n-1}^\times\) with all lines in
\(E_n^\times/E_{n-1}^\times\) except the base line.  Every character label
occurs \(d\) times on the full quotient, and the deleted base line has label
one, proving \((\mathsf{CBK}_6)\) and the moment formula.

Finally, the characteristic-two Binet identity applied to the roots
\(X+c\) and \(X+c+1\), whose product is \(X^2+X+a\), gives
\((\mathsf{CBK}_7)\).  Its specialization at zero and
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-bit-kummer-phase} give
\((\mathsf{CBK}_8)\).  Lean kernel-checks the deleted-uniform-fibre count
and the characteristic-two norm collapse in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}; the
cyclotomic product, projective-line identification, and Binet specialization
remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[universal translation and marked-endpoint exhaustion]
\label{prop:fermat-upper-block-universal-translate}
Retain Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-upper-block-factorization} and put
\[
 F_\xi(Y)=(Y+1)^d+\xi Y^d.
\]
Then
\[
 B_\xi(X)=F_\xi(X+c),\qquad
 \prod_{\xi\in\mu_\ell}F_\xi(Y)=Y^q+Y+1.             \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_9\)}
\]
Thus the complete labelled factor family modulo common translation is
universal: the Conway ancestry occurs only in the marked evaluation point
\(Y=c\).  More precisely, if \(C=\chi(c)\), then
\[
 F_\xi(c)=c^d(C+\xi),\qquad
 F_1(c)=c^d(C+1)=S_d(a).                              \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_{10}\)}
\]
Hence the unique factor vanishing at the marked endpoint has label \(C\),
the failure assertion is that this label is one, and the desired
maximal-order assertion is that it is not one.

All simultaneous-translation invariants are selector-blind.  If \(d=1\),
then \(B_1=1\) and the desired nonvanishing is automatic.  Assume \(d>1\).
In the raw factor normalization above one has
\[
 \operatorname{disc}(B_1)=1,                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_{11}\)}
\]
and, for distinct labels,
\[
 \operatorname{Res}(B_1,B_\eta)=(1+\eta)^{d-1}
 \quad(\eta\ne1),
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Res}(B_\xi,B_\eta)=(\xi+\eta)^d
 \quad(\xi,\eta\ne1).                                \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_{12}\)}
\]
The factor symmetries likewise contain no marked information:
\[
 B_\xi(X+1)=\xi B_{\xi^{-1}}(X),
 \qquad \operatorname{Frob}_q^{\mathrm{coeff}}(B_\xi)
   =\xi^{-1}B_\xi.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_{13}\)}
\]

The individual coefficients may retain the translation, but exhaust to the
same Fibonacci word:
\[
 [X^j]B_1=\binom dj S_{d-j}(a)\qquad(0\leq j<d).      \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_{14}\)}
\]
If \(\binom dj\) is odd and \(A_{n-1}\) is the selected minimal
polynomial of \(a\), then
\[
 \Norm_{E_{n-1}/\F_2}([X^j]B_1)
   =\operatorname{Res}_T(A_{n-1}(T),S_{d-j}(T)).      \tag{\(\mathsf{CBK}_{15}\)}
\]
When \(\binom dj\) is odd, equivalently, coefficient vanishing says
\(\ord(w)\mid d-j\).  The norm of the constant coefficient \(j=0\) is
the original selected resultant.  Thus these data supply no
selector-blind second invariant: every single coefficient or Hasse jet is
a shifted selected divisibility problem.  Any gain must add genuinely
ancestry-sensitive input, possibly through a relation involving one or
more shifted values.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first identity is the definition after the common translation.  The
cyclotomic product gives
\[
 \prod_{\xi\in\mu_\ell}\bigl((Y+1)^d+\xi Y^d\bigr)
 =(Y+1)^{q+1}+Y^{q+1}=Y^q+Y+1,
\]
proving \((\mathsf{CBK}_9)\).  Since
\((c+1)/c=w\) and \(w^d=C\), evaluation at \(c\) proves
\((\mathsf{CBK}_{10})\).

Translation does not change discriminants or resultants, so compute with
the \(F_\xi\).  At a root \(y\) of \(F_1\),
\[
 F_1'(y)=\frac{y^{d-1}}{y+1},\qquad
 \prod y=F_1(0)=1,\qquad \prod(y+1)=F_1(1)=1.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{CBK}_{11})\).  At a root of \(F_1\), the first
resultant in \((\mathsf{CBK}_{12})\) follows directly from
\(F_\eta(y)=(1+\eta)y^d\) and \(\prod y=1\).  For \(\xi\ne1\), the raw
leading coefficient is \(1+\xi\) and
\(\prod y=(1+\xi)^{-1}\); the same root evaluation gives the second
resultant exactly.  Direct substitution and \(c^q=c+1\) give
\((\mathsf{CBK}_{13})\), where the Frobenius acts coefficientwise.

Finally the binomial theorem gives
\[
 [X^j]B_1
 =\binom dj\bigl((c+1)^{d-j}+c^{d-j}\bigr)
 =\binom dj S_{d-j}(a),
\]
the last equality being the same characteristic-two Binet identity as in
\((\mathsf{CBK}_7)\).  Taking the norm over the selected Frobenius orbit
is the monic resultant in \((\mathsf{CBK}_{15})\).  Lean kernel-checks the
universal trace-one collapse and the marked phase-defect identity; the
cyclotomic product, resultant products, and selected finite-field
specialization remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[weighted reciprocity and a circular cyclotomic-difference selector]
\label{prop:fermat-weighted-selector}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-canonical-global-lift}, put
\(\Delta=(\mathbf Z/\ell\mathbf Z)^\times\), and label the primes above two
by \(\mathfrak q_a=\tau_a\mathfrak q_1\).  For \(x\in M^\times\) define its
weighted two-adic first moment
\[
 \mathcal E_2(x)=
 \sum_{a\in\Delta}a\,v_{\mathfrak q_a}(x)
 \pmod\ell.                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_1\)}
\]
Then
\[
 \prod_{\mathfrak q\mid2}(W_n,x)_{\mathfrak q,\ell}
   =H^{\mathcal E_2(x)}.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_2\)}
\]
Consequently, if the principal divisor \((x)\) is supported only above
\(2\ell\),
global reciprocity gives the exact single-orbit identity
\[
 H^{\mathcal E_2(x)}
   =\prod_{\lambda\mid\ell}(W_n,x)_{\lambda,\ell}^{-1}. \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_3\)}
\]
A proof of \(H\ne1\) could therefore come from a mixed element with
\(\mathcal E_2(x)\ne0\), together with an independently evaluated
right-hand side.

Neither unmixed factor can provide such an element.  One has
\[
 \mathcal E_2(x)=0
 \quad\text{for every }x\in\mathcal K_n^\times
 \text{ and every }x\in\mathbf Q(\zeta_\ell)^\times.  \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_4\)}
\]
In particular this excludes classical Gauss, Jacobi, and Stickelberger
principalizers lying in the cyclotomic factor.  Indeed, if
\(D=\langle2\rangle\subset\Delta\) is the decomposition group at two, then
\(-1\in D\), because \(2^{2^n}\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).  Hence a divisor from
the cyclotomic factor has
\[
 v_{\mathfrak q_a}(x)=v_{\mathfrak q_{-a}}(x),
\]
and the \(a,-a\) contributions to \((\mathsf{FWS}_1)\) cancel.  A usable
selector must break this inverse-pair symmetry with genuinely
\(\mathcal K_n\)-specific data.

The lifted recursion also contributes no hidden compensator.  It gives
\[
 W_n=\mathcal A_{n-1}C_n^{-2}
    =\left(\prod_{j<n}C_j\right)C_n^{-2}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_{4a}\)}
\]
For \(j<n\), the two-adic residue of \(C_j\) has order prime to \(\ell\),
so \(C_j\) is a local \(\ell\)-th power above two.  Applying reciprocity
to each lower \(C_j\) therefore gives, for every \(x\) as above,
\[
 \prod_{\lambda\mid\ell}(C_j,x)_{\lambda,\ell}=1,
\qquad
 \prod_{\lambda\mid\ell}(W_n,x)_{\lambda,\ell}
 =\left(\prod_{\lambda\mid\ell}
        (C_n,x)_{\lambda,\ell}\right)^{-2}.           \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_{4b}\)}
\]
Thus complete lower ancestry cancels from the \(\ell\)-adic compensator:
the right side of \((\mathsf{FWS}_3)\) is again the current selected
\(C_n\)-character, up to the invertible scalar \(-2\).

The most natural mixed principal element is already exactly circular.  Put
\[
 h=F_n/\ell,\qquad T=W_n^h,\qquad x=T-\zeta_\ell.
\]
Then
\[
 \Norm_{M/\mathcal K_n}(x)=\Phi_\ell(T),               \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_5\)}
\]
and, after reduction at the prime above two,
\[
 \Phi_\ell(w_n^h)=0
 \iff w_n^h\ne1
 \iff H\ne1.                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{FWS}_6\)}
\]
Thus \((x)\) has any divisor support above two exactly when the desired
maximal-order condition is already true; in the failure case
\(w_n^h=1\) and \(\Phi_\ell(1)=\ell\equiv1\pmod2\).  The pure id\`ele with
component two at \(\mathfrak q_1\) and one at the other primes does have
first moment one and evaluates to \(H\), but it is not principal.  Making it
into a principal selector whose divisor is supported above \(2\ell\) would
require an \(\ell\)-adic ray-class compensator.  Its existence and the
evaluation of the right side of \((\mathsf{FWS}_3)\) are precisely the
remaining selected relative ray-class problem.  The lifted Conway norm
recursion neither forces that coefficient to vanish nor proves it nonzero.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
At \(\mathfrak q_a\), tame bilinearity and
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-canonical-global-lift} give
\[
 (W_n,x)_{\mathfrak q_a,\ell}
 =H^{a v_{\mathfrak q_a}(x)}.
\]
Multiplication proves \((\mathsf{FWS}_2)\), and the Hilbert product formula
proves \((\mathsf{FWS}_3)\).  Valuations of an element of \(\mathcal K_n\)
are constant on the full orbit.  For an element of
\(\mathbf Q(\zeta_\ell)\), they are constant on the decomposition cosets
of \(D\); pairing \(a\) with \(-a\) proves \((\mathsf{FWS}_4)\).
The first identity in \((\mathsf{FWS}_{4a})\) is
\(W_n=\mathcal A_{n-1}C_n^{-2}\), and the second follows by expanding
\(\mathcal A_{n-1}\).  At two, every lower residue has order dividing
\(\prod_{r<n}F_r\), which is coprime to \(\ell\); the principal-unit
part is also uniquely \(\ell\)-divisible.  Its local symbols are therefore
trivial.  At places away from \(2\ell\), both entries are units and the tame
symbol is trivial.  The product formula applied to each \(C_j\), followed
by multiplicativity, proves \((\mathsf{FWS}_{4b})\).

Equation \((\mathsf{FWS}_5)\) is the defining product for
\(\Phi_\ell\).  The reduction \(t=w_n^h\) satisfies \(t^\ell=1\).  In
characteristic two and for odd \(\ell\), the geometric sum
\(\Phi_\ell(t)=1+t+\cdots+t^{\ell-1}\) vanishes exactly when \(t\ne1\),
which proves \((\mathsf{FWS}_6)\).  Lean independently checks the
inverse-pair/coset first-moment cancellation and this odd geometric-sum
dichotomy.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[an affine mixed selector and its oriented tame residue]
\label{prop:fermat-affine-selector}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-weighted-selector}, assume \(n\geq2\), put
\[
 q=2^{2^n},\qquad
 M_0=\mathcal K_{n-1}(\zeta_\ell),\qquad
 \mathfrak r=\mathfrak q_1\cap M_0,\qquad
 \eta=\zeta_\ell\bmod\mathfrak r\in\F_{q^2}.
\]
There are algebraic integers \(U,V\in\mathcal O_{\mathcal K_{n-1}}\)
such that, for
\[
 R=U+V\zeta_\ell\in M_0,\qquad x=C_n-R\in M,
\]
one has
\[
 v_{\mathfrak q_1}(x)=1,\qquad
 v_{\mathfrak q_a}(x)=0\quad(a\ne1),\qquad
 (W_n,x)_{\lambda,\ell}=1\quad(\lambda\mid\ell).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_1\)}
\]
In particular \(\mathcal E_2(x)=1\).  Define the finite oriented tame
residue
\[
 \mathcal T_R=
 \prod_{\mathfrak v\nmid2\ell}
 \left(\overline{W_n}^{\,v_{\mathfrak v}(x)}\right)^{
             (|k(\mathfrak v)|-1)/\ell}\in\mu_\ell.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_2\)}
\]
Here inverse reduction identifies the copy of \(\mu_\ell\) in each residue
field with the fixed global \(\mu_\ell\subset M\).
Then global reciprocity evaluates the selected symbol exactly as
\[
 \boxed{\qquad H=\mathcal T_R^{-1}.\qquad}           \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_3\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \Norm_{M/M_0}(x)
 =\mathcal A_{n-1}+R+R^2.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_4\)}
\]
At conjugate components of a divisor of the right side, the two values of
\(W_n\) are reciprocal.  Consequently the ordinary norm or resultant
forgets \(\mathcal T_R\): equation \(\mathsf{AFS}_3\) is an oriented
half-resultant rather than an unweighted norm formula.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(\ell\mid q+1\) and \(\ell\nmid q-1\), the reduction
\(\eta\) does not lie in \(\F_q\).  Hence
\(\{1,\eta\}\) is an \(\F_q\)-basis of \(\F_{q^2}\), and there
are unique \(u,v\in\F_q\), with \(v\ne0\), such that
\[
 c_n=u+v\eta.
\]
For every sufficiently large integer \(N\), simultaneous Chinese
remaindering in \(\mathcal O_{\mathcal K_{n-1}}\) chooses initial lifts
\(U\equiv u\) and \(V\equiv v\) at the prime above two while putting
both in \(\mathfrak l^N\) at every prime
\(\mathfrak l\mid\ell\).  With the convention
\(\mathfrak q_a=\tau_a\mathfrak q_1\), comparison through
\(\tau_a^{-1}\) gives
\[
 \zeta_\ell\bmod\mathfrak q_a=\eta^{a^{-1}},\qquad
 x\bmod\mathfrak q_a
 =v(\eta-\eta^{a^{-1}}).
\]
Because \(\eta\) has order \(\ell\), this vanishes exactly for \(a=1\).
The extension at two is unramified, so two is a uniformizer.  Replacing
\(U\) by \(U+2T\) changes \(x/2\bmod\mathfrak q_1\) by
\(-\overline T\in\F_q\).  Choose the residue of \(T\) to avoid the
single forbidden value, and simultaneously impose
\(T\in\mathfrak l^N\) at every \(\mathfrak l\mid\ell\).
This proves the first two assertions of \(\mathsf{AFS}_1\) without
changing any reduction modulo two.

For \(\lambda\mid\ell\), put \(y=C_n-U\in\mathcal K_n^\times\).
The chosen \(U\) is \(\ell\)-adically deep and \(C_n\) is a global unit, so
\(y\) is a local unit.  Taking \(N\) beyond the finitely many local
\(\ell\)-th-power thresholds makes
\[
 \frac{x}{y}=1-\frac{V\zeta_\ell}{y}
 \in M_\lambda^{\times\ell}
\]
at every such \(\lambda\).  The pointwise identity
\(\mathsf{GL}_{7a}\) gives
\((W_n,y)_{\lambda,\ell}=1\), and bilinearity proves the last assertion
of \(\mathsf{AFS}_1\).  Notice that this kills the pairing; it does not
assert that \(x\) itself is an \(\ell\)-th power.

At \(\mathfrak q_1\), the tame formula and
\(v_{\mathfrak q_1}(x)=1\) give
\((W_n,x)_{\mathfrak q_1,\ell}=H\).  Every other prime above two
contributes one, every prime above \(\ell\) contributes one by
\(\mathsf{AFS}_1\), and the remaining finite factors are exactly
\(\mathsf{AFS}_2\).  The Hilbert product formula proves
\(\mathsf{AFS}_3\).

The top conjugation fixes \(R\) and sends \(C_n\) to \(-1-C_n\).  Hence
\[
 (C_n-R)(-1-C_n-R)=\mathcal A_{n-1}+R+R^2,
\]
which proves \(\mathsf{AFS}_4\).  If a split oriented component has
\(C_n\equiv r\), then
\(\mathcal A_{n-1}+r+r^2=0\) and
\[
 W_n\equiv\frac{\mathcal A_{n-1}}{r^2}
 =-\frac{r+1}{r}.
\]
The conjugate component has \(C_n\equiv-1-r\) and sees the reciprocal
value.  At a ramified component \(r=-1/2\), this value is one.  Thus the
full norm combines and cancels the two orientations, whereas the divisor
of \(x\) retains only one.  Lean independently checks the affine
interpolation, quadratic norm, and reciprocal-orientation identities; the
simultaneous approximation, valuation statements, Hilbert symbols, and
global reciprocity remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the affine selector norm is genuinely mixed]
\label{cor:fermat-affine-norm-mixed}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-affine-selector}, let
\(\mathfrak p\) be the unique prime of \(\mathcal K_{n-1}\) above two,
and put
\[
 g=\Norm_{M/M_0}(x)=\mathcal A_{n-1}+R+R^2.
\]
The primes of \(M_0\) above \(\mathfrak p\) are naturally indexed by
\(\Delta/\{\mathord\pm1\}\); write
\(\mathfrak r_{\bar a}=\mathfrak q_a\cap M_0\).  Their normalized
valuations satisfy
\[
 v_{\mathfrak r_{\bar a}}(g)=
 \begin{cases}
  1,&\bar a=\bar1,\\
  0,&\bar a\ne\bar1.
 \end{cases}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_{4c}\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \operatorname{Stab}_{\Delta}(g)\subseteq\{\mathord\pm1\},
 \qquad
 [\mathcal K_{n-1}(g):\mathcal K_{n-1}]
 \geq\frac{\ell-1}{2}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_{4d}\)}
\]
In particular \(g\notin\mathcal K_{n-1}\).  Thus the relative norm of an
affine principalizer isolating the named prime cannot lie in the lower
Conway field.  The norm element, and its prime-above-two valuation divisor,
has \(\Delta\)-orbit size at least \((\ell-1)/2\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The residue size at \(\mathfrak p\) is \(q\), and
\(q\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).  Hence the decomposition group of
\(\mathfrak p\) in
\(M_0/\mathcal K_{n-1}\) is \(\{\mathord\pm1\}\): the extension is
unramified there, has residue degree two, and has
\((\ell-1)/2>1\) primes above \(\mathfrak p\).
The top involution sends
\(\mathfrak q_a\) to \(\mathfrak q_{-a}\), so
\(\mathfrak r_{\bar a}\) splits in \(M/M_0\) as the pair
\(\mathfrak q_a,\mathfrak q_{-a}\), each with residue degree one.
The element \(x=C_n-R\) is integral.  Equation
\(\mathsf{AFS}_1\) therefore gives
\[
 v_{\mathfrak r_{\bar a}}(g)
 =v_{\mathfrak q_a}(x)+v_{\mathfrak q_{-a}}(x),
\]
which is exactly \(\mathsf{AFS}_{4c}\).

If \(\tau_b(g)=g\), its unique prime-above-two support must be fixed, so
\(\mathfrak r_{\bar b}=\mathfrak r_{\bar1}\), equivalently
\(b=\mathord\pm1\).  More precisely,
\[
 \operatorname{Stab}_{\Delta}(g)
 =\operatorname{Gal}\bigl(M_0/\mathcal K_{n-1}(g)\bigr),
\]
so the Galois orbit--stabilizer equality proves
\(\mathsf{AFS}_{4d}\).  Equivalently, every element descended from
\(\mathcal K_{n-1}\) has the same valuation at all primes above
\(\mathfrak p\), whereas \(g\) has the nonconstant vector
\((1,0,\ldots,0)\).

The companion Lean file checks the two abstract group-action cores: an
invariant function on a transitive orbit is constant, and a symmetry
preserving a uniquely supported value fixes that support point.  The
cyclotomic decomposition, norm-valuation formula and Galois covariance, and
the stabilizer-index/Galois-degree passage remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[affine normalization loses no oriented ray data]
\label{cor:fermat-affine-normalization}
Let \(s\) be the top involution of \(M/M_0\) and put
\(Y=2C_n+1\), so \(s(Y)=-Y\).  For any
\(x_0\in M^\times\) with \(x_0\ne s(x_0)\), define
\[
 z=\frac{Y}{x_0-s(x_0)},\qquad x=zx_0.
\]
Then \(z\in M_0^\times\), \(x-s(x)=Y\), and
\[
 x=C_n-R\qquad\text{for some }R\in M_0.             \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_{4a}\)}
\]
The fractional divisor added by \(z\) is \(s\)-invariant.  Its two
oriented tame values are reciprocal, so it contributes trivially to the
anti-invariant \(W_n\)-character.  Hence every nonsymmetric oriented
principal divisor has an affine representative carrying the same oriented
Artin value.

This is a fractional-divisor normalization, not an integral principalizer
theorem: \(z\) can have poles or nonunit components at two or \(\ell\).
It need not preserve the simple-zero conditions, the vanishing
\(\ell\)-adic pairings, or \(S\)-unit support in
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-affine-selector}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The denominator \(x_0-s(x_0)\) is anti-invariant, as is \(Y\); therefore
\(s(z)=z\).  It follows that
\[
 x-s(x)=z\bigl(x_0-s(x_0)\bigr)=Y.
\]
Since \(C_n-s(C_n)=2C_n+1=Y\), the element
\(R=C_n-x\) is fixed by \(s\), proving \(\mathsf{AFS}_{4a}\).
The divisor of an element of \(M_0^\times\) has equal multiplicities on
each split pair.  Top conjugation sends \(W_n\) to \(W_n^{-1}\), so the
two residue values cancel.  Lean checks the fixed-scalar and anti-trace
identities; the divisor-character interpretation is paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the local oriented residue cover is rational]
\label{cor:fermat-oriented-residue-rational}
At a split tame support prime, write \(r=R\bmod\mathfrak p\) for the
orientation selected by \(x=C_n-R\).  Then
\[
 W_n=-\frac{r+1}{r},\qquad
 z^\ell=W_n
 \iff
 r=-\frac{1}{1+z^\ell}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_{4b}\)}
\]
Thus the local Kummer cover of the oriented residue is rational.  In
particular no positive-genus Weil bound applied only to this residue equation
can force its value to be a nonpower; the remaining datum is which global
oriented divisor the selected principal element carries.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Substitution gives
\[
 -\frac{(-1/(1+z^\ell))+1}{-1/(1+z^\ell)}=z^\ell.
\]
Conversely solving \(z^\ell=-(r+1)/r\) for \(r\) gives the displayed
formula whenever the denominators are nonzero.  Lean checks this rational
parametrization over an arbitrary field.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Jacobi collapse of the affine half-resultant]
\label{prop:fermat-affine-jacobi-collapse}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-affine-selector}, put
\[
 A=\mathcal A_{n-1},\qquad g=A+R+R^2,
 \qquad \mathfrak a_R=(g)\mathfrak r^{-1},
\]
and take the approximation depth there sufficiently large that
\(g/A\) is an \(\ell\)-th power at every prime above \(\ell\).
For \(z\in M_0^\times\) and an integral ideal \(\mathfrak b\) prime to
\(\ell\) such that \(z\) is a unit at every
\(\mathfrak p\mid\mathfrak b\), write
\[
 \left(\frac{z}{\mathfrak b}\right)_\ell
 =\prod_{\mathfrak p\mid\mathfrak b}
   \overline z^{\,v_{\mathfrak p}(\mathfrak b)
      (|k(\mathfrak p)|-1)/\ell}\in\mu_\ell.
\]
Then \(\mathfrak a_R\) is integral and prime to \(2\ell\), and
\begin{align}
 \mathcal T_R
 &=\left(\frac{AR^{-2}}{\mathfrak a_R}\right)_\ell
  =\left(\frac{R}{\mathfrak a_R}\right)_\ell^{-2}
  =\left(\frac{R}{\mathfrak r}\right)_\ell^2,       \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_1\)}\\
 H&=\left(\frac{R}{\mathfrak r}\right)_\ell^{-2}.  \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_2\)}
\end{align}
More generally,
\[
 \left(\frac{B}{\mathfrak a_R}\right)_\ell=1       \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_3\)}
\]
for every global unit \(B\in\mathcal O_{\mathcal K_{n-1}}^\times\), so
every lower \(C_i\) and \(\mathcal A_i\) cancels separately.  Since
\(R\bmod\mathfrak r=c_n\),
\[
 \mathcal T_R=c_n^{\,2(q^2-1)/\ell},\qquad
 H=c_n^{-2(q^2-1)/\ell}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_4\)}
\]
Consequently the half-resultant is nontrivial exactly when the original
selected Conway--Fermat symbol is nontrivial.  The affine selector is a
noncircular principal representative, but its Jacobi evaluation is an exact
normal form for the original obstruction, not a proof of nonvanishing.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-affine-selector} gives
\(v_{\mathfrak r}(g)=1\), makes \(g\) a unit at every other prime above
two, and makes it a unit at every prime above \(\ell\).  Hence
\(\mathfrak a_R\) is integral and prime to \(2\ell\).  At a prime in its
support, the selected top orientation has \(C_n\equiv R\), and therefore
\(W_n\equiv AR^{-2}\); at a ramified collision the same residue is one.
The norm multiplicities agree, proving the first equality of
\(\mathsf{AJ}_1\).

For any lower global unit \(B\), reciprocity for \((B,g)_{\ell}\) has no
contribution away from the divisor of \(g\) and the primes above \(\ell\).
At the latter primes, \(g/A\) is an \(\ell\)-th power, so
\((B,g)=(B,A)\).  The product of these remaining symbols is one by applying
reciprocity to the two global units \(B,A\).  Thus
\((B/(g))_\ell=1\).  At \(\mathfrak r\), the residue of \(B\) lies in
\(\F_q^\times\), while \(\ell\mid q+1\) and \(\ell\nmid q-1\); hence
\((B/\mathfrak r)_\ell=1\).  Since
\((g)=\mathfrak r\mathfrak a_R\), this proves \(\mathsf{AJ}_3\), and
the case \(B=A\) gives
\[
 \mathcal T_R=\left(\frac{R}{\mathfrak a_R}\right)_\ell^{-2}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_5\)}
\]

It remains to locate the \(R\)-symbol.  Let \(\mathfrak b\) be the
prime-to-\(\ell\) part of the principal divisor \((R)\), and put
\[
 K_\ell=\prod_{\lambda\mid\ell}(A,R)_{\lambda,\ell}.
\]
Reciprocity for \((A,R)\), with \(A\) a global unit, gives
\[
 1=\left(\frac{A}{\mathfrak b}\right)_\ell K_\ell. \tag{\(\mathsf{AJ}_6\)}
\]
The ideals \((R)\) and \((g)\) are coprime, since a common prime would
divide the global unit \(A=g-R-R^2\).  In reciprocity for \((R,g)\), the
primes of \(g\) contribute \((R/(g))_\ell\); the primes of
\(\mathfrak b\) contribute \((A/\mathfrak b)_\ell^{-1}\), because
\(g\equiv A\pmod R\); and the primes above \(\ell\) contribute
\(K_\ell^{-1}\), because \(g/A\) is an \(\ell\)-th power and the Hilbert
symbol is alternating.  Equation \(\mathsf{AJ}_6\) therefore yields
\[
 \left(\frac{R}{(g)}\right)_\ell=1.
\]
Using \((g)=\mathfrak r\mathfrak a_R\) gives
\((R/\mathfrak a_R)_\ell=(R/\mathfrak r)_\ell^{-1}\), which with
\(\mathsf{AJ}_5\) proves \(\mathsf{AJ}_1\).  Equation
\(\mathsf{AFS}_3\) proves \(\mathsf{AJ}_2\).  Finally
\(R\bmod\mathfrak r=c_n\) and \(|k(\mathfrak r)|=q^2\), proving
\(\mathsf{AJ}_4\).  The multiplicative reduction from
\(\mathsf{AJ}_3\) and the product-one relation to
\(\mathsf{AJ}_1\)--\(\mathsf{AJ}_2\) is independently checked in Lean;
the Jacobi and Hilbert reciprocity argument is paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[even the unrestricted norm-two equation has no solution]
\label{cor:fermat-no-norm-two-affine-selector}
Retain the fields of Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-affine-selector}.  Even
without imposing the selector congruences on \(R\), no \(R\in M_0\) satisfies
\[
 \mathcal A_{n-1}+R+R^2=2.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_5\)}
\]
For the selected \(R\), lower-field values are already excluded by
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-affine-norm-mixed}; the present statement is
stronger in a different direction because it rules out the equation for
every \(R\in M_0\).  It does not exclude a general
\(\{2,\ell\}\)-unit value on the left of \(\mathsf{AFS}_5\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Put \(D=1-4\mathcal A_{n-1}\).  A solution of
\(\mathsf{AFS}_5\) would give
\[
 (2R+1)^2=D+8.                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{AFS}_6\)}
\]
At the unique two-adic completion of \(\mathcal K_{n-1}\), the unit \(D\)
defines the unramified quadratic extension
\(\mathcal K_n/\mathcal K_{n-1}\), and
\((D+8)/D\in1+8\mathcal O\) is a square.  Thus \(D+8\) has the same
nontrivial unramified local square class as \(D\).  Consequently
\[
 L=\mathcal K_{n-1}(\sqrt{D+8})
\]
is a quadratic subfield of \(M_0\) whose base-two localization is the
unramified quadratic field.

On the other hand, \(\mathsf{GL}_4\) makes
\(M_0/\mathcal K_{n-1}\) cyclic of degree \(\ell-1\).  Its unique
quadratic subfield is
\(\mathcal K_{n-1}(\sqrt{\ell})\), because
\(\ell\equiv1\pmod4\).  In fact every
\(\ell\mid F_n\), \(n\geq2\), satisfies
\(\ell\equiv1\pmod8\), so \(\ell\) is a square in
\(\mathbf Q_2\); hence
\(\mathcal K_{n-1}(\sqrt\ell)/\mathcal K_{n-1}\) splits at the chosen
prime above two.  It therefore cannot equal \(L\), contradicting uniqueness
of the quadratic subfield of \(M_0/\mathcal K_{n-1}\).  Lean checks the
algebraic equivalence between \(\mathsf{AFS}_5\) and
\(\mathsf{AFS}_6\); the local and global square-class argument is
paper-level.
\end{proof}
Classical power reciprocity does evaluate the oriented tame half-resultant:
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-affine-jacobi-collapse} collapses it to the
single base-field symbol \((R/\mathfrak r)_\ell^2\).  No cited theorem proves
that symbol nontrivial; because \(R\bmod\mathfrak r=c_n\), doing so is exactly
the original selected Conway--Fermat problem.  Iwasawa's
formula~\cite{Iwasawa68} computes a local symbol
from a critical unit coefficient but is an equality, not a nonvanishing
theorem.  Rohrlich's Jacobi-sum characters~\cite{Rohrlich86} and Helou's
cyclotomic-unit formulas~\cite{Helou95} remain in the pure cyclotomic factor
annihilated by \((\mathsf{FWS}_4)\); the latter reduces orthogonality to
additional Fermat-quotient congruences rather than excluding it universally.
The integral Brumer--Stark theorem of
Dasgupta--Kakde--Silliman--Wang~\cite{DKSW23} assumes a totally real base and
an abelian CM extension; the explicit class-field construction of
Dasgupta--Kakde~\cite{DK24} has the same structural scope.  Here
\(M/\mathcal K_n\) has the required split
prime but \(\mathcal K_n\supset\mathbf Q(\zeta_3)\) is not totally real,
whereas the cyclotomic presentation over \(\mathbf Q\) is CM but two has
residue degree \(2^{n+1}\), not one.  Thus none of these theorems proves the
remaining named symbol nontrivial.

\begin{proposition}[the first selected \(\ell\)-ray coefficient is an explicit
Witt polynomial]
\label{prop:fermat-ell-ray-coefficient}
Retain the lifted chain of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-canonical-global-lift}, let
\(\mathfrak l\) be a prime of \(\mathcal K_n\) above \(\ell\), and put
\(F=\mathcal K_{n,\mathfrak l}\).
Assume that the displayed Conway order is \'etale along this branch, equivalently
\[
 2\bar C_i+1\ne0\quad(0\leq i\leq n)                \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_0\)}
\]
in the successive residue fields.  This holds, in particular, if
\(\ell\) divides none of the absolute norms of
\(D_i=1-4\mathcal A_i\) for \(i<n\).  Under this hypothesis
\(F/\mathbf Q_\ell\) is unramified.  Put
\(k=\mathcal O_F/\mathfrak l\mathcal O_F\),
\(|k|=\ell^f\), and write \([x]\in\mathcal O_F\) for the Teichmuller lift
of \(x\in k\).  Define the second-Witt addition defect
\[
 \mathfrak d_\ell(x)
 =\overline{\frac{[x]+1-[x+1]}{\ell}}\in k.
\]
It is explicit:
\[
 \mathfrak d_\ell(x)^\ell
 =\sum_{r=1}^{\ell-1}\frac{(-1)^r}{r}x^r.           \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_1\)}
\]

Write, modulo \(\ell^2\),
\[
 C_i=[\bar C_i](1+\ell b_i),\qquad
 \mathcal A_i=[\bar{\mathcal A}_i](1+\ell s_i),
 \qquad s_{-1}=0.
\]
Then \(s_i=s_{i-1}+b_i\), and the lifted Conway equation gives
\[
 (2\bar C_i+1)b_i
  =(\bar C_i+1)s_{i-1}-\mathfrak d_\ell(\bar C_i).   \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_2\)}
\]
For the standard Fermat quotient
\[
 q_{\mathfrak l}(W_n)
 =\overline{\frac{W_n^{\ell^f-1}-1}{\ell}},
\]
one therefore has
\[
 q_{\mathfrak l}(W_n)
 =2b_n-s_{n-1}
 =\frac{s_{n-1}-2\mathfrak d_\ell(\bar C_n)}
        {2\bar C_n+1}.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_3\)}
\]
Equivalently, set \(P_{-1}=0\), \(Q_{-1}=1\), and recursively
\[
 \begin{split}
 P_i&=(3\bar C_i+2)P_{i-1}
       -\mathfrak d_\ell(\bar C_i)Q_{i-1},\\
 Q_i&=(2\bar C_i+1)Q_{i-1}.
 \end{split}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_4\)}
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 q_{\mathfrak l}(W_n)\ne0
 \iff
 P_{n-1}-2\mathfrak d_\ell(\bar C_n)Q_{n-1}\ne0.
 \quad}                                              \tag{\(\mathsf{ER}_5\)}
\]
Thus the first ramified local Kummer coefficient is an explicit
full-ancestry truncated-log polynomial.  Its nonvanishing makes the local
character of \(W_n\) nontrivial at the unique prime \(\lambda\) of
\(F(\zeta_\ell)\) above \(\mathfrak l\), but it does
\emph{not} imply \(H\ne1\): it neither evaluates that character on the
distinguished prime above two nor supplies a useful local second argument.
Indeed \((W_n,2)_{\lambda,\ell}=1\) pointwise by
\((\mathsf{GL}_{7a})\).  If \((\mathsf{ER}_0)\) fails, the ramified unit filtration
must replace this first-Witt expansion; the denominator-cleared identities
remain valid, but \((\mathsf{ER}_5)\) is not available by cancellation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The binomial expansion modulo \(\ell^2\) first gives
\[
 \mathfrak d_\ell(x^\ell)
 =\sum_{r=1}^{\ell-1}\frac{(-1)^r}{r}x^r.
\]
Teichmuller Frobenius gives
\(\mathfrak d_\ell(x^\ell)=\mathfrak d_\ell(x)^\ell\), proving
\((\mathsf{ER}_1)\).  Teichmuller multiplicativity gives
\(s_i=s_{i-1}+b_i\).  Expanding
\(C_i^2+C_i+\mathcal A_{i-1}=0\) to first order and dividing by the
nonzero residue \(\bar C_i\) gives \((\mathsf{ER}_2)\).  Since
\(W_n=\mathcal A_{n-1}C_n^{-2}\), the chosen Fermat-quotient convention
gives \(q_{\mathfrak l}(W_n)=2b_n-s_{n-1}\); eliminating \(b_n\)
proves \((\mathsf{ER}_3)\).  Induction in \((\mathsf{ER}_2)\) gives
\(s_i=P_i/Q_i\) and hence \((\mathsf{ER}_4)\)--\((\mathsf{ER}_5)\).
In \(F(\zeta_\ell)\), one has
\(\ell=u(\zeta_\ell-1)^{\ell-1}\) for a unit \(u\).  Hence a nonzero
Fermat quotient gives a nonzero class in
\(U_{\ell-1}/U_\ell\), so \(W_n\) is not a local \(\ell\)-th power.
The relative discriminant identity
\[
 \Norm_{\mathcal K_i/\mathcal K_{i-1}}(D_i)
 =1+4\mathcal A_{i-1}+16\mathcal A_{i-1}^3
\]
makes the excluded derivative primes explicit.  Lean kernel-checks this
identity, the one-step cleared parent update, the cleared terminal identity,
and their generic nonvanishing equivalence.  It does not formalize the
recursive \(P_i,Q_i\) induction, Teichmuller expansion, or Fermat-quotient
interpretation, which remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the last simple ramified branch has a nonzero leading class]
\label{cor:fermat-simple-ramified-ray}
Suppose that \((\mathsf{ER}_0)\) fails, and let \(r\leq n\) be the last
non-\'etale index on the chosen \(\ell\)-adic branch.  Assume that its
discriminant zero is simple:
\[
 \mathfrak l_i=\mathfrak l\cap\mathcal K_i,\qquad
 F_i=\mathcal K_{i,\mathfrak l_i},\qquad
 v_{r-1}(D_{r-1})=1,
 \qquad D_{r-1}=1-4\mathcal A_{r-1}.                \tag{\(\mathsf{RR}_0\)}
\]
Here \(v_i\) denotes the normalized valuation on \(F_i\), and every
Teichmuller bracket below is taken in \(F_n\), equivalently in its maximal
unramified subfield.
Put \(Y_r=2C_r+1\).  Then \(Y_r\) is a uniformizer of the ramified
quadratic completion and
\[
 \mathcal A_r\equiv[-1/8](1-Y_r)\pmod {Y_r^2}.     \tag{\(\mathsf{RR}_1\)}
\]
In particular its leading parent coefficient is \(s_r=-1\).

Assume for \(r<j\leq n\) that \(2\bar C_j+1\ne0\), and for
\(r<j<n\) that the branch-sensitive factors \(3\bar C_j+2\ne0\).
If \(r<n\), the later first-order coefficients obey
\[
 (2\bar C_j+1)s_j=(3\bar C_j+2)s_{j-1},            \tag{\(\mathsf{RR}_2\)}
\]
and the terminal norm-one unit has
\[
 \begin{split}
 s_{n-1}
   &=-\prod_{j=r+1}^{n-1}
       \frac{3\bar C_j+2}{2\bar C_j+1}\ne0,\\
 \operatorname{coeff}_{Y_r}(W_n)
   &=-\frac{s_{n-1}}{2\bar C_n+1}\ne0.
 \end{split}                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{RR}_3\)}
\]
If \(r=n\), the Cayley identity
\[
 W_n=\frac{1+Y_n}{1-Y_n}
\]
instead gives leading coefficient two directly.  In either case \(W_n\)
is not an \(\ell\)-th power in the corresponding completion after adjoining
\(\zeta_\ell\).  Thus the local Kummer character is genuinely nontrivial
through every last-simple-ramification branch satisfying the displayed
noncritical conditions.

This still does not prove \(H\ne1\).  A nonzero weight-one equivariant
character may be nonzero on this ramified inertia line and vanish on the
entire distinguished prime-above-two orbit.  The companion Lean file checks
the displayed equivariance, inertia nonvanishing, and selected-orbit
vanishing in an explicit two-coordinate toy model.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The equation \(Y_r^2=D_{r-1}\) and \((\mathsf{RR}_0)\) show that the
quadratic completion is tamely ramified and that \(Y_r\) is a uniformizer.
Rational units differ from their Teichmuller lifts first at \(\ell\)-depth,
which has \(Y_r\)-depth at least two after this simple ramified quadratic
step.
Since
\[
 C_r=\frac{-1+Y_r}{2},\qquad
 \mathcal A_{r-1}\equiv[1/4]\pmod {Y_r^2},
\]
one obtains \((\mathsf{RR}_1)\).  This quadratic step is totally ramified,
so lower-field principal perturbations acquire \(Y_r\)-depth at least two;
the later steps are unramified because \(r\) was the last non-\'etale
index.  Their Teichmuller addition defects likewise have depth at least
two.  Writing
\[
 \mathcal A_j=[\bar{\mathcal A}_j](1+Y_rs_j),\qquad
 C_j=[\bar C_j](1+Y_rb_j)\pmod {Y_r^2},
\]
therefore gives
\[
 (2\bar C_j+1)b_j=(\bar C_j+1)s_{j-1},
 \qquad s_j=s_{j-1}+b_j.
\]
Elimination proves \((\mathsf{RR}_2)\).  At the terminal step it gives
\[
 (2\bar C_n+1)(s_{n-1}-2b_n)=-s_{n-1},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{RR}_3)\).  Because \(r\) was the last non-\'etale
stage, \(Y_r\) remains a uniformizer thereafter.  Put
\(L=F_n(\zeta_\ell)\) and \(d=e(L/F_n)\).  Then
\[
 d\mid\ell-1,\qquad v_L(Y_r)=d,\qquad
 e_L:=v_L(\ell)=e(F_n/\mathbf Q_\ell)d\geq2d.
\]
The Teichmuller factor of \(W_n\) is already an \(\ell\)-th power because
\(\ell\) is coprime to the order of the residue-field unit group.  If
\(z\in U_m\), the first possible depth of \(z^\ell-1\) is at least
\(\min\{\ell m,e_L+m\}\), with cancellation only raising it.  Neither
term can equal \(d\): one has \(\ell m>d\) and \(e_L+m>d\).
Hence a principal unit with the nonzero depth-\(d\) leading class in
\((\mathsf{RR}_3)\) cannot be an \(\ell\)-th power.  The case \(r=n\)
follows from the displayed Cayley identity and
\((1+Y)/(1-Y)-1=2Y/(1-Y)\).

If the lower completion before the critical step is unramified and
\(\mathcal A_{r-1}=[1/4](1+\ell s)\), then
\[
 \frac{D_{r-1}}{\ell}\equiv q_{\mathfrak l}(4)-s,
 \qquad q_{\mathfrak l}(4)=2\mathfrak d_\ell(-1/2).
\]
Thus \(s\ne q_{\mathfrak l}(4)\) is exactly the simple-discriminant case;
equality is a genuine second-Witt or higher-filtration branch, not a failure
of the first-order algebra.  Lean kernel-checks the Cayley identities, the
homogeneous recurrence, the terminal nonvanishing, and these two critical
coefficient identities.  The local valuation interpretation remains
paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the remaining value is one specified Artin class]
\label{prop:fermat-ray-character}
Put \(\mathcal E=M(\theta)\), \(\theta^\ell=W_n\), and let
\(\mathfrak f_W\) be the Artin conductor of \(\mathcal E/M\), supported
above \(\ell\).  Since \(W_n\) is fixed by \(\Delta\) and
\(\mu_\ell\subset M\), the extension \(\mathcal E/\mathcal K_n\) is
Galois; hence \(\mathfrak f_W\) is \(\Delta\)-stable.  With arithmetic
Frobenius, the Artin--Kummer character
\[
 \chi_W:\operatorname{Cl}_{\mathfrak f_W}(M)/
              \operatorname{Cl}_{\mathfrak f_W}(M)^\ell
       \longrightarrow\mu_\ell,
 \qquad
 \chi_W([\mathfrak a])
 =\frac{\operatorname{Art}_{\mathcal E/M}(\mathfrak a)(\theta)}{\theta}
\]
satisfies
\[
 \boxed{\quad \chi_W([\mathfrak q_1])=H,\quad}       \tag{\(\mathsf{RCF}_1\)}
\]
and, for \(a\in\Delta\),
\[
 \chi_W(\tau_a x)=\chi_W(x)^a.                      \tag{\(\mathsf{RCF}_2\)}
\]
Consequently the desired \(\ell\)-primary Popovych condition is exactly
\[
 [\mathfrak q_1]\notin\ker\chi_W.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{RCF}_3\)}
\]
If \([\mathcal E:M]=\ell\), this says precisely that
\(\mathfrak q_1\) is inert rather than split in \(\mathcal E/M\).

This formulation also exhausts the standard group-theoretic information.
Every \(\Delta\)-fixed ray class is killed by \(\chi_W\), and
\(\chi_W([\mathfrak q_a\mathfrak q_{-a}])=1\).  Conversely, the orbit
relations alone admit base value one or any element of \(\mu_\ell\).
Reflection
theorems identify the \(\Delta\)-trivial Kummer radical generated by
\([W_n]\) with the reflected cyclotomic ray eigenspace, but compare ranks;
they do not decide whether the projection of \([\mathfrak q_1]\) pairs
nontrivially with \([W_n]\).  After enlarging \(\mathfrak f_W\) to a
sufficiently large \(\Delta\)-invariant modulus induced from
\(\mathcal K_n\), ambiguous ray-class formulas count fixed classes and norm
indices, but still cannot evaluate \((\mathsf{RCF}_1)\).  The desired
condition is stronger than existence: even after proving \(\chi_W\)
nontrivial, one must evaluate it at one distinguished mixed class.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Local Artin reciprocity at \(\mathfrak q_1\) and the Hilbert-symbol
orientation of Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-canonical-global-lift} give
\((\mathsf{RCF}_1)\).  Because \(W_n\) is fixed by \(\Delta\) while
\(\tau_a\) acts on \(\mu_\ell\) by the \(a\)-th power,
Artin equivariance gives \((\mathsf{RCF}_2)\).  A nonidentity Frobenius in
a cyclic degree-\(\ell\) extension has order \(\ell\), proving the
inert/split assertion.

If a source class is fixed by some \(a\ne1\), equivariance says its image
equals its own \(a\)-th power and hence is one.  The inverse-pair assertion
is immediate from \((\mathsf{RCF}_2)\).  Finally an equivariant orbit map
has the form \(a\mapsto a h\) in additive \(\F_\ell\)-coordinates, with
arbitrary base scalar \(h\); the orbit relations permit both \(h=0\) and
\(h\ne0\).  These abstract fixed-class and scalar-freedom statements are
kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.  Gras's reflection theorem
\cite[Theorems~4.4 and~5.18]{Gras98} and the Chevalley--Gras formula of
Li--Yu~\cite[Theorem~2.1]{LiYu20}, applied after the stated modulus
enlargement, have exactly the rank/counting scope just described and do not
evaluate the named Artin class.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Hasse norm and dihedral descent boundary]
\label{cor:fermat-hasse-dihedral}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ray-character} with \(n\geq2\).  Then
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 H=1
 \iff
 2\in\Norm_{\mathcal E/M}(\mathcal E^\times).
 \quad}                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_1\)}
\]
Thus failure of the Popovych \(\ell\)-condition is exactly solvability of
one global cyclic norm equation.

If \(H=1\), choose \(y\in\mathcal E^\times\) with
\(\Norm_{\mathcal E/M}(y)=2\), put
\(G=\operatorname{Gal}(\mathcal E/M)\), and let
\(P=\sum_{\gamma\in G}n_\gamma\gamma\in\mathbf Z[G]\).  Then
\[
 \Norm_{\mathcal E/M}(y^P)
 =2^{\operatorname{aug}(P)},
 \qquad
 \operatorname{aug}(P)=\sum_{\gamma\in G}n_\gamma.  \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_{1a}\)}
\]
Hence every unweighted operation in the cyclic Kummer direction has the
same norm valuation at every \(\mathfrak q_a\mid2\).  In particular, if
some \(y^P\) descends to \(M\), then
\((y^P)^\ell=2^{\operatorname{aug}(P)}\), so its own valuation vector is
constant.  It cannot equal the affine selector, supported only at
\(\mathfrak q_1\), or its mixed relative norm, supported only on the
\(\{\mathord\pm1\}\)-coset of that prime.  A coupling
would have to weight the \(\Delta\)-orbit, which is precisely the missing
mixed ray-class operation.

Assume now that \([\mathcal E:M]=\ell\), put
\(M'=\mathcal K_{n-1}(\zeta_\ell)\), and let \(\iota\) be the top
involution of \(M/M'\).  It extends to \(\mathcal E\) by
\(\iota(\theta)=\theta^{-1}\).  If
\(\sigma(\theta)=\zeta_\ell\theta\), then
\[
 \iota\sigma\iota^{-1}=\sigma^{-1},                 \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_2\)}
\]
so \(\operatorname{Gal}(\mathcal E/M')\) is dihedral.  The involution
exchanges \(\mathfrak q_1\) and \(\mathfrak q_{-1}\).  For their common
prime \(\mathfrak r\) of \(M'\), choosing a prime of \(\mathcal E\) above
\(\mathfrak q_1\) gives the Frobenius representative \(\sigma^h\)
determined by \(H=\zeta_\ell^h\).  Every homomorphism from this dihedral group to an
abelian group kills \(\sigma\).  Consequently every abelian quotient or
descent of this specific Kummer extension sees the same image for
\(h=0\) and \(h\ne0\); a successful lower-field descent must retain its
nonabelian dihedral Frobenius.

Nevertheless the norm equation itself has an exact nonabelian reflection
descent.  Put \(L=\mathcal E^{\langle\iota\rangle}\), so
\([L:M']=\ell\).  More explicitly, if
\(t=\theta+\theta^{-1}\), and if the classical power sums are defined by
\[
 D^{\mathrm{cl}}_0(T)=2,\qquad D^{\mathrm{cl}}_1(T)=T,\qquad
 D^{\mathrm{cl}}_{m+2}(T)
 =T D^{\mathrm{cl}}_{m+1}(T)-D^{\mathrm{cl}}_m(T),
\]
then
\[
 L=M'(t),\qquad
 D^{\mathrm{cl}}_\ell(t)=W_n+W_n^{-1}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_{2a}\)}
\]
For every \(a\in M'^\times\),
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 a\in\Norm_{\mathcal E/M}(\mathcal E^\times)
 \iff
 a\in\Norm_{L/M'}(L^\times).
 \quad}                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_3\)}
\]
In particular, when \([\mathcal E:M]=\ell\),
\[
 H=1\iff2\in\Norm_{L/M'}(L^\times).                  \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_4\)}
\]
The equivalence is stable under every completion.  For a place \(v\) of
\(M'\), put
\[
 \mathcal E_v=\mathcal E\otimes_{M'}M'_v,
 \qquad M_v=M\otimes_{M'}M'_v,
 \qquad L_v=L\otimes_{M'}M'_v.
\]
Then, for every \(a\in(M'_v)^\times\),
\[
 a\in\Norm_{L_v/M'_v}(L_v^\times)
 \iff
 \operatorname{diag}(a)
   \in\Norm_{\mathcal E_v/M_v}(\mathcal E_v^\times).
                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_5\)}
\]
Consequently, for every \(a\in M'^\times\), this non-Galois prime-degree
extension satisfies the Hasse norm principle:
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 a\in\Norm_{L/M'}(L^\times)
 \iff
 a\in\Norm_{L_v/M'_v}(L_v^\times)
       \quad\hbox{for every place }v.
 \quad}                                                \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_6\)}
\]
At a prime \(\mathfrak r_{\bar a}\mid2\), one has the sharper dichotomy
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 H=1&L_v\simeq(M'_v)^\ell,\\
 H\ne1&L_v/M'_v\text{ is the unramified extension of degree }\ell.
 \end{array}                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_7\)}
\]
In the second row every norm has valuation divisible by \(\ell\), whereas
\(v(2)=1\).  All local conditions away from the primes above two are
automatic.  Thus the
reflection field retains the nonabelian marked Frobenius, but it creates no
new relative class-group or global norm obstruction: its norm-two equation
is exactly the original selected local splitting question at two.

The quadratic discriminant resolvent is blind as well.  Put
\(A=W_n+W_n^{-1}\in M'\).  Since \([\mathcal E:M]=\ell\), one has
\(A^2-4\ne0\): otherwise \(W_n=\pm1\), hence \(W_n\) is an
\(\ell\)-th power because \(\ell\) is odd.  In fact the coefficient and
its discriminant already lie in the lower ancestry:
\[
 A=\mathcal A_{n-1}^{-1}-2,
 \qquad
 A^2-4=\frac{D_{n-1}}{\mathcal A_{n-1}^2}.
\]
Then
\[
 \operatorname{disc}_T\bigl(D^{\mathrm{cl}}_\ell(T)-A\bigr)
 =(-1)^{\ell(\ell-1)/2}\ell^\ell
   (A^2-4)^{(\ell-1)/2}
 \in(M'^\times)^2.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_8\)}
\]
Indeed \(\ell\equiv1\pmod4\), so the sign is positive and
\((\ell-1)/2\) is even, while the quadratic Gauss subfield of
\(\mathbf Q(\zeta_\ell)\subset M'\) contains \(\sqrt\ell\).
Equivalently, both the identity and the nontrivial \(\ell\)-cycle in
\(\mathsf{HD}_7\) are even permutations.  Neither the polynomial nor field
discriminant can distinguish success from failure.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
For a cyclic Kummer extension, local norm theory says that \(2\) is a norm
at a place \(v\) exactly when \((W_n,2)_{v,\ell}=1\).  Away from
\(2\ell\) both entries are tame units, and at three \(W_n\) is already an
\(\ell\)-th power.  Every place above \(\ell\) is pointwise trivial by
\((\mathsf{GL}_{7a})\), and the archimedean places are complex.  At
\(\mathfrak q_a\mid2\), the symbol is \(H^a\), so all two-adic local norm
conditions hold exactly when \(H=1\).  The cyclic Hasse norm theorem proves
\((\mathsf{HD}_1)\)~\cite{Hasse31}.

For \(P\in\mathbf Z[G]\), norm invariance under every
\(\gamma\in G\) gives
\[
 \Norm(y^P)
 =\prod_{\gamma\in G}\Norm(y)^{n_\gamma}
 =2^{\sum n_\gamma},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{HD}_{1a})\).  In particular the common further norm
is \(\Norm_{\mathcal E/M'}(y)=4\), with constant prime-above-two
valuations; this is fully compatible with the mixed affine-norm theorem
rather than contradicting it.

The top involution sends \(W_n\) to \(W_n^{-1}\), proving that the stated
extension of \(\iota\) is well-defined; direct evaluation on \(\theta\)
gives \((\mathsf{HD}_2)\).  At the prime of \(\mathcal K_{n-1}\) above
two, arithmetic Frobenius in
\(\operatorname{Gal}(M/\mathcal K_{n-1})=\langle\iota\rangle\times\Delta\)
is the diagonal element \(\iota\tau_{-1}\), because the residue size is
\(q\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).  Hence \(\iota\tau_{-1}\) stabilizes
\(\mathfrak q_1\), so \(\iota(\mathfrak q_1)=\mathfrak q_{-1}\); the
chosen Frobenius above \(\mathfrak r\) is the stated translation.  Finally,
in any abelian quotient the images of
\(\sigma\) and \(\sigma^{-1}\) are equal by \((\mathsf{HD}_2)\).  The
image of \(\sigma\) therefore has order dividing both two and the odd prime
\(\ell\), hence is trivial.  The abstract augmentation identity
underlying \(\mathsf{HD}_{1a}\) and this final dihedral group-theoretic step
are independently kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.

To prove \(\mathsf{HD}_3\), first let \(z\in L\).  Its \(M'\)-embeddings
are the restrictions of \(\sigma^j\), \(0\leq j<\ell\), so
\[
 \Norm_{L/M'}(z)
 =\prod_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\sigma^j(z)
 =\Norm_{\mathcal E/M}(z).
\]
The identity \(\mathsf{HD}_{2a}\) is the classical Dickson--Chebyshev
power-sum formula applied to \(t=\theta+\theta^{-1}\).  If two conjugates
\(\zeta_\ell^j\theta+\zeta_\ell^{-j}\theta^{-1}\) and
\(\zeta_\ell^k\theta+\zeta_\ell^{-k}\theta^{-1}\) coincide, then either
\(j=k\) or \(\zeta_\ell^{j+k}\theta^2=1\).  The latter implies
\(W_n^2=1\), hence that \(W_n\) is an \(\ell\)-th power because \(\ell\) is
odd, contrary to \([\mathcal E:M]=\ell\).  Thus the \(\ell\) conjugates are
distinct and \(t\) generates the reflection fixed field.
Conversely, suppose \(\Norm_{\mathcal E/M}(y)=a\in M'^\times\), put
\(r=(\ell+1)/2\), and define
\[
 z=\frac{(y\iota(y))^r}{a}.
\]
Then \(\iota(z)=z\), hence \(z\in L\), while conjugating the cyclic norm
by \(\iota\) gives \(\Norm(\iota(y))=a\).  Therefore
\[
 \Norm_{L/M'}(z)=\Norm_{\mathcal E/M}(z)
 =\frac{(a^2)^r}{a^\ell}=a^{2r-\ell}=a.
\]
This proves \(\mathsf{HD}_3\), and \(\mathsf{HD}_4\) follows from
\(\mathsf{HD}_1\).

The proof just given is an identity in finite etale algebras and therefore
commutes with the base change \(M'\to M'_v\).  In particular the same
formula proves \(\mathsf{HD}_5\).  If \(a\) is a local norm from \(L_v\)
for every \(v\), equation \(\mathsf{HD}_5\) makes its diagonal image a
local norm from \(\mathcal E/M\) at every place of \(M\).  The cyclic
Hasse norm theorem produces a global \(y\in\mathcal E^\times\), and the
symmetrization above produces the required global \(z\in L^\times\).
This proves \(\mathsf{HD}_6\).
As an independent cross-check, Hoshi--Yamasaki's metacyclic norm-torus
theorem gives both the Hasse norm principle and weak approximation here:
the odd dihedral group has trivial Schur multiplier and the reflection
stabilizer is cyclic~\cite[Theorem~3.2 and Example~4.6]{HoshiYamasaki25}.
Thus, once the norm fibre is nonempty, finitely many additional local open
conditions on a witness cannot supply a new obstruction.

At \(\mathfrak r_{\bar a}\), the extension \(M/M'\) splits into the two
primes \(\mathfrak q_a,\mathfrak q_{-a}\).  If \(H=1\), their Frobenius
translations are trivial, so this prime splits completely in
\(\mathcal E/M'\), and hence in \(L/M'\).  If \(H\ne1\), the Frobenius is a nonidentity
\(\sigma^h\); since \(\ell\) is prime it generates
\(\langle\sigma\rangle\), which acts transitively on the \(\ell\) cosets
of the reflection subgroup.  This makes \(L_v/M'_v\) unramified of degree
\(\ell\) and proves \(\mathsf{HD}_7\).  The remaining local assertions are
the place-by-place calculation already used for \(\mathsf{HD}_1\).

For \(f_A(T)=D^{\mathrm{cl}}_\ell(T)-A\), the classical Chebyshev
description gives
\[
 f_A(T)=2T_\ell(T/2)-A,
 \qquad
 f_A'(T)=\ell U_{\ell-1}(T/2).
\]
The latter polynomial has leading coefficient \(\ell\) and roots
\(x_j=2\cos(j\pi/\ell)\), \(1\le j<\ell\).  At these roots,
\(D^{\mathrm{cl}}_\ell(x_j)=2(-1)^j\).  With
\(m=(\ell-1)/2\), therefore,
\[
 \operatorname{Res}(f_A',f_A)
 =\ell^\ell\prod_{j=1}^{\ell-1}\bigl(2(-1)^j-A\bigr)
 =\ell^\ell\bigl((2-A)(-2-A)\bigr)^m
 =\ell^\ell(A^2-4)^m.
\]
The monic discriminant formula supplies the sign in \(\mathsf{HD}_8\),
and the square-class argument in the statement proves its final assertion.
The two lower-ancestry identities follow directly from
\(W_n=-(C_n+1)/C_n\),
\(C_n(C_n+1)=-\mathcal A_{n-1}\), and
\(D_{n-1}=1-4\mathcal A_{n-1}\).  Thus even the reflection polynomial is
lower-defined; the selected information is its factorization at the named
prime above two, not a hidden top-field coefficient.
Moreover
\(\operatorname{disc}(\mathcal O_{M'}[t])
 =\mathfrak d_{L/M'}[\mathcal O_L:\mathcal O_{M'}[t]]^2\)
as ideals.  Hence neither the polynomial discriminant nor the relative
field-discriminant ideal retains the selected Frobenius; the index changes
the discriminant only by a square.

Lean checks the generic reflection symmetrization, its exponent identity,
and the resulting fixed-witness equivalence in a commutative group.  The
finite-etale base-change interpretation, cyclic Hasse theorem, fixed-field
degree, discriminant calculation, and prime-valuation statements remain
paper-level.  If \(W_n\) is already a
global \(\ell\)-th power, then \(\mathcal E=M\) and the dihedral construction
degenerates; that case is itself a stronger global failure and is not covered
by \(\mathsf{HD}_3\)--\(\mathsf{HD}_7\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the unramified Euler factor is the selected datum]
\label{prop:selected-euler-factor}
Retain Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-hasse-dihedral}, assume
\([\mathcal E:M]=\ell\), and put
\[
 Q=|k(\mathfrak q_1)|=|k(\mathfrak r)|,\qquad T=Q^{-s}.
\]
Use arithmetic Frobenius and write
\(\chi(\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathfrak q_1})=H\) for the local cyclic
Kummer character.  Then
\[
 L_{\mathfrak q_1}(T,\chi^a)=(1-H^aT)^{-1}.          \tag{\(\mathsf{EF}_1\)}
\]
Let
\[
 G=D_{2\ell}=\langle\sigma,\iota:
 \sigma^\ell=\iota^2=1,
 \iota\sigma\iota^{-1}=\sigma^{-1}\rangle,
 \qquad
 \Pi=\operatorname{Ind}_{\langle\iota\rangle}^{G}\mathbf1.
\]
At \(\mathfrak r\), the Frobenius \(\sigma^h\), where
\(H=\zeta_\ell^h\), acts on the \(\ell\) reflection cosets by
translation.  Consequently
\[
 L_{\mathfrak r}(T,\Pi)=
 \begin{cases}
  (1-T)^{-\ell},&H=1,\\
  (1-T^\ell)^{-1},&H\ne1,
 \end{cases}                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{EF}_2\)}
\]
and, for the augmentation quotient \(\Pi_0=\Pi/\mathbf1\),
\[
 L_{\mathfrak r}(T,\Pi_0)=
 \prod_{a=1}^{\ell-1}L_{\mathfrak q_1}(T,\chi^a)
 =
 \begin{cases}
  (1-T)^{-(\ell-1)},&H=1,\\[1mm]
  \dfrac{1-T}{1-T^\ell},&H\ne1.
 \end{cases}                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{EF}_3\)}
\]
Thus the named Euler factor sees failure exactly, but only by containing
the original unknown \(H\).

All standard conductor and normalized local-root-number data at this
prime are blind.  If \(\psi\) has conductor zero and the Haar measure is
self-dual, then
\[
 a(\chi^a)=a(\Pi)=a(\Pi_0)=0,\qquad
 \varepsilon_{\mathfrak r}(s,\chi^a,\psi)
 =\varepsilon_{\mathfrak r}(s,\Pi,\psi)
 =\varepsilon_{\mathfrak r}(s,\Pi_0,\psi)=1.          \tag{\(\mathsf{EF}_4\)}
\]
The same local statement applies to the ordinary, cubic, and
conductor-five reflection fields above: at the selected prime they are
likewise split or unramified of odd prime degree.  Hence discriminant,
Artin conductor, epsilon factor, and local root number cannot evaluate
any of the selected \(Z/O/C/D\) Frobenius values.  This is a local
no-go only; a new global ancestry identity could in principle correlate
ramified factors elsewhere with \(H\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation~\((\mathsf{EF}_1)\) is the definition of the unramified local
factor.  Translation by zero fixes all reflection cosets, whereas a
nonzero translation of the prime-order group is one \(\ell\)-cycle.
This gives \((\mathsf{EF}_2)\).  Removing the invariant line gives
\((\mathsf{EF}_3)\), equivalently
\[
 \prod_{a=0}^{\ell-1}(1-H^aT)=1-T^\ell
 \quad(H\ne1).
\]
Every representation in the statement is unramified.  Deligne's
conductor-zero normalization gives epsilon factor one for an unramified
one-dimensional character; multiplicativity over its complex
eigencharacters proves \((\mathsf{EF}_4)\)
\cite[\S\S3.4--3.6 and Theorem~4.1(1)]{Deligne73}.
With geometric Frobenius, \(H\) is replaced by \(H^{-1}\), leaving every
split/nontrivial dichotomy unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the full Artin package leaves the named factor free]
\label{prop:selected-global-artin}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:selected-euler-factor}, put
\[
 F=M'=\mathcal K_{n-1}(\zeta_\ell),\qquad
 K=M=\mathcal K_n(\zeta_\ell),\qquad
 m=\frac{\ell-1}{2},\qquad d=[F:\Q].
\]
For a nontrivial character \(\chi\) of
\(\operatorname{Gal}(\mathcal E/K)\), the augmentation representation
has the exact decomposition
\[
 \Pi_0\simeq
 \bigoplus_{a\in\F_\ell^\times/\{\mathord\pm1\}}
   \operatorname{Ind}_{K/F}\chi^a.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{GAF}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 L_F(s,\Pi_0)=\frac{\zeta_L(s)}{\zeta_F(s)}
 =\prod_{a\in\F_\ell^\times/\{\mathord\pm1\}}
    L_K(s,\chi^a).}                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{GAF}_2\)}
\]
Both \(F\) and \(L\) are totally imaginary.  With
\(\Gamma_{\mathbb C}(s)=2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)\), the completed quotient is
\[
 \Lambda_F(s,\Pi_0)=
 \left(\frac{|D_L|}{|D_F|}\right)^{s/2}
 \Gamma_{\mathbb C}(s)^{md}L_F(s,\Pi_0),            \tag{\(\mathsf{GAF}_3\)}
\]
and satisfies
\[
 \boxed{\Lambda_F(s,\Pi_0)=\Lambda_F(1-s,\Pi_0),
 \qquad W(\Pi_0)=+1.}                               \tag{\(\mathsf{GAF}_4\)}
\]
Thus the global sign is independent of \(H\).

Let \(\mathfrak f_W\) be the common conductor of the nonzero powers
\(\chi^a\).  It is supported above \(\ell\), and
\[
 \mathfrak f_F(\Pi_0)=\mathfrak d_{L/F}
 =\bigl(\mathfrak d_{K/F}\,
       \Norm_{K/F}\mathfrak f_W\bigr)^m.            \tag{\(\mathsf{GAF}_5\)}
\]
If \(v\nmid\ell\) is ramified in \(K/F\), then for every \(a\)
the local induced representation is
\(\mathbf1\oplus\eta_v\), where \(\eta_v\) is the ramified quadratic
character of \(K_v/F_v\).  Hence
\[
 L_v(T_v,\Pi_0)=(1-T_v)^{-m},\qquad W_v(\Pi_0)=1.   \tag{\(\mathsf{GAF}_6\)}
\]
The remaining \(\ell\)-adic Gauss factors have global product one by
\((\mathsf{GAF}_4)\); none contains the unramified value \(H\).

By contrast, there are \(m\) primes of \(F\) above two, and multiplying
their augmentation factors gives
\[
 \prod_{\mathfrak r\mid2}L_{\mathfrak r}(T,\Pi_0)
 =\begin{cases}
   (1-T)^{-m(\ell-1)},&H=1,\\[1mm]
   \left(\dfrac{1-T}{1-T^\ell}\right)^m,&H\ne1.
  \end{cases}                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{GAF}_7\)}
\]
This product distinguishes success from failure only because it already
contains the named unknown Frobenius.  Conductor, discriminant, gamma
factor, ramified local constants, and the functional-equation sign do not
select between its two rows.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The nontrivial characters of the rotation subgroup occur in inverse pairs
under the reflection, which gives \((\mathsf{GAF}_1)\).  Artin formalism
for the reflection permutation representation gives
\((\mathsf{GAF}_2)\).  Since
\[
 r_2(L)-r_2(F)=\frac{\ell d-d}{2}=md,
\]
the quotient of the completed Dedekind zeta functions is exactly
\((\mathsf{GAF}_3)\), and the two zeta functional equations prove
\((\mathsf{GAF}_4)\).

Every nonzero power of \(\chi\) has the same kernel and conductor.
The conductor--discriminant formula, followed by induction from \(K\) to
\(F\), proves \((\mathsf{GAF}_5)\).  At a finite
\(v\nmid\ell\) ramified in \(K/F\), the Kummer extension is unramified.
Quadratic inertia is a reflection in the odd dihedral group and is normal
in its decomposition group.  A reflection subgroup is self-normalizing,
so the decomposition group is that reflection subgroup itself; the
rotation extension therefore splits locally.  This gives
\(\mathbf1\oplus\eta_v\) and the Euler factor in
\((\mathsf{GAF}_6)\).  A quadratic local root number has fourth power one,
while Fermat divisors satisfy \(\ell\equiv1\pmod8\), so \(4\mid m\) and
\(W(\eta_v)^m=1\).  Deligne's induction and epsilon-factor formalism
supplies the standard local normalization~\cite{Deligne73}.

Finally, the primes above two are indexed by
\(a\in\F_\ell^\times/\{\mathord\pm1\}\).  At each one the Frobenius is
the translation represented by \(H^a\).  It is the identity for every
\(a\) exactly when \(H=1\); otherwise it is one \(\ell\)-cycle.
Multiplying the two cases of \((\mathsf{EF}_3)\) over the \(m\) primes
proves \((\mathsf{GAF}_7)\).  The representation-theoretic and
number-field identities in this proposition remain paper-level; Lean
checks only the underlying odd-dihedral abelianization and local
split/inert permutation cores recorded earlier.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[only the top generator carries the Brauer obstruction]
\label{cor:fermat-top-brauer}
Retain Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-hasse-dihedral}, put
\(M_0=\mathcal K_{n-1}(\zeta_\ell)\), and write the
\(\ell\)-torsion norm-residue symbol additively.  Then
\[
 \{\mathcal A_{n-1},2\}_\ell=0
 \quad\text{in }\operatorname{Br}(M_0)[\ell].        \tag{\(\mathsf{TB}_1\)}
\]
If \(s\) is the top involution and
\[
 \alpha=\{C_n,2\}_\ell,
 \qquad
 \beta=\{W_n,2\}_\ell
 \quad\text{in }\operatorname{Br}(M)[\ell],
\]
then
\[
 \alpha+s\alpha=0,
 \qquad
 \boxed{\ \beta=-2\alpha.\ }                       \tag{\(\mathsf{TB}_2\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 H\ne1\iff\beta\ne0\iff\alpha\ne0.                \tag{\(\mathsf{TB}_3\)}
\]
Thus every lower ancestral factor has zero symbol with two after adjoining
\(\zeta_\ell\); the whole obstruction is the anti-invariant class of the
single top generator.  Corestriction kills the class at issue, so this
reduction gives no inductive nonvanishing theorem by itself.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
We prove \((\mathsf{TB}_1)\) place by place.  Away from \(2\ell\), both
entries are units and the tame symbol is trivial.  At a place above two,
the completion of \(M_0\) is unramified with residue field
\(\F_{q^2}\), where \(q=2^{2^n}\), while the reduction of
\(\mathcal A_{n-1}\) lies in \(\F_q^\times\).  Since
\(\gcd(\ell,q-1)=1\), its residue is an \(\ell\)-th power, and
\(\ell\)-powering is an automorphism of the pro-two principal units.
Thus \(\mathcal A_{n-1}\) is already a local \(\ell\)-th power.  At a
place above \(\ell\), the decomposition-stabilizer argument of
\((\mathsf{GL}_{7a})\), now with
\([\mathcal K_{n-1}:\mathbf Q]=2^n\), kills the symbol pointwise.  All
infinite places are complex.  The identical argument applies to every lower
unit \(C_i\) and \(\mathcal A_i\), \(i<n\).  Brauer--Hasse--Noether proves
\((\mathsf{TB}_1)\).

Projection for corestriction gives
\[
 \operatorname{cor}_{M/M_0}(\alpha)
 =\{\Norm(C_n),2\}_\ell
 =\{\mathcal A_{n-1},2\}_\ell=0.
\]
Restricting back to \(M\) yields \(\alpha+s\alpha=0\).  Finally
\(W_n=\mathcal A_{n-1}C_n^{-2}\) and bilinearity give
\(\beta=-2\alpha\).  Multiplication by two is invertible on
\(\ell\)-torsion, proving \((\mathsf{TB}_3)\).  Lean independently checks
the abstract anti-invariant and coboundary identities in
\texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[four-point saturation of a Fermat failure]
\label{prop:fermat-four-plane}
Fix \(n\geq2\), put

\[
 c=c_n,\qquad d=c_{n-1},\qquad b=a_{n-2},\qquad
 Q=|E_{n-2}|=2^{2^{n-1}}.
\]

Then, for one \(\epsilon\in\F_2\),

\[
 c^Q=c+d+\epsilon,\qquad c^{Q^2}=c+1.              \tag{\(\mathsf{FP}_1\)}
\]

Consequently, if \(\ell\mid F_n\) and \(c\) is an \(\ell\)-th power
in \(E_n\), the complete affine plane

\[
 c+\F_2+\F_2d=\{c,c+1,c+d,c+d+1\}                 \tag{\(\mathsf{FP}_2\)}
\]

consists of \(\ell\)-th powers.  Its product is nevertheless the
old-field element

\[
 \Norm_{E_n/E_{n-2}}(c)=b^3,                        \tag{\(\mathsf{FP}_3\)}
\]

which is automatically an \(\ell\)-th power.  Thus the exact four-point
saturation and its norm are compatible with failure; they do not supply
the missing nonresidue.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The tower equations give

\[
 d^2+d=b,\qquad c^2+c=bd=d^3+d^2,\qquad d^Q=d+1.
\]

For \(s=c^Q+c\), subtraction of the equation for \(c\) from its
\(Q\)-th power gives \(s^2+s=b\).  Hence
\(s\in\{d,d+1\}\), proving the first identity in
\((\mathsf{FP}_1)\); applying \(Q\)-Frobenius once more gives the
second.  Frobenius preserves the subgroup of \(\ell\)-th powers, so
one power value forces all four values in \((\mathsf{FP}_2)\).
Finally,

\[
 c(c+1)=bd,\qquad
 (c+d)(c+d+1)=b(d+1),
\]

and their product is \(b^2d(d+1)=b^3\).  Since
\(\ell\mid Q^2+1\) is coprime to \(|E_{n-2}^\times|=Q-1\), every
old-field element is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(E_n\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[CM form and the Kummer-cover obstruction]
\label{prop:fermat-elliptic-kummer}
On \(\mathcal E:y^2+y=x^3+x^2\), let \(O\) be the origin
and \(T=(0,0)\).  Then
\(\mathcal E(\F_2)=\langle T\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/5\mathbb Z\), with
\[
 -T=(0,1),\qquad 2T=(1,1),\qquad -2T=(1,0).
\]
On the normalized Conway correspondence, the function
\(\mathfrak c=1/(u+1)\) is
\[
 \mathfrak c=\frac{x}{x^2+x+y},\qquad
 \operatorname{div}(\mathfrak c)=[-T]+2[O]-3[-2T].     \tag{\(\mathsf{EC}_1\)}
\]
The automorphism \(\mathcal A\) satisfies
\[
 \mathcal A^2(P)=T-P,\qquad
 \mathfrak c(\mathcal A^2P)=\mathfrak c(P)+1.          \tag{\(\mathsf{EC}_2\)}
\]
If \(\pi\) is \(2\)-power Frobenius and \(i=\pi+1\), then
\(i^2=-1\), \(i(T)=2T\), and, after possibly replacing
\(\mathcal A\) by its inverse,
\[
 \mathcal A=\tau_{2T}\circ i.
\]
Both orientations fix \(R_0=-2T\).

For \(n\geq4\), set
\[
 Q=2^{2^{n-1}},\qquad R=\sqrt Q=2^{2^{n-2}},
\]
and let \(P_n\) be the distinguished Conway point.  If
\(X_n=P_n-R_0\), then
\[
 \Frob_Q(X_n)=\pm iX_n,\qquad
 X_n\in\ker([R]\mp i).                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{EC}_3\)}
\]
Each kernel is cyclic of order \(R^2+1=Q+1\).

For every prime \(\ell\mid Q^2+1\), the Kummer cover
\[
 \mathcal C_\ell:\qquad z^\ell=\mathfrak c
\]
is geometrically connected, ramified exactly above
\(-T,O,-2T\), and has genus
\[
 g(\mathcal C_\ell)=\frac{3\ell-1}{2}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{EC}_4\)}
\]
The automorphism \(\mathcal A\) does not lift to
\(\mathcal C_\ell\).  Hence the quarter-turn on the base supplies no
action on the Kummer fibre above \(P_n\); the surviving Conway
condition is exactly
\[
 \mathfrak c(P_n)=c_n\notin
 \bigl(\F_{Q^4}^{\times}\bigr)^\ell.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{EC}_5\)}
\]
It is the Artin-symbol problem for one distinguished fibre of this
ramified cover, not a consequence of the CM action on \(\mathcal E\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In the blow-up coordinates of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-elliptic}, \(U=u+1\), \(V=v+1\),
\(X=U/V\), \(Y=V\), and
\((x:y:z)=(Z:X:X+Y+Z)\).  On \(z=1\), homogeneous representatives give
\[
 V=\frac{1+x+y}{x},\qquad U=\frac{y(1+x+y)}{x^2}.
\]
Since \(y(1+x+y)=xy+x^3+x^2=x(x^2+x+y)\), this proves the formula for
\(\mathfrak c=1/U\).  With \(d=x^2+x+y\), direct intersection gives
\[
 \operatorname{div}(x)=[T]+[-T]-2[O],\qquad
 \operatorname{div}(d)=[T]+3[-2T]-4[O].
\]
Indeed \(d=0\) gives \(y=x^2+x\), whence the curve equation gives
\(x(x+1)^3=0\), and \(d\) has pole order four at \(O\).  Subtraction
proves \((\mathsf{EC}_1)\).

The function \(v=(y+1)/x\) has pole divisor \([O]+[T]\), so the deck
involution of its degree-two map is \(J(P)=T-P\).
Since \(\mathcal A^2\) fixes \(v\) and is nontrivial, it equals \(J\).
Also \(\mathcal A^2\) sends \(u\) to \(u^{-1}\), and therefore
\[
 \mathfrak c(\mathcal A^2P)
 =\frac1{u(P)^{-1}+1}
 =\frac{u(P)}{u(P)+1}
 =\mathfrak c(P)+1,
\]
proving \((\mathsf{EC}_2)\).

The Frobenius polynomial of \(\mathcal E/\F_2\) is
\(\pi^2+2\pi+2=0\).  Thus \(i=\pi+1\) satisfies \(i^2=-1\), and it
acts on \(\mathcal E(\F_2)\) as multiplication by two.
Write \(\mathcal A=\tau_S\circ\alpha\), with \(\alpha(O)=O\).
As \(\mathcal A\) is defined over \(\F_2\), \(\alpha\) commutes with
\(\pi\).  The centralizer of \(\pi\) in the supersingular endomorphism
algebra is \(\mathbb Q(i)\), whose integral units are
\(\{\pm1,\pm i\}\).  The linear part of \(\mathcal A^2\) is
\([-1]\), so \(\alpha=\pm i\).  Comparing translation parts gives
\[
 \mathcal A=\tau_{2T}\circ i
 \quad\text{or}\quad
 \mathcal A=\tau_{-T}\circ(-i);
\]
these are inverse and both fix \(R_0=-2T\).

For \(n\geq4\), \(2^{n-1}\) is divisible by eight.  Since
\(\pi^8=[16]\),
\[
 \pi^{2^{n-1}}=[2^{2^{n-2}}]=[R].
\]
Translation of \(R_0\) to the origin makes
\(\mathcal A^{\pm1}\) the linear maps \(\pm i\), proving
\((\mathsf{EC}_3)\).  The separable endomorphisms \([R]\mp i\) have
degree \(R^2+1\).  For every odd prime \(p\), the Tate module
\(T_p(\mathcal E)\) is free of rank one over \(\mathbb Z_p[i]\)
(the polynomial \(X^2+1\) is separable away from \(2\)).  Hence the
\(p\)-primary kernel is the additive quotient
\(\mathbb Z_p[i]/(R\mp i)\).  The matrix of \(R\mp i\) over
\(\mathbb Z_p\) has entries with gcd one and determinant \(R^2+1\),
so its first Smith invariant is one.  Combining the odd primary parts
shows that the geometric kernel is cyclic of order \(R^2+1\).

Every prime \(\ell\mid Q^2+1\) is coprime to \(15\).  Thus the
valuations \(1,2,-3\) in \((\mathsf{EC}_1)\) are nonzero modulo
\(\ell\).  Kummer theory makes \(z^\ell=\mathfrak c\) geometrically
connected and totally ramified at exactly those three points.
Riemann--Hurwitz gives
\[
 2g(\mathcal C_\ell)-2=3(\ell-1),
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{EC}_4)\).

It remains to prove non-lifting.  Choose
\(\mathcal A=\tau_{2T}\circ i\), put
\(B_j=\mathcal A^j(O)\), and retain \(R_0=-2T\).  Then
\[
 (B_0,B_1,B_2,B_3)=(O,2T,T,-T).
\]
For \(D_j=\operatorname{div}(\mathfrak c\circ\mathcal A^{-j})\),
pullback gives
\[
 D_j=2[B_j]+[B_{j+3}]-3[R_0].                          \tag{\(\mathsf{EC}_6\)}
\]
If \(\sum_{j=0}^3r_jD_j\equiv0\pmod\ell\), comparison at the four
\(B_j\)'s gives \(2r_j+r_{j+1}=0\) cyclically.  Iteration gives
\(15r_0=0\), and \(\ell\nmid15\) forces every \(r_j=0\).  Hence
\[
 \mathfrak c,\quad\mathfrak c\circ\mathcal A,\quad
 \mathfrak c\circ\mathcal A^2,\quad
 \mathfrak c\circ\mathcal A^3
\]
are linearly independent in
\[
 \overline{\F}_2(\mathcal E)^\times/
 \overline{\F}_2(\mathcal E)^{\times\ell}.
\]
A lift of \(\mathcal A\) would normalize the cyclic deck group, hence
send \(z\) to \(gz^r\) for
\(r\in(\mathbb Z/\ell\mathbb Z)^\times\), and would require
\(\mathfrak c\circ\mathcal A=g^\ell\mathfrak c^r\), contradicting
this independence.

Finally, set
\[
 \chi(t)=t^{(Q^4-1)/\ell},\qquad
 \tau=\chi(\mathfrak c(P_n)).
\]
Since \(\Frob_Q^2(P_n)=\mathcal A^2(P_n)\),
\((\mathsf{EC}_2)\) gives
\(\mathfrak c(P_n)^{Q^2}=\mathfrak c(P_n)+1\).
Because \(Q^2\equiv-1\pmod\ell\), the four CM/Frobenius character
values are only
\[
 \tau,\qquad\tau^{\pm Q},\qquad\tau^{-1},\qquad
 \tau^{\mp Q}.
\]
All norm identities among them are tautological.  This proves neither
\(\tau=1\) nor \(\tau\ne1\): the desired assertion remains
\((\mathsf{EC}_5)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Miller-unit and generalized-Jacobian location]
\label{prop:fermat-generalized-jacobian}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-elliptic-kummer}, put
\(S=-2T\), \(q=Q^2\), and \(K_n=\F_{Q^4}=\F_{q^2}\).  Then

\[
 \mathfrak c^{-1}=f_{3,S}
\]

up to a nonzero scalar, where \(f_{3,S}\) is the Miller tripling
function with divisor

\[
 \operatorname{div}(f_{3,S})=3[S]-[3S]-2[O].          \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_1\)}
\]

For every prime \(\ell\mid q+1\),

\[
 \#\mathcal E(K_n)=(q-1)^2,\qquad
 \mathcal E(K_n)[\ell]=0,\qquad
 [\ell]:\mathcal E(K_n)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal E(K_n). \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_2\)}
\]

Let \(B=\{-T,O,-2T\}\) and \(U=\mathcal E\setminus B\).  The Kummer
class of \(\mathfrak c\) on \(U\) has nonzero tame residue vector

\[
 (1,2,-3)\pmod\ell.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_3\)}
\]

It is therefore ramified and does not come from an unramified
\(\mu_\ell\)-class on \(\mathcal E\).  In the generalized Jacobian

\[
 1\longrightarrow\mathbb G_m^2\longrightarrow J_B
 \longrightarrow\mathcal E\longrightarrow0,          \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_4\)}
\]

Hilbert 90 and \((\mathsf{GJ}_2)\) give

\[
 J_B(K_n)/\ell J_B(K_n)
 \cong(K_n^\times/K_n^{\times\ell})^2.                \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_5\)}
\]

Thus the residue vector has a genuinely ramified toric projection;
the ordinary \(K_n\)-rational elliptic quotient cannot detect that
projection.  Ordinary \(K_n\)-rational Tate--Lichtenbaum and formal-
group data cannot by themselves decide \((\mathsf{EC}_5)\).  This
does not assert that the ramified class evaluates nontrivially at the
distinguished point, nor does it exclude a calculation using
non-rational \(\mathcal E[\ell]\) or the full extension structure of
\(J_B\).  An elliptic completion must evaluate that
generalized-Jacobian Miller-unit residue.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The point \(T\) has order five, so \(3S=3(-2T)=-T\).  Equation
\((\mathsf{EC}_1)\) therefore gives

\[
 \operatorname{div}(\mathfrak c^{-1})
 =3[S]-[3S]-2[O],
\]

which proves \((\mathsf{GJ}_1)\).

For \(n\geq4\), the Frobenius relation used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-elliptic-kummer} gives

\[
 \pi^{2^{n+1}}=[q].
\]

This is Frobenius on \(K_n=\F_{2^{2^{n+1}}}\), so

\[
 \#\mathcal E(K_n)=\deg([q]-[1])=(q-1)^2.
\]

As \(\ell\mid q+1\) is odd, it is coprime to \(q-1\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{GJ}_2)\); multiplication by \(\ell\) is likewise an
automorphism of the characteristic-two formal group because its
linear coefficient is a unit.

The divisor \((\mathsf{EC}_1)\) gives the residue vector
\((\mathsf{GJ}_3)\), which is nonzero because
\(\ell\nmid6\).  The standard generalized-Jacobian sequence for the
three-point reduced rational modulus is \((\mathsf{GJ}_4)\).  Its
torus is split; Hilbert 90 makes the map on \(K_n\)-points surjective,
and the kernel created on passing modulo \(\ell\) vanishes because
\(\mathcal E(K_n)[\ell]=0\).  This proves \((\mathsf{GJ}_5)\) and the
stated scope of the toric obstruction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact extension character and cohomological exhaustion]
\label{prop:fermat-gj-character}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-generalized-jacobian} and put
\[
 D_1=[-T]-[-2T],\qquad D_2=[O]-[-2T]
\]
in \(\operatorname{Div}^0(B)\).  The character lattice of the split
torus in \(J_B\) is
\[
 X^*(\mathbb G_m^2)=\operatorname{Div}^0(B)
 =\mathbb ZD_1\oplus\mathbb ZD_2,
\]
and the extension is classified, under
\(\mathcal E\cong\operatorname{Pic}^0(\mathcal E)\), by
\[
 \lambda(aD_1+bD_2)=(a+2b)T.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_6\)}
\]
Thus its entire extension class is rational five-torsion.  Moreover
\[
 r:=\operatorname{div}(\mathfrak c)=D_1+2D_2,
 \qquad\lambda(r)=5T=O,
\]
so \(r\) defines a character \(\varphi_r:J_B\to\mathbb G_m\).  If
\(j_T:U\to J_B\) is the generalized Abel map normalized by
\(j_T(T)=0\), then
\[
 \varphi_r(j_T(P))=\mathfrak c(P).                    \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_7\)}
\]
For every prime \(\ell\mid q+1\), even the non-rational geometric
\(\mathcal E[\ell]\) supplies no further rational Kummer class:
\[
 H^1(K_n,\mu_\ell^2)\xrightarrow{\sim}
 H^1(K_n,J_B[\ell]),
 \qquad
 J_B(K_n)/\ell J_B(K_n)\cong
 (K_n^\times/K_n^{\times\ell})^2.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_8\)}
\]
In torus coordinates dual to \((D_1,D_2)\), the character is
\((x,y)\mapsto xy^2\), hence the nonzero linear functional
\((x,y)\mapsto x+2y\) modulo \(\ell\).  Naturality of Kummer theory gives
\[
 \varphi_{r,*}\!\left(\delta_J(j_T(P_n))\right)
   =P_n^*[\mathfrak c]=[\mathfrak c(P_n)]
   \in K_n^\times/K_n^{\times\ell}.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{GJ}_9\)}
\]
Consequently \((\mathsf{EC}_5)\) is exactly the assertion that the
normalized Abel class of \(P_n\) avoids the hyperplane \(x+2y=0\).
Torus translation above any fixed elliptic point realizes every value
of this functional.  Hence the CM point, the full extension class, and
non-rational elliptic \(\ell\)-torsion do not force
\((\mathsf{EC}_5)\); the remaining datum is the explicit toric coordinate
of this one distinguished normalized Abel lift.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a reduced three-point rational modulus, the generalized-Jacobian
torus is \((\mathbb G_m^3)/\mathbb G_m\), with character lattice
\(\operatorname{Div}^0(B)\).  The standard extension-class map sends a
boundary divisor to its class in \(\operatorname{Pic}^0(\mathcal E)\).
Here
\[
 (-T)-(-2T)=T,\qquad O-(-2T)=2T,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{GJ}_6)\).  Equation \((\mathsf{EC}_1)\) gives
\(r=D_1+2D_2\), and its image is \(T+4T=O\).  Characters of \(J_B\)
are precisely the torus characters whose pushout extension class
