vanishes, so \(r\) yields \(\varphi_r\).  The generalized-Albanese
universal property gives \((\mathsf{GJ}_7)\) after normalization at
\(T\).  Indeed, \(T\notin B\) and
\(\mathfrak c(T)\in\F_2^\times=\{1\}\).

The \(K_n\)-Frobenius on \(\mathcal E\) is \([q]\), and
\(q\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).  It therefore acts as \([-1]\) on
\(\mathcal E[\ell]\).  Since \(\ell\) is odd,
\[
 \mathcal E[\ell](K_n)=0,
 \qquad
 H^1(K_n,\mathcal E[\ell])
 =\mathcal E[\ell]/(\Frob-1)\mathcal E[\ell]=0.
\]
The cohomology sequence of
\[
 0\longrightarrow\mu_\ell^2\longrightarrow J_B[\ell]
 \longrightarrow\mathcal E[\ell]\longrightarrow0
\]
then proves the first isomorphism in \((\mathsf{GJ}_8)\).  Lang's
theorem and Kummer theory prove the second.  The natural Kummer square
for \(\varphi_r\), together with \((\mathsf{GJ}_7)\), gives
\((\mathsf{GJ}_9)\).  Finally
\(\mathbb G_m^2(K_n)/\ell\to J_B(K_n)/\ell\) is an isomorphism:
surjectivity uses bijectivity of \([\ell]\) on \(\mathcal E(K_n)\),
and injectivity uses \(\mathcal E(K_n)[\ell]=0\).  The character
\((x,y)\mapsto xy^2\) is surjective on the \(\ell\)-quotient, proving
the fixed-fibre statement and the asserted exact boundary.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[explicit Rosenlicht coordinate and its reciprocity boundary]
\label{prop:fermat-rosenlicht}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-gj-character}, write
\(S=-2T\), \(P=P_n\), and put \(X=P-S\).  For
\(\ell\mid q+1\), let \(Z\in\mathcal E(K_n)\) be the unique point with

\[
 [\ell]Z=P-T,
 \qquad W=T+Z.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_1\)}
\]

If \(e\ell\equiv1\pmod{5(Q+1)}\), then

\[
 Z=[e]X+[2e]T,
 \qquad W=[e]X+[2e+1]T,                             \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_2\)}
\]

so the division point remains an explicit translate of the same cyclic
CM kernel as \(X\).  Choose a Miller function \(F=F_{\ell,Z}\) with

\[
 \operatorname{div}(F)
 =\ell[Z]-[[\ell]Z]-(\ell-1)[O].
\]

In the Rosenlicht coordinates dual to
\(D_1=[-T]-[S]\), \(D_2=[O]-[S]\), the normalized Abel Kummer class of
\(P\) is

\[
 \rho_1=\frac{F(2T)}{F(S)},
 \qquad
 \rho_2=\frac{F(2T)}{F(-T)},                       \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_3\)}
\]

and its Miller-unit character is exactly

\[
 [\mathfrak c(P)]=[\rho_1\rho_2^2]
 \quad\hbox{in }K_n^\times/K_n^{\times\ell}.       \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_4\)}
\]

There is also a formula avoiding the \(\ell\)-division point.  On
\(\mathcal E:y^2+y=x^3+x^2\), set

\[
 A_5=1+x+x^2+y+xy,
 \qquad B_5=1+x+xy,
 \qquad g_1=\frac{A_5}{B_5},
 \qquad g_2=\frac1{B_5}.
\]

Then

\[
 \operatorname{div}(g_1)=5D_1,
 \qquad \operatorname{div}(g_2)=5D_2,
 \qquad \mathfrak c^5=g_1g_2^2.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_5\)}
\]

If \(5\bar5\equiv1\pmod\ell\), the full normalized Abel class in
\(J_B(K_n)/\ell J_B(K_n)\) has coordinates

\[
 \bigl([g_1(P)]^{\bar5},[g_2(P)]^{\bar5}\bigr).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_6\)}
\]

More explicitly, for the consecutive Conway variables
\(u=u_n\), \(v=u_{n-1}\), put

\[
 U=u+1,\qquad V=v+1,\qquad D=V^2+V+U.
\]

Then the normalized point has \(x=V/D\), \(y=U/D\), and

\[
 g_1(P)=
 \frac{D^2+D(U+V)+V^2+UV}{D^2+VD+UV},
 \qquad
 g_2(P)=\frac{D^2}{D^2+VD+UV},                       \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_7\)}
\]

with \(g_1(P)g_2(P)^2=U^{-5}\).

These formulas compute the missing toric coordinate exactly, but do not
force it to be nonzero modulo \(\ell\).  Indeed, Weil reciprocity gives

\[
 \mathfrak c(P)
 =\mathfrak c(W)^\ell
   \frac{F(2T)^3}{F(S)F(-T)^2}.                      \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_8\)}
\]

Applying reciprocity instead to
\(G(Y)=\mathfrak c(Y+T)\), whose divisor is
\([S]+2[-T]-3[2T]\), returns precisely the inverse identity

\[
 \frac{F(S)F(-T)^2}{F(2T)^3}
 =\frac{\mathfrak c(W)^\ell}{\mathfrak c(P)}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{RC}_9\)}
\]

Thus the boundary Miller values and the rational five-torsion splitting
exhaust the Rosenlicht coordinate, but collapsing them by the evident
reciprocity law is exactly equivalent to the original Kummer value
\([\mathfrak c(P)]\); it supplies no independent nonresidue constraint.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(P=X+3T\), one has \(P-T=X+2T\).  Multiplication by \(\ell\)
is invertible both on the cyclic kernel containing \(X\) and on
\(\langle T\rangle\), which proves \((\mathsf{RC}_2)\).

Put \(h(Y)=F(Y-T)\).  Translation of the displayed divisor of \(F\)
gives

\[
 \operatorname{div}(h)=\ell[W]-[P]-(\ell-1)[T].
\]

The Rosenlicht evaluation of this principal relation against \(D_1,D_2\)
is

\[
 \frac{h(S)}{h(-T)}=\frac{F(2T)}{F(S)},
 \qquad
 \frac{h(S)}{h(O)}=\frac{F(2T)}{F(-T)},
\]

which proves \((\mathsf{RC}_3)\).  Weil reciprocity between \(h\) and
\(\mathfrak c\), using

\[
 \operatorname{div}(\mathfrak c)=[-T]+2[O]-3[S],
 \qquad \mathfrak c(T)=1,
\]

gives \((\mathsf{RC}_8)\), and hence
\((\mathsf{RC}_4)\).

Direct intersection with the Weierstrass equation, including the local
orders at the indicated five-torsion points, gives

\[
 \operatorname{div}(A_5)=5[-T]-5[O],
 \qquad
 \operatorname{div}(B_5)=5[S]-5[O].
\]

Thus \(g_1,g_2\) have the divisors asserted in
\((\mathsf{RC}_5)\).  Both sides of
\(\mathfrak c^5=g_1g_2^2\) have the same divisor and value one at
\(T\), proving the exact identity.  Since five is invertible modulo
\(\ell\), this proves \((\mathsf{RC}_6)\).  Substitution of
\(x=V/D\), \(y=U/D\) gives \((\mathsf{RC}_7)\); here
\(\mathfrak c(P)=1/U\), so its last identity is also immediate from
\((\mathsf{RC}_5)\).

Finally, the divisor of \(G\) follows by translating that of
\(\mathfrak c\).  Weil reciprocity between \(F\) and \(G\) is
\((\mathsf{RC}_9)\), proving the exact neutrality statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[five-point equivariant generalized-Jacobian exhaustion]
\label{prop:fermat-five-point-exhaustion}
Retain the Conway point \(P=P_n\), the quarter-turn
\(\mathcal A\), and \(S=-2T\).  Put
\[
 B_0=O,\qquad B_1=2T,\qquad B_2=T,\qquad B_3=-T,\qquad
 D=\mathcal E(\F_2)=\{S,B_0,B_1,B_2,B_3\}.
\]
For \(j\in\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z\), set
\[
 e_j=[B_j]-[S],\qquad
 f_j=\mathfrak c\circ\mathcal A^{-j},\qquad
 r_j=\operatorname{div}(f_j)=2e_j+e_{j+3}.           \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_1\)}
\]
Then \(D\) is \(\mathcal A\)-invariant, and the four classes \(r_j\)
form a basis of
\(\operatorname{Div}^0(D)\otimes\mathbb F_\ell\) for every prime
\(\ell\mid F_n\), \(n\geq2\).

Let \(J_D\) be the generalized Jacobian of the five-point reduced
modulus, let \(K_n=\F_{Q^4}\), where
\(Q=2^{2^{n-1}}\), and choose any
\(R\in\mathcal E(\F_{16})\setminus D\).  If \(j_R\) denotes the
generalized Abel map normalized at \(R\), then
\[
 \mathfrak c(P)\in K_n^{\times\ell}
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 [j_R(P)]=0\text{ in }J_D(K_n)/\ell J_D(K_n).          \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_2\)}
\]
Thus even the simultaneous Rosenlicht data of the complete
quarter-turn orbit contain no obstruction beyond the original Kummer
value.

For \(n\geq3\), put \(z=[j_R(P)]\) and let
\(\sigma=\Frob_Q\).  If
\(\sigma(P)=\mathcal A^\epsilon(P)\), with
\(\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}\), then
\[
 Qz=\mathcal A_*^\epsilon z.                          \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_3\)}
\]
Inside the split rank-four torus quotient this eigenspace is
one-dimensional over \(\mathbb F_\ell\), and evaluation by
\(\varphi_{r_0}\)
is an isomorphism from it to
\(K_n^\times/K_n^{\times\ell}\).  Under this isomorphism \(z\) maps
to \([\mathfrak c(P)]\).  Consequently the full
\(\mathcal A\)-invariant generalized Jacobian, its rational
five-torsion extension class, and the exact Frobenius quarter-turn
relation determine precisely one scalar---the scalar in
\((\mathsf{EC}_5)\)---but do not determine whether that scalar
vanishes.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The automorphism \(\mathcal A\) fixes \(S\) and cycles the other four
\(\F_2\)-points as
\[
 O\longmapsto2T\longmapsto T\longmapsto -T\longmapsto O.
\]
Equation \((\mathsf{EC}_6)\) gives
\((\mathsf{FE}_1)\).  In the basis \((e_0,e_1,e_2,e_3)\), the four
rows \(r_j\) form the circulant matrix \(2I+C\), where \(C\) is a
four-cycle.  Hence
\[
 \det(2I+C)=2^4-1=15.                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_4\)}
\]
No prime divisor of \(F_n\), \(n\geq2\), divides \(15\), proving the
basis assertion.  Notice also that the union of the supports of the
four \(f_j\) is exactly \(D\).

The Frobenius polynomial of \(\mathcal E/\F_2\) gives
\(\#\mathcal E(\F_{16})=25\), so a point
\(R\in\mathcal E(\F_{16})\setminus D\) exists.  Every \(f_j(R)\) is
a nonzero element of \(\F_{16}\); since
\(\gcd(\ell,15)=1\), it is an \(\ell\)-th power already in
\(\F_{16}\).  The five-point generalized Jacobian fits into
\[
 1\longrightarrow\mathbb G_m^4\longrightarrow J_D
 \longrightarrow\mathcal E\longrightarrow0.
\]
As in \((\mathsf{GJ}_2)\),
\(\#\mathcal E(K_n)=(Q^2-1)^2\), so multiplication by \(\ell\) is
bijective on \(\mathcal E(K_n)\).  Hilbert 90 and the absence of
rational \(\ell\)-torsion therefore give
\[
 J_D(K_n)/\ell J_D(K_n)
 \simeq (K_n^\times/K_n^{\times\ell})^4.              \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_5\)}
\]
For \(r_j=\operatorname{div}(f_j)\), let
\(\varphi_{r_j}:J_D\to\mathbb G_m\) be the corresponding principal
boundary character.  Generalized Abel evaluation is
\[
 \varphi_{r_j}(j_R(P))=\frac{f_j(P)}{f_j(R)}
 \quad\text{in }K_n^\times/K_n^{\times\ell}.          \tag{\(\mathsf{FE}_6\)}
\]
Writing \(\sigma=\Frob_Q\), if \(\mathfrak c(P)\) is an
\(\ell\)-th power, then
\(\sigma(P)=\mathcal A^{\pm1}(P)\) and Frobenius stability of
\(K_n^{\times\ell}\) make every \(f_j(P)\) an \(\ell\)-th power.
Equations \((\mathsf{FE}_4)\)--\((\mathsf{FE}_6)\) then give
\(j_R(P)=0\) modulo \(\ell\).  The converse follows by evaluating
\(\varphi_{r_0}\), since \(f_0=\mathfrak c\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{FE}_2)\).

Suppose now that \(n\geq3\).  Then \(\sigma\) fixes \(\F_{16}\), and
hence \(R\).  Moreover
\[
 \#J_D(\F_{16})=15^4\cdot25
\]
is prime to \(\ell\).  Therefore the affine base-point correction
\([j_R(\mathcal A^\epsilon R)]\) vanishes modulo \(\ell\), and
\[
 \sigma j_R(P)=j_R(\sigma P)
 =\mathcal A_*^\epsilon j_R(P)+j_R(\mathcal A^\epsilon R)
\]
reduces to \((\mathsf{FE}_3)\).  On the right side of
\((\mathsf{FE}_5)\), \(\sigma\) acts on the scalar Kummer quotient
as multiplication by \(Q\), while \(\mathcal A_*^\epsilon\) acts on
the rank-four cocharacter lattice as a four-cycle.  Since
\(Q^2\equiv-1\pmod\ell\), \(Q\) has exact order four modulo
\(\ell\).  The \(Q\)-eigenspace of that four-cycle is consequently
one-dimensional.

Finally, if \(\varphi_{r_0}\) vanished on a vector in this eigenspace, its four
\(\mathcal A^*\)-translates \(r_j\) would all vanish there.  The basis
assertion would force the vector to be zero.  Thus \(\varphi_{r_0}\) is nonzero,
and hence an isomorphism, on the one-dimensional eigenspace.  Its
value is \([f_0(P)/f_0(R)]=[\mathfrak c(P)]\), completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[equivariant Kummer countermodels at the actual ordinal degree]
\label{prop:fermat-equivariant-kummer-countermodel}
Retain the elliptic curve \(\mathcal E\), its order-four automorphism
\(\mathcal A\), the five-point boundary
\(D=\mathcal E(\F_2)\), and the four functions
\[
 f_j=\mathfrak c\circ\mathcal A^{-j},
 \qquad j\in\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z,
\]
from Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-five-point-exhaustion}.  Let
\(\ell\nmid15\) be an odd prime, and let \(Y_\ell\) be the
normalization of the Kummer cover
\[
 z_j^\ell=f_j,\qquad j=0,1,2,3.                     \tag{\(\mathsf{EK}_1\)}
\]
Then \(Y_\ell/\mathcal E\) is geometrically connected of degree
\(\ell^4\), is ramified exactly over the five points of \(D\), and
has
\[
 2g(Y_\ell)-2=5\ell^4-5\ell^3.                     \tag{\(\mathsf{EK}_2\)}
\]
The order-four automorphism lifts as
\[
 \widetilde{\mathcal A}(P,z_0,z_1,z_2,z_3)
 =(\mathcal A P,z_3,z_0,z_1,z_2).                  \tag{\(\mathsf{EK}_3\)}
\]

Let \(Q\) be a power of two with \(\ell\mid Q^2+1\).  If
\[
 Q+1-2g(Y_\ell)\sqrt Q>5\ell^3,                    \tag{\(\mathsf{EK}_4\)}
\]
then there exists
\(P\in\mathcal E(\F_{Q^4})\setminus D\) such that
\[
 \Frob_Q(P)=\mathcal A(P),\qquad
 f_j(P)\in\F_{Q^4}^{\times\ell}\quad(0\leq j<4).    \tag{\(\mathsf{EK}_5\)}
\]
In particular \(\mathfrak c(P)\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.

Condition \((\mathsf{EK}_4)\) holds at an actual Conway--Fermat field
size:
\[
 Q=2^{256},\qquad
 \ell=2424833\mid F_9=2^{512}+1.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{EK}_6\)}
\]
Thus the exact field degree, elliptic transition, quarter-turn
Frobenius relation, and complete four-function Kummer orbit admit
structural failure points.  This is not a counterexample to
\(\delta_9=F_9\): the constructed point is not asserted to be the one
selected by the full Conway ancestry.  It proves that an
ancestry-insensitive CM, isogeny, generalized-Jacobian, or reciprocity
argument cannot force the required nonresidue.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The divisor vectors of the four functions are the rows of
\(2I+C\), where \(C\) is a four-cycle.  Their determinant is \(15\),
so they are independent modulo \(\ell\).  Kummer theory over the
geometric function field therefore makes \((\mathsf{EK}_1)\)
connected of degree \(\ell^4\).

The union of the supports of the four divisors is exactly \(D\).
At each of its five points the valuation vector is nonzero modulo
\(\ell\), so inertia has order \(\ell\) and there are
\(\ell^3\) geometric points above it.  Since the base curve has genus
one, Riemann--Hurwitz gives
\[
 2g(Y_\ell)-2
 =5\ell^4\left(1-\frac1\ell\right),
\]
which is \((\mathsf{EK}_2)\).  The identity
\[
 f_j(\mathcal A P)=f_{j-1}(P)
\]
proves that \((\mathsf{EK}_3)\) preserves the four Kummer equations
and has order four.

Twist \(Y_\ell\) over \(\F_Q\) by the cocycle sending arithmetic
\(Q\)-Frobenius to \(\widetilde{\mathcal A}\).  Its
\(\F_Q\)-points correspond to geometric points \(y\) satisfying
\[
 \Frob_Q(y)=\widetilde{\mathcal A}(y).
\]
Hasse--Weil and \((\mathsf{EK}_4)\) give more such points than the at
most \(5\ell^3\) points above the branch boundary.  Choose a
nonbranch point.  Four Frobenius iterations give
\(\Frob_Q^4(y)=y\), so all its coordinates lie in \(\F_{Q^4}\).
Projecting to \(\mathcal E\) and reading the four \(z_j\) proves
\((\mathsf{EK}_5)\).

Finally, \(2424833\) is a prime divisor of \(F_9\) and is smaller than
\(2^{22}\).  Its primality and the divisibility
\(2424833\mid 2^{512}+1\) are independently kernel-checked in the
companion Lean file.  Hence
\[
 g(Y_\ell)<3\ell^4<2^{90},\qquad
 2g(Y_\ell)\sqrt Q<2^{219},\qquad
 5\ell^3<2^{69},
\]
whereas \(Q=2^{256}\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{EK}_4)\) and \((\mathsf{EK}_6)\).
\end{proof}

When $F_n$ is prime, irreducibility and $\delta_n>1$ force
$\delta_n=F_n$, recovering Lenstra's Fermat-prime rule.  When $F_n$ is
composite, irreducibility forces only one nontrivial divisor: it does not
force every prime-power factor.  The condition $\delta_n=F_n$ is exactly
the maximal-coset-order hypothesis studied for Conway's quadratic tower
by Popovych~\cite{Popovych18}.  It is strictly weaker than primitivity of
$c_n$, which already fails at $n=2$ since
$\ord(c_2)=5\cdot17=85<255$; Popovych uses the all-level
hypothesis instead to prove that products such as $c_0c_n$ are primitive.
Wiedemann's related iterated quadratic tower
poses the analogous full-Fermat-order problem~\cite{Wiedemann88}, but
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-wiedemann-separation} proves that its
selected norm-one element is not Frobenius-identifiable with the
Conway element after the first levels.  Thus
the apparently easy ``singleton even implies zero'' clause already
contains a longstanding explicit maximal-coset-order problem.
Popovych verified the Conway condition only through $n=11$; the recent
analysis of Cagliero--Herman--Szechtman still retains the quotient order
as an undetermined divisor~\cite{CHS25}.  Their Euler--Lucas argument does
give the unconditional congruence
\[
 \delta_n\equiv1\pmod{2^{n+2}},\qquad
 \delta_n\geq1+2^{n+2}\quad(n\geq2),
\]
but this remains far below \(F_n\) and does not force any prescribed prime
factor of a composite \(F_n\).  In fact this congruence cannot distinguish
the selected order from any proper candidate.  For every prime
\(\ell\mid F_n\), one has \(\ord_\ell(2)=2^{n+1}\).  Since
\(\ell\equiv1\pmod8\), the element two is a square modulo \(\ell\), and
a square root of two has order \(2^{n+2}\); hence
\(\ell\equiv1\pmod{2^{n+2}}\).  It follows that every nontrivial divisor
\(d\mid F_n\) already satisfies

\[
d\equiv1\pmod{2^{n+2}},\qquad \ord_d(2)=2^{n+1}.
\]

\paragraph{Exact selected-factor screen.}
The companion script \path{experiments/fermat_selected_screen.py}
reconstructs the selected polynomial without a precomputed Conway table:
starting from \(A_{-1}(Y)=Y+1\), it iterates
\[
 A_i(X)=\operatorname{Res}_Y\bigl(A_{i-1}(Y),X^2+XY+Y^3\bigr)
\]
and evaluates \(S_{F_n/\ell}(X)\bmod A_{n-1}(X)\) by exact
binary-polynomial fast doubling.  The companion Lean file kernel-checks the
cubic Horner reduction and the multiplication-matrix determinant giving each
resultant step; the complete bit-packed iteration remains script-level.  For
a published prime divisor \(\ell\mid F_n\), the criterion above says that a
zero remainder is a genuine failure of the \(\ell\)-coordinate, whereas a
nonzero remainder certifies only that published coordinate.  The current
FermatSearch tables give the following exact screen~\cite{FermatSearch}:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{c|r|r|c|c|c}
\(n\) & \(\deg A_{n-1}\) & \(\mathrm{wt}(A_{n-1})\) & published primes
& zero residues & residual cofactor \\
\hline
12 & 4096 & 2093 & 6 & 0 & \(C_{1133}\) \\
13 & 8192 & 4119 & 4 & 0 & \(C_{2391}\) \\
14 & 16384 & 8179 & 1 & 0 & \(C_{4880}\) \\
15 & 32768 & 16369 & 3 & 0 & \(C_{9808}\) \\
16 & 65536 & 32809 & 2 & 0 & \(C_{19694}\) \\
17 & 131072 & 65521 & 2 & 0 & \(C_{39395}\) \\
18 & 262144 & 131087 & 2 & 0 & \(C_{78884}\)
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Here every displayed database factor is a proved prime occurring to the first
power, while the displayed \(C_d\) are exact cofactors proved composite but
not factored.  The script checks exact divisibility but imports primality from
that source; its degrees and hashes are reproducibility fingerprints rather
than standalone formal certificates.  The optional FLINT backend agrees
bit-for-bit with the dependency-free reducer at the orientation gate and
records the complete per-factor fingerprints in
\path{experiments/fermat_selected_screen_results.md}.  Thus no selected
counterexample occurs at any currently published prime from \(F_{12}\) through
\(F_{18}\), but none of these levels is proved maximal and the all-level problem
remains open.

There is also an exact bridge to the current primitive-quadratic
classification.  Assume \(n\geq2\) and the lower Fermat levels are maximal, so that
\(a=a_{n-1}\) has order \((q-1)/3\) in \(\F_q^\times\), where
\(q=2^{2^n}\).  Since \(v_3(q-1)=1\), the element \(c_0^2a\) is
primitive, while \(x=c_0c_n\) has irreducible polynomial
\[
 X^2+c_0X+c_0^2a.                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{VQ}\)}
\]
Vega's theorem~\cite{Vega26} makes every irreducible quadratic with
nonzero trace coefficient and primitive constant term primitive when
\(q+1\) is prime, a power of two, or twice a prime.  His converse
classification~\cite{VegaNec26} proves that these are exactly the
universal field sizes.  Here \(q+1=F_n\) is odd, so the theorem closes
precisely the already-easy Fermat-prime levels; every composite Fermat
level lies outside the universal classification.  Vega's general
coefficient recurrence specializes in \((\mathsf{VQ})\) to
\(h_i=c_0^{i-1}S_i(a)\), and therefore returns the same selected first
zero rather than bounding it.

The newer first-zero theorem of Zhu--Wu~\cite[Theorem~4.1]{ZhuWu26}
identifies the binomial and multiplicative orders of an irreducible
quadratic from its first vanishing Lucas coefficient.  Applied here, it
recovers exactly the reverse-Dickson/Lucas criterion already isolated
above; it does not prove that the first zero is \(F_n\).  Their later
primitive-coefficient criterion assumes both that the constant term is
primitive and that \(q+1=2^m\pi\) with \(m>0\) and \(\pi\) an odd prime.
Although the rescaling \((\mathsf{VQ})\) supplies the primitive constant,
\(q+1=F_n\) is odd and may be composite.  Thus that theorem does not
cover the selected composite-Fermat polynomial.

Thus the Euler--Lucas congruence is automatic from
\(1<\delta_n\mid F_n\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-kummer-tail} further
shows that the missing \(\ell\)-primary class is born at one edge and merely
propagated by \(3/2\) through every future edge, while
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-dickson-conway-resultant} proves that even the
complete compatible root tower is one torsor of parallel rays.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-weighted-selector} proves that all unmixed classical
cyclotomic principalizers have zero selector weight and that the natural
mixed principal element has two-adic support exactly when the target is
already true.  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ell-ray-coefficient} computes the
first local ramified coefficient as an ancestry-sensitive Witt polynomial
at every \'etale contracted prime, but local nontriviality does not evaluate the
selected prime above two.  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ray-character}
identifies the remaining obligation exactly as
\(\chi_W([\mathfrak q_1])\ne1\) in one mixed ray class group.
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-hasse-dihedral} equivalently asks that two not be a
global norm from the associated cyclic Kummer extension and proves that every
abelian descent of its lower dihedral closure kills the selected translation.
No all-level proof or counterexample is presently known.

\subsection{Comparison with the cubic arm}

These two embedded arms are instances of the same projective statement.  The
quadratic arm asks that the class of $c_n$ generate
$E_n^\times/E_{n-1}^\times$.  In the cubic arm the natural map
\[
 U_k\longrightarrow \F_{q_k^3}^{\times}/\F_{q_k}^{\times}
\]
is an isomorphism, and the increment from $C_{k-1}$ to $C_k$ asks that
the class of $\gamma_k$
generate this quotient.  Equivalently, the projective class of the
multiplication-by-$\gamma_k$ matrix generates a Singer cycle in
$\operatorname{PGL}_3(q_k)$.  The $0/1/4$ rule therefore contains a
quadratic and a cubic maximal-projective-order conjecture among its four exact
arms, rather than
two unrelated order coincidences.

\subsection{The singleton-even conjecture \texorpdfstring{\((Z)\)}{(Z)}}

The singleton-even part of the zero arm is exactly the assertion
$\delta_n=F_n$ for all $n$.  The results above identify the recursively
selected fibotomic factor and exhaust the trace, norm, elliptic, and
generalized-Jacobian data currently available without evaluating its
Kummer class.  Thus the remaining theorem is not another coarse
invariant: it is the nonvanishing of the explicit distinguished
Miller-unit value in Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-five-point-exhaustion}.
Equivalently, in the nontrivial Kummer case, failure is the assertion that
two is a norm from the explicit degree-\(\ell\) reflection field
\[
 M'\bigl(\theta+\theta^{-1}\bigr)/M',\qquad
 D^{\mathrm{cl}}_\ell(\theta+\theta^{-1})=W_n+W_n^{-1}.
\]
This non-Galois norm form is the sharpest lower-field target retained by the
full dihedral ancestry; its abelian quotients erase the marked Frobenius.
However, the explicit symmetrization is valid over every completion and
proves the Hasse norm principle for this reflection field.  The norm-two
equation fails locally exactly when the named prime above two has nontrivial
translation Frobenius, and has no additional global class-group obstruction.
Thus even this nonabelian descent is an exact reformulation of the selected
birth symbol, not an independent route to its nonvanishing.

\section{\texorpdfstring{The exceptional \(2\cdot3^k\) arm}{The exceptional 2 times 3-power arm}}\label{sec:exception}

\subsection{Why component data do not decide the excess}

$\Qset(f(p))$ alone does not determine $m_p$:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ccc}
\toprule
$\Qset$ & $m=4$ & $m=1$ \\
\midrule
$\{9\}$   & $p=19$,   $f=18$  & $p=73$,   $f=9$ \\
$\{81\}$  & $p=163$,  $f=162$ & $p=2593$, $f=81$ \\
$\{243\}$ & $p=1459$, $f=486$ & $p=487$,  $f=243$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The split for $\Qset=\{9\}$ is explained by the order
criterion: $\ord(\kappa_9+1)=3^3\cdot(2^9-1)=27\cdot511=13797$ and
$511=7\cdot73$, so $73\mid\ord(\kappa_9+1)$ but
$19\nmid\ord(\kappa_9+1)$; adding $m=4$ changes the order and introduces
the factor $19$.

The lower bound of the exception column is unconditional:
$m_p\geq4$ for all $p$ with $f(p)=2\cdot3^k$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:exception}).

\emph{Conjecture.} The matching upper bound $m_p\leq4$ on that column;
its exact current-level formulation is $D'_k$ in
Conjecture~\ref{conj:dk}, not the Gaussian-period conjecture $C_k$.

\subsection{The corrected norm and current-factor target}
\label{sec:m4}

The $m=4$ translate $\kappa_{3^k}+4$ involves the nimber $4\in\F_{16}$,
which does not lie in $\F_2(\zeta)$ (as $4\nmid2\cdot3^k$); the
translate therefore lies in the degree-$4\cdot3^k$ compositum
$\F_{2^{4h}}$, where $h=3^k$.

\begin{proposition}[the corrected norm]\label{prop:m4norm}
Let $\sigma=\Frob^{2h}$ generate
$\mathrm{Gal}(\F_{2^{4h}}/\F_{2^{2h}})$. Then $\sigma(4)=5$, and with
$\omega:=\zeta^{h}=\kappa_2$,
\[
  N:=\Norm_{\F_{2^{4h}}/\F_{2^{2h}}}(\kappa_{3^k}+4)
   =(\kappa+4)(\kappa+5)
   =\kappa^2+\kappa+\omega .
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
$\F_{16}\cap\F_{2^{2h}}=\F_4$ because $\gcd(4,2\cdot3^k)=2$, so
$\sigma|_{\F_{16}}$ is the nontrivial element of
$\mathrm{Gal}(\F_{16}/\F_4)$, which swaps the two roots $4,5$ of the
Artin--Schreier minimal polynomial $y^2+y+\omega$ of $4$ over $\F_4$.
(Equivalently: $2h\equiv2\pmod4$ and the Frobenius orbit of $4$ is
$4\to6\to5\to7$, so $\sigma(4)=4^{2^2}=5$.) Then
$(\kappa+4)(\kappa+5)=\kappa^2+\kappa+(4\nimmul5)$ and
$4\nimmul5=4^2+4=\omega$ by the same Artin--Schreier relation.
\end{proof}

The corrected norm collapses the $m=4$ test into the \emph{same} sparse
trinomial model $\F_2[x]/(x^{2h}+x^h+1)$ as the rest of the $3$-power
analysis: no compositum arithmetic is needed.

\begin{corollary}[in-field criterion]\label{cor:m4crit}
For a prime $p$ with $f(p)=2\cdot3^k$ and $v_p(2^{2h}-1)=1$,
write $\overline{N}=N^{2^h}$ and $M_k:=\overline{N}/N=N^{2^h-1}\in U$.
Then
\[
  m_p=4
  \iff
  p\mid\ord(M_k)
  \iff
  M_k^{(2^h+1)/p}\neq1 .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
$m_p\geq4$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:exception}, so $m_p=4$ iff
$\kappa+4$ has no $p$-th root. By Propositions~\ref{prop:power}
and~\ref{prop:norm} with $E=4h$, $f=2h$ (and
$v_p(2^{4h}-1)=v_p(2^{2h}-1)=1$ by lifting the exponent, since
$p\nmid2$), that holds iff $N^{(2^{2h}-1)/p}\neq1$. Since
$\F_{2^{2h}}^{\times}=L^{\times}\times U$ with coprime orders,
$p\mid2^h+1$, and $(2^{2h}-1)/p=(2^h-1)\cdot\bigl((2^h+1)/p\bigr)$, the
test equals $M_k^{(2^h+1)/p}\neq1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact current-level form of the exception conjecture]
\label{prop:dk-current-level}
Let $k\geq1$.  Retain the notation above, put
$q=2^h$, $n=3^{k+1}=3h$,
and write
\[
 \zeta:=\kappa_{3^k}\in K:=\F_{2^{2h}},\qquad
 \widetilde K:=\F_{2^{4h}},\qquad \beta:=\zeta+4.
\]
Thus $\zeta$ is a primitive $n$-th root of unity.  Define
\[
 N:=\Norm_{\widetilde K/K}(\beta)
   =\zeta^2+\zeta+\zeta^h,
 \qquad M_k:=N^{q-1},
 \qquad \Psi_k:=\frac{\Phi_{2\cdot3^k}(2)}3,
\]
and
\[
 R_k(Y):=\operatorname{Res}_X
 \left(X^{2h}+X^h+1,\,Y+X^h+X^2+X\right)\in\F_2[Y].
\]
Then $R_k$ is the irreducible minimal polynomial of $N$ over $\F_2$
and has degree $2h$.  The prime divisors of $\Psi_k$ are exactly the
primes $\ell\ne3$ with $\ord_\ell(2)=2\cdot3^k$, with full valuations
\[
 v_\ell(\Psi_k)=v_\ell(2^{2h}-1)=v_\ell(2^h+1).
\]
Moreover the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item $m_\ell=4$ for every prime $\ell$ with
      $f(\ell)=2\cdot3^k$;
\item $\Psi_k\mid\ord(M_k)$;
\item $N\notin(K^\times)^\ell$ for every prime $\ell\mid\Psi_k$;
\item $R_k(X^\ell)$ is irreducible over $\F_2$ for every prime
      $\ell\mid\Psi_k$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The order identity $\ord_{3^{k+1}}(2)=2h$ makes
$X^{2h}+X^h+1$ the irreducible minimal polynomial of $\zeta$.
We first prove that $N=\zeta^h+\zeta^2+\zeta$ also has degree $2h$.
Suppose $N^{2^j}=N$, and put $a\equiv2^j\pmod{3h}$.  The resulting
vanishing polynomial, of degree less than $3h$, has support equal to
the symmetric difference of
\[
 \{ah,a,2a\}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\{h,1,2\}.                \tag{1}
\]
Every multiple of $X^{2h}+X^h+1$ of degree less than $3h$ has support
a disjoint union of triples $\{r,r+h,r+2h\}$.
If $a\equiv2\pmod3$, the support in (1) contains $h$ and $2h$ but not
$0$, a contradiction.  If $a\equiv1\pmod3$, the $h$-terms cancel and
each residue class modulo $h$ occurs at most twice; a nonempty union of
such triples is again impossible.  Hence the support is empty, so
$\{a,2a\}=\{1,2\}$ modulo $3h$.  Since $h\geq3$, this forces $a=1$.
Thus the Frobenius orbit of $N$ has length $2h$, and the displayed
resultant is its irreducible minimal polynomial.

Writing $A=2^{3^{k-1}}$ gives
$\Phi_{2\cdot3^k}(2)=A^2-A+1\equiv3\pmod9$, so division by $3$
removes exactly the prime dividing the cyclotomic index.  The standard
cyclotomic-divisor criterion now says that the prime divisors of
$\Psi_k$ are precisely the $\ell$ with $\ord_\ell(2)=2h$.  Their
entire primary contribution to $2^{2h}-1$ lies in this cyclotomic
factor and, since $\ell\nmid2^h-1$, in $2^h+1$.  This proves the
valuation statement.

Fix $\ell\mid\Psi_k$.  Propositions~\ref{prop:translates}
and~\ref{prop:exception} make the translates
$\zeta,\zeta+1,\zeta+2,\zeta+3$ into $\ell$-th powers, so
$m_\ell\geq4$.  The corrected norm and the exact power criterion give
\[
 \beta^{(2^{4h}-1)/\ell}
 =N^{(2^{2h}-1)/\ell},
\]
and therefore
\[
 m_\ell=4\iff N\notin(K^\times)^\ell.                    \tag{2}
\]
This uses the full $\ell$-primary valuation and has no squarefreeness
or non-Wieferich hypothesis.  Since $q-1\equiv-2\pmod\ell$, raising
to $q-1$ is invertible on $K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell$, so
\[
 N\in(K^\times)^\ell\iff M_k\in(K^\times)^\ell.           \tag{3}
\]
The entire $\ell$-primary part of $K^\times$ lies in its norm-one
factor of order $q+1$.  In a cyclic group, nonmembership in the
subgroup of $\ell$-th powers is equivalent to the order containing the
full $\ell$-primary part.  Equations (2)--(3) prove the equivalence of
the first three assertions.

Finally $\mu_\ell\subset K$.  Kummer theory says that
$X^\ell-N$ is irreducible over $K$ exactly when
$N\notin(K^\times)^\ell$.  Since $K=\F_2(N)$, Capelli's lemma says in
turn that this is equivalent to irreducibility of $R_k(X^\ell)$ over
$\F_2$, completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[$D'_k$: the exact current exception factor]
\label{conj:dk}
\emph{Conjecture.} One has
\[
 D'_k:\qquad \Psi_k\mid\ord(M_k).
\]
By Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}, this is exactly the
$m=4$ upper bound for the primes at level $f=2\cdot3^k$.
\end{conjecture}

\subsection{Projection, fibotomic, and translated-binomial forms}

\begin{proposition}[exact current-layer projection and dominant-factor sieve]
\label{prop:dk-projection}
Let \(k\geq2\), put
\[
 s=3^{k-1},\qquad h=3s,\qquad a=2^s,\qquad q=a^3=2^h,
 \qquad \Lambda_k=a^2-a+1=3\Psi_k,
\]
and retain \(N,M_k,\zeta,\omega=\zeta^h\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}.  Define
\[
 P_k:=M_k^{a+1}.
\]
Then \(P_k^{\Lambda_k}=1\), and for every prime
\(\ell\mid\Psi_k\),
\[
 v_\ell(\ord P_k)=v_\ell(\ord M_k).                    \tag{\(\mathsf D_1\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 P_k=\frac{\zeta^4+\zeta+\omega}
           {\omega^2\zeta^4+\zeta^3+1}.               \tag{\(\mathsf D_2\)}
\]
\[
 [\F_2(P_k):\F_2]=2h.                                 \tag{\(\mathsf D_3\)}
\]
and
\[
 v_3(\ord P_k)=
 \begin{cases}1,&k\text{ odd},\\0,&k\text{ even}.
 \end{cases}                                           \tag{\(\mathsf D_4\)}
\]
Set \(c_k=3\) for odd \(k\) and \(c_k=1\) for even \(k\).  Then
\[
 \ord(P_k)\geq c_k(2h+1).                              \tag{\(\mathsf D_5\)}
\]
Consequently, if \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\) and
\[
 \ell>\frac{\Lambda_k}{c_k(2h+1)},                    \tag{\(\mathsf D_6\)}
\]
then \(P_k\), and hence \(M_k\), contains the full
\(\ell\)-primary part of \(\Lambda_k\) in its order.  In particular
\(D'_k\) follows whenever \(\Psi_k\) is prime; this proves \(D'_2\)
and \(D'_3\) analytically, since \(\Psi_2=19\) and
\(\Psi_3=87211\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The factorization
\[
 q+1=(a+1)(a^2-a+1)=(a+1)\Lambda_k
\]
and \(M_k^{q+1}=1\) give \(P_k^{\Lambda_k}=1\).  Also
\(\gcd(\Lambda_k,a+1)=3\).  Since \(k\geq2\), one has
\(a\equiv-1\pmod9\), so
\(\Lambda_k\equiv3\pmod9\) and
\(\gcd(\Psi_k,a+1)=1\).  Exponentiation by \(a+1\) therefore preserves
all the primary valuations belonging to \(\Psi_k\), proving
\((\mathsf D_1)\).

The lifted congruence
\[
 2^{3^{k-1}}\equiv3^k-1\pmod{3^{k+1}}
\]
gives, modulo \(3h\),
\[
 a\equiv h-1,\quad a^2\equiv h+1,\quad
 a^3\equiv-1,\quad a^4\equiv2h+1.                     \tag{\(\mathsf D_7\)}
\]
As \(M_k=N^{a^3-1}\),
\[
 P_k=\frac{N^{a^4}N^{a^3}}{N^aN}.
\]
Using \(N=\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega\) and \((\mathsf D_7)\), direct Frobenius
expansion gives
\begin{align*}
 N^a&=\omega^2\zeta^{-2}+\omega\zeta^{-1}+\omega^2,\\
 N^{a^3}&=\zeta^{-2}+\zeta^{-1}+\omega^2,\\
 N^{a^4}&=\omega\zeta^2+\omega^2\zeta+\omega.
\end{align*}
Multiplying \(N^{a^3}\) and \(N^a\) by the same cancelling factor
\(\zeta^2\), and using \(1+\omega+\omega^2=0\), yields
\begin{align*}
 N^{a^4}(\zeta^2N^{a^3})&=\zeta^4+\zeta+\omega,\\
 (\zeta^2N^a)N&=\omega^2\zeta^4+\zeta^3+1,
\end{align*}
which proves \((\mathsf D_2)\).

The minimal polynomial of \(\zeta\) over \(\F_2\) is
\(X^{2h}+X^h+1\).  Write the numerator and denominator in
\((\mathsf D_2)\) as
\(C,D\).  Since \(P_k^q=P_k^{-1}\), membership in \(\F_q\) would force
\(P_k=1\).  But \(C=D\), after reducing
\(\zeta^{2h}=\zeta^h+1\), would give
\[
 \zeta^{h+4}+\zeta^h+\zeta^3+\zeta+1=0,               \tag{\(\mathsf D_8\)}
\]
a nonzero polynomial of degree \(h+4<2h\).

If \(P_k\in\F_{a^2}\), its order divides
\(\gcd(\Lambda_k,a^2-1)=3\), so
\(P_k\in\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}\).  The first case was excluded; the
other two respectively give
\[
 \zeta^{h+3}+\zeta=0
\]
and
\[
 \zeta^{h+4}+\zeta^{h+3}+\zeta^4+\zeta^3+\zeta+1=0.
\]
Both are again nonzero polynomials of degree below \(2h\).  Every proper
divisor of \(2h=2\cdot3^k\) divides either \(h\) or \(2h/3\); hence every
proper subfield of \(\F_{2^{2h}}\) lies in either \(\F_q\) or
\(\F_{a^2}\).  This proves \((\mathsf D_3)\).

For the \(3\)-part, observe that
\[
 P_k^{\Lambda_k/3}
 =M_k^{(q+1)/3}
 =N^{(q^2-1)/3}
 =\Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_4}(N).                         \tag{\(\mathsf D_9\)}
\]
Choose \(u\in\F_{16}\) with \(u^2+u=\omega\).  Then
\(u^4=u+1\), \(u^5=\omega\), and \(u\) has order \(15\).  The
extensions \(\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_4\) and \(\F_{16}/\F_4\) are linearly
disjoint, and over \(\F_{16}\) the minimal polynomial of \(\zeta\)
is \(X^h+\omega\), irreducible because
\(\ord_{3h}(16)=h\).  Since
\[
 N=(\zeta+u)(\zeta+u+1),
\]
base change in \((\mathsf D_9)\) gives
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_4}(N)
 =(u^h+\omega)((u+1)^h+\omega).
\]
The residues of \(h=3^k\) modulo \(15\) are \(3,9,12,6\) as
\(k\equiv1,2,3,0\pmod4\); reduction with \(u^4=u+1\) gives
\[
 P_k^{\Lambda_k/3}=
 \begin{cases}\omega^2,&k\text{ odd},\\1,&k\text{ even}.
 \end{cases}
\]
Because \(v_3(\Lambda_k)=1\), this proves \((\mathsf D_4)\).

Let \(e=\ord(P_k)\).  From \((\mathsf D_3)\),
\(\ord_e(2)=2h\).  Since
\(e\mid\Lambda_k\) and \(e\notin\{1,3\}\), some prime
\(r\mid\Psi_k\) divides \(e\); every such \(r\) has
\(\ord_r(2)=2h\), so \(r\equiv1\pmod{2h}\) and
\(r\geq2h+1\).  When \(k\) is odd, \((\mathsf D_4)\) also gives
\(3r\mid e\), which proves \((\mathsf D_5)\).

If the full \(\ell\)-primary part of \(\Lambda_k\) were missing from
\(e\), then \(e\mid\Lambda_k/\ell\).  This contradicts
\((\mathsf D_5)\) under \((\mathsf D_6)\).
Equation \((\mathsf D_1)\) transfers the conclusion back to \(M_k\), and the
last assertions follow.
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[fibotomic form of the current power-residue test]
\label{prop:dk-fibotomic}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection}, put
\[
 U=\{x\in\F_{q^2}^{\times}:x^{q+1}=1\},\qquad
 \pi(x)=\frac{x}{(x+1)^2}=\frac1{x+x^{-1}},
\]
and set \(Z=\pi(M_k)\).  The map \(\pi\) is two-to-one from
\(U\setminus\{1\}\) onto
\[
 \mathcal T_q=\{z\in\F_q:
   \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(z)=1\},
\]
with fibres \(\{x,x^{-1}\}\).

For an odd prime \(\ell\), define
\[
 H_\ell(W)=\sum_{j=0}^{(\ell-1)/2}
   \binom{\ell-j-1}{j}W^j\in\F_2[W].
\]
If \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), then
\[
 M_k\in U^\ell
 \iff
 \text{there is }W\in\mathcal T_q\text{ with }
 W^\ell+Z H_\ell(W)^2=0.                               \tag{\(\mathsf F\)}
\]
The polynomial
\[
 \mathcal F_{\ell,Z}(W)=W^\ell+ZH_\ell(W)^2
\]
either has no root in \(\F_q\), or splits there into \(\ell\)
distinct linear factors.  Finally \(H_\ell\) is squarefree and is a
product of exactly \((\ell-1)/(2h)\) irreducible polynomials over
\(\F_2\), each of degree \(h\).  Thus \(D'_k\) is equivalently the
assertion that \(\mathcal F_{\ell,Z}\) is rootless over \(\F_q\) for
every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(x\in U\), one has \(x^q=x^{-1}\), so \(\pi(x)\in\F_q\).
The equation \(\pi(x)=z\) is
\[
 X^2+z^{-1}X+1=0.
\]
The characteristic-two quadratic criterion says that this polynomial
is irreducible over \(\F_q\) exactly when
\(\operatorname{Tr}(z)=1\).  Its roots are inverse and have norm one.
Counting, or the same quadratic argument in reverse, proves the stated
two-to-one description.

The defining polynomial identity is
\[
 \frac{X^\ell+1}{(X+1)^\ell}
 =H_\ell\!\left(\frac{X}{(X+1)^2}\right).              \tag{\(\mathsf H\)}
\]
If \(M_k=T^\ell\) with \(T\in U\), put \(W=\pi(T)\).  Identity
\((\mathsf H)\) gives
\[
 Z=\pi(T^\ell)=\frac{W^\ell}{H_\ell(W)^2},
\]
so \(W\) satisfies \((\mathsf F)\).  Conversely, lift a trace-one
solution \(W\) to \(T\in U\).  Then \((\mathsf H)\) gives
\(\pi(T^\ell)=\pi(M_k)\), hence \(T^\ell=M_k\) or \(M_k^{-1}\).
Replacing \(T\) by \(T^{-1}\) in the second case proves
\(M_k\in U^\ell\).

In fact every \(\F_q\)-root of \(\mathcal F_{\ell,Z}\) has trace one.
Indeed, \(H_\ell(W)\ne0\) at a root, since otherwise the equation
would force \(W=0\), whereas \(H_\ell(0)=1\).  Lift a nonzero root
through \(\pi(T)=W\).  If \(\operatorname{Tr}(W)=0\), the quadratic
criterion puts \(T\) in \(\F_q\).  Identity \((\mathsf H)\) then gives
\(\pi(T^\ell)=Z\), but for \(y\in\F_q\setminus\{1\}\),
\[
 \pi(y)=\left(\frac{y}{y+1}\right)^2+\frac{y}{y+1}
\]
has absolute trace zero, whereas \(Z=\pi(M_k)\) has trace one.  This
is a contradiction.

If one root exists, therefore lift it to \(T\in U\) and orient \(T\)
so that \(T^\ell=M_k\).  Then the
\(\ell\) elements
\[
 \pi(T\xi),\qquad \xi\in\mu_\ell,
\]
are roots in \(\F_q\).  They are distinct: an inverse collision would
give \(T^2\in\mu_\ell\), hence \(M_k^2=1\), whereas \(M_k\ne1\)
because \(N\notin\F_q\).  Since \(\mathcal F_{\ell,Z}\) has degree
\(\ell\), it splits completely.  Its derivative is \(W^{\ell-1}\),
and trace-one roots are nonzero, so they are simple.

Finally, the roots of \(H_\ell\) are
\[
 \pi(\xi),\qquad \xi\in\mu_\ell\setminus\{1\},
\]
with \(\xi\) and \(\xi^{-1}\) identified.  Their Frobenius-orbit
length is the least \(d>0\) with \(2^d\equiv\pm1\pmod\ell\).  Since
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), this least \(d\) is \(h\).  The number and degrees
of the factors follow, and the roots are distinct, proving
squarefreeness.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Conway \(C\)-to-\(D\) selector bridge]
\label{prop:cd-selector-bridge}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic}; in particular \(k\geq2\).  Put
\[
 h=3^k,\qquad t=2^{h/3},\qquad q=2^h=t^3,
\]
and write
\[
 \gamma=\gamma_k=\zeta+\zeta^{-1},\qquad
 g_k=\gamma^2+\gamma+1.
\]
For the corrected exceptional norm
\(N=\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega\), with
\(\overline N=N^q\), one has
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}(N)=g_k,
 \qquad
 \Norm_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}(N)=g_k^{t^2}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 N^2+g_kN+g_k^{t^2}=0.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_2\)}
\]
The auxiliary periods are norm coherent:
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(g_k)=g_{k-1},
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(g_k)=1.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_3\)}
\]
In particular
\[
 \F_2(g_k)=\F_2(\gamma_k)=\F_q,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(g_k)=1.                \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_4\)}
\]

The coefficient \(Z=\pi(M_k)\) in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic} is therefore the explicit
ancestral power
\[
 \boxed{Z=g_k^{t^2-2}.}                              \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_5\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \gcd(t^2-2,t^3-1)=1,                                \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_6\)}
\]
so \(Z\) and \(g_k\) have the same multiplicative order and generate
the same field over \(\F_2\).  If
\[
 w=\frac N{g_k},
\]
then
\[
 w^2+w+Z=0,
 \qquad
 \overline w=w+1,
 \qquad
 M_k=\frac{w+1}{w}=w^{q-1}.                          \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_7\)}
\]
For every current prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), failure at \(\ell\) is
equivalent to each of
\[
 M_k\in U^\ell,\qquad
 w\in(\F_{q^2}^{\times})^\ell,\qquad
 \mathcal F_{\ell,g_k^{t^2-2}}(W)
   \text{ having a root in }\F_q.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_8\)}
\]
The two roots \(w,w+1=w^q\) have the same \(\ell\)-power status, while
their Kummer classes are opposite.  Thus the remaining datum is the
unordered opposite pair \(\{[w],-[w]\}\), equivalently the norm-one
phase \(\{M_k,M_k^{-1}\}\); it is not a choice of orientation between
the two roots.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The \(q\)-Frobenius sends \(\zeta\) to \(\zeta^{-1}\) and
\(\omega\) to \(\omega^2\).  Hence
\[
 N+\overline N
 =\zeta^2+\zeta^{-2}+\zeta+\zeta^{-1}+\omega+\omega^2
 =\gamma^2+\gamma+1=g_k.
\]
The relation \(\zeta^t=\omega\zeta^{-1}\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}, together with direct expansion, gives
\[
 N\overline N
 =1+\gamma+\gamma^t+(\gamma^t+\gamma)^2.
\]
The three conjugates of \(\gamma_k\) over the preceding cubic field
sum to zero, so
\(\gamma+\gamma^t+\gamma^{t^2}=0\).  Therefore
\[
 N\overline N
 =1+\gamma^{t^2}+\gamma^{2t^2}=g_k^{t^2},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{CD}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{CD}_2)\).

Let \(x_1,x_2,x_3\) be the roots of
\(P(X)=X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}\).  Since
\(X^2+X+1=(X+\omega)(X+\omega^2)\),
\[
 \begin{split}
 \prod_{i=1}^3(x_i^2+x_i+1)
 &=P(\omega)P(\omega^2)\\
 &=(\gamma_{k-1}+\omega^2)
   (\gamma_{k-1}+\omega)
 =g_{k-1}.
 \end{split}
\]
This is the norm identity in \((\mathsf{CD}_3)\).  The relative trace
is
\[
 \sum_i(x_i^2+x_i+1)
 =\left(\sum_i x_i\right)^2+\sum_i x_i+1=1.
\]
The element \(\gamma\) is at most quadratic over \(\F_2(g_k)\), while
\([\F_2(\gamma):\F_2]=h\) is odd.  Hence that possible quadratic
index is one, proving the field equality in \((\mathsf{CD}_4)\).
Transitivity applied to the relative trace one proves the absolute
trace assertion.

For \(M_k=\overline N/N\), elementary cancellation gives
\[
 \pi(M_k)
 =\frac{\overline N/N}{(\overline N/N+1)^2}
 =\frac{N\overline N}{(N+\overline N)^2}
 =g_k^{t^2-2},
\]
proving \((\mathsf{CD}_5)\).  If an integer divides both
\(t^2-2\) and \(t^3-1\), then it divides \(2t-1\), and hence divides
seven.  Since \(k\geq2\), one has \(t\equiv1\pmod7\), and therefore
seven does not divide \(t^2-2\).
This proves \((\mathsf{CD}_6)\), and exponentiation by \(t^2-2\) is
therefore an automorphism of the cyclic group \(\F_q^\times\).

Dividing \((\mathsf{CD}_2)\) by \(g_k^2\) gives the first identity in
\((\mathsf{CD}_7)\); the remaining two follow from relative trace one.
For a current prime, \(\ell\mid q+1\) and \(\ell\nmid q-1\).
The direct product \(\F_{q^2}^\times=\F_q^\times\times U\) gives
\[
 U^\ell=U\cap(\F_{q^2}^\times)^\ell,
\]
because \(\ell\)-powering is an automorphism of \(\F_q^\times\).
Multiplication by \(q-1\) is consequently invertible on the
one-dimensional \(\ell\)-power quotient of \(\F_{q^2}^\times\), so
\[
 w^{q-1}\in U^\ell
 \iff w\in(\F_{q^2}^\times)^\ell.
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic} supplies the final equivalence in
\((\mathsf{CD}_8)\).  Frobenius preserves \(\ell\)-th powers and
sends \(w\) to \(w+1\), proving the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[what the selector bridge does not transfer]
The bridge transports the complete selected \(D\)-coefficient through
the recursively selected \(C\)-period and the norm-coherent tower
\((g_k)\), but it is not an implication \(C_k\Rightarrow D'_k\).
The current primes in the two arms satisfy respectively
\(\ord_\ell(2)=h\) and \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), so they are disjoint.
At a \(D\)-current prime every element of \(\F_q^\times\), including
\(g_k\) and \(Z\), is automatically an \(\ell\)-th power.  The missing
class lives in the selected quadratic root \(w\), not in its base-field
trace or norm.  There is no known uniform exponent, coprime to \(q-1\),
which expresses \(g_k=\gamma_k^2+\gamma_k+1\) as a power of
\(\gamma_k\); the map \(x\mapsto x^2+x+1\) need not preserve
multiplicative order.  Thus \(C\)-primitivity supplies no order transfer
to \(g_k\).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[actual-selector primitivity still does not transfer]
\label{prop:dk-actual-selector-countermodel}
There is an exact finite-field model which retains a primitive degree-nine
scalar, the literal rational selector of Proposition~\ref{prop:cd-selector-bridge},
and the trace-one Artin--Schreier coupling, but fails the current
\(D\)-condition at \(19\).  Namely, in
\[
 E=\F_2[z]/(z^{18}+z^9+1)=\F_{2^{18}},
\]
put
\[
 \gamma=(z+z^{-1})^{25},\qquad
 g=\gamma^2+\gamma+1,\qquad
 Z=g^{62}.
\]
Then \(\gamma\in\F_{2^9}\) has order \(511\).  Nevertheless, with
\[
 M=z^{10},\qquad w=(z^{10}+1)^{-1},
\]
one has
\[
 Z=\frac{M}{(M+1)^2}=w^2+w,\qquad
 w^{512}=w+1,\qquad
 \frac{w+1}{w}=M,\qquad \ord(M)=27,               \tag{\(\mathsf{ACD}_1\)}
\]
and \(w\) is a nineteenth power in \(E^\times\).  Thus primitivity of
\(\gamma\), even after applying the exact selected rational map and
retaining the correct trace-one quadratic root, does not force the
new \(19\)-primary coordinate.

This is not a counterexample to the selected implication
\(C_2\Rightarrow D'_2\).  Its lower norm is
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^9}/\F_{2^3}}(g)=\mathtt{0x8048},
\]
whereas the recursively selected lower coefficient is
\(\mathtt{0x9001}\).  Complete cubic ancestry removes precisely the
freedom used by the model: Frobenius conjugation preserves both the
Artin--Schreier equation and nineteenth-power status.  Hence any proof of
the actual implication must use the selected norm ancestry, not merely
primitivity, the rational formula, degree, trace, or the quadratic root.

The exact degree-nine census makes this boundary quantitative.  Among the
\(432\) primitive values of \(\gamma\), \(216\) give a trace-one selector and
exactly \(9\) of those fail at \(19\); the failures form one absolute
Frobenius orbit.  Imposing the selected lower norm leaves \(60\) primitive
values and no failures.  Imposing in addition the selected lower trace leaves
\(9\) values and still no failures.  This is finite evidence at \(k=2\), not
a proof that norm ancestry forces success at every level.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In the polynomial basis \(1,z,\ldots,z^{17}\), exact binary arithmetic
gives
\[
 \gamma=\mathtt{0x14831},\quad
 g=\mathtt{0x16d03},\quad
 Z=\mathtt{0x279cf},\quad
 w=\mathtt{0x1b7b6}.
\]
The checks
\[
 \gamma^{73}=\mathtt{0x9000}\ne1,
 \qquad \gamma^7=\mathtt{0x154b}\ne1
\]
prove \(\ord(\gamma)=511=7\cdot73\).  Direct multiplication gives
\((\mathsf{ACD}_1)\), and
\(y=\mathtt{0x203ba}\) satisfies \(y^{19}=w\).
The same exact calculation gives
\(g^{73}=\mathtt{0x8048}\ne\mathtt{0x9001}\).  Exhausting all primitive
exponents gives the count triples/pairs
\[
 (432,216,9),\qquad (60,0),\qquad (9,0),
\]
for respectively primitive/trace-one/failing values, selected-norm
fibre/failures, and selected-norm-and-trace fibre/failures.  The failing
exponents are
\[
 25,50,67,100,134,200,268,289,400,
\]
one doubling orbit modulo \(511\).
These identities are independently rerun by the maintained C/D experiment.
Lean kernel-checks the
generic fact that a ring map preserves simultaneously an
Artin--Schreier equation and a chosen \(\ell\)-th root; the finite
certificate and its selected-norm mismatch remain script-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[selected trace and norm still do not transfer]
\label{prop:dk-selected-fibre-countermodel}
There is an exact \(k=4\) finite-field model which retains
\(C\)-primitivity, the literal rational selector of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cd-selector-bridge}, and the selected lower trace and
norm, but fails the current \(D\)-condition at \(\ell=163\).

Put \(t=2^{27}\), \(q=t^3=2^{81}\), choose a primitive
\(243\)-rd root \(z\in\F_{q^2}\), and define
\[
 \gamma_4=z+z^{-1},\qquad
 a=\gamma_4^3+\gamma_4=\gamma_3,
 \qquad C=a^2+a+1=g_3.
\]
There is an element \(b\in\F_t\) for which
\[
 K=\F_t[X]/(X^3+X^2+bX+C)\cong\F_q
\]
is a field.  Writing \(g=X\bmod(X^3+X^2+bX+C)\), one can choose
\(\gamma\in K\) such that
\[
 \gamma^2+\gamma+1=g,\qquad \ord(\gamma)=q-1.       \tag{\(\mathsf{STN}_1\)}
\]
Moreover, with
\[
 Z=g^{t^2-2},\qquad L=K[w]/(w^2+w+Z),
\]
one has
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K/\F_t}(g)=1,\qquad
 \Norm_{K/\F_t}(g)=C,\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K/\F_2}(Z)=1,\qquad
 w^{(q^2-1)/163}=1.                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{STN}_2\)}
\]
Thus the selected Artin--Schreier root \(w\) is a \(163\)-rd power even though its
\(C\)-antecedent is primitive and both selected lower coordinates in
\((\mathsf{STN}_2)\) agree with the Conway tower.

The missing ancestry coordinate is exactly
\[
 e_2(g,g^t,g^{t^2})=b,\qquad b\ne a+1.              \tag{\(\mathsf{STN}_3\)}
\]
For the actual selected cubic, the three elementary symmetric coordinates
are \((1,a+1,a^2+a+1)\).  Hence this is not a counterexample to
\(C_4\Rightarrow D'_4\): it proves that trace and norm, even together with
primitivity and the literal rational selector, do not suffice; the middle
coefficient is the remaining full-ancestry datum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The maintained exact verifier
\path{experiments/cyclotomic_3k_family.py} uses the ambient polynomial
basis \(\F_2[z]/(z^{162}+z^{81}+1)\).  Its fixed coefficient is
\[
 b=\mathtt{0x8240041049041000209009000249201249000241}.
\]
It checks \(g^{t^3}=g\), while \(g^t\ne g\) and
\(g^{t^2}\ne g\), certifying irreducibility of the cubic; it then checks
the three symmetric identities in \((\mathsf{STN}_2)\) and
\((\mathsf{STN}_3)\).  The factorization
\[
 q-1=7\cdot73\cdot2593\cdot71119\cdot262657\cdot97685839
\]
and one nonidentity power for each prime factor certify
\(\ord(\gamma)=q-1\).  Absolute trace one makes the quadratic for \(w\)
irreducible, and the final displayed power certifies that \(w\) is a
\(163\)-rd power.  The concatenated fixed witness has SHA--256 fingerprint
\[
 \mathtt{6d3366ea5cc7a3a0b42a1f0cb4a48adf19eef95892b3a21c32786e88cf7424b0}.
\]

Geometrically, after splitting the relative cubic, the trace--norm fibre is
the plane cubic
\[
 xy(1+x+y)=C.
\]
For the selected \(C\ne0,1\) it is smooth of genus one.  The function
\(e_2\) is nonconstant on this fibre, and exact Conway ancestry cuts it by
the additional equation \(e_2=a+1\), leaving the selected Frobenius orbit.
Consequently point counts or character sums on the whole genus-one fibre can
prove generic intersections but cannot evaluate that fixed degree-three
divisor.

Lean kernel-checks the symmetric identities forcing trace one, middle
coefficient \(a+1\), and norm \(a^2+a+1\) from the selected cubic ancestry.
The explicit finite-field witness and its primitivity and power tests remain
script-level exact computation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one selected cubic step still does not transfer]
\label{prop:dk-one-step-countermodel}
Even the complete one-step cubic coefficient, together with primitivity on
both sides, does not imply the exceptional \(D\)-condition without the
full lower Conway ancestry.

Put \(t=2^{27}\), \(q=t^3=2^{81}\), and use the same cyclotomic ambient
field \(\F_2[z]/(z^{162}+z^{81}+1)\) as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-fibre-countermodel}.  There is a primitive
element
\[
 a=\mathtt{0x9209240041201241041008008040048008040040}
 \in\F_t
\]
such that \(X^3+X+a\) is irreducible over \(\F_t\).  In
\[
 K=\F_t[X]/(X^3+X+a)
\]
put \(\gamma=X\).  Then both \(a\) and \(\gamma\) are primitive in their
respective multiplicative groups and
\[
 \boxed{\ \gamma^3+\gamma=a.\ }                      \tag{\(\mathsf{OSC}_1\)}
\]
For \(g=\gamma^2+\gamma+1=(1,1,1)\) in the basis
\((1,\gamma,\gamma^2)\), its three relative conjugates satisfy the complete
selected symmetric fingerprint
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K/\F_t}(g)=1,\qquad
 e_2(g,g^t,g^{t^2})=a+1,\qquad
 \Norm_{K/\F_t}(g)=a^2+a+1.                          \tag{\(\mathsf{OSC}_2\)}
\]
Nevertheless, with \(Z=g^{t^2-2}\) and
\[
 L=K[w]/(w^2+w+Z),
\]
one has
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K/\F_2}(Z)=1,\qquad
 w^{(q^2-1)/163}=1.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{OSC}_3\)}
\]
Thus a primitive lower ancestor, a primitive selected cubic child, the
literal rational \(D\)-selector, and all three exact one-step coefficients
remain compatible with \(163\)-failure.

This is not a counterexample to \(C_4\Rightarrow D'_4\).  The element \(a\)
is not the Conway-selected \(\gamma_3\); it does not continue the recursive
ancestry below this step.  Conversely, for a fixed actual lower
\(a=\gamma_{k-1}\), the three equations in
\((\mathsf{OSC}_2)\) give the single cubic
\[
 T^3+T^2+(a+1)T+(a^2+a+1),                           \tag{\(\mathsf{OSC}_4\)}
\]
so the slice consists only of the selected Frobenius orbit.  Any
division-polynomial, Jacobi, or character evaluation on that zero-dimensional
slice is therefore exactly the original \(D\)-evaluation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The maintained verifier
\path{experiments/cyclotomic_3k_family.py} checks the fixed witness.  The
factorizations
\[
 t-1=7\cdot73\cdot262657,
\]
\[
 q-1=7\cdot73\cdot2593\cdot71119\cdot262657\cdot97685839
\]
and the corresponding nonidentity powers certify primitivity of \(a\) and
\(\gamma\).  The equalities \(\gamma^q=\gamma\) and \(\gamma^t\ne\gamma\)
certify irreducibility of \(X^3+X+a\), while
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{K/\F_2}(Z)=1\) makes the Artin--Schreier quadratic
irreducible.  It checks \((\mathsf{OSC}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{OSC}_3)\)
directly; the fixed witness has SHA--256 fingerprint
\[
 \mathtt{5c5b45506d7f0b94c9b58727bdc1d38112f9f3c8f9f61dc5000d17724360e032}.
\]
The generic symmetric calculations giving \((\mathsf{OSC}_2)\) are already
kernel-checked in \texttt{Ogdoad.Excess}.  Lean now also checks that
\(x^2+x+1\) has only the pair \(x,x+1\) in each fibre and that the three
selected symmetric coordinates expand to \((\mathsf{OSC}_4)\).  The fixed
finite-field primitivity and \(163\)-power certificates remain exact
script-level computation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\(C\)-tower-faithful exceptional countermodel]
\label{prop:dk-tower-countermodel}
Let \(k\geq1\), \(h=3^k\), \(q=2^h\), and retain the compatible
selected Conway roots \(\zeta_j=\kappa_{3^j}\), so
\(\zeta_{j-1}=\zeta_j^3\).  Thus
\(\zeta_k\in\F_{q^2}\) has order \(3h\) and
\(\zeta_k^q=\zeta_k^{-1}\).  Put
\[
 \gamma_k=\zeta_k+\zeta_k^{-1},\qquad
 Z_k^\circ=\gamma_k^{-1}
   =\frac{\zeta_k}{(\zeta_k+1)^2},\qquad
 w_k^\circ=\frac1{\zeta_k+1}.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_1\)}
\]
Then
\[
 (w_k^\circ)^2+w_k^\circ=Z_k^\circ,\qquad
 (w_k^\circ)^q=w_k^\circ+1,\qquad
 \frac{w_k^\circ+1}{w_k^\circ}
 =(w_k^\circ)^{q-1}=\zeta_k.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_2\)}
\]
The coefficient \(Z_k^\circ\in\F_q\) has exact degree \(h\) over
\(\F_2\), satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(Z_k^\circ)=1,         \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_3\)}
\]
and, for \(k\geq2\), is norm coherent:
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(Z_k^\circ)
 =Z_{k-1}^\circ.                                     \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_4\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \ord(Z_k^\circ)=\ord(\gamma_k),                     \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_5\)}
\]
so \(C_k\) makes \(Z_k^\circ\) primitive in \(\F_q^\times\).

Now let \(k\geq2\), let \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), and put
\(a=v_\ell(q+1)=v_\ell(\Psi_k)\).  Then this model misses the entire
current primary factor:
\[
 \zeta_k\in U^{\ell^a},\qquad
 w_k^\circ\in(\F_{q^2}^\times)^{\ell^a},\qquad
 v_\ell\!\left(
   \ord\frac{w_k^\circ+1}{w_k^\circ}
 \right)=0.                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_6\)}
\]
This is not a counterexample to \(D'_k\): its coefficient is
\(Z_k^\circ=\gamma_k^{-1}\), whereas the actual Conway coefficient is
\[
 Z_{D,k}=g_k^{t^2-2},\qquad
 g_k=\gamma_k^2+\gamma_k+1,\qquad t=2^{h/3}.
\]
It proves that trace one, exact degree, norm-coherent actual
\(C\)-period ancestry, and even \(C_k\)-primitivity of an
Artin--Schreier constant give no direct current-primary order transfer.
It does not rule out an implication \(C_k\Rightarrow D'_k\) for the
actual selector; any such implication must use the special rational
function selecting \(Z_{D,k}\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since
\((\zeta_k+1)^2=\zeta_k(\zeta_k+\zeta_k^{-1})\),
the identities in \((\mathsf{TC}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{TC}_2)\) are direct.
The element \(\zeta_k\) has irreducible quadratic
\[
 X^2+\gamma_kX+1
\]
over \(\F_q\).  Dividing by \(\gamma_k^2\) gives an
Artin--Schreier equation with coefficient \(\gamma_k^{-2}\), so that
coefficient has absolute trace one.  Absolute trace is invariant under
squaring, hence \(\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma_k^{-1})=1\).  The field
\(\F_2(\gamma_k)\) has degree \(h\), and inversion preserves it, which
proves the degree assertion.  Proposition~\ref{prop:tower} gives
\(\Norm(\gamma_k)=\gamma_{k-1}\); inversion proves
\((\mathsf{TC}_4)\).  Equation \((\mathsf{TC}_5)\) is immediate.

For a current prime, \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), so
\(\ell\nmid3h\).  The element \(\zeta_k\), whose order is \(3h\), is
therefore an \(\ell^a\)-th power in the norm-one group \(U\).
Also \(\ell\nmid q-1\).  In the direct product
\[
 \F_{q^2}^\times=\F_q^\times\times U,
\]
\(\ell^a\)-powering is an automorphism on the first factor, while
raising the second factor to \(q-1\) is invertible on its
\(\ell\)-primary power quotient.  The last identity in
\((\mathsf{TC}_2)\) therefore gives
\(w_k^\circ\in(\F_{q^2}^\times)^{\ell^a}\), and the order assertion
follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the actual exceptional root's lower norm]
\label{prop:dk-normalized-root-norm}
Retain \(k\geq2\), \(h=3^k\), \(t=2^{h/3}\), and the actual normalized
root
\[
 w_k=\frac{N_k}{g_k},\qquad
 N_k=\zeta_k^2+\zeta_k+\omega
\]
from Proposition~\ref{prop:cd-selector-bridge}.  Put
\(\eta=\zeta_k^3\).  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_{2^{2h/3}}}(w_k)
 =\frac{\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1}{g_{k-1}}
 =\frac{\eta^2}{\eta^2+\omega\eta+1}.}                \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_1\)}
\]
For every current prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), even this exact lower norm
is automatically an \(\ell^{v_\ell(\Psi_k)}\)-th power in
\(\F_{2^{2h/3}}^\times\).  It therefore carries no current Kummer
coordinate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:twisted}'s twisted norm formula for \(N_k\),
divided by the norm-coherence formula for \(g_k\), gives the first expression in
\((\mathsf{TN}_1)\).  In characteristic two,
\[
 g_{k-1}
 =\eta^{-2}(\eta^4+\eta^3+\eta^2+\eta+1)
 =\eta^{-2}
   (\eta^2+\omega\eta+1)
   (\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1),
\]
which gives the second.  Finally
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), so
\(\ell\nmid 2^{2h/3}-1\).  Powering by the full current
\(\ell\)-primary factor is therefore bijective in the lower
multiplicative group.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[coprime torus projections of the exceptional root]
\label{prop:dk-coprime-projections}
Retain \(k\geq2\), and put
\[
 t=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad Q=t^2,\qquad
 A=Q+t+1,\qquad B=Q-t+1=3\Psi_k,
\]
\[
 \Gamma=AB=Q^2+Q+1,\qquad
 K=\F_{t^6},\qquad E=\F_{t^2}.
\]
For the actual normalized root \(w=w_k\), set
\[
 H=\Norm_{K/E}(w),\qquad R=w^{Q-1}.
\]
Then
\[
 R\in\ker\Norm_{K/E}=\mu_\Gamma,\qquad
 \gcd(A,B)=1,                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_1\)}
\]
and its two coprime projections are exactly
\[
 \boxed{R^A=P_k=M_k^{t+1},}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_2\)}
\]
\[
 \boxed{R^B=Z_{D,k}^{\,t-1}
       =g_k^{(Q-2)(t-1)}.}                          \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_3\)}
\]
Thus \(P_k\) is literally the minus-torus coordinate of the relative
coboundary \(R\); the complementary plus coordinate is the
\(g_k\)-coboundary.

For every \(\ell^a\Vert\Psi_k\),
\[
 v_\ell(\ord w)=v_\ell(\ord R)=v_\ell(\ord P_k),\qquad
 \ell\nmid\ord(R^B),                                \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_4\)}
\]
and the exact full-primary criterion is
\[
 v_\ell(\ord P_k)=a
 \iff P_k^{B/\ell}\ne1
 \iff w\notin(K^\times)^\ell.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_5\)}
\]
There is no squarefreeness assumption here.

The lower norm has the exact compatibility
\[
 \boxed{P_k^{B/3}=H^{(Q-1)/3}.}                     \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_6\)}
\]
By \((\mathsf D_9)\), this common value is \(\omega^2\) for odd \(k\)
and one for even \(k\).  Hence the lower norm determines precisely
the already-known \(3\)-component, and no current \(\ell\)-component.

More generally, the map
\[
 \Theta:K^\times\longrightarrow E^\times\times\mu_\Gamma,
 \qquad x\longmapsto
 \bigl(\Norm_{K/E}(x),x^{Q-1}\bigr)
                                                              \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_7\)}
\]
has kernel \(\mu_3\), and its image is exactly
\[
 \left\{(H_0,R_0):
 H_0^{(Q-1)/3}=R_0^{\Gamma/3}\right\}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_8\)}
\]
Consequently, after fixing \(H\) and \(R^B\), multiplication of \(R\)
by an arbitrary current Sylow element of \(\mu_B\) preserves both
data while \(R^A\) ranges freely through that current coordinate.
These compatible variations need not preserve the selected
Artin--Schreier equation; that equation is the remaining coupling.

The coupling itself is lossless but supplies no order evaluation.
If \(x^{t^3}=x+1\), put \(Z=x^2+x\), and form \(H,R\) from \(x\) as
above.  Then
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{t^3}/\F_t}(Z)=H^{t+1},\qquad
 Z^{t-1}=R^B.                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_9\)}
\]
The map
\[
 \Xi:\F_{t^3}^\times\longrightarrow\F_t^\times\times\mu_A,
 \qquad
 z\longmapsto\bigl(\Norm_{\F_{t^3}/\F_t}(z),z^{t-1}\bigr)
                                                              \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{10}\)}
\]
is a bijection.  Thus \(H^{t+1}\) and \(R^B\) reconstruct a unique
  \(Z\).  Such a reconstructed scalar comes from an Artin--Schreier
 pair \(x,x+1\in K\setminus\F_{t^3}\) satisfying
 \(x^{t^3}=x+1\) exactly when
 \[
  \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{t^3}/\F_2}(Z)=1.
 \]
For the actual selected data, the two roots \(w,w+1\) replace
\(P_k\) only by \(P_k^{-1}\), leaving its order unchanged.

This reconstruction has an exact one-variable order form.  With
\[
 M=\frac{w+1}{w},\qquad Z=w^2+w,\qquad P=M^{t+1},
\]
one has
\[
 M+M^{-1}=Z^{-1},\qquad
 \boxed{P+P^{-1}=\mathcal D_{t+1}(Z^{-1},1).}        \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{11}\)}
\]
Consequently, for every \(\ell^a\Vert\Psi_k\),
\[
 \boxed{
 P^{B/\ell}=1
 \iff
 \mathcal D_{(t^3+1)/\ell}(Z^{-1},1)=0.}           \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{12}\)}
\]
Thus the selected trace-one scalar determines the inverse pair
\(\{P,P^{-1}\}\) and rewrites failure as one Dickson critical value;
it does not evaluate that value.

The aggregate reconstruction is rigid on the Artin--Schreier locus.  For any
root \(x^{t^3}=x+1\), put
\[
 Z_x=x^2+x,\qquad H_x=\Norm_{K/E}(x),\qquad
 C_x=(x^{Q-1})^B,\qquad
 P_x=\left(\frac{x+1}{x}\right)^{t+1}.
\]
If two such roots \(x,y\) satisfy
\[
 H_x^{t+1}=H_y^{t+1},\qquad C_x=C_y,
\]
then
\[
 y\in\{x,x+1\},\qquad P_y\in\{P_x,P_x^{-1}\}.      \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{13}\)}
\]
For the actual selected data, fixing the exact norm \(H\) sharpens this to
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 k=2&\{w,w+1\}\text{ have the same }(H,C_x),\\
 k\geq3&w\text{ is the unique Artin--Schreier root with the fixed }(H,C_x).
 \end{array}                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{14}\)}
\]
This is a rigidity theorem, not a nonprimitive countermodel: once the exact
Artin--Schreier coupling is imposed, the projection data reconstruct the
selected minus coordinate instead of bounding its order independently.

Finally the selected \(C\)-dependence of the plus projection is the
explicit reciprocal-quartic identity
\[
 \eta_k=\gamma_k^{t-1}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \boxed{
 \bigl(g_k^{t-1}\bigr)^2
 =\frac{\eta_k^4+\eta_k^3+1}
        {\eta_k^4+\eta_k+1}.}                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{15}\)}
\]
 The denominator in the reciprocal-quartic identity is nonzero.  Indeed
 \(X^4+X+1\) is irreducible over \(\F_2\), while
 \(\eta_k\in\F_{2^{3^k}}\) and \(\gcd(4,3^k)=1\).  Therefore any
 proof of \(C_k\Rightarrow D'_k\) that proceeds through these
 projections would have to control this special rational image and the
 selected Artin--Schreier coupling, then still evaluate the disjoint
 minus coordinate \(P_k\); primitive order does not transfer directly.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The identities
\[
 AB=Q^2+Q+1,\qquad \gcd(A,B)=1
\]
are direct; the gcd uses that \(t\equiv-1\pmod3\).  Since
\(\Norm_{K/E}(R)=R^{1+Q+Q^2}=1\), one has \(R\in\mu_\Gamma\).
Also
\[
 (Q-1)A=(t^3-1)(t+1),
\]
and \(w^{t^3-1}=M_k\), proving \((\mathsf{DCP}_2)\).
Using \(w^{t^3}=w+1\), one obtains
\[
 \begin{split}
 R^B
 &=w^{(Q-1)(Q-t+1)}
   =\frac{w^{t^4}w^t}{w^{t^3}w}\\
 &=\frac{(w^t+1)w^t}{(w+1)w}
   =(w^2+w)^{t-1}=Z_{D,k}^{t-1},
 \end{split}
\]
and \((\mathsf{CD}_5)\) gives the last expression in
\((\mathsf{DCP}_3)\).

A current \(\ell\) divides \(B/3\) and is coprime to
\((Q-1)A\).  Raising to \(Q-1\) and then to \(A\) therefore preserves
its full primary order, while \(R^B\) kills it.  The cyclic Euler
criterion proves \((\mathsf{DCP}_4)\)--\((\mathsf{DCP}_5)\).
Furthermore
\[
 P_k^{B/3}=R^{\Gamma/3}
 =w^{(Q-1)\Gamma/3}=H^{(Q-1)/3},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{DCP}_6)\).

The kernel of \(\Theta\) consists of the elements of \(E^\times\)
whose cube is one, hence is \(\mu_3\).  Every image pair satisfies
the compatibility in \((\mathsf{DCP}_8)\).  Conversely, both the image
and the compatible-pair set have
\[
 \frac{(Q-1)\Gamma}{3}
\]
elements: the latter condition is the equality of two surjective
maps to \(\mu_3\).  This proves \((\mathsf{DCP}_8)\).  A current Sylow
element \(c\in\mu_B\) satisfies \(c^B=c^{\Gamma/3}=1\), while
\(c\mapsto c^A\) is an automorphism of its Sylow group, proving the
variation assertion.

For an Artin--Schreier root, \(Z=x^{t^3+1}\).  Expanding exponents gives
\[
 (t^3+1)(t^2+t+1)
 =(t+1)(t^4+t^2+1),
\]
which is the norm identity in \((\mathsf{DCP}_9)\); the second is the
same exponent calculation used in \((\mathsf{DCP}_3)\).
Since
\[
 |\F_{t^3}^\times|=(t-1)A,\qquad \gcd(t-1,A)=1,
\]
\(\Xi\) is injective and the two sides have the same cardinality, so
it is bijective.  The characteristic-two quadratic criterion gives
the stated trace-one boundary and the two conjugate roots.  When the
trace is zero the quadratic instead has two roots in \(\F_{t^3}\), so
they do not satisfy the required nontrivial Frobenius translation.

The first identity in \((\mathsf{DCP}_{11})\) is a direct calculation, and
the Dickson identity
\(\mathcal D_n(u+u^{-1},1)=u^n+u^{-n}\) gives the second.  Since \(P\)
has odd order,
\[
 \mathcal D_{B/\ell}(P+P^{-1},1)=0
 \iff P^{B/\ell}=1.
\]
Dickson composition and \((t+1)B=t^3+1\) prove
\((\mathsf{DCP}_{12})\).

If \(x,y\) satisfy the hypotheses of \((\mathsf{DCP}_{13})\), then
\((\mathsf{DCP}_9)\) and injectivity of \(\Xi\) give \(Z_x=Z_y\).
Thus \((x+y)^2+(x+y)=0\), so \(y=x\) or \(x+1\), and the displayed
claim about \(P_y\) follows by inversion.  Moreover
\[
 \Norm_{K/E}(w+1)=\Norm_{K/E}(w^{t^3})=H^t.
\]
For the actual norm, \((\mathsf{TN}_1)\) gives, with
\(\eta=\zeta_k^3\),
\[
 H=\frac{\eta^2}{\eta^2+\omega\eta+1},\qquad
 H^t=\frac1{\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1}.
\]
Their equality is equivalent to
\[
 (\eta^3+\omega)(\eta+\omega^2)
 =\eta^4+\omega^2\eta^3+\omega\eta+1=0.
\]
Since \(\eta\) has order \(3^k\), for \(k\geq2\) this occurs exactly
when \(k=2\), proving \((\mathsf{DCP}_{14})\).  The statement concerns
coordinates that actually arise from Artin--Schreier roots; an arbitrary
ambient pair in \((\mathsf{DCP}_8)\) need not do so.

For the reciprocal-quartic identity, put \(y=\zeta_k^2\).  Directly from the
selected period formulas,
\[
 \eta_k=\frac{\omega+\omega^2y}{1+y},\qquad
 y=\frac{\eta_k+\omega}{\eta_k+\omega^2},\qquad
 g_k^2=\frac{y^5+1}{y^2(y+1)},\qquad
 y^t=\omega^2y^{-1}.
\]
Substitution, followed by \(1+\omega+\omega^2=0\), gives the displayed
reciprocal-quartic quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the actual exceptional terminal resolvent]
\label{prop:dk-terminal-resolvent}
Retain \(k\geq2\), and write
\[
 t=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad Q=t^2,\qquad q=t^3,\qquad
 a=\gamma_{k-1},\qquad C=a^2+a+1.
\]
Put \(r_i=\gamma_k^{t^i}\), with indices modulo three, and define the
oriented cyclic resolvents
\[
 \mathcal K=\sum_i r_i^2r_{i+1},\qquad
 \mathcal K'=\sum_i r_ir_{i+1}^2,\qquad
 \chi=\mathcal K/a.
\]
Then
\[
 \mathcal K+\mathcal K'=a,\qquad
 \mathcal K\mathcal K'=a^2+1,\qquad
 \boxed{\chi^2+\chi=1+a^{-2}.}                      \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_1\)}
\]
Reversing cyclic orientation sends \(\chi\) to \(\chi+1\).

The actual selected scalar
\[
 Y=Z_{D,k}^{-1}=g_k^{\,2-Q}\in\F_q
\]
has the irreducible monic polynomial over \(\F_t\)
\[
 \boxed{
 m_\chi(T)=T^3+
 \frac{a^2\chi+a^2+1}{C}T^2+
 \frac{a^2(a+1)\chi+a^3+a+1}{C}T+C.}               \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_2\)}
\]
Equivalently, its denominator-free equation is
\[
 CT^3+(a\mathcal K+a^2+1)T^2
 +(a(a+1)\mathcal K+a^3+a+1)T+C^2=0.               \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_3\)}
\]
For every current prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), with
\(n=(q+1)/\ell\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \text{failure at }\ell
 \iff m_\chi(T)\mid\mathcal D_n(T,1).}             \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_4\)}
\]

This divisor has no independent norm shadow.  If
\(V=\mathcal D_n(Y,1)\), then
\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{Res}_T(m_\chi,\mathcal D_n)
 =\Norm_{\F_q/\F_t}(V)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_t}(V)^2,}             \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_5\)}
\]
and these three quantities vanish exactly under failure.

Finally, for \(Z=Y^{-1}\), orientation reversal changes its relative trace by
\[
 \frac{a^2(a+1)}{C^2}
 =\left(\frac{a+1}{C}\right)^2+\frac{a+1}{C},       \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_6\)}
\]
and leaves its relative norm \(C^{-1}\) fixed.  Therefore absolute trace and
lower norm cannot distinguish the two orientations.  The actual
\(t\)-Frobenius does select one of them; the comparison proves blindness of
these scalar invariants, not ambiguity of the Conway datum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The \(r_i\) are the roots of \(X^3+X+a\), so their elementary symmetric
functions are \(e_1=0,e_2=1,e_3=a\).  The cyclic-resolvent identities give
\[
 \mathcal K+\mathcal K'=e_1e_2+e_3=a,\qquad
 \mathcal K\mathcal K'=e_1^3e_3+e_2^3+e_3^2=1+a^2,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{DTR}_1)\).

Put \(x_i=r_i^2+r_i+1\).  Direct symmetric reduction gives
\[
 e_1(x)=1,\qquad e_2(x)=a+1,\qquad e_3(x)=C,\qquad
 \mathcal K_x:=\sum_i x_i^2x_{i+1}=a\mathcal K+C.
\]
The three conjugates of \(Y\) are \(Y_i=x_i^2/x_{i-1}\).  Reducing their
elementary symmetric functions over the common denominator
\(C=x_0x_1x_2\) gives
\[
 \sum_iY_i=\frac{a\mathcal K+a^2+1}{C},\qquad
 \sum_{i<j}Y_iY_j
 =\frac{a(a+1)\mathcal K+a^3+a+1}{C},\qquad
 \prod_iY_i=C.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{DTR}_2)\)--\((\mathsf{DTR}_3)\).
Here \(a,C\ne0\), and \(\gcd(Q-2,q-1)=1\).  Exponentiation by \(2-Q\)
therefore recovers \(g_k\) from \(Y\), so \(Y\) has relative degree three and
the polynomial is irreducible.  Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-coprime-projections},
specifically \((\mathsf{DCP}_{12})\), and the minimal-polynomial criterion
prove \((\mathsf{DTR}_4)\).

Let \(M=M_k\) and \(\xi=M^n\in\mu_\ell\).  Since
\(Y=M+M^{-1}\), Dickson's identity gives \(V=\xi+\xi^{-1}\).
Moreover \(\ord_\ell(t)=6\), hence \(t^2-t+1\equiv0\pmod\ell\).  With
\(x=\xi\) and \(y=\xi^t\), direct multiplication of the three relative
conjugates gives
\[
 \Norm(V)=\operatorname{Tr}(V)^2,\qquad
 xy\operatorname{Tr}(V)=(x+y)(x+1)(y+1).
\]
The second expression vanishes exactly when \(\xi=1\); thus both sides of
\((\mathsf{DTR}_5)\) vanish exactly under failure.  The first equality in
\((\mathsf{DTR}_5)\) is the root-product formula for the irreducible
\(m_\chi\).  Finally take the reciprocal of \((\mathsf{DTR}_2)\) and replace
\(\chi\) by \(\chi+1\).  The relative norm stays \(C^{-1}\), while the trace
changes by the first term in \((\mathsf{DTR}_6)\); the displayed
Artin--Schreier identity proves equality of the absolute traces.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[translated-binomial form of $D'_k$]
\label{prop:dk-translated-binomial}
Retain \(k\geq2\), \(h=3^k\), and choose
\(u\in\F_{16}\) with \(u^2+u=\omega\).  Put

\[
 L=\F_{16^h}=\F_{2^{4h}},\qquad \beta=\zeta+u.
\]

Then the minimal polynomial of \(\beta\) over \(\F_{16}\) is the
irreducible degree-\(h\) polynomial

\[
 B_k(Y)=(Y+u)^h+\omega.
\]

For every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item the full \(\ell\)-primary part occurs in \(\ord(M_k)\);
\item \(\beta\notin(L^\times)^\ell\);
\item \(B_k(X^\ell)=(X^\ell+u)^h+\omega\) is irreducible over
      \(\F_{16}\).
\end{enumerate}
More precisely, \(B_k(X^\ell)\) is either irreducible of degree
\(h\ell\), or is a product of exactly \(\ell\) irreducibles of degree
\(h\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since

\[
 \ord_{3h}(16)
 =\frac{\ord_{3h}(2)}{\gcd(\ord_{3h}(2),4)}=h,
\]

the element \(\zeta\) has degree \(h\) over \(\F_{16}\).  As
\(\zeta^h=\omega\), its minimal polynomial there is
\(X^h+\omega\); translation by \(u\) gives \(B_k\).

Let \(K=\F_{2^{2h}}\).  The corrected norm is

\[
 N=\Norm_{L/K}(\beta)=\beta^{2^{2h}+1}.
\]

Because \(2^{2h}\equiv1\pmod\ell\), this norm induces multiplication
by two on \(L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell\).  Hence it first gives

\[
 \beta\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff N\in(L^\times)^\ell.
\]

The natural map

\[
 K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell
 \longrightarrow L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell
\]

is injective: restriction followed by the degree-two norm is
multiplication by two, which is invertible modulo the odd prime
\(\ell\).  Since \(N\in K\), this sharpens the equivalence to

\[
 \beta\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff N\in(K^\times)^\ell,
\]

and Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level} identifies the negation
with the full \(\ell\)-primary assertion.  Kummer theory and Capelli's
lemma give the irreducibility equivalence.

If \(\beta=\alpha^\ell\) with \(\alpha\in L\), every root of
\(B_k(X^\ell)\) lies in \(L\), because \(\mu_\ell\subset L\).  Its
\(\ell\)-th power is a conjugate of \(\beta\), of degree \(h\) over
\(\F_{16}\); the root therefore has degree both at least and at most
\(h\).  All irreducible factors consequently have degree \(h\), and
there are exactly \(\ell\) of them.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[short translated cyclotomic period]
\label{cor:dk-short-period}
Let \(\chi:L^\times\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order
\(\ell\), realize its values as complex roots of unity, and write
\(\chi(\beta)=\xi_\ell^r\).  Then

\[
 \mathcal J_{k,\ell}
 :=\sum_{\theta^h=\omega}\chi(\theta+u)
 =\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\xi_\ell^{\,r16^j},
\]

and

\[
 \beta\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff \mathcal J_{k,\ell}=h.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The roots of \(X^h+\omega\) are \(\zeta^{16^j}\), \(0\leq j<h\),
and \(u\in\F_{16}\).  Thus

\[
 \chi(\zeta^{16^j}+u)
 =\chi((\zeta+u)^{16^j})
 =\xi_\ell^{\,r16^j}.
\]

If \(r=0\), every summand is one.  Conversely, equality with the
positive real number \(h\) forces equality in the complex triangle
inequality and hence forces every summand, in particular the first,
to be one.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[full \(\F_{16}\)-line saturation]
\label{prop:dk-line-saturation}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial}, put
\(s=3^{k-1}\), and choose \(u\) so that it has order fifteen and
\(\omega=u^5\).  For a prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), write

\[
 \mathcal C=L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell,
 \qquad v(c)=[\zeta+c]\quad(c\in\F_{16}),
 \qquad A=2^s\pmod\ell,
\]

using additive notation for \(\mathcal C\).  Then \(A\) has order six,
\(A^2-A+1=0\), and \(A^3=-1\).  The four base-field classes vanish,

\[
 v(c)=0\qquad(c\in\F_4),                              \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,1}\)}
\]

while for \(c\in\F_{16}^\times\),

\[
 v(\omega c^{-2^s})=A v(c),\qquad
 v(c^{-2^h})=-v(c).                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,2}\)}
\]

For the twelve exponents not divisible by five one has

\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
 j & \{m:v(u^m)=A^jv(u)\}\\ \hline
 0&\{1,4\}\\
 1&\{3,12\}\\
 2&\{11,14\}\\
 3&\{7,13\}\\
 4&\{6,9\}\\
 5&\{2,8\}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,3}\)}
\]

Consequently

\[
 \zeta+u\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff \zeta+c\in(L^\times)^\ell
      \quad\hbox{for every }c\in\F_{16}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,4}\)}
\]

If \(D'_k\) holds for \(\ell\), none of the twelve translates with
\(c\notin\F_4\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  Equivalently, for an
exact-order-\(\ell\) complex character \(\chi\) of \(L^\times\) and
\(t=\chi(\zeta+u)\),

\[
 \sum_{c\in\F_{16}}\chi(\zeta+c)
 =4+2\sum_{j=0}^5t^{A^j},                              \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16}\)}
\]

and failure of \(D'_k\) is exactly the extremal value sixteen.
Thus Frobenius, conjugation, and all \(\F_4\)-translates generate only
scalar multiples of the one unresolved Kummer class \(v(u)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
One has \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h=6s\), proving the assertions about \(A\).
For \(c=0\), the element \(\zeta\) has \(\ell\)-coprime order \(3h\).
For \(c\in\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}\),
Propositions~\ref{prop:translates} and~\ref{prop:exception} give order
dividing \(3h(2^h-1)\), again coprime to \(\ell\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{L}_{16,1})\); constants in \(\F_{16}^\times\) and
\(\zeta\) also have zero class.

The congruence \(2^s\equiv h-1\pmod{3h}\) gives
\(\zeta^{2^s}=\omega\zeta^{-1}\).  Discarding scalar factors with
zero class,

\[
 A v(c)=[(\zeta+c)^{2^s}]
 =[\omega\zeta^{-1}+c^{2^s}]
 =[\zeta+\omega c^{-2^s}]
 =v(\omega c^{-2^s}).
\]

Likewise \(2^h\equiv-1\pmod{3h}\) and modulo \(\ell\), so

\[
 -v(c)=[(\zeta+c)^{2^h}]
 =[\zeta^{-1}+c^{2^h}]
 =[\zeta+c^{-2^h}].
\]

On exponents modulo fifteen the two transformations are

\[
 R(m)=5-(2^s\bmod15)m,\qquad
 S(m)=-(2^h\bmod15)m.
\]

For odd \(k\) they are \((5-2m,7m)\), and for even \(k\) they are
\((5-8m,13m)\).  Iterating either pair on the twelve residues outside
\(5\mathbb Z/15\mathbb Z\) gives the table
\((\mathsf{L}_{16,3})\).  Since every \(A^j\) is nonzero modulo
\(\ell\), all twelve classes vanish exactly when \(v(u)\) vanishes,
proving \((\mathsf{L}_{16,4})\) and its nonvanishing counterpart.

Applying \(\chi\) to the table gives each \(t^{A^j}\) twice, while
the four \(\F_4\)-terms are one.  If \(t=1\), the sum is sixteen;
conversely equality with the positive real number sixteen forces every
unit-modulus summand to be one by equality in the triangle inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[adjacent quartic Kummer reduction]
\label{prop:dk-adjacent-quartic}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation}.  Thus
\[
 h=3^k,\quad s=h/3,\quad
 K=\mathbb F_{4^h}=\mathbb F_4(\zeta),\quad
 L=\mathbb F_{16^h},\quad A=2^s\pmod\ell,
\]
where \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), and choose \(u\in\mathbb F_{16}\) of
order fifteen with \(u^5=\omega=\zeta^h\).  Put
\[
 N=\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega,\qquad
 N'=\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega^2,\qquad
 D=\zeta^4+\zeta,\qquad W=D+1.
\]
