Then \(D\) is unconditionally an \(\ell\)-th power in \(K\), while
in the cyclic Kummer quotient \(K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell\) one has
\[
 \boxed{[W]=(1+A^5)[N].}                              \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_1\)}
\]
The scalar \(1+A^5\) is nonzero modulo \(\ell\).  Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff W\notin(K^\times)^\ell.}                      \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_2\)}
\]
Thus the selected exceptional assertion is equivalently the adjacent-value
exclusion
\[
 D\in(K^\times)^\ell,
 \qquad D+1\notin(K^\times)^\ell.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_3\)}
\]
In fact every nonzero translate gives an equivalent detector:
\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff D+c\notin(K^\times)^\ell
 \quad\text{for any (equivalently every) }c\in\mathbb F_4^\times.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_{3'}\)}
\]
On failure the complete affine line
\(D+\mathbb F_4\) lies in \((K^\times)^\ell\).

This criterion has an immediate cubic form.  Put
\[
 K_-=\mathbb F_{4^s},\qquad a=\zeta^3\in K_-.
\]
The element \(W\) has irreducible minimal polynomial
\[
 \boxed{
 m_W(T)=T^3+T^2+T+\Phi_5(a)}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_4\)}
\]
over \(K_-\), and
\[
 \Norm_{K/K_-}(W)=\Phi_5(a)\in(K_-^\times)^\ell.    \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_5\)}
\]
Hence Capelli's criterion gives the sparse exact target
\[
 \boxed{
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff
 X^{3\ell}+X^{2\ell}+X^\ell+\Phi_5(a)
 \text{ is irreducible over }K_-.}                  \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_6\)}
\]
On failure the displayed polynomial is a product of exactly \(\ell\)
distinct irreducible cubics.  Equations
\((\mathsf{AQ}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{AQ}_6)\) are exact reductions, not a
nonvanishing proof: the norm in \((\mathsf{AQ}_5)\) is automatically an
\(\ell\)-th power, so the new form isolates rather than resolves the
selected additive coupling between \(D\) and \(D+1\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Use additive notation in
\(\mathcal C_L=L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell\), and retain
\(v(c)=[\zeta+c]\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation}.  For every
\(b\in\mathbb F_{16}\),
\[
 \prod_{c\in b+\mathbb F_4}(\zeta+c)
 = (\zeta+b)^4+(\zeta+b)=D+b^4+b.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_7\)}
\]
The four affine \(\mathbb F_4\)-cosets, together with the orbit table
\((\mathsf L_{16,3})\), therefore give
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
b+\mathbb F_4&\prod_{c\in b+\mathbb F_4}(\zeta+c)
 &\text{class in }\mathcal C_L\\ \hline
\mathbb F_4&D&0\\
u+\mathbb F_4&D+1&2(1+A^5)v(u)\\
u^3+\mathbb F_4&D+\omega^2&2(A+A^2)v(u)\\
u^6+\mathbb F_4&D+\omega&2(A^3+A^4)v(u).
\end{array}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_8\)}
\]
Here, for example,
\(u+\mathbb F_4=\{u,u^2,u^4,u^8\}\); the other two rows follow from
\(u^3+\mathbb F_4=\{u^3,u^{11},u^{12},u^{14}\}\) and
\(u^6+\mathbb F_4=\{u^6,u^7,u^9,u^{13}\}\).

The quadratic constant-field norm gives
\[
 N=(\zeta+u)(\zeta+u^4),\qquad
 N'=(\zeta+u^2)(\zeta+u^8),
\]
so the same table gives
\[
 [N]_L=2v(u),\qquad [N']_L=2A^5v(u).
\]
Direct multiplication in characteristic two gives
\[
 NN'=(\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega)
      (\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega^2)
    =\zeta^4+\zeta+1=W.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_9\)}
\]
Restriction
\[
 K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell
 \longrightarrow L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell
\]
is injective: norm after restriction is multiplication by
\([L:K]=2\), which is invertible modulo the odd prime \(\ell\).
Thus \((\mathsf{AQ}_8)\)--\((\mathsf{AQ}_9)\) descend to
\((\mathsf{AQ}_1)\), and the first row of
\((\mathsf{AQ}_8)\) shows that \(D\) is an \(\ell\)-th power already
in \(K\).  Since \(A\) has exact order six modulo \(\ell\), the equality
\(1+A^5=0\) would give \(A^5=A^3\), hence \(A^2=1\), a contradiction.
Likewise \(1+A\ne0\), so the other two coefficients
\(2A(1+A)\) and \(2A^3(1+A)\) in
\((\mathsf{AQ}_8)\) are nonzero.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level} identifies
\([N]\ne0\) with \(D'_k\), proving
\((\mathsf{AQ}_2)\)--\((\mathsf{AQ}_3)\).  Together with the other two
nonzero coefficients and injectivity, this also proves
\((\mathsf{AQ}_{3'})\) and its failure statement.

The degree-three Frobenius of \(K/K_-\) sends
\(\zeta\) to \(\zeta^{4^s}=\omega\zeta\), hence sends
\(D\) successively to \(\omega D\) and \(\omega^2D\).  Since
\(D\ne0\), the three conjugates
\[
 1+D,\qquad1+\omega D,\qquad1+\omega^2D
\]
are distinct.  Their product polynomial is
\[
 \prod_{i=0}^2\bigl(T+(1+\omega^iD)\bigr)
 =T^3+T^2+T+(1+D^3).
\]
With \(a=\zeta^3\), one has \(D=\zeta(a+1)\), and therefore
\[
 1+D^3=1+a(a+1)^3
 =a^4+a^3+a^2+a+1=\Phi_5(a).                        \tag{\(\mathsf{AQ}_{10}\)}
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{AQ}_4)\).  It also proves the norm identity in
\((\mathsf{AQ}_5)\).  The value is nonzero because \(a\) has
\(3\)-power order \(h\), whereas a root of \(\Phi_5\) has order five;
it is an \(\ell\)-th power because
\(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(4)=h\), so
\(\ell\nmid|K_-^\times|=4^s-1\).

Finally \(\mu_\ell\subset K\).  For the prime \(\ell\), Kummer theory
says that \(X^\ell-W\) is irreducible over \(K\) exactly when \(W\)
is not an \(\ell\)-th power.  Capelli's lemma applied to the irreducible
cubic \(m_W\) proves \((\mathsf{AQ}_6)\).  If
\(W=y^\ell\) in \(K\), every \(\rho^ry\), \(\rho\in\mu_\ell\), has
degree exactly three over \(K_-\), because its \(\ell\)-th power has
degree three.  The composition therefore splits into exactly \(\ell\)
distinct irreducible cubics.  This proves the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exceptional gap and Conway-block product blindness]
\label{prop:dk-gap}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation}, so \(k\geq2\),
and put \(K=\mathbb F_{2^{2h}}\).  For every
prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\),
\[
 m_\ell=4\qquad\hbox{or}\qquad m_\ell\geq16.             \tag{\(\mathsf{G}_{16}\)}
\]
Thus a failure of \(D'_k\) cannot produce an excess in the interval
\(5,\ldots,15\).

There is also no contradiction obtained by multiplying a complete
finite-Conway-subfield block.  More precisely, for \(a\geq1\), put
\[
 C_a=\mathbb F_{2^{2^a}},\qquad Q_a=|C_a|=2^{2^a},
 \qquad L_a=\mathbb F_{2^{\,2^a h}}=K C_a .
\]
Then, in \(L_a\),
\[
 \prod_{c\in C_a}(\zeta+c)
   =\zeta^{Q_a}+\zeta
   =\zeta\bigl(1+\zeta^{Q_a-1}\bigr)
   \in (L_a^\times)^\ell .                            \tag{\(\mathsf{G}_{C_a}\)}
\]
Consequently even an extension of the affine-line saturation from
\(\mathbb F_{16}\) to any complete finite Conway subfield would have a
product identity which is automatically invisible in the unresolved
\(\ell\)-Kummer quotient.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose the element \(u\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation} to be the finite nimber \(4\).
If \(m_\ell\ne4\), then the lower bound
\(m_\ell\geq4\) and the exact power criterion say that
\(\zeta+4\) is an \(\ell\)-th power in
\(L=\mathbb F_{2^{4h}}\).  Equation
\((\mathsf L_{16,4})\) then makes every \(\zeta+c\),
\(c\in\mathbb F_{16}\), an \(\ell\)-th power.  The finite nimbers
\(0,1,\ldots,15\) are exactly the elements of \(\mathbb F_{16}\), so
none of these sixteen translates can be the excess.  This proves
\((\mathsf G_{16})\).

For the second assertion, the roots of \(X^{Q_a}+X\) are precisely the
elements of \(C_a\), which gives the displayed product formula.  Since
\(2^a\) is even, lifting the exponent gives
\[
 v_3(Q_a-1)=v_3(2^{2^a}-1)=1.
\]
The element \(\zeta\) has order \(3h=3^{k+1}\), so
\(\zeta^{Q_a-1}\) has exact order \(h\) and lies in
\[
 \mathbb F_{2^{\operatorname{ord}_h(2)}}
   =\mathbb F_{2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}}.
\]
This lower field embeds in \(K\), hence in every \(L_a\).
On the other hand \(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=2h\).  Hence \(\ell\)
divides neither \(3h\) nor the multiplicative order of that lower
field.  It follows that both \(\zeta\) and
\(1+\zeta^{Q_a-1}\) are \(\ell\)-th powers in \(L_a\), proving
\((\mathsf G_{C_a})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[constant-extension line criterion and an explicit
exceptional bound]
\label{prop:dk-constant-line}
Retain \(k\geq2\), \(h=3^k\),
\[
 K=\F_{4^h}=\F_4(\zeta),
 \qquad \ell\mid\Psi_k,
\]
and let \(\chi:K^\times\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order \(\ell\).
For \(a\geq1\), put
\[
 t=2^{a-1},\qquad
 C_a=\F_{2^{2^a}}=\F_{4^t},\qquad
 L_a=KC_a=\F_{4^{ht}},
\]
and let
\[
 \chi_a=\chi\circ\Norm_{L_a/K},\qquad
 S_{a,k,\ell}=\sum_{c\in C_a}\chi_a(\zeta+c).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_1\)}
\]
Then
\[
 \zeta+C_a\subset (L_a^\times)^\ell
 \iff S_{a,k,\ell}=4^t.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_2\)}
\]
More precisely, attach to \(\chi\) the primitive even Dirichlet
character modulo \(X^h+\omega\) over \(\F_4[X]\), and write its
\(L\)-polynomial as
\[
 L_\chi(T)
  =(1-T)\prod_{j=1}^{h-2}(1-\alpha_jT),
 \qquad |\alpha_j|=2.
\]
Then constant-field extension gives the exact selected moment
\[
 S_{a,k,\ell}
   =-\left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{h-2}\alpha_j^t\right),
 \qquad
 |S_{a,k,\ell}|\leq1+(h-2)2^t.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_3\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 2^t\geq h-1
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 m_\ell\leq4^t-1.                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_4\)}
\]
If \(t\) is the least power of two at least
\(\lceil\log_2(h-1)\rceil\), then
\[
 m_\ell<4^t<16h^4.                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_5\)}
\]
Thus this gives a direct polynomial upper bound on the exceptional
column, but not an absolute one.  For any fixed \(a\), the Riemann--Weil
bound in \((\mathsf{CE}_3)\) is compatible with the extremal value
\(S_{a,k,\ell}=4^t\) for all sufficiently large \(h\).  A fixed-window
proof by this route must therefore prove the selected nonextremality
\[
 S_{a,k,\ell}\ne4^t
\]
uniformly in \(k\) and \(\ell\).  At \(a=2\), this is exactly the
full-\(\F_{16}\)-line formulation of \(D'_k\); larger fixed \(a\) give
genuinely weaker selected least-nonresidue assertions rather than a
consequence of the four maximal-order arms.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(h\) is odd and \(t\) is a power of two, the fields \(K\) and
\(C_a\) are linearly disjoint over \(\F_4\), and
\([L_a:K]=t\).  Norm followed by inclusion on the cyclic
\(\ell\)-Kummer quotients is multiplication by \(t\).  As
\(\ell\nmid t\), the norm induces an isomorphism
\[
 L_a^\times/(L_a^\times)^\ell
   \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
 K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_6\)}
\]
In particular \(\chi_a\) has exact order \(\ell\), its kernel is
\((L_a^\times)^\ell\), and no term \(\zeta+c\) vanishes because
\(\zeta\) has odd degree \(h>1\) over \(\F_4\), whereas every element
of \(C_a\) has degree dividing \(t\).  Equation
\((\mathsf{CE}_2)\) now follows from equality in the complex triangle
inequality: a sum of \(4^t\) roots of unity equals the positive real
number \(4^t\) precisely when every summand is one.

The polynomial \(X^h+\omega\) remains irreducible after extension of
constants to \(C_a\), again because \(\gcd(h,t)=1\).  Since
\(\ell\ne3\), the order-\(\ell\) character is trivial on
\(\F_4^\times\), so the attached Dirichlet character is even.
The character \(\chi_a\) is exactly its constant-field norm lift.
If the reciprocal roots of \(L_\chi\) are
\(1,\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_{h-2}\), constant extension raises them
to their \(t\)-th powers.  The degree-one coefficient of the extended
\(L\)-polynomial is on the one hand
\[
 \sum_{c\in C_a}\chi_a(\zeta+c)=S_{a,k,\ell},
\]
and on the other hand the negative of the sum of those reciprocal
roots.  This proves the equality in \((\mathsf{CE}_3)\); the
function-field Riemann hypothesis gives \(|\alpha_j|=2\), proving its
inequality.  Notice that \(1\) is the reciprocal root of the
trivial even factor \(1-T\), so neither the sign nor the additive
constant is being suppressed.

If \(2^t\geq h-1\), then
\[
 1+(h-2)2^t
 \leq1+(2^t-1)2^t
 =4^t-2^t+1
 <4^t.
\]
Equations \((\mathsf{CE}_2)\)--\((\mathsf{CE}_3)\) therefore supply
some \(c\in C_a\) for which \(\zeta+c\) is not an \(\ell\)-th power
in \(L_a\), and hence is not one in its defining subfield.  The finite
nimbers \(0,1,\ldots,4^t-1\) are exactly the elements of \(C_a\),
proving \((\mathsf{CE}_4)\).

Finally put \(R=\lceil\log_2(h-1)\rceil\).  The least power of two
\(t\geq R\) satisfies
\[
 t<2R<2(\log_2 h+1).
\]
Hence
\[
 4^t<4^{2(\log_2 h+1)}=16h^4,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{CE}_5)\).  For fixed \(t\), the right side of
the bound in \((\mathsf{CE}_3)\) eventually exceeds \(4^t\), proving
the stated limitation.  The case \(a=2\) has \(t=2\) and
\(C_a=\F_{16}\), so Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation} gives
the final identification with \(D'_k\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[composite-conductor cyclotomic-unit form of \(D'_k\)]
\label{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-gap}, put \(N_0=15h\), and
choose \(\theta\in L^\times\) so that
\[
 \theta^5=\zeta,\qquad \theta^h=u.
\]
Then \(\theta\) has exact order \(N_0\), and
\[
 \rho:=\frac{\zeta}{u}=\theta^{5-h}
\]
also has exact order \(N_0\).  If
\(\widetilde\rho\) is a primitive complex \(N_0\)-th root of unity,
\[
 \varepsilon=1-\widetilde\rho
 \in\mathbb Q(\mu_{N_0})^\times
\]
is a global cyclotomic unit.  There is a prime
\(\mathfrak p\mid2\) whose residue field is \(L=\mathbb F_{2^{4h}}\)
and for which
\[
 \overline\varepsilon=1+\rho,\qquad
 \zeta+u=u(1+\rho).
\]
For every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\),
\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff
 \left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak p}\right)_\ell\ne1.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CU}_{15h}\)}
\]
Equivalently, the chosen prime over two has nontrivial Frobenius in the
Kummer extension obtained by adjoining an \(\ell\)-th root of
\(\varepsilon\) after adjoining \(\mu_\ell\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The elements \(\zeta\) and \(u\) have orders \(3h\) and \(15\), and
their \(h\)-th and fifth powers agree:
\(\zeta^h=u^5=\omega\).  The compatible pair therefore lifts in the
cyclic group of order \(\operatorname{lcm}(3h,15)=15h\) to the stated
\(\theta\).  Its two displayed images have their full orders, so
\(\theta\) has order \(15h\).  Moreover
\[
 \gcd(5-h,15h)=1
\]
because \(h\) is a power of three and is not divisible by five; hence
\(\rho\) is primitive of the same order.

The conductor \(15h\) has two distinct prime divisors, so
\(\Phi_{15h}(1)=1\), which makes \(1-\widetilde\rho\) a unit.  Also
\[
 \operatorname{ord}_{15h}(2)
 =\operatorname{lcm}\bigl(\operatorname{ord}_{3h}(2),
                           \operatorname{ord}_5(2)\bigr)
 =\operatorname{lcm}(2h,4)=4h.
\]
Thus a prime over two has residue field \(L\), and it may be chosen to
send \(\widetilde\rho\) to \(\rho\).  Reduction gives the two displayed
identities.  Finally \(\ell\nmid15\), so \(u\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial} identifies \(D'_k\) at
\(\ell\) with \(\zeta+u\) being a non-\(\ell\)-th-power; the standard
power-residue/Frobenius criterion now gives
\((\mathsf{CU}_{15h})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exceptional failure as simultaneous Jacobi flatness]
\label{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}
Let \(k\geq2\), put
\[
 h=3^k,\qquad M=15h,\qquad Q=2^{4h},
\]
and let the prime \(\ell\) divide \(\Psi_k\).  Choose the reduction
\(z\in\mathbb F_Q^\times\) of the selected primitive \(M\)-th root in
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, choose a generator \(g\) with
\(g^{(Q-1)/M}=z\), and form the mixed Jacobi sums
\[
 J_j=\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_Q}
 \chi_M(x)^j\chi_\ell(1-x)
 \qquad(1\leq j<M)
\]
of Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase}.  At a chosen
\(\ell\)-adic place put \(\pi=\zeta_\ell-1\).  Then failure of
\(D'_k\) at \(\ell\) is equivalent to the simultaneous first-order
flatness
\[
 J_j\equiv-1\pmod{\pi^2}
 \qquad(1\leq j<M).                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}\)}
\]
Thus mixed Jacobi sums see the complete exceptional Kummer vector, but
the missing theorem is exactly that this vector is not identically
zero; Stickelberger ideal factorization alone does not evaluate its
first local phase.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write the unique coprime-order decomposition
\[
 z=ab,\qquad \ord(a)=3h,\qquad \ord(b)=5,
\]
and put \(q=2^h\), \(L=\mathbb F_{q^4}=\mathbb F_Q\), and
\(K_0=\mathbb F_{q^2}\).  By
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, failure at \(\ell\) says that
\(1+ab\) is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(L\).  The norm
\(L^\times\to K_0^\times\) acts on the one-dimensional
\(\ell\)-Kummer quotient by
\[
 q^2+1\equiv2\pmod\ell,
\]
because \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\).  It is therefore an isomorphism on that
quotient, and
\[
 U=(1+ab)(1+ab^{-1})
\]
is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(K_0\).

Since \(a^q=a^{-1}\),
\[
 (1+a)^{q-1}=a^{-1},
 \qquad
 (1+a^5)^{q-1}=a^{-5}.
\]
Here \(q-1\equiv-2\pmod\ell\), while \(a\) has order prime to
\(\ell\).  Hence both \(1+a\) and \(1+a^5\) are \(\ell\)-th powers.
If
\[
 U'=(1+ab^2)(1+ab^{-2}),
\]
then
\[
 UU'=\Phi_5(a)=\frac{1+a^5}{1+a}
\]
is an \(\ell\)-th power.  Thus \(U'\) is an \(\ell\)-th power, and the same norm
isomorphism implies that \(1+ab^2\) is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(L\).

Now
\[
 \ord_M(2)=4h,\qquad \varphi(M)=8h.
\]
The primitive \(M\)-th roots consequently form two Frobenius orbits,
represented by \(ab\) and \(ab^2\).  These representatives are not in
the same orbit: a power \(2^j\) fixing the \(3h\)-component has
\(j\equiv0\pmod{2h}\), whereas no such exponent sends \(b\) to
\(b^2\).  Hence \(1+v\) is an \(\ell\)-th power for every primitive
\(M\)-th root \(v\).

It remains to treat proper-order powers of \(z\).  If \(d\mid M\) is
proper, the divisibility
\(2h\mid\ord_d(2)\) can occur only for \(d=3h\).  For an element
\(v\) of that order, \(v^q=v^{-1}\), and
\[
 (1+v)^{q-1}=v^{-1}.
\]
As above, \(q-1\equiv-2\pmod\ell\) and
\(\ell\nmid\ord(v)\), so \(1+v\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  For every
other proper \(d\), the smallest finite field containing \(v\) has
unit group of order prime to \(\ell\); its \(\ell\)-power map is an
automorphism.  We have therefore proved
\[
 1-z^a\in(\mathbb F_Q^\times)^\ell
 \qquad(1\leq a<M).
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase} now gives
\((\mathsf{DJ})\).  Conversely, \((\mathsf{DJ})\) makes every Fourier
coefficient \(C_j\) vanish, so \((\mathsf{MJ}_2)\) at \(a=1\) says
that \(1-z\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  This is precisely failure of
\(D'_k\) at \(\ell\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the exceptional Jacobi vector has one selected coordinate]
\label{prop:dk-jacobi-top}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}, and for

\[
 U=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times
\]

define

\[
 \lambda(\mathord\pm2^i)=2^i\pmod\ell
 \qquad(0\leq i<4h).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_1\)}
\]

Then \(\lambda:U\to\mathbb F_\ell^\times\) is a primitive even
Dirichlet character modulo \(M\).  If

\[
 L(a)=\operatorname{ind}_\ell(1-z^a),\qquad
 G_j=\sum_{a\in U}\lambda(a)\overline{\zeta_M}^{ja},
\]

then

\[
 L(a)=
 \begin{cases}
  0,&(a,M)>1,\\
  \lambda(a)L(1),&(a,M)=1,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad
 C_j=L(1)G_j.                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_2\)}
\]

Moreover

\[
 G_1G(\lambda^{-1})=\lambda(-1)M=M\ne0\pmod\ell,     \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_3\)}
\]

and consequently

\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff L(1)\ne0
 \iff C_1\ne0
 \iff J_1\not\equiv-1\pmod{(\zeta_\ell-1)^2}.        \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_4\)}
\]

Thus the simultaneous Jacobi flatness in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} is a rank-one condition, not
\(M-1\) independent conditions.

This coordinate is exactly the first regulator of the quadratic-twist
antiunit in Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-antiunit}.  With

\[
 E_1=(1-ab)(1-ab^{-1}),\qquad
 E_2=(1-ab^2)(1-ab^{-2}),\qquad d_k=E_1/E_2,
\]

one has

\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(E_1)=2L(1),\qquad
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(E_2)=-2L(1),\qquad
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)=4L(1),                 \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_5\)}
\]

and hence

\[
 C_1=\frac{G_1}{4}\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k).      \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_6\)}
\]

In particular the exact remaining exceptional theorem is the single
nonvanishing assertion

\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)\ne0
 \qquad(\ell\mid\Psi_k).                              \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_7\)}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The group \(U\) has order \(\varphi(M)=8h\), while
\(\ord_M(2)=4h\).  Moreover \(-1\notin\langle2\rangle\), because a
power of two congruent to \(-1\) modulo \(3h\) has exponent
\(h\pmod{2h}\), whereas a power of two congruent to \(-1\) modulo
five has exponent \(2\pmod4\), and these conditions are incompatible
for odd \(h\).  Hence

\[
 U=\{\mathord\pm2^i:0\leq i<4h\}.
\]

Since \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), the rule in \((\mathsf{DJ}_1)\) is
well-defined, multiplicative, and even.  It is primitive.  Indeed it
cannot factor modulo \(M/3\): the kernel of
\(U\to(\mathbb Z/(M/3)\mathbb Z)^\times\) has order three, whereas
the kernel of \(\lambda\) has order four.  It cannot factor modulo
\(M/5=3h\) either.  The CRT class \(r\) with

\[
 r\equiv1\pmod{3h},\qquad r\equiv2\pmod5
\]

belongs to the reduction kernel but satisfies \(\lambda(r)=-1\): in
the factorization

\[
 \lambda=\lambda_-\chi_5,
\]

the first factor is trivial on \(r\), while \(\chi_5(2)=-1\).
Removing either prime divisor of \(M\) therefore destroys the
character, which proves primitivity.

If \((a,M)>1\), then \(z^a\) has proper order.  The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} shows unconditionally that
every such \(1-z^a\) is an \(\ell\)-th power, and hence \(L(a)=0\).
If \((a,M)=1\), write \(a=\mathord\pm2^i\).  Frobenius covariance
gives \(L(2^i)=2^iL(1)\); replacing \(z^{2^i}\) by its inverse
changes \(1-z^{2^i}\) only by an \(M\)-torsion factor, whose
\(\ell\)-index is zero.  This proves the first identity in
\((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\).  Substitution into the Fourier coefficient
\((\mathsf{MJ}_1)\) of Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase}
gives \(C_j=L(1)G_j\).

The standard Gauss identity for a primitive Dirichlet character gives
\((\mathsf{DJ}_3)\).  Here \(\ell\nmid M\), so \(G_1\ne0\).
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit} identifies \(D'_k\) with
\(L(1)\ne0\), while the first-order Jacobi expansion identifies
\(C_1\ne0\) with the final congruence in \((\mathsf{DJ}_4)\).

Finally the two exponents carrying \(ab\) and \(ab^{-1}\) are
trivial under the quadratic character modulo five, so both factors of
\(E_1\) have index \(L(1)\).  The exponents carrying \(ab^2\) and
\(ab^{-2}\) have quadratic character \(-1\), so both factors of
\(E_2\) have index \(-L(1)\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{DJ}_5)\), and \((\mathsf{DJ}_6)\)--\((\mathsf{DJ}_7)\)
follow because four and \(G_1\) are nonzero modulo \(\ell\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[canonical four-Jacobi detector]
\label{prop:dk-four-jacobi}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}.  Write the primitive
Dirichlet character as \(\lambda=\lambda_-\chi_5\), where
\(\chi_5=(\frac{\cdot}{5})\).  Decompose the selected character
\(\chi_M\) of Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase} uniquely as \(AB\),
where \(A,B\) have orders \(3h,5\), and set
\(X=\chi_\ell\) with the normalization
\(X(g)=\zeta_\ell\) used there.  For \(r=1,2,3,4\), let
\(j_r\bmod M\) be the CRT class
\[
 j_r\equiv1\pmod{3h},\qquad j_r\equiv r\pmod5,
\]
and put \(J_r=J(AB^r,X)\).  In the fraction field of the local
cyclotomic ring define
\[
 \mathcal D_{k,\ell}=\frac{J_1J_4}{J_2J_3}.
\]
Then, with \(\pi=\zeta_\ell-1\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal D_{k,\ell}
 \equiv1-4\pi C_1
 =1-\pi G_1\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)
 \pmod{\pi^2}.}                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 D'_k\text{ fails at }\ell
 \iff \mathcal D_{k,\ell}\equiv1\pmod{\pi^2},           \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_2\)}
\]
and
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)
 =-G_1^{-1}\overline{\frac{\mathcal D_{k,\ell}-1}{\pi}}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_3\)}
\]

This four-term quotient is canonical and minimal under the conductor-five
symmetries.  Indeed, an inversion-invariant integer weight vector on the four
phases has the form \((u,v,v,u)\); zero augmentation forces \(v=-u\),
and the resulting detector line over \(\F_\ell\) is spanned by
\((1,-1,-1,1)\).  The primitive integral weights are its two signs.

There is also a single-antiunit form.  For a multiplicative character
\(C\), fixed in the conductor-five coordinate, such that \(C^2\) and
the displayed characters are nontrivial, put
\[
 \mathcal H_5(C)=
 \frac{J(CB,CB^{-1})}{J(CB^2,CB^{-2})}
 =\frac{G(CB)G(CB^{-1})}{G(CB^2)G(CB^{-2})}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_4\)}
\]
Here \(G(\cdot)\) denotes the finite-field Gauss sum; the common
\(G(C^2)\) denominator has cancelled.
Then
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal D_{k,\ell}=\frac{\mathcal H_5(A)}{\mathcal H_5(AX)}.}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_5\)}
\]
For \(C=A\) or \(AX\), let \(E\) be the field generated by the values
of \(C\).  Conductor-five conjugation \(B\mapsto B^{-1}\) fixes
\(\mathcal H_5(C)\), so it lies in
\(E(\zeta_5+\zeta_5^{-1})\).  The nontrivial automorphism of this
quadratic extension, induced by \(B\mapsto B^2\), sends
\(\mathcal H_5(C)\) to its inverse.  Hence
\[
 \Norm_{E(\sqrt5)/E}\mathcal H_5(C)=1.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_6\)}
\]
If the forward \(X\)-tangent is normalized by
\[
 \partial_X\log\mathcal H_5(A)
 =\overline{\frac{\mathcal H_5(AX)/\mathcal H_5(A)-1}{\pi}},
\]
then \((\mathsf{FJ}_3)\) reads
\(\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)=G_1^{-1}\partial_X\log\mathcal H_5(A)\).
Order-five product formulas evaluate the invariant norm and therefore
erase, rather than evaluate, this anti-invariant tangent.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The four CRT classes have
\[
 C_{j_1}=C_{j_4}=C_1,
 \qquad C_{j_2}=C_{j_3}=-C_1.
\]
Indeed \(G_j=\lambda(j)^{-1}G_1\), while
\(\lambda(j_r)=\chi_5(r)\).
Since \(J_r\equiv-1+\pi C_{j_r}\pmod{\pi^2}\), and every
denominator is a \(\pi\)-adic unit,
\[
 \frac{(1-\pi C_1)^2}{(1+\pi C_1)^2}
 \equiv1-4\pi C_1\pmod{\pi^2}.
\]
Now use \((\mathsf{DJ}_6)\) and the nonvanishing of \(G_1\) to obtain
\((\mathsf{FJ}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{FJ}_3)\).  The minimality assertion
is the displayed two-dimensional linear calculation.

Finally the Gauss--Jacobi identity gives
\[
 \frac{J(AB,X)J(AB^{-1},X)}
      {J(AB^2,X)J(AB^{-2},X)}
 =\frac{\mathcal H_5(A)}{\mathcal H_5(AX)},
\]
because the common factor \(G(X)^2\) cancels.  Replacing \(B\) by \(B^2\)
interchanges numerator and denominator in \((\mathsf{FJ}_4)\), while
replacing it by \(B^{-1}\) only swaps factors.  This proves the last
claims.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[relative Eisenstein determinant]
\label{prop:dk-eisenstein-determinant}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-four-jacobi}, and put
\[
 S=2^{2h},\qquad K=\F_S,\qquad L=\F_{S^2}=\F_Q.
\]
The characters \(A\) and \(X\) are the norm lifts of unique characters
\(a_0,x_0\) of \(K^\times\), while \(B|_{K^\times}=1\):
\[
 A=a_0\circ\Norm_{L/K},\qquad
 X=x_0\circ\Norm_{L/K}.
\]
For \(r=1,2\), define the relative Eisenstein sums
\[
 \mathcal E_r(c)=
 \sum_{\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(z)=1}(c\circ\Norm_{L/K})(z)B^r(z).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_1\)}
\]
For every \(c\) with \(c^2\ne1\), one has
\[
 G_L((c\circ\Norm)B^r)=G_K(c^2)\mathcal E_r(c),
 \qquad \mathcal E_r(c)=\mathcal E_{-r}(c),                 \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_2\)}
\]
and the four-Jacobi antiunit has the exact quadratic-relative form
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal H_5(c\circ\Norm)
       =\left(\frac{\mathcal E_1(c)}{\mathcal E_2(c)}\right)^2,}
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal D_{k,\ell}
 =\left(
   \frac{\mathcal E_1(a_0)\mathcal E_2(a_0x_0)}
        {\mathcal E_2(a_0)\mathcal E_1(a_0x_0)}
  \right)^2.}                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_3\)}
\]

Normalize \(X(z)=\zeta_\ell^{\nu(z)}\).  At the selected prime above
\(\ell\), set
\[
 e_r=\overline{\mathcal E_r(a_0)},\qquad
 u_r=\sum_{\operatorname{Tr}z=1}\overline{A(z)B^r(z)}\,\nu(z).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_4\)}
\]
Every \(e_r\) is nonzero, and
\[
 \mathcal D_{k,\ell}
 \equiv1-2\pi\left(\frac{u_1}{e_1}-\frac{u_2}{e_2}\right)
 \pmod{\pi^2}.                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_5\)}
\]
Consequently the surviving selected coordinate is exactly the determinant
\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)
 =2G_1^{-1}\frac{\Delta_{k,\ell}}{e_1e_2},\qquad
 \Delta_{k,\ell}:=u_1e_2-u_2e_1,}                            \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_6\)}
\]
and therefore
\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell\iff\Delta_{k,\ell}\ne0.          \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_7\)}
\]

The first-order complete-\(B\)-orbit Hasse--Davenport relation does not
decide this determinant.  Indeed, for any conductor-five-fixed \(C\),
Frobenius gives
\[
 y_1=G(CB)=G(CB^{-1}),\qquad
 y_2=G(CB^2)=G(CB^{-2}),
\]
while the order-five product formula has the form
\[
 G(C)y_1^2y_2^2
 =-G(C^5)\prod_{j=0}^4G(B^j).                                \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_8\)}
\]
Thus it constrains only the invariant product \(y_1y_2\), and after
the \(X\)-linearization only
\(u_1/e_1+u_2/e_2\).  Over the odd residue characteristic, this one
symmetric linear relation leaves the anti-invariant difference
\(u_1/e_1-u_2/e_2\) free, and in particular is compatible with its
vanishing.  Therefore the first-order relation \((\mathsf{ES}_8)\) cannot
prove \((\mathsf{ES}_7)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
One has \(3h,\ell\mid S-1\), whereas \(S\equiv-1\pmod5\).
Thus \(A\) and \(X\) descend through the norm and \(B\) is trivial on
\(K^\times\).  Decompose the Gauss sum over the fibres of
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}\).  On a nonzero fibre, write \(z=tw\) with
\(t\in K^\times\) and \(\operatorname{Tr}(w)=1\).  Since
\(\Norm_{L/K}(t)=t^2\), its contribution separates into
\(G_K(c^2)\mathcal E_r(c)\).  The trace-zero fibre is \(K\), and its
contribution vanishes because \(c^2\) is nontrivial.  This proves the first
identity in \((\mathsf{ES}_2)\).  The map \(z\mapsto z^S\) fixes trace and
norm and sends \(B\) to \(B^{-1}\), proving the second.

Substitute \((\mathsf{ES}_2)\) into \((\mathsf{FJ}_4)\).  The base-field
Gauss factors cancel and the two Frobenius-paired factors agree, giving the
first formula in \((\mathsf{ES}_3)\); \((\mathsf{FJ}_5)\) gives the second.
Since
\[
 X(z)=(1+\pi)^{\nu(z)}\equiv1+\pi\nu(z)\pmod{\pi^2},
\]
one has
\[
 \mathcal E_r(a_0x_0)
 \equiv\mathcal E_r(a_0)+\pi u_r\pmod{\pi^2}.
\]
Gauss sums over fields of characteristic two are units at every prime above
the odd number \(\ell\); hence \((\mathsf{ES}_2)\) makes each \(e_r\) a
unit.  Expanding the square in \((\mathsf{ES}_3)\) proves
\((\mathsf{ES}_5)\), and comparison with \((\mathsf{FJ}_1)\) proves
\((\mathsf{ES}_6)\)--\((\mathsf{ES}_7)\).

For a unit-circle form of the same determinant, every trace-one element of
\(L\) is uniquely
\[
 z=t^{-1}v,\qquad v\in\ker\Norm_{L/K}\setminus\{1\},
 \qquad t=v+v^{-1}.
\]
Thus \(e_r\) is a quintic Soto--Andrade sum weighted by
\(a_0^{-2}(t)\), while \(u_r\) inserts the extra weight
\(-2\operatorname{ind}_{x_0}(t)\).  Their determinant is genuinely the
anti-quintic Wronskian, rather than an unweighted full-field Fourier
coefficient.

Finally, apply the order-five Hasse--Davenport formula to the complete
\(B\)-orbit of \(C\).  In characteristic two the usual \(C(5^{-5})\)
factor is one, which gives \((\mathsf{ES}_8)\).  Its invariance under
\(B\mapsto B^2\) shows directly that its first-order term is the symmetric
sum.  Since that sum imposes no nonvanishing condition on the alternating
difference, the final assertion follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[semiprimitive parity obstruction on the mixed line]
\label{prop:dk-mixed-semiprimitive-no-go}
Let \(h=3^k\), let the prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), and retain the
primitive mixed character of conductor \(M\ell=15h\ell\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}.  There is no exponent \(t\) for
which
\[
  2^t\equiv-1\pmod{5\ell}.
  \tag{\(\mathsf{SMP}_1\)}
\]
In particular, two is not semiprimitive modulo \(M\ell\).  Hence the
semiprimitive Gauss--Jacobi evaluations available for the pure
\(\ell\)-character and the proper-conductor components do not evaluate
the surviving primitive mixed coefficient \(C_1=G_1L(1)\).  More
precisely, the mixed conductor has decomposition index
\[
 \frac{\varphi(M\ell)}{\ord_{M\ell}(2)}=2(\ell-1),
 \tag{\(\mathsf{SMP}_2\)}
\]
whereas the pure \(M\)-component has index two and the pure
\(\ell\)-component is semiprimitive.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The cyclotomic-divisor criterion gives
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), and \(2^h\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).  The unique
element of order two in \(\langle2\rangle\) is therefore reached exactly
at exponents
\[
  t\equiv h\pmod{2h}.
\]
Since \(h\) is odd, every such \(t\) is odd.  On the other hand,
\(\ord_5(2)=4\), so \(2^t\equiv-1\pmod5\) forces
\(t\equiv2\pmod4\), which is even.  Thus
\((\mathsf{SMP}_1)\) is impossible.  Because \(5\ell\mid M\ell\),
semiprimitivity modulo \(M\ell\) is impossible as well.  The final
statement follows: the mixed character is precisely the component whose
first-order value is \(G_1L(1)\), while the semiprimitive proper
components are the already forced-flat coordinates of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}.

Finally \(\gcd(M,\ell)=1\), so
\[
 \ord_{M\ell}(2)
 =\operatorname{lcm}(\ord_M(2),\ord_\ell(2))
 =\operatorname{lcm}(4h,2h)=4h,
\]
while \(\varphi(M\ell)=8h(\ell-1)\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{SMP}_2)\).  Thus the two explicitly evaluable constituent
Gauss sums leave a mixed Gauss sum of growing decomposition index in
their Jacobi quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[only a weighted exceptional resolvent sees the first line]
\label{cor:dk-weighted-only}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}, put
\(U=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times\), and choose integer lifts
\(w_j\equiv\lambda(j)\pmod\ell\).  Then
\[
 \prod_{j\in U}(-J_j)\equiv1\pmod{\pi^2},
 \qquad
 \prod_{j\in U}(-J_j)^{w_j}
 \equiv1-8h\pi C_1\pmod{\pi^2}.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_1\)}
\]
The same unweighted congruence holds after adjoining all nonunit
indices \(1\leq j<M\).  Consequently every ordinary equal-weight
Hasse--Davenport product is flat whether or not \(D'_k\) holds, while
the weighted product is nonflat exactly when \(D'_k\) holds.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Changing variables in the Gauss sum of \((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\) gives
\(G_j=\lambda(j)^{-1}G_1\), hence
\(C_j=\lambda(j)^{-1}C_1\) for \(j\in U\).  Since
\(-J_j\equiv1-\pi C_j\pmod{\pi^2}\), the unweighted linear term is
\[
 -\pi C_1\sum_{j\in U}\lambda(j)^{-1}=0,
\]
whereas the weighted term is
\(-\pi|U|C_1=-8h\pi C_1\).  Here \(\ell\nmid8h\), so
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top} proves the detector assertion.
For nonunit \(j\), Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} already
gives \(J_j\equiv-1\pmod{\pi^2}\), proving the final extension.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the ordinary cyclotomic derivative is blind]
\label{prop:dk-cyclotomic-derivative-no-go}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}, write \(M=15h\), and
let \(\widetilde\rho\) be the complex primitive \(M\)-th root whose
reduction at the selected prime above two is \(z\).  Put
\(Q=2^{4h}\), choose a generator \(g\in\F_Q^\times\), and normalize
\[
 z=g^{(Q-1)/M},\qquad
 \xi=g^{(Q-1)/\ell},\qquad
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(g^v)=v\pmod\ell.
\]
Then the selected Euler residue is exactly
\[
 (1-z)^{(Q-1)/\ell}=\xi^{L(1)}.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_1\)}
\]
By contrast, the reduction of the ordinary cyclotomic derivative is
unconditionally an \(\ell\)-th power:
\[
 \overline{\Phi'_M(\widetilde\rho)}
 =z^{-1}\frac{z^h-1}{(z^{3h}-1)(z^{5h}-1)}
 \in(\F_Q^\times)^\ell.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_2\)}
\]
Thus neither \(\Phi'_M(\widetilde\rho)\) nor any formula depending
only on its ordinary reduction can evaluate the selected exponent
\(L(1)\).

There is an exact weighted replacement, but it is the original target.
For \(a\in U=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times\), choose integer lifts
\(r_a\equiv\lambda(a)^{-1}\pmod\ell\) and put, modulo global
\(\ell\)-th powers,
\[
 \varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}
   =\prod_{a\in U}(1-\widetilde\rho^a)^{r_a}.
\]
Its selected reduction satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell
   (\overline{\varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}})
 =8hL(1).                                            \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_3\)}
\]
Consequently \(D'_k\) holds at \(\ell\) exactly when this weighted
circular unit has nontrivial \(\ell\)-power residue at the selected
prime above two.

Finally, if \(\widetilde\lambda\) is the characteristic-zero lift of
the primitive even nontrivial character \(\lambda\), then
\[
 B_{1,\widetilde\lambda^{-1}}
 =-L(0,\widetilde\lambda^{-1})=0.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_4\)}
\]
This vanishing is independent of the exceptional target: for example
\(D'_2\) holds analytically by Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection},
so its selected \(L(1)\) is nonzero although
\((\mathsf{ED}_4)\) vanishes.  Hence the first generalized Bernoulli
value cannot be a nonzero scalar multiple of the selected Kummer index.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation \((\mathsf{ED}_1)\) is the Euler criterion after writing
\(1-z=g^v\): both sides are \(g^{v(Q-1)/\ell}\), and
\(v\bmod\ell=L(1)\).

Since \(M=5\cdot3^{k+1}=15h\), one has the polynomial identity
\[
 \Phi_M(X)
 =\frac{(X^M-1)(X^h-1)}{(X^{3h}-1)(X^{5h}-1)}.
\]
Differentiating at the primitive root \(\widetilde\rho\) gives
\[
 \Phi'_M(\widetilde\rho)
 =M\widetilde\rho^{-1}
   \frac{\widetilde\rho^h-1}
        {(\widetilde\rho^{3h}-1)(\widetilde\rho^{5h}-1)}.
\]
After reduction in characteristic two the odd scalar \(M\) becomes
one, proving the displayed equality in \((\mathsf{ED}_2)\).  The
element \(z\) has order \(M\), prime to \(\ell\).  Moreover
\(z^h,z^{3h},z^{5h}\) have orders \(15,5,3\), respectively, so the
three factors involving them lie in \(\F_{16}^\times\) or
\(\F_4^\times\).  All four factors therefore have order prime to
\(\ell\), and each is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(\F_Q^\times\).

The choice of the lifts \(r_a\) changes
\(\varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}\) only by an \(\ell\)-th power.  Using
\((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\) gives
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell
   (\overline{\varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}})
 =\sum_{a\in U}\lambda(a)^{-1}L(a)
 =\sum_{a\in U}L(1)=8hL(1),
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{ED}_3)\); here \(\ell\nmid8h\).
Finally, for any nontrivial even character \(\chi\) modulo \(M\),
pairing \(a\) with \(M-a\) in
\[
 B_{1,\chi}=M^{-1}\sum_{a=1}^{M}a\chi(a)
\]
and using \(\sum_a\chi(a)=0\) gives \(B_{1,\chi}=0\).  Apply this to
\(\chi=\widetilde\lambda^{-1}\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{ED}_4)\) and the final separation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the wild regulator is explicit but homogeneous]
\label{prop:dk-wild-regulator-no-go}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}.  Let
\(K_0/\mathbb Q_\ell\) be the unramified extension of degree
\(f=\operatorname{ord}_M(\ell)\) containing \(\mu_M\), with residue
field \(k_0\),
and for an unramified unit \(y\)
define
\[
 q_\ell(y)=\frac{y^{\ell^f-1}-1}{\ell}\pmod\ell.
\]
For \(\tau\in\mu_M\setminus\{1\}\), one has the finite truncated-log
formula
\[
 q_\ell(1-\tau)
 =-\frac1{1-\tau}
   \sum_{a=1}^{\ell-1}
    \frac{\tau^{a\ell^{f-1}}}{a}                    \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_1\)}
\]
in the residue field.  For the characteristic-zero antiunit
\(d_k=E_1/E_2\) of \((\mathsf{DJ}_5)\), put
\[
 \begin{split}
 A_d={}&q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta)
       +q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta^{-1})\\
      &-q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta^2)
       -q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta^{-2}).
 \end{split}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_2\)}
\]
If \([\overline d_k]\) is its Teichm\"uller part, then
\[
 d_k=[\overline d_k]
       \bigl(1-\ell A_d+O(\ell^2)\bigr).             \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_3\)}
\]

Now pass to \(K=K_0(\zeta_\ell)\), with
\(\pi=\zeta_\ell-1\).  For a mixed local selector
\[
 w=[\overline w](1+\pi B_w+O(\pi^2)),
\]
the critical explicit norm-residue formula has the form
\[
 (d_k,w)_{K,\ell}
 =\zeta_\ell^{
   \operatorname{Tr}_{k_0/\mathbb F_\ell}
       (c_\ell A_dB_w)},                             \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_4\)}
\]
where \(c_\ell\) is a nonzero scalar depending only on the
uniformizer normalization.  Base-field selectors have \(B_w=0\).
For the canonical weighted Jacobi resolvent
\[
 \mathcal R_\lambda
 =\prod_{j\in(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times}(-J_j)^{w_j},
 \qquad w_j\equiv\lambda(j)\pmod\ell,
\]
one has
\[
 B_w=-8hC_1=-8hG_1L(1),
 \qquad \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)=4L(1).          \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_5\)}
\]
Thus the wild pairing with the only canonical selector that isolates the
exceptional character is homogeneous in the still-unknown \(L(1)\).
When exceptional failure holds, both the selected Kummer symbol and the
critical wild term are one.  Explicit wild reciprocity therefore computes
the local regulator \(A_d\), but does not force
\(\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)\ne0\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because \(\tau^{\ell^f}=\tau\), expand
\((1-\tau)^{\ell^f}-(1-\tau)\) modulo \(\ell^2\).
The only interior binomial coefficients of valuation one have indices
\(a\ell^{f-1}\), \(1\leq a<\ell\), and
\[
 \frac1\ell\binom{\ell^f}{a\ell^{f-1}}
 \equiv\frac{(-1)^{a-1}}a\pmod\ell.
\]
Since \(\ell^{f-1}\) is odd, multiplication by
\((-\tau)^{a\ell^{f-1}}\), followed by division by \(1-\tau\),
proves \((\mathsf{WL}_1)\).  The Fermat quotient is additive on
products and subtractive on quotients modulo \(\ell\), which gives
\((\mathsf{WL}_2)\).  If
\(d_k=[\overline d_k](1+\ell c+O(\ell^2))\), then
\(\ell^f-1\equiv-1\pmod\ell\), so
\(q_\ell(d_k)=-c\); this proves \((\mathsf{WL}_3)\).

In \(K\), the first entry of the pairing lies in
\(U_{\ell-1}\) and the second in \(U_1\).  The critical case of
Iwasawa's explicit norm-residue formula~\cite{Iwasawa68} is the
nondegenerate residue pairing \((\mathsf{WL}_4)\); the unit converting
\(\ell\) to \(\pi^{\ell-1}\) is absorbed into \(c_\ell\).
Finally, first-order expansion of the displayed weighted product and
\((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\) give
\[
 \mathcal R_\lambda
 \equiv1-\pi\sum_{j\in U}\lambda(j)C_j
 =1-8h\pi C_1\pmod{\pi^2}.
\]
Equation \((\mathsf{DJ}_5)\) supplies the second identity in
\((\mathsf{WL}_5)\), completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[binary cyclic-parity form of exceptional failure]
\label{prop:dk-cyclic-parity}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}, set
\[
 E=\frac{Q-1}{\ell},\qquad
 S_M(X)=1+X+\cdots+X^{M-1},
\]
and let \(R_{k,\ell}(X)\) be the degree-\(<M\) reduction of
\((1+X)^E\) modulo \(X^M-1\).  Then failure of \(D'_k\) at \(\ell\)
is equivalent to
\[
 R_{k,\ell}(X)=1+S_M(X)
 \quad\text{in }\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^M-1).               \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_1\)}
\]
Equivalently, among the binary submasks \(u\) of \(E\), the number
with \(u\equiv0\pmod M\) is even, while the number in every nonzero
residue class modulo \(M\) is odd.

Moreover, raising \((\mathsf{DP}_1)\) to the \(\ell\)-th power gives
only the unconditional identity
\[
 (1+X)^{Q-1}=1+S_M(X)
 \quad\text{in }\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^M-1).               \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_2\)}
\]
Thus ordinary exponent, determinant, or product consequences of
\((\mathsf{DP}_1)\) cannot rule out exceptional failure; the
remaining assertion is exactly that this selected binary-residue
parity pattern never occurs.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} established
that failure is equivalent to
\[
 1-z^a\in(\mathbb F_Q^\times)^\ell
 \qquad(1\leq a<M).                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_3\)}
\]
Here \(\ell\mid Q-1\), and in the cyclic group
\(\mathbb F_Q^\times\) an element \(v\) is an \(\ell\)-th power
exactly when \(v^E=1\).  Consequently \((\mathsf{DP}_3)\) says that
\(R_{k,\ell}(\xi)=1\) at every nonidentity \(M\)-th root \(\xi\).
At \(\xi=1\), it has value \(0\).  Since \(M\) is odd,
\(X^M-1\) is squarefree, and \(1+S_M\) has exactly these values.
This proves \((\mathsf{DP}_1)\), including its converse by evaluation
at \(z\).

Lucas's theorem gives
\[
 (1+X)^E=\sum_{u\subseteq_2 E}X^u,
\]
so cyclic reduction proves the submask-parity formulation.  Finally
\(S_M^2=MS_M=S_M\), hence \(1+S_M\) is idempotent.  The right side of
\((\mathsf{DP}_1)\) is therefore unchanged on taking its
\(\ell\)-th power.  Identity \((\mathsf{DP}_2)\) is unconditional:
at \(X=1\) both sides vanish, while at every other \(M\)-th root
\(\xi\in\mathbb F_Q\), Fermat gives
\((1+\xi)^{Q-1}=1\).  Squarefree interpolation completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[single-deletion words exhaust the code and design bounds]
\label{prop:parity-code-no-go}
Let \(m\) be odd and put
\[
 A_m=\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^m-1),\qquad
 J_m=1+X+\cdots+X^{m-1},\qquad v_r=J_m+X^r.
\]
Then
\[
 v_r=X^r(1+J_m),\qquad
 \operatorname{supp}(v_r)=\mathbb Z/m\mathbb Z\setminus\{r\},
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_1\)}
\]
and, at the \(m\)-th roots in a splitting field,
\[
 v_r(1)=0,\qquad v_r(\xi)=\xi^r\quad(\xi\ne1).       \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_2\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \gcd(v_r,X^m-1)=X+1,                                \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_3\)}
\]
so the cyclic code generated by \(v_r\) is exactly the binary
single-parity-check code \([m,m-1,2]\).  Its coefficient support and
its finite-field Fourier support both have size \(m-1\).

The same support is the trivial cyclic difference set
\[
 (m,m-1,m-2),                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_4\)}
\]
whose nontrivial complex character sums have squared absolute value
one.  Adding the exceptional monomial gives
\[
 v_r+X^r=J_m,                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_5\)}
\]
the generator of the repetition code, whose Fourier support has size
one.

Under cubic failure, Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-cyclic-parity} gives
the word \(v_r\); under exceptional failure,
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity} gives \(v_0\).  Hence cyclic
distance bounds, support-uncertainty inequalities, ordinary
difference-set multiplier conditions, and character-magnitude
conditions all admit the two failure patterns exactly.  After
\((\mathsf{CD}_5)\), the associated vanishing sum is merely the full
regular polygon \(J_m(\zeta)=0\), which decomposes through the proper
conductor primes.  These methods discard the same primitive CRT
coordinate isolated in \((\mathsf{CP}_7)\) and
\((\mathsf{DP}_1)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Translation fixes \(J_m\), so
\(X^r(1+J_m)=X^r+J_m=v_r\), proving
\((\mathsf{CD}_1)\).  Since \(m\) is odd,
\(J_m(1)=1\) in characteristic two, while
\(J_m(\xi)=0\) for every nonidentity \(m\)-th root.  This proves
\((\mathsf{CD}_2)\).  The polynomial \(X^m-1\) is squarefree, so
one is the only common root and \((\mathsf{CD}_3)\) follows.  The
ideal generated by \(X+1\) is the even-weight code, giving the stated
parameters.

Every nonzero group element has exactly \(m-2\) representations as a
difference of two elements outside a fixed singleton.  This proves
\((\mathsf{CD}_4)\); equivalently, a nontrivial complex character has
sum \(-\chi(r)\) over the support.  Equation \((\mathsf{CD}_5)\) is
immediate.  The two applications are exactly
\((\mathsf{CP}_3)\) and \((\mathsf{DP}_1)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[higher two-adic lifts carry no odd Kummer obstruction]
\label{prop:two-adic-kummer-no-go}
Let \(\mathcal O\) be the ring of integers of a finite unramified
extension of \(\mathbb Q_2\), with residue field \(K\), and let
\(\ell\) be odd.  Then
\[
 1+2\mathcal O\longrightarrow1+2\mathcal O,
 \qquad x\longmapsto x^\ell                         \tag{\(\mathsf{2K}_1\)}
\]
is an automorphism.  Under the Teichm\"uller splitting
\[
 \mathcal O^\times=[K^\times]\times(1+2\mathcal O), \tag{\(\mathsf{2K}_2\)}
\]
a unit is therefore an \(\ell\)-th power exactly when its residue in
\(K^\times\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.

The statement applies coordinatewise to the unramified lift of
\(\mathbb F_2[C_m]\) for odd \(m\), because that group algebra is
squarefree and hence a product of finite fields.  Consequently
modulo-four, modulo-eight, and higher carry refinements of the cubic or
exceptional parity identities cannot supply an independent obstruction:
the principal-unit lift is uniquely \(\ell\)-divisible once the
selected residue Kummer coordinate is fixed.  The same conclusion
applies to the Fermat and ordinary arms.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(u\in1+2\mathcal O\), the polynomial \(Y^\ell-u\) has the root
one modulo two and derivative \(\ell Y^{\ell-1}\), which is a unit.
Hensel's lemma gives a unique root in \(1+2\mathcal O\).  Thus
\((\mathsf{2K}_1)\) is bijective.  The standard Teichm\"uller
decomposition gives \((\mathsf{2K}_2)\) and the power criterion.  The
coordinatewise assertion follows from the squarefreeness of
\(X^m-1\) in characteristic two when \(m\) is odd.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exceptional half-block norm and its automatic symmetries]
\label{prop:dk-half-block}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity}, and put
\[
 A=2^{2h},\qquad u=\frac{A-1}{\ell},\qquad
 R=\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^M-1),\qquad e=1+S_M.
\]
Let \(I=eR\), with identity \(e\), and set
\[
 U=1+X\in I^\times,\qquad \tau(X)=X^A,\qquad F=U^u.
\]
Then
\[
 F^\ell=\frac{\tau(U)}U,\qquad
 F\tau(F)=U^{u(A+1)},                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_1\)}
\]
and exceptional failure is exactly
\[
 F\tau(F)=e.                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_2\)}
\]
On the selected primitive \(M\)-th-root component of
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, put
\[
 w=(1+\rho)(1+\rho^A)\in\mathbb F_A^\times,\qquad
 \upsilon=w^u\in\mu_\ell.
\]
Then failure is precisely \(\upsilon=1\).

There is no further Hilbert--90 or quadratic-norm obstruction to
\((\mathsf{DH}_2)\).  Indeed \(\tau(U)/U\) belongs to the norm-one
group of \(\mathbb F_{A^2}/\mathbb F_A\), whose order is \(A+1\);
since \(\gcd(\ell,A+1)=1\), it has a unique \(\ell\)-th root in that
group.  Failure says only that the distinguished root \(F=U^u\) is
this norm-one root.

The coefficient symmetries are automatic as well.  The
\(\tau\)-fixed residues modulo \(M\) are exactly the multiples of
five.  For such a residue \(j\),
\[
 [X^j]F\tau(F)
 =\sum_{\substack{a\pmod M\\(1+A)a=j}}[X^a]F.          \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_3\)}
\]
After identifying the fixed subgroup with \(C_{3h}\), the right side
is, up to an invertible multiplier of its indices, the cyclic
reduction of \((1+Y)^u\); unconditionally,
\[
 (1+Y)^u=e_{3h}\quad\hbox{in }\mathbb F_2[C_{3h}].    \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_4\)}
\]
Thus the fixed-residue part of the exceptional parity pattern is
forced before the selected Kummer question is asked.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(U^A=\tau(U)\), the first identity in
\((\mathsf{DH}_1)\) is
\[
 F^\ell=U^{A-1}=\frac{\tau(U)}U.
\]
The second follows by multiplying \(U^u\) and
\(\tau(U)^u=U^{Au}\).  Since
\[
 u(A+1)=\frac{A^2-1}{\ell}=\frac{Q-1}{\ell},
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity} proves
\((\mathsf{DH}_2)\).  On a primitive component, \(\tau\) is the
quadratic Frobenius of
\(\mathbb F_{A^2}/\mathbb F_A\), and evaluation gives
\(F\tau(F)=w^u=\upsilon\).  The norm-one assertion and uniqueness
then follow from
\(\ell\mid A-1\) and \(\gcd(A-1,A+1)=1\).

One has
\[
 \gcd(A-1,M)=3h,\qquad \gcd(A+1,M)=5.
\]
Hence the fixed residues of multiplication by \(A\) are precisely
the multiples of five.  In the convolution coefficient of such a
residue, swapping the two selected submasks preserves the residue.
Every off-diagonal pair cancels in characteristic two, leaving only
the diagonal pairs; this is \((\mathsf{DH}_3)\).  Multiplication by
\((A+1)/5\) is invertible modulo \(3h\), so the displayed pushforward
is the reduction of \((1+Y)^u\), up to a permutation of coefficients.

Put \(q=2^h\).  If \(\xi\ne1\) is a \(3h\)-th root of unity, then
\(\xi^q=\xi^{-1}\), and therefore
\[
 (1+\xi)^{q-1}=\xi^{-1}.
\]
Also \(u=(q-1)(q+1)/\ell\), while
\(3h\mid(q+1)/\ell\).  It follows that
\((1+\xi)^u=1\).  At \(\xi=1\) it vanishes, so squarefree
interpolation proves \((\mathsf{DH}_4)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-coordinate trace form of the exceptional phase]
\label{prop:dk-selected-trace}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-half-block}, put \(s=h/3\), and let
\[
 B\equiv2^s\pmod\ell.
\]
Then \(B\) has order six modulo \(\ell\) and
\(B^2-B+1=0\).  For \(\upsilon\in\mu_\ell\subset
\mathbb F_{2^{6s}}\), define
\[
 \mathcal T(\upsilon)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathbb F_{2^{6s}}/\mathbb F_{2^s}}(\upsilon)
 =\sum_{j=0}^{5}\upsilon^{B^j}.
\]
One has the exact equivalence
\[
 \mathcal T(\upsilon)=0\iff\upsilon=1.                \tag{\(\mathsf{DT}_1\)}
\]
Consequently the exceptional assertion at \(\ell\) is exactly
\[
 \mathcal T\!\left(
   \bigl((1+\rho)(1+\rho^A)\bigr)^{(A-1)/\ell}
 \right)\ne0.                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{DT}_2\)}
\]
This is a single relative-trace coordinate of the selected residue
symbol.  It is equivalent to, rather than a generic norm consequence
of, the missing nontriviality.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The order identity \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h=6s\) proves the assertions about
\(B\).  Put \(x=\upsilon\) and \(y=\upsilon^B\).  The six powers of
\(B\), together with \(B^2=B-1\), give the elementary factorization
\[
 xy\,\mathcal T(\upsilon)
 =(x+y)(x+1)(y+1).                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{DT}_3\)}
\]
If \(\upsilon\ne1\), then \(x\ne1\), \(y\ne1\), and
\(x\ne y\), because \(\ell\) is prime and
\(B\not\equiv1\pmod\ell\).  Thus the right side is nonzero.  Conversely
\(\mathcal T(1)=6=0\) in characteristic two, proving
\((\mathsf{DT}_1)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-half-block} identifies
failure with \(\upsilon=1\), so its negation is
\((\mathsf{DT}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Cyclotomic antiunit and regulator bounds}

\begin{proposition}[quadratic-twist antiunit and the two exceptional zeros]
\label{prop:dk-antiunit}
Retain Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, put
\(m=3h\), and write the unique coprime-order decomposition
\[
 \widetilde\rho=\alpha\beta,
 \qquad \ord(\alpha)=m,\qquad \ord(\beta)=5.
\]
Set
\[
 s=\beta+\beta^{-1},\qquad s'=\beta^2+\beta^{-2},
\]
and define the cyclotomic antiunit
\[
 d_k:=
 \frac{(1-\alpha\beta)(1-\alpha\beta^{-1})}
      {(1-\alpha\beta^2)(1-\alpha\beta^{-2})}
 =\frac{\alpha^2-s\alpha+1}{\alpha^2-s'\alpha+1}
 =\frac{\alpha+\alpha^{-1}-s}
        {\alpha+\alpha^{-1}-s'}.                        \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_1\)}
\]
Then \(d_k\) belongs to
\[
 F_k^{\mathrm{tw}}=\mathbb Q(\alpha+\alpha^{-1},\sqrt5),
\]
is fixed by complex conjugation, and is inverted by the nontrivial
automorphism of
\(F_k^{\mathrm{tw}}/\mathbb Q(\alpha+\alpha^{-1})\).  In particular its relative
norm is one.

The invariant conductor-five norm loses exactly the information carried
by \(d_k\):
\[
 \Norm_{\mathbb Q(\mu_{5m})/\mathbb Q(\mu_m)}(1-\widetilde\rho)
 =\Phi_5(\alpha)=\frac{1-\alpha^5}{1-\alpha}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_2\)}
\]
More precisely, reduce at the prime selected in
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, write
\[
 a=\overline\alpha,\quad b=\overline\beta,\quad
 c=b+b^{-1}\in\F_4\setminus\F_2,
\]
and put, in \(K=\F_{2^{2h}}\),
\[
 U_k=a^2+ca+1,\qquad U'_k=a^2+c^2a+1,\qquad
 R_k=\frac{U_k}{U'_k}=\overline{d_k}.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_3\)}
\]
For every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), the element \(\Phi_5(a)\) is an
\(\ell\)-th power in \(K\), and
\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff U_k\notin (K^\times)^\ell
 \iff R_k\notin (K^\times)^\ell.                        \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_4\)}
\]
Thus the two conductor-five places over the cubic field carry inverse
Kummer classes; their invariant product is identically blind.

This antiunit is an exact quadratic lift of a conjugate of the cubic
Gaussian period.
If
\[
 q=2^h,\qquad \gamma=a+a^{-1},\qquad
 A=\gamma^2+\gamma+1,
\]
then
\[
 R_k^q=R_k^{-1},\qquad
 R_k+R_k^{-1}=A^{-1},\qquad
 \frac{R_k}{(R_k+1)^2}=A.                                \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_5\)}
\]
After choosing compatible roots \(a_k^3=a_{k-1}\) and keeping \(c\)
fixed, the degree-three norm swaps the two factors:
\[
 \Norm(U_k)=U'_{k-1},\qquad
 \Norm(U'_k)=U_{k-1},\qquad
 \Norm(R_k)=R_{k-1}^{-1}.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_6\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \mathcal E_k:=R_k^{(-1)^k}
 \quad\text{satisfies}\quad
 \Norm(\mathcal E_k)=\mathcal E_{k-1}.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_7\)}
\]

Finally the cubic and exceptional arms are the untwisted and
quadratic-modulo-five twists of the same simple exceptional zero.  On
the current Kummer line the \(3\)-power cyclotomic generator acts as
\(+2\) for \(\ell\mid\Phi_h(2)\), and as \(-2\) for
\(\ell\mid\Phi_h(-2)=\Phi_{2h}(2)\).  With \(r=h/3\), the relative
norm polynomial
\[
 g_r(X)=1+X^r+X^{2r}
\]
has a simple zero at \(X=2\) in the cubic case and at \(X=-2\) in the
exceptional case.  Hence the norm coherence in
\((\mathsf{QT}_7)\) transports old factors but annihilates the current
one.  It does not prove the still-open nonvanishing in
\((\mathsf{QT}_4)\); both arms require the distinguished first-order
unit regulator in their respective \(\pm2\)-eigenspaces.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The coprime orders \(m\) and \(5\) give the unique decomposition
\(\widetilde\rho=\alpha\beta\).  The four automorphisms of
\(\mathbb Q(\mu_{5m})/\mathbb Q(\mu_m)\) fix \(\alpha\) and send
\(\beta\) through the four primitive fifth roots.  Therefore
\[
 \prod_{j=1}^4(1-\alpha\beta^j)
 =\Phi_5(\alpha)=\frac{1-\alpha^5}{1-\alpha},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{QT}_2)\).  The two values \(s,s'\) are the
roots of \(X^2+X-1\), so their interchange is the nontrivial
automorphism of \(\mathbb Q(\sqrt5)\).  It interchanges the numerator
and denominator of \(d_k\).  Complex conjugation replaces \(\alpha\)
by \(\alpha^{-1}\) and multiplies both quadratic factors by
\(\alpha^{-2}\), so their quotient is fixed.  This proves all the
characteristic-zero assertions preceding \((\mathsf{QT}_2)\).

The residue of \(\alpha\) has order \(m\) and lies in \(K\), while the
residue of \(\beta\) lies in \(\F_{16}\).  Since
\[
 2^{2h}\equiv1\pmod m,\qquad 2^{2h}\equiv-1\pmod5,
\]
the local norm from \(L=\F_{2^{4h}}\) to \(K\) gives
\[
 \Norm_{L/K}(1+ab)=(1+ab)(1+ab^{-1})=U_k.
\]
Also \(2^{2h}+1\equiv5\pmod{15}\), so
\(\Norm_{L/K}(u)=u^5=\omega\).  Hence the corrected norm \(N\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial} is \(\omega U_k\).
Because \(\ell\ne3\), it has the same \(\ell\)-Kummer class as
\(U_k\).

It remains to show that the invariant product is blind.  If \(x=1+a\),
then \(a^q=a^{-1}\) and
\[
 x^{q-1}=a^{-1}.
\]
Here \(\ell\nmid q-1\), whereas \(a\) has order \(m\), prime to
\(\ell\).  Projection to the \(\ell\)-Sylow subgroup of \(K^\times\)
therefore shows that \(1+a\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  The same argument
applies to \(1+a^5\).  Thus
\[
 U_kU'_k=\Phi_5(a)=\frac{1+a^5}{1+a}
 \in (K^\times)^\ell.
\]
In additive notation on the one-dimensional Kummer quotient this says
\([U'_k]=-[U_k]\), and hence \([R_k]=2[U_k]\).  Since \(\ell\) is
odd, Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial} now proves
\((\mathsf{QT}_4)\).

As \(h\) is odd, \(q\)-Frobenius sends \(c\) to \(c^2\).  Directly,
\[
 U_k^q=a^{-2}U'_k,\qquad (U'_k)^q=a^{-2}U_k,
\]
which gives \(R_k^q=R_k^{-1}\).  Moreover
\[
 U_k+U'_k=a,\qquad U_kU'_k=\Phi_5(a).
\]
It follows that
\[
 R_k+R_k^{-1}
 =\frac{a^2}{\Phi_5(a)}
 =\frac1{(a+a^{-1})^2+(a+a^{-1})+1}.
\]
The last identity in \((\mathsf{QT}_5)\) is the characteristic-two
identity
\(R/(R+1)^2=1/(R+R^{-1})\).

The nonvanishing in \((\mathsf{QT}_4)\) is unchanged when \(a\) is
replaced by any primitive \(m\)-th root or \(c\) is interchanged with
\(c^2\): the former roots form one Frobenius orbit, and odd Frobenius
powers merely perform the latter interchange.  We may therefore choose
\(a_k^3=a_{k-1}\).  Factoring
\[
 U_k=(1+a_kb)(1+a_kb^{-1})
\]
and, after the harmless quadratic scalar extension adjoining \(b\),
taking the product over the three conjugates obtained by multiplying
\(a_k\) by a cube root of unity gives
\[
 \Norm(U_k)
 =(1+a_{k-1}b^3)(1+a_{k-1}b^{-3})=U'_{k-1}.
\]
The same calculation with \(b^2\) gives
\(\Norm(U'_k)=U_{k-1}\), proving
\((\mathsf{QT}_6)\)--\((\mathsf{QT}_7)\).

For the last assertion, let \(\sigma\) be the \(3\)-power cyclotomic
generator.  In the untwisted real field its reduction is Frobenius, so
it acts as \(2\) on the current \(\ell\)-Kummer line.  In the quadratic
twist, rational Frobenius is the product of \(\sigma\) and the
\(\sqrt5\)-involution.  The latter reduces to \(q\)-Frobenius and acts
as \(q=-1\) because \(\ell\mid q+1\); hence \(\sigma\) acts as
\(-2\).  If \(\ell\mid\Phi_h(2)\), then \(2^r\) is a primitive cube
root modulo \(\ell\).  If \(\ell\mid\Phi_h(-2)\) and \(\ell\ne3\),
then \((-2)^r\) is a primitive cube root instead.  Thus \(g_r\) vanishes
at the asserted scalar.  Its derivative there is
\[
 g_r'(X)=rX^{r-1}(1+2X^r)\ne0\pmod\ell:
\]
one has \(\ell\nmid r\), and a primitive cube root equal to
\(-1/2\) would force \(\ell\mid9\).  The zero is therefore simple.
The final open/non-open distinction follows: the norm operator vanishes
on the current line, while its first derivative only identifies the
one-dimensional regulator coordinate and does not evaluate \(d_k\) in
that coordinate.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the four binary Euler blocks are the antiunit phase]
\label{prop:dk-four-block-antiunit}
Retain Propositions~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity} and
\ref{prop:dk-antiunit}.  Put
\[
 q=2^h,\qquad Q=q^4,\qquad
 d=\frac{q+1}{\ell},\qquad E=\frac{Q-1}{\ell},
\]
and define
\[
 A_0=q-d,\qquad B_0=d-1.
\]
Then \(A_0+B_0=q-1\), so \(A_0\) and \(B_0\) are complementary
\(h\)-bit words, and
\[
 \boxed{
 E=A_0+B_0q+A_0q^2+B_0q^3,\qquad
 \operatorname{wt}_2(E)=2h.}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{DB}_1\)}
\]
Thus the complete binary expansion of the exceptional Euler exponent has
four alternating blocks.

Use the residue notation from \((\mathsf{QT}_3)\): in
\(L=\F_{q^4}\), write
\[
 \rho=ab,\qquad
 \ord(a)=3h,\qquad \ord(b)=5,
\]
and let \(R_k=\overline{d_k}\in K=\F_{q^2}\).  Then the four-block
factorization has the exact oriented value
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 (1+\rho)^E
 &= (1+\rho)^{A_0}(1+\rho^q)^{B_0}
    (1+\rho^{q^2})^{A_0}(1+\rho^{q^3})^{B_0}\\
 &=\left(
   \frac{(1+\rho^q)(1+\rho^{q^3})}
        {(1+\rho)(1+\rho^{q^2})}
   \right)^d
 =R_k^{-d}.
 \end{aligned}}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{DB}_2\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff (1+\rho)^E\ne1
 \iff R_k^d\ne1.}                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{DB}_3\)}
\]
Hence the four binary submask blocks in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity} and the conductor-five antiunit in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-antiunit} are inverse presentations of the same
selected phase, not independent constraints.  The identity does not prove
that this phase is nontrivial.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The order identity \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\) gives \(\ell\mid q+1\).
Since \(q+1=\ell d\), direct algebra gives
\[
 \frac{q^4-1}{\ell}
 =d(q-1)(1+q^2)
 =A_0+B_0q+A_0q^2+B_0q^3,
\]
which is \((\mathsf{DB}_1)\).  The equality
\(q-d=(q-1)-(d-1)\) says exactly that the two \(h\)-bit blocks are
bitwise complements, proving the weight assertion.

In characteristic two, Frobenius and \((\mathsf{DB}_1)\) give the first
line of \((\mathsf{DB}_2)\).  Put
\[
 D_0=(1+\rho)(1+\rho^{q^2}),\qquad
 N_0=(1+\rho^q)(1+\rho^{q^3}).
\]
These are nonzero because \(\rho\) has order \(15h\), and
\(D_0^q=N_0\).  Therefore
\[
 D_0^{q-d}N_0^{d-1}=(N_0/D_0)^d,
\]
which proves the middle equality.

Now \(a^q=a^{-1}\), \(a^{q^2}=a\), and
\(b^q\in\{b^2,b^{-2}\}\), while
\(b^{q^2}=b^{-1}\).  Hence
\[
 D_0=U_k,\qquad N_0=a^{-2}U'_k,\qquad
 \frac{N_0}{D_0}=a^{-2}R_k^{-1}.
\]
Also \(3h\mid q+1\), and \(\ell\nmid3h\), so \(3h\mid d\).
Thus \(a^{-2d}=1\), proving the last equality in
\((\mathsf{DB}_2)\).

Finally \(R_k^q=R_k^{-1}\), so \(R_k\) lies in the cyclic norm-one
group of order \(q+1=\ell d\).  Its Kummer class is trivial exactly when
\(R_k^d=1\).  Equations \((\mathsf{DP}_3)\) and
\((\mathsf{QT}_4)\) now give \((\mathsf{DB}_3)\).

Lean kernel-checks the four-block exponent identity and its
cross-multiplied commutative-group form.  The finite-field Frobenius,
binary-complement, and antiunit specializations remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[alternating translated root chain and the full-norm boundary]
\label{prop:dk-alternating-translate-chain}
Put \(E_j=\F_{2^{2\cdot3^j}}\), choose compatible primitive
\(3^{j+1}\)-st roots \(a_j\in E_j\) with \(a_j^3=a_{j-1}\), and put
\(\gamma_j=a_j+a_j^{-1}\), so that \(\gamma_0=1\) and
\(\gamma_j^3+\gamma_j=\gamma_{j-1}\).  Fix the orientation
\(c_k=c\in\F_4\setminus\F_2\) used in \((\mathsf{QT}_3)\), and define
recursively
\[
 c_{j-1}=c_j^2,\qquad u_j=\gamma_j+c_j\quad(0\le j\le k).
\]
Then, for every \(j\ge1\),
\[
 \boxed{\quad u_j^3+c_j u_j^2+c_j u_j=u_{j-1},\qquad
 \Norm_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(u_j)=u_{j-1}.\quad}             \tag{\(\mathsf{AT}_1\)}
\]
More precisely,
\[
 T^3+c_jT^2+c_jT+u_{j-1}                              \tag{\(\mathsf{AT}_2\)}
\]
is the irreducible minimal polynomial of \(u_j\) over \(E_{j-1}\).
The same translate is an Artin--Schreier root of the actual auxiliary
coefficient:
\[
 u_j^2+u_j=g_j=\gamma_j^2+\gamma_j+1.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{AT}_3\)}
\]

Let \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\) be current, so
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2\cdot3^k\).  Then exceptional failure at \(\ell\) is
exactly
\[
 u_k\in(E_k^\times)^\ell.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AT}_4\)}
\]
Equivalently, for the normalized root \(w_k\) of
\((\mathsf{CD}_7)\),
\[
 w_k\in(E_k^\times)^\ell
 \iff u_k\in(E_k^\times)^\ell.                     \tag{\(\mathsf{AT}_5\)}
\]
This is an equivalence of the two vanishing tests; it does not identify
\(w_k\) with \(u_k\), nor their Kummer classes with a specified scalar.
Their Artin--Schreier coefficients are respectively
\(Z_k=g_k^{2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}-2}\) and \(g_k\).

Under failure choose \(v_k\in E_k^\times\) with \(v_k^\ell=u_k\).
For every \(j<k\), let \(v_j\in E_j^\times\) be the unique element with
\(v_j^\ell=u_j\).  Then
\[
 \boxed{\quad \Norm_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(v_j)=v_{j-1}
        \quad(1\le j\le k),\qquad v_0=u_0=c_0^2.\quad} \tag{\(\mathsf{AT}_6\)}
\]
Consequently a hypothetical failure is compatible with the complete
selected cubic recursion, every downward multiplicative norm, and the
unique lower Kummer roots, all the way to \(\F_4\).  Thus no descent
which factors only through norms or Kummer corestriction can contradict
failure at the bottom.  This does not exclude a nonlinear
additive--multiplicative relation involving an \(\ell\)-th root and the
full twisted cubics \((\mathsf{AT}_2)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(c_j^2+c_j=1\), substituting
\(\gamma_j=u_j+c_j\) into
\(\gamma_j^3+\gamma_j=\gamma_{j-1}\) gives the first identity in
\((\mathsf{AT}_1)\).  The finite-field degree calculation
\([E_{j-1}(\gamma_j):E_{j-1}]=3\)---equivalently, the quadratic constant
extension is disjoint from the selected cubic extension---shows that
\(T^3+T+\gamma_{j-1}\) remains irreducible over \(E_{j-1}\).
Translation by \(c_j\) then proves \((\mathsf{AT}_2)\), and its constant
coefficient gives the norm identity.  Directly,
\[
 (\gamma_j+c_j)^2+(\gamma_j+c_j)
 =\gamma_j^2+\gamma_j+(c_j^2+c_j)=g_j,
\]
which is \((\mathsf{AT}_3)\).

At the top level, \((\mathsf{QT}_3)\) has
\[
 U_k=a_k^2+c_ka_k+1
     =a_k(a_k+a_k^{-1}+c_k)=a_ku_k.
\]
Because \(\ell\nmid3^{k+1}=\ord(a_k)\), the element \(a_k\) is an
\(\ell\)-th power in \(E_k^\times\).  Hence \((\mathsf{QT}_4)\)
gives \((\mathsf{AT}_4)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:cd-selector-bridge}
gives failure iff \(w_k\) is an \(\ell\)-th power, proving
\((\mathsf{AT}_5)\) without identifying the two roots.

For \(j<k\), the order identity for \(\ell\) gives
\(\ell\nmid 2^{2\cdot3^j}-1\); hence \(\ell\)-powering is bijective on
\(E_j^\times\).  Multiplicativity of norm now gives
\[
 \Norm(v_j)^\ell=\Norm(v_j^\ell)
 =\Norm(u_j)=u_{j-1}=v_{j-1}^\ell,
\]
and injectivity downstairs proves \((\mathsf{AT}_6)\).  Finally
\(u_0=1+c_0=c_0^2\), while
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2\cdot3^k\mid\ell-1\) implies
\(\ell\equiv1\pmod3\); thus \((c_0^2)^\ell=c_0^2\), giving the stated
terminal root.

Lean kernel-checks the characteristic-two translate, Artin--Schreier,
and symmetric product identities, multiplication by a known power, and
the norm-of-a-power-root uniqueness step.  The finite-field tower,
order, and Kummer interpretation remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[alternating twisted-cubic Kummer fibre]
\label{prop:dk-twisted-cubic-fibre}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-alternating-translate-chain}, and let
\(\ell\mid\Psi_k\) be current.  For \(0\leq i<k\), let
\(v_i\in E_i^\times\) be the unique element satisfying
\[
 v_i^\ell=u_i.
\]
Thus \(v_0=u_0=c_0^2\), and
\[
 \Norm_{E_i/E_{i-1}}(v_i)=v_{i-1}\qquad(1\leq i<k).
 \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_1\)}
\]
Use the generalized cubic power map of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-iterated-fibre},
\[
 \Psi_{\ell,E}(C,D)
 =
 \bigl(T_\ell(C,D,E),T_\ell(D,EC,E^2)\bigr).
\]
For \(1\leq j\leq k\), put \(K_j=E_{j-1}\) and define the fixed-constant
coefficient fibre
\[
 \mathcal X_j
 =
 \Psi_{\ell,v_{j-1}}^{-1}(c_j,c_j)
 \subset\mathbb A^2_{K_j}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_2\)}
\]
Then \(\mathcal X_j\) is finite reduced \'etale of geometric degree
\(\ell^2\).  Every \(K_j\)-rational point \((C,D)\) gives an
irreducible cubic
\[
 X^3+CX^2+DX+v_{j-1}.                                \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_3\)}
\]
If \(J\) denotes the Jacobian determinant of
\(\Psi_{\ell,v_{j-1}}\), then at every geometric point of
\(\mathcal X_j\),
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 (CD+v_{j-1})J
 =c_j^2+u_{j-1}
 =\gamma_{j-1}\ne0.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_4\)}
\]
Its rational-point counts are
\[
 \#\mathcal X_j(K_j)=1\qquad(1\leq j<k),              \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_5\)}
\]
where the unique point is the coefficient pair of the minimal
polynomial of \(v_j\), while
\[
 \#\mathcal X_k(E_{k-1})
 =
 \begin{cases}
 0,&u_k\notin(E_k^\times)^\ell,\\
 \ell,&u_k\in(E_k^\times)^\ell.
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_6\)}
\]

More explicitly, put
\[
 Q=|E_{k-1}|=2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}},\qquad
 P_k(X)=X^3+c_kX^2+c_kX+u_{k-1}.
\]
Under failure choose \(v_k^\ell=u_k\) and a primitive
\(\rho\in\mu_\ell\subset E_k\).  For \(r\in\mathbb Z/\ell\mathbb Z\)
set
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 C_r={}&
 \rho^rv_k+\rho^{rQ}v_k^Q+\rho^{rQ^2}v_k^{Q^2},\\
 D_r={}&
 \rho^{r(1+Q)}v_k^{1+Q}
 +\rho^{r(Q+Q^2)}v_k^{Q+Q^2}
 +\rho^{r(Q^2+1)}v_k^{Q^2+1}.
 \end{aligned}                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_7\)}
\]
Then \(C_r,D_r\in E_{k-1}\), the resulting cubics are pairwise
distinct and irreducible, and
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 P_k(X^\ell)
 =
 \prod_{r\bmod\ell}
 \left(X^3+C_rX^2+D_rX+v_{k-1}\right).
 \quad}                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{TF}_8\)}
\]
On success \(P_k(X^\ell)\) is irreducible of degree \(3\ell\).
Every factor in \((\mathsf{TF}_8)\) has constant coefficient
\(v_{k-1}\), whose unique norm-coherent descendants are precisely
\(v_{k-2},\ldots,v_0=c_0^2\).  Thus every bad top lift retains the
same complete selected lower Kummer ancestry.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(i<k\), the order identity
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2\cdot3^k\) gives
\(\ell\nmid|E_i^\times|\), so \(\ell\)-powering is bijective on
\(E_i^\times\).  This proves existence and uniqueness of \(v_i\);
the norm identity in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-alternating-translate-chain} then proves
\((\mathsf{TF}_1)\).

Let \(y_1,y_2,y_3\) be the three roots of
\[
 X^3+c_jX^2+c_jX+u_{j-1}.
\]
Choose independently an \(\ell\)-th root \(x_1\) of \(y_1\) and an
\(\ell\)-th root \(x_2\) of \(y_2\), and set
\[
 x_3=\frac{v_{j-1}}{x_1x_2}.
\]
Since \(y_1y_2y_3=u_{j-1}=v_{j-1}^{\ell}\), one has
\(x_3^\ell=y_3\).  Hence there are exactly \(\ell^2\) geometric source
triples with product \(v_{j-1}\).  The distinct target roots recover
the labels after powering, so two such triples give the same coefficient
point only when they agree.  This proves the geometric degree.

The universal identity
\[
 (CD+E)J=AB+E^\ell
\]
from Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-iterated-fibre}, specialized to
\(A=B=c_j\) and \(E=v_{j-1}\), gives
\[
 (CD+v_{j-1})J
 =c_j^2+v_{j-1}^{\ell}
 =c_j^2+u_{j-1}.
\]
But
\[
 u_{j-1}=\gamma_{j-1}+c_{j-1}
         =\gamma_{j-1}+c_j^2,
\]
so the right-hand side is \(\gamma_{j-1}\).  This is nonzero:
\(\gamma_{j-1}=a_{j-1}+a_{j-1}^{-1}=0\) would imply
\(a_{j-1}^2=1\), contradicting its primitive \(3^j\)-order.
Thus both \(CD+v_{j-1}\) and \(J\) are nonzero at every point, proving
reducedness and \'etaleness.

A reducible cubic \((\mathsf{TF}_3)\) over the finite field \(K_j\)
would have a \(K_j\)-root.  Its \(\ell\)-th power would be a
\(K_j\)-root of the irreducible selected target cubic, which is
impossible.  Hence every rational point is irreducible.

For \(j<k\), \(\ell\)-powering is bijective on \(E_j^\times\), so the
three target roots have one Frobenius orbit of unique preimages.  Their
common coefficient pair is therefore the sole \(K_j\)-rational point.
Its constant coefficient is \(v_{j-1}\), because its norm has
\(\ell\)-th power \(u_{j-1}\) and powering is injective on
\(K_j^\times\).  This proves \((\mathsf{TF}_5)\).

At \(j=k\), a rational point supplies an \(\ell\)-th root in \(E_k\)
of a conjugate of \(u_k\), hence, after Frobenius conjugation, an
\(\ell\)-th root of \(u_k\).  Thus there are no rational points on
success.  Under failure the \(\ell\) elements
\(\rho^rv_k\) are exactly the roots above the chosen root \(u_k\).
Two of them could give the same coefficient pair only if
\(\rho^rv_k\) were a \(Q\)-Frobenius conjugate of
\(\rho^sv_k\).  Raising to the \(\ell\)-th power would give
\(u_k=u_k^{Q^i}\), forcing \(i=0\) by the selected cubic's
irreducibility, and then \(r=s\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{TF}_6)\).

Finally \(\ord_\ell(Q)=3\), so
\(1+Q+Q^2\equiv0\pmod\ell\).  Frobenius cycles the three roots
\[
 \rho^rv_k,\quad \rho^{rQ}v_k^Q,\quad
 \rho^{rQ^2}v_k^{Q^2},
\]
and their product is
\[
 \rho^{r(1+Q+Q^2)}\Norm_{E_k/E_{k-1}}(v_k)=v_{k-1}.
\]
Their elementary coefficients are exactly
\((\mathsf{TF}_7)\), proving \((\mathsf{TF}_8)\).
On success, \(X^\ell-u_k\) is irreducible over \(E_k\) by Kummer
theory, and Capelli's theorem makes \(P_k(X^\ell)\) irreducible over
\(E_{k-1}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fourier diagonalization of the twisted Kummer fibre]
\label{prop:dk-weighted-fibre}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-twisted-cubic-fibre}, put
\[
 K=E_{k-1},\qquad Q=|K|,\qquad
 e=\frac{Q^3-1}{\ell},
\]
and choose, in an algebraic closure, \(v^\ell=u_k\) and a primitive
\(\rho\in\mu_\ell\subset E_k\).  Define \(C_r,D_r\) by
\((\mathsf{TF}_7)\), whether or not they are \(K\)-rational, and put
\[
 \widehat C_m=\sum_{r\bmod\ell}\rho^{-mr}C_r,\qquad
 \widehat D_m=\sum_{r\bmod\ell}\rho^{-mr}D_r.
\]
All indices below are modulo \(\ell\).  The transforms have exactly six
nonzero coordinates:
\[
 \widehat C_m=
 \begin{cases}
 v,&m=1,\\
 v^Q,&m=Q,\\
 v^{Q^2},&m=Q^2,\\
 0,&\text{otherwise},
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{WF}_1\)}
\]
and
\[
 \widehat D_m=
 \begin{cases}
 v^{1+Q},&m=1+Q=-Q^2,\\
 v^{Q+Q^2},&m=Q+Q^2=-1,\\
 v^{Q^2+1},&m=Q^2+1=-Q,\\
 0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{WF}_2\)}
\]
Thus the first weighted resolvent is literally
\(\widehat C_1=v\); the three \(D\)-coordinates contain no further
datum.

More precisely, put
\[
 n=v^{1+Q+Q^2},\qquad
 \Delta=\frac{n}{v_{k-1}}\in\mu_\ell,\qquad
 \Omega=u_k^e\in\mu_\ell.
\]
Then
\[
 n^\ell=u_{k-1},\qquad
 \boxed{\Delta^{Q-1}=\Omega.}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{WF}_3\)}
\]
The element \(n\), and hence \(\Delta\), is unchanged under
\(v\mapsto\rho^av\).  Since \(\gcd(Q-1,\ell)=1\),
\[
 \begin{split}
 D'_k\text{ fails at }\ell
 &\iff \Omega=1
 \iff \Delta=1
 \iff n=v_{k-1}\\
 &\iff C_r,D_r\in K\quad\text{for every }r.
 \end{split}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{WF}_4\)}
\]
The last equality retains the complete lower ancestry exactly:
\(v_{k-1}\) has the unique norm-coherent descendants
\(v_{k-2},\ldots,v_0=c_0^2\).  No descent of the individual
\(C_r,D_r\) is asserted.

There is also a sharp rational-eigenweight boundary.  Let
\(\mu_\ell\) act on \(E_k(V)\) by \(V\mapsto\rho V\).  For every
\(m\in\mathbb Z/\ell\mathbb Z\),
\[
 \{F:F(\rho V)=\rho^mF(V)\}
   =V^mE_k(V^\ell).                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{WF}_5\)}
\]
If \(F(V)=V^mR(V^\ell)\), with \(F(v)\) defined and nonzero, then
\[
 F(v)^{Q^3}=\Omega^mF(v).                            \tag{\(\mathsf{WF}_6\)}
\]
Consequently, for \(m\ne0\),
\[
 F(v)\in E_k\iff\Omega=1.
\]
Thus every nonzero pure Kummer eigenweight carries an invertible power
of the original selected obstruction; it cannot supply a second
condition.  Mixed-weight cancellations, vanishing specializations, and
independently normalized characteristic-zero constructions lie outside
this assertion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The character orthogonality relation is
\[
 \sum_{r\bmod\ell}\rho^{r(a-m)}
 =\begin{cases}1,&a=m,\\0,&a\ne m,\end{cases}
\]
because \(\ell=1\) in characteristic two.  Applying it termwise to
\((\mathsf{TF}_7)\) proves \((\mathsf{WF}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{WF}_2)\).
The congruence \(1+Q+Q^2=0\pmod\ell\) shows that
\(n\) is independent of the chosen root \(v\).  The selected norm
identity gives
\[
 n^\ell
 =u_k^{1+Q+Q^2}
 =\Norm_{E_k/K}(u_k)
 =u_{k-1}.
\]
Therefore \(n/v_{k-1}\in\mu_\ell\), and
\[
 \Delta^{Q-1}
 =v^{(1+Q+Q^2)(Q-1)}
 =v^{Q^3-1}
 =u_k^{(Q^3-1)/\ell}
 =\Omega,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{WF}_3)\).  Since \(Q-1\) is invertible modulo
\(\ell\), the first four equivalences in \((\mathsf{WF}_4)\) follow.
If all \(C_r\) lie in \(K\), Fourier inversion gives
\(v=\widehat C_1\in E_k\); the converse follows because \(Q\)-Frobenius
then cycles each displayed triple.  This proves the last equivalence.

Finally, dividing a weight-\(m\) rational function by \(V^m\) gives a
weight-zero function, proving \((\mathsf{WF}_5)\).  Since
\(v^{Q^3}=\Omega v\), specialization gives
\((\mathsf{WF}_6)\).

Lean kernel-checks the pseudonorm root, root-choice invariance, normalized
defect--Euler identity, and pure-eigenweight monodromy.  The finite-field
Fourier transform, fixed-field description, and selected specialization
remain paper-level.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[mixed-weight saturation of the twisted Kummer fibre]
\label{prop:dk-mixed-weight-collapse}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-weighted-fibre}, put
\[
 F=E_k,\qquad u=u_k,\qquad
 \mathcal A=F[T]/(T^\ell-u),
\]
and let \(t\) be the class of \(T\).  Root scaling
\(\tau(t)=\rho t\) gives the exact grading
\[
 \mathcal A=\bigoplus_{m\bmod\ell}\mathcal A_m,\qquad
 \mathcal A_m=Ft^m,\qquad
 \mathcal A^{\langle\tau\rangle}=F.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{MW}_1\)}
\]
Under the Fourier identification of \((\mathsf{WF}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{WF}_2)\),
the six nonzero modes are
\[
 t,\ t^Q,\ t^{Q^2},\
 t^{1+Q},\ t^{Q+Q^2},\ t^{Q^2+1}.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{MW}_2\)}
\]
They generate \(\mathcal A\), and the quadratic modes are just products
of the linear ones:
\[
 \widehat D_{1+Q}=\widehat C_1\widehat C_Q,\quad
 \widehat D_{Q+Q^2}=\widehat C_Q\widehat C_{Q^2},\quad
 \widehat D_{Q^2+1}=\widehat C_{Q^2}\widehat C_1.    \tag{\(\mathsf{MW}_3\)}
\]
The three complementary mixed products all equal the pseudonorm
\[
 \boxed{
 \widehat C_1\widehat D_{Q+Q^2}
 =\widehat C_Q\widehat D_{Q^2+1}
 =\widehat C_{Q^2}\widehat D_{1+Q}
 =n=t^{1+Q+Q^2}.}                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{MW}_4\)}
\]
Writing \(s=(1+Q+Q^2)/\ell\), one has \(n=u^s\); its normalized defect is
the \(\Delta\) of \((\mathsf{WF}_3)\), with
\(\Delta^{Q-1}=\Omega\).

More generally, if a monomial has total exponent
\(W=\ell a+m\), \(0\leq m<\ell\), then
\[
 t^W=u^at^m.                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{MW}_5\)}
\]
Every polynomial expression in the coefficient family therefore has a
unique weight decomposition \(\sum_m a_mt^m\), and every expression
