coordinate but does not evaluate it; if \(\ell\mid d\), the formula is
tautological.  Equivalently, the surviving quantities
\(\Theta_{q,s}\), the cubic \(L(1)\), and the exceptional antiunit
index are distinct instances of the same selected-prime ray-coordinate
problem, not consequences of conductor-local reciprocity.

The abstract proposition also applies to a chosen characteristic-zero
lift of the singleton-even Conway minimal polynomial, whose constant
coefficient is one and whose root is therefore a global unit.  That
lift is noncanonical and the resulting mixed local coordinate is still
unevaluated.  No claim here replaces the separate synchronized-phase
problem for zero arms with three or more components.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Finiteness of the ordinary ideal class group supplies the stated
choice of \(d\) and a generator of \(\mathfrak P^d\).  Only the
exceptional local calculation is not already part of the arm-specific
propositions.  At five, \(\beta\) reduces to one, so the
two numerator and two denominator factors agree.  At three,
\(\alpha\) reduces to one and direct cancellation gives the displayed
element.  Its residue order divides \(3^4-1=80\); the current primes are
different from two, three, and five.  Raising to the \(\ell\)-th power is
therefore an automorphism on this residue group, and also on the
pro-three and pro-five principal units.  The remaining assertions are
the respective selected-symbol identifications and
Proposition~\ref{prop:selected-principalizer}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Jacobi failure is the second ramified torsion layer]
\label{prop:jacobi-second-layer}
Let \(M\) and \(\ell\) be coprime odd integers, with \(\ell\) prime,
and suppose \(M\ell\mid Q-1\).  Over \(\F_Q\), let
\[
 X_{M,\ell}:\qquad y^\ell=1-x^M,
 \qquad J=\operatorname{Jac}(X_{M,\ell}),
 \qquad \pi=\zeta_\ell-1.
\]
For nontrivial characters of orders dividing \(M\) and \(\ell\), put
\[
 J_{j,t}=\sum_{a\in\F_Q}
     \chi_M(a)^j\chi_\ell(1-a)^t
 \quad(1\leq j<M,\ 1\leq t<\ell).
\]
Then
\[
 g(X_{M,\ell})=\frac{(M-1)(\ell-1)}2,
 \qquad
 P_X(T)=\prod_{j=1}^{M-1}\prod_{t=1}^{\ell-1}
             (1+J_{j,t}T).                            \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_1\)}
\]
In particular, the Frobenius eigenvalues are
\(\alpha_{j,t}=-J_{j,t}\).

The simultaneous first-order flatness condition
\[
 J_{j,1}\equiv-1\pmod{\pi^2}
 \qquad(1\leq j<M)                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_2\)}
\]
is equivalent to
\[
 J[\pi^2]\subseteq J(\F_Q).                          \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_3\)}
\]
Consequently it implies the exact divisibility
\[
 \ell^{2(M-1)}\mid\#J(\F_Q).                        \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_4\)}
\]
The congruence modulo \(\pi\), and hence rationality of
\(J[\pi]\), is unconditional.  Failure is exactly rationality through
the second ramified layer and may lift further; it does not assert
rationality of the full \(\ell\)-torsion.

For the cubic arm take
\[
 M=3^{k+1},\qquad Q=2^{2\cdot3^k};
\]
for the exceptional arm, with \(k\geq2\), take
\[
 M=15\cdot3^k,\qquad Q=2^{4\cdot3^k}.
\]
Their respective mixed-Jacobi failure criteria are exactly
\((\mathsf{JT}_2)\).  In both cases
\[
 \ell<\sqrt Q+1.                                     \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_5\)}
\]
Thus even the much stronger hypothetical inclusion
\(J[\ell]\subseteq J(\F_Q)\), which would force the divisor
\(\ell^{2g}\), is compatible with the Weil bound
\[
 \#J(\F_Q)\leq(\sqrt Q+1)^{2g}.                      \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_6\)}
\]
Neither the point-count bound nor the polarization excludes the actual
second-layer failure pattern.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The polynomial \(1-x^M\) is squarefree and \((M,\ell)=1\), so the
superelliptic genus formula gives the first identity in
\((\mathsf{JT}_1)\).  To count points, expand the number of
\(\ell\)-th roots of \(1-x^M\) by \(\ell\)-characters, and expand the
number of \(M\)-th roots of a field element by \(M\)-characters.  The
two nontrivial character sums are exactly the \(J_{j,t}\); the unique
point at infinity supplies the remaining one.  Hence
\[
 \#X_{M,\ell}(\F_Q)=Q+1+\sum_{j,t}J_{j,t}.
\]
The same calculation over every finite extension, together with
Hasse--Davenport, gives the second identity in
\((\mathsf{JT}_1)\).

Let \(\mathcal O=\mathbb Z_\ell[\zeta_\ell]\) and
\[
 R=\mathcal O[T]\big/\bigl((T^M-1)/(T-1)\bigr).
\]
Because \(\ell\nmid M\), this is finite \(\acute{e}\)tale of rank
\(M-1\) over \(\mathcal O\).  The automorphisms
\((x,y)\mapsto(\zeta_Mx,y)\) and
\((x,y)\mapsto(x,\zeta_\ell y)\) act on \(J\).  After the unramified
extension adjoining \(\zeta_M\), the quotient by either deck group is
genus zero, so neither the \(M\)- nor the \(\ell\)-invariant part of
the Tate module occurs.  Because \(\ell\nmid M\), the \(M\)-action is
\(\ell\)-adically semisimple; \(T_\ell J\) is
\(\mathcal O\)-torsion-free, and formula \((\mathsf{JT}_1)\) shows
that every nontrivial \(M\)-character component is free of
\(\mathcal O\)-rank one.  Descent from this unramified splitting then
makes \(T_\ell J\) rank-one projective over \(R\).  Frobenius acts on
its character lines by the \(\alpha_{j,1}\), up to harmless inversion
of \(j\) according to the eigenspace convention.

Every automorphism \(\zeta_\ell\mapsto\zeta_\ell^t\) sends \(\pi\)
to a unit multiple of \(\pi\) and sends
\(J_{j,1}\) to \(J_{j,t}\).  Therefore
\((\mathsf{JT}_2)\) says exactly that Frobenius is the identity modulo
\(\pi^2\) on \(T_\ell J\).  This is equivalent to
\((\mathsf{JT}_3)\).  Since
\[
 |R/\pi^2R|=\ell^{2(M-1)},
\]
equation \((\mathsf{JT}_4)\) follows.  Reduction modulo \(\pi\)
makes every \(\ell\)-character value one away from zero, giving
\(J_{j,t}\equiv-1\pmod\pi\) without any failure hypothesis.

For the cubic arm, put \(n=3^k\) and \(x=2^{n/3}\).  A current prime
satisfies
\[
 \ell\leq x^2+x+1<x^3+1=\sqrt Q+1.
\]
For the exceptional arm, with \(h=3^k\),
\[
 \ell\leq\Psi_k<2^{2h}=\sqrt Q.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{JT}_5)\), and raising it to the power \(2g\)
proves compatibility with \((\mathsf{JT}_6)\).
Finally \(Q\equiv1\pmod\ell\), so the Weil pairing permits rational
\(\ell\)-torsion.  More sharply, for the present \(\ell\geq7\), the
established \(\mathcal O\)-freeness gives
\(J[\pi^2]=\pi^{\ell-3}J[\ell]\).  The Rosati involution sends
\(\zeta_\ell\) to \(\zeta_\ell^{-1}\), so \(\pi^\dagger\) is a unit
times \(\pi\).  For
\(x=\pi^{\ell-3}x_0\) and \(y=\pi^{\ell-3}y_0\), the product
\((\pi^{\ell-3})^\dagger\pi^{\ell-3}\) has \(\pi\)-valuation
\(2\ell-6\geq\ell-1=v_\pi(\ell)\); the Weil pairing of \(x\) and
\(y\) is therefore one.  Thus \(J[\pi^2]\) is totally isotropic, and
its rank respects the polarization bound.  This completes the claimed
geometric no-go.
\end{proof}

\section{Consequences and the Conjectural Frontier}\label{sec:bounded}

\subsection{Boundedness}

Lenstra conjectured that \(m_p\) is globally bounded
\cite{Lenstra77}.  His unconditional rules give positive excess on
singleton odd component sets and the lower bound \(m_p\geq4\) when
\(f(p)=2\cdot3^k\), but do not give an absolute upper bound.  The
four-arm equivalence of Theorem~\ref{thm:rule-four-arms} shows exactly
what would settle the question: Conjecture~\ref{conj:rule} implies
\[
  m_p\in\{0,1,4\}\qquad\text{for every odd prime }p.
\]

There is also an exact formulation of the weaker boundedness question
which does not ask for any maximal-order arm.  It identifies precisely
what a direct fixed-window proof would still have to evaluate.

\begin{proposition}[finite Conway-line criterion for absolute boundedness]
\label{prop:absolute-conway-line}
For \(a\geq0\), let
\[
 C_a=\F_{2^{2^a}}=\{0,1,\ldots,2^{2^a}-1\}
\]
be the corresponding finite Conway subfield.  For an odd prime \(p\),
put \(x_p=\kappa_{f(p)}\) and
\[
 L_{p,a}=\F_2(x_p)C_a.
\]
Then
\[
 m_p<2^{2^a}
 \iff
 \text{some }c\in C_a\text{ is such that }
 x_p+c\notin L_{p,a}^{p},                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AB}_1\)}
\]
where \(L^p=\{y^p:y\in L\}\).  Consequently
\[
 \sup_p m_p<\infty
 \iff
 \text{for some fixed }a,\quad
 \forall p\ \exists c\in C_a:\ x_p+c\notin L_{p,a}^{p}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{AB}_2\)}
\]
Thus absolute boundedness is exactly uniform nonsaturation of one
fixed finite-Conway-subfield affine line.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(c\in C_a\), put \(\beta=x_p+c\), and let
\(F_c=\F_2(\beta)\).  Since \(x_p=\beta+c\), one has
\[
 L_{p,a}=F_cC_a.
\]
Hence \([L_{p,a}:F_c]\) divides \([C_a:\F_2]=2^a\), and is therefore
prime to the odd prime \(p\).  Extension from \(F_c\) to \(L_{p,a}\)
is injective on \(p\)-Kummer quotients: restriction followed by norm is
multiplication by \([L_{p,a}:F_c]\), which is invertible modulo \(p\).
Thus \(\beta\) is a \(p\)-th power in its defining finite field exactly
when it is a \(p\)-th power in the common field \(L_{p,a}\).

The finite nimbers below \(2^{2^a}\) are exactly \(C_a\), so the
definition of the excess gives \((\mathsf{AB}_1)\).  A fixed \(a\)
therefore gives the uniform bound \(2^{2^a}-1\).  Conversely, from a
uniform bound \(B\), choose \(a\) with \(2^{2^a}>B\); every witnessing
translate then lies in \(C_a\).  This proves \((\mathsf{AB}_2)\).
\end{proof}

The theorem also explains why a generic finite-field argument is
insufficient.  Proposition~\ref{prop:translate-no-go} constructs
arbitrarily large exact-degree elements whose translates in any fixed
finite window are all \(p\)-th powers.  Any proof must therefore use
the distinguished Conway-tower identities.

The available uniform results are substantial but incomplete.
The zero arm is proved for primitive support and completely at the first
two power-of-two two-spine levels \(h=12,24\); the ordinary odd-spine
arm has projective reductions and an absolute-Frobenius signed-ball sieve; the
cubic and exceptional arms have explicit partition-regulator bounds.
On the exceptional column, Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-constant-line}
also gives the direct effective estimate
\[
 m_\ell<16(3^k)^4
 \qquad\bigl(f(\ell)=2\cdot3^k,\ k\geq2\bigr).
\]
Its proof shows why this does not become absolute: a fixed Conway line
is one fixed constant-extension power sum of a selected Dirichlet
\(L\)-polynomial, and the Riemann--Weil bound is eventually compatible
with complete saturation of that line.
Each leaves smaller current primary factors untreated.  Consequently
neither global boundedness nor the nonexistence of an excess
\(m_p\geq5\) is presently proved.

\subsection{The four remaining order problems}\label{sec:moves}

The proof frontier is most cleanly stated in the same four coordinates
as Theorem~\ref{thm:rule-four-arms}.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{\(\mathbf{\Alph*}\).},leftmargin=*]
\item \emph{Zero arm.}
There are two exact maximal-order problems.  At a singleton-even level,
prove
\[
  \delta_n=F_n
\]
in Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-arm}.  The fibotomic recursion,
elliptic correspondence, and equivariant five-point generalized
Jacobian reduce this to the nonvanishing of one explicit distinguished
Kummer value; they also prove that the available trace, norm, and
boundary-character data are circular.  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-root-ancestry}
pushes the minimal-counterexample hypothesis through unique
\(\ell\)-th roots: every lower trace, norm, and maximal-order identity
survives automatically, while top failure is exactly one Dickson
power-sum equation.  Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-explicit-lower-root}
removes even the auxiliary root choice: for
\(d=(q+1)/\ell\) the lower coefficient is
\((a_{n-1}^d)^{q/2}\).  The resulting equation is still the same
selected nonvanishing target, rather than a lower-level contradiction.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-dickson-dichotomy}
proves that its Dickson polynomial has no intermediate factorization:
failure is complete splitting and success is irreducibility, with the
same Kummer scalar appearing in the exact resultant.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-trace-divisor} then transfers failure to the
selected divisibility \(\mathcal P_{\ell,n}\mid H_m\): the absolute norm
of a reverse-Dickson polynomial, containing the actual minimal polynomial
of \(a_{n-1}\), must divide the trace-one polynomial.  Its factorization
is one irreducible of degree \(m\ell\) on success and \(\ell\)
irreducibles of degree \(m\) on failure.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-top-bit-trace} identifies the selected
ancestry more rigidly: absolute trace in the Conway bit basis is exactly
the top-bit functional, and \(a_{n-1}=q/2\) is the least trace-one
nimber.  After translation by that literal top bit, failure is the
selected divisibility
\(P_{\ell,a}(Z+a)\mid\sum_{i<m}Z^{2^i}\) inside the trace-zero
linearized polynomial.  This centers the obstruction but does not exclude
it; transported nonselected Kummer cosets yield the same type of divisor.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-top-bit-countermodel} makes the limitation
sharp: after an adapted binary labeling, nonselected full-degree Kummer
cosets simultaneously realize the top-bit trace rule, the centered
divisor, all Moore-span bounds, and the adjoint trace identities.  Such a
labeling need not respect Conway multiplication, so the construction is
not a counterexample; it proves that additive linearization cannot replace
the missing multiplicative-ancestry argument.
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-upper-block-factorization} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-upper-block-universal-translate} retain the
literal least upper Conway interval.  Its complete character histogram,
factor degrees, discriminants, pairwise resultants, and root-difference
data are universal after one common translation.  The marked endpoint is
the only remaining datum: its trivial-label test is
\(B_1(0)=S_d(a_{n-1})\).  Every individual coefficient or Hasse-jet test
is a shifted Fibonacci value, and its selected norm is the corresponding
shifted resultant.  Thus the entire labelled block supplies no
selector-blind second invariant; any gain must add genuinely
ancestry-sensitive input, possibly through a relation among shifted values.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-two-step-quartic} keeps two consecutive
selected levels at once: its sparse quartic composition is irreducible
on success and splits into exactly \(\ell\) irreducible quartics on
failure.  Thus even two-level degree data preserve, rather than exclude,
the bad Kummer pattern.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-quartic-conic} gives every bad quartic's
exact coefficient conic and an inverse formula recovering its selected
Dickson trace.  It is therefore a lossless coordinate chart on the same
failure fibre, not a new constraint capable of excluding that fibre.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-wiedemann-separation} also rules out the
most direct transfer from Wiedemann's tower: although both targets are
norm-one Fermat-order assertions, their recursive drivers have
incompatible multiplicative orders and cannot be related by Frobenius.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-one-step-no-go} is stronger than a
predecessor-only warning.  Its degree-sixteen predecessor has order
\((2^{16}-1)/3\), and the exact resultant successor has order
\((2^{32}-1)/3=F_1F_2F_3F_4\); nevertheless the following norm-one
quotient has order \(F_5/641\).  Thus even maximal propagation of every
old Fermat factor through the successor does not determine its new
Fermat component.  The proof uses the internal direct product of the
base-field and norm-one factors, avoiding the spurious factor-three
ambiguity left by an order-lcm argument.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-remainder-descent} now restores the
complete actual ancestry: dividing any candidate section by
\(X^2+YX+Y^3\) pushes selected divisibility to two exact coefficient
divisibilities at the preceding minimal polynomial, and iteration
terminates at \(A_{-1}=Y+1\).  Thus the preceding countermodel's
alternative degree-sixteen factor does not certify divisibility on the
actual path.
However, the resulting leaf nonvanishing is equivalent to the original
selected Conway--Fermat obstruction; the descent itself supplies no
coprimality.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-remainder-coordinate-exhaustion} makes the
remaining Euclidean boundary exact.  Recursive coordinate splitting is an
\(\F_2\)-linear isomorphism from the reduced residue to all \(2^n\) leaf
bits, and the complete Euclidean/subresultant tail after the first division
is information-equivalent to that residue.  Across unrestricted polynomials
the raw first quotient and the complete leaf vector are independent.  Thus a
surviving quotient argument must use a special Fibonacci coefficient identity
relating the actual quotient \(Q_{S_d}\) to its residue, rather than formal
division or ancestry alone.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-quotient-parity-defects} treats the first
special derivative/next-value identity: its reduced defects are exactly the
odd and even coefficient blocks of the selected remainder and together
reconstruct it.  Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-first-quotient-digit} then shows
that under failure the first \(A\)-adic quotient digit is the existing
choice-free Kummer root divided by \(aA'(a)\).  A surviving coefficient
proof must use higher \(A\)-adic digits, a genuinely global relation in the
unreduced quotient, or directly force one parity block nonzero.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-higher-quotient-window-collapse} now makes
that remaining local boundary exact.  The first \(4M\) low coefficients
and first \(M\) leading coefficients of the hypothetical quotient agree
with the unconditional full-Fermat cofactor, while in the completed
local ring \(S_d\) is merely the parameter \(A\) multiplied by a unit.
All principal-ideal powers and unmarked completed-local invariants are
therefore unchanged by a formal-coordinate automorphism.  Fixed-coordinate
higher digits, middle/global quotient coefficients, and genuinely
ancestry-sensitive relations remain outside this collapse.
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-factor-sensitive-window} sharpens both endpoint
windows to their exact factor-dependent boundaries.  If
\(R=2^{v_2(\ell-1)}\), the quotients agree through the first \(R\) low
and \(R/4\) leading coefficients, then differ by one at both next
coefficients.  Thus even the first exposed endpoint defects encode only the
two-adic valuation of the arithmetic factor; later, middle, cross-endpoint,
and fixed-\(A\)-coordinate relations remain the genuine quotient frontier.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fixed-adic-jet-blocks} resolves the first two
fixed-coordinate Hasse blocks as well.  Every canonical digit through
\(q_{2R-2}\) is forced by \(A\), the factor valuation, and the existing first
digit: the apparent order-\(R\) escape folds back through the unconditional
identity \(X^RH=S_d+(1+T_s)S_d'\).  The first term not reduced by this
identity occurs at Hasse order \(2R\) and is a provably nonzero partial-trace
prefactor times an explicit half-index Fibonacci value.  That value is
automatically nonzero in the even half-index branch.  In the odd branch its
vanishing is equivalent to the unknown selected order dividing
\(\Gamma_{n,\ell}=\gcd(d,3R+1)\); each prime in this gcd is also a primitive
divisor of an explicit smaller base-three generalized Fermat number and is
one modulo \(12M\).  This yields the sharper \((12M+1)^2\) factor window and
an unconditional exclusion when \(M-s\leq n+3\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-odd-jet-recovery} further proves that a vanished
odd \(2R\)-jet is followed by an explicit nonzero Hasse jet unless the
selected cofactor is exactly \(d=3R+1\); only that endpoint can remain
Hasse-flat.  The gcd
is one for every published factor in the pinned \(F_{12},\ldots,F_{18}\)
screen, so this first free jet is nonzero there even under hypothetical
failure.  This is still only higher-jet information: the later nonzero jet
does not contradict divisibility, and odd-branch gcds at untested levels,
later digits, and global relations remain open.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-semiconjugacy-flag} pushes the surviving odd
collision through the complete selected ancestry to a lower exact resultant.
Writing \(v=v_2(s)\), hypothetical failure forces one explicit iterated
quadratic resultant to vanish modulo \(A_{v-1}\); a vanished odd
order-\(2R\) jet forces the factor-independent divisibility
\(A_{v-1}\mid D_{n-v}\), with \(D_j\ne0\) and \(\deg D_j<3^j\).
This excludes the collision whenever \(3^{n-v}\leq2^v\).  It is a genuine
lower selected-ancestry obstruction, not a maximal-order theorem: a nonzero
Hasse jet remains compatible with \(A_{n-1}\mid S_d\), and the descended
condition is automatic when \(v=0\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-equivariant-kummer-countermodel} makes the
generalized-Jacobian boundary sharp at the actual \(n=9\) ordinal
degree: a connected four-function Kummer cover and its quarter-turn
twist contain points satisfying the complete equivariant Kummer orbit
with \(\mathfrak c(P)\) an \(\ell\)-th power.  What those points lack
is precisely the full selected Conway ancestry.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-additive-saturation} saturates that failure
fibre under translation by the half-field.  It gives the sharper
conditional sieve \(\ell\leq\sqrt q/2\); the exact obstruction is a
collision of the selected roots under
\(z\mapsto z^{\sqrt q}+z\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-relative-trace-axes} factors the collision
equation completely.  After centering a pair, it says that the ratio of
one selected transported value and one fibotomic value lies either in
the half-field multiplicative group or in its relative norm-one torus;
the two alternatives are disjoint.  Equivalently, \(a^{-1}\) must be
the value of \(\mathcal D_\ell(\tau(s+s^{-1}),1)\) for a fibotomic
\(s\) and an axis scalar \(\tau\); full Conway ancestry forces the
corresponding \(J\)-value to be \(a_{n-2}\), but does not evaluate it.
The proposition also constructs nonselected
failure cosets with the first kind of collision whenever
\(\ell\leq\sqrt q\), including the interval
\(\sqrt q/2<\ell\leq\sqrt q\).  Thus proving the actual Conway ratio
avoids both axes remains additional selected content, rather than a
consequence of the one-step trace identities or the square-root count.
Remark~\ref{rem:fermat-trace-gap} proves that the resulting degree and
coefficient-gap constraints remain compatible with every composite
Fermat case.  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-coset-semiconjugacy}
identifies \(H_m\) with the inversion quotient of the norm-one torus
and \(P_{\ell,a}\) with one transported \(\mu_\ell\)-coset.
Remark~\ref{rem:fermat-coset-support} gives the exact fibotomic strata
of that coset and proves their capacity constraints automatic.  Thus
the selected divisor is sharper but still unevaluated: additive and
support data are realized by many nonselected cosets.  For
the complete trace-one factorization,
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-complementary-resultant} proves that
\(S_d\) and its cofactor \(J_{N,d}\) give complementary selected
resultants whose sum is one.  The differentiated factorization supplies an
inverse on the nonvanishing side, while the complete fibotomic factorization
has exactly one zero selected resultant.  Its position is the same unknown
order \(\delta_n\); factor/cofactor splitting and derivative resultants
therefore add the opposite terminal bit, not a second constraint.  This
rules out that proof class while leaving open an ancestry-sensitive identity
involving a polynomial outside the factorization.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-selected-crt-projectors} strengthens this
terminal boundary.  The differentiated factors are the two canonical CRT
support idempotents, independent of the chosen final B\'ezout or subresultant
certificate.  Every proper additive trace through the literal quadratic
ancestry kills both selected bits; every norm and multiplication determinant
returns exactly the original bit; and multiplication trace and discriminant
vanish in both branches.  Thus terminal Euclidean support-projector linear
algebra carries no hidden orientation.  Intermediate quotient-polynomial
data, before the support collapse, remain outside this no-go.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-failure-block-collapse} independently closes
the shifted-Fibonacci variant.  Under failure every \(d\)-shifted block is
a scalar copy of the initial block, and its scalar
\(S_{d+1}(a)\) is exactly the existing choice-free lower \(\ell\)-th root.
Thus coupled consecutive-value, Cassini, continued-fraction, and
post-reduction Euclidean identities add no selected condition.
Coefficient-sensitive intermediate quotients before passage to the failure
factor remain outside this collapse.  For
multicomponent zero sets,
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-two-spine-support} proves that the Conway
class is nontrivial in the primitive-support quotient under its stated
two-spine hypotheses, but generation of a composite quotient remains
open.  Proposition~\ref{prop:two-spine-cross-ratio-sieve} turns the
exact Hilbert--90 representative into a cyclic-variation order lower
bound and proves every primary factor above its explicit complementary
threshold; the smaller factors remain unevaluated.

\item \emph{Ordinary odd-spine arm.}
Evaluate the selected power-residue class
\(\Theta_{q,s}\) of Proposition~\ref{prop:singleton-transverse} and
prove its full projective order.  This includes the \(11\)-chain and
the \(23,29,47,179,\ldots\) components.  The relative-unit formulation
and Proposition~\ref{prop:singleton-conjugate-ball} prove every primary
factor above its sharper explicit signed-ball threshold, so the unresolved
content is confined to the smaller factors.  The formerly source-pinned
\(m_{359}=1\) row is now independently certified by the exact Hilbert-root
calculation below.  Proposition~\ref{prop:safe-prime-trace-detector}
generalizes its last reduction to every safe-prime component:
nontriviality is one absolute trace bit, but the selected Conway norm
still has to be evaluated.  Remark~\ref{rem:base-selector-cancel} shows
that the unweighted orbit product in global reciprocity still cancels
over the \(p-1\) lifts for every selector from the base cyclotomic
field.  Proposition~\ref{prop:weighted-selector-reciprocity} proves that
pure order-\(p\) selectors also have zero weight.  The mixed Jacobi
family escapes both cancellations, but
Corollary~\ref{cor:ordinary-mixed-jacobi} identifies the target with one
inverse-Fourier coordinate of its first local phase.  Proving that
coordinate nonzero remains the theorem.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-mirimanoff-ray} computes the previously
implicit wild side at the actual full conductor.  Its truncated-log
regulator \(A_\lambda\) vanishes exactly when the relative cyclotomic
unit is locally a \(p\)-th power; otherwise the local character has
conductor exactly \(\lambda^2\).  Global reciprocity then pairs the
explicit Stickelberger valuation vector with
\(A_\lambda C_{j,\lambda}\).  This is a genuine conductor-sensitive
refinement, but it still contains the same selected first Jacobi phase.
If all \(A_\lambda\) vanish, the extension is everywhere unramified and
is either trivial or, if nontrivial, defines a \(p\)-class-group
character.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-binary-sections} gives an equivalent
characteristic-two boundary: failure means that every nonconstant binary
section of \((1+x)^{\mathcal L_{r,a}/p}\) vanishes at \(x_{a-1}\), or,
after descent, that the actual Conway minimal polynomial
\(P_{\alpha_r}\) divides every selected section polynomial \(R_{e,c}\).
Corollary~\ref{cor:ordinary-sections-no-go} proves that norm and complement
symmetry are automatic under this pattern.  The remaining theorem is an
explicit selected-polynomial nondivisibility, not a generic binomial-step
statement.  Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-tower-no-go} and
Remark~\ref{rem:ordinary-common-sections} prove this distinction sharp:
the simultaneous divisibilities occur in arbitrarily many full-degree
irreducible factors while preserving the complete \(r\)-power ancestry,
smooth degree, multiplicative orders, and full \(p\)-primary test.  They
also occur among scalings inside any fixed bottom Kummer coset.  Only the
actual Conway scaling \(c=1\) remains unevaluated.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-affine-marked-scalar} proves that this
mark cannot be recovered from the surrounding affine orbit.  Rescaling a
Kummer generator permutes the complete translate set and its full character
histogram, while moving the distinguished value from \(\chi(1+x)\) to
\(\chi(1+ux)\).  Hence every symmetric affine-orbit invariant is blind;
the now-certified \(p=359\) trace bit is precisely the missing mark
\(u=1\) fixed by Conway ancestry.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-fixed-translate-countermodel} removes even
fixed translation and ordinal-minimal labeling as generic escape routes:
one exact degree-twenty model has \(c=1\), full Kummer degree, simultaneous
binary-section failure, trivial norm-one reciprocity symbols, and a lower
element labeled as the least fifth-power nonresidue.  It is not a Conway
counterexample because that lower element has order five rather than
\(\ord(\alpha_5)=15\), changing the cyclotomic conductor from \(75\) to
\(25\).  Thus the surviving theorem must use the full multiplicative
ancestry and the Conway-selected prime inside that conductor.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-full-conductor} makes that boundary
exact.  The full \(r\)-valuation in the Conway Kummer carry gives
\(P_{x_s}(X)=P_{\alpha_r}(X^{r^s})\), so wrong-conductor models are
cyclotomically disjoint from the selected factor.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-full-conductor} specializes the whole
actual dependency chain at the first independent safe-prime row.  It gives
one degree-\(3504820\), \(3447\)-term polynomial for the marked
\(\kappa_{179}\), and identifies \(m_{359}=1\) with its exact Euler gcd,
Capelli composition, and safe-prime trace.  Preserving that polynomial and
the mark leaves no countermodel freedom: marked power status is invariant
under every finite-field isomorphism.  Sparsity therefore packages the
selected evaluation but does not decide it.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-factor-certificate} makes a short
independent verification target exact: one degree-\(19580\) factor over
\(\F_{2^{179}}\), together with a 171-bit exponentiation at its value one,
would give a roughly \(438\)-KB certificate.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-hilbert-certificate} now instantiates
that target in an equivalent root-coordinate form.  Its checked-in
\(438103\)-byte element satisfies \(y^{179}=A\); an exact
\(19579\)-step resultant computes
\(\theta=\Norm(1+y)\), whose \(359\)-phase is nontrivial.  This proves the
actual selected value \(m_{359}=1\) by a locally replayable finite
calculation, with the algebraic reduction proved in the text.
The pinned Peeters commit can
be followed through its mixed-radix reductions to the literal marked element
\(1+\kappa_{179}\); its \texttt{\{359,1\}} row and \(177379\)-second uncached
timing are therefore evidence of an executed exact fixed-mark calculation,
not merely a copied table entry.  The historical repository additionally
retains two complete progress traces, with elapsed times \(177379\) and
\(177717\) seconds, that both finish with a \(1743227\)-term nonidentity
result.  The checkout's source-audit script verifies the historical blob
hashes and both endpoint pairs.  Since no final coefficient vector was
retained upstream, these runs corroborate rather than constitute the local
certificate.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-nested-compression} gives the exact
next-row boundary.  The certified \(359\)-class constructs a root
\(y^{359}=1+\kappa_{179}\); its inner degree-\(179\) norm reduces
\(m_{719}=1\) to one Euler test in \(\F_{2^{7029220}}\).  Equivalently,
one may take any degree-\(179\) factor of
\((T^{359}+1)^{179}+(\kappa_{89}+1)\) over that field and evaluate it at
one.  The two lower norm shadows are explicit, but twisting inside their
fixed norm fibre realizes every \(719\)-phase, so they cannot decide the
row.  Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-certificate} compresses a
replayable certificate from a roughly \(158\)-MB factor over
\(\F_{2^{7029220}}\) to two \(438103\)-byte payloads over \(\F_{2^{179}}\)
and one final Euler test in \(\F_{2^{64261}}\).  The first payload \(a\) is
checked exactly by
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-root-certificate}, while
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-evaluation} supplies the monic
minimal-polynomial payload, recomputes the crossed norm, and obtains a
nontrivial \(719\)-phase.  Thus \(m_{719}=1\) is now proved by a locally
replayable exact finite computation together with the paper-level crossed
tower reduction.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-727-certificate} advances the exact runtime
boundary once more without a large payload.  Here
\(\ord_{727}(2)=121=11^2\), so the actual selected polynomial
\(P_{\alpha_{11}}(X^{121})\) has degree only \(2420\) and five terms.  A
self-contained binary-polynomial calculation proves it irreducible, checks
that translate zero is still a \(727\)-th power, and gives a nontrivial
\(727\)-phase at translate one.  Hence \(m_{727}=1\); this finite row does
not alter the still-open universal ordinary-spine assertion.  It is also the
deliberate endpoint of row-by-row certification: the implementation keeps
\(p=733\) as an explicit refusal boundary, but the mathematical target from
here is a uniform selected-Conway nondivisibility proof, not the next finite
row.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-dependency-descent} then pushes any
hypothetical failure through each odd-Kummer singleton
\(0/1\)-excess dependency satisfying its hypotheses by taking all
\(Q\) polynomial sections.  This retains the actual
selected ancestry without loss, but ends in the same unevaluated
selected-factor divisibilities.  Multicomponent and excess-\(4\) nodes
still require their synchronized orbit resultants, while a two-primary
node uses its separate quadratic ancestry.

\item \emph{Cubic arm.}
Prove \(C_k\) for every \(k\): equivalently, for each
\(\ell^a\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)\), prove
\[
  v_\ell(\ord\gamma_k)=a.
\]
The norm tower propagates all old factors, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-partitions} settles primary factors above
its explicit threshold.  The derivative regulator, secant saturation,
and reciprocity calculations all recover the same one-dimensional
Kummer class without evaluating it.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-circulant-no-go}
proves that the apparent \(3^k\)-determinant of the conjugate
circular-unit lattice acts as one after reduction and admits equivariant
abstract models of every current-factor index, even while preserving
maximal lower-field norm.  Meanwhile
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-recursion-no-go} proves that the coefficient
recursion does not preserve primitivity even when it preserves
irreducibility.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-cyclic-parity} makes the
remaining assertion purely combinatorial in the cyclic group algebra:
the binary subset sums of \((2^{3^k}-1)/\ell\) cannot have parity one at
every residue except a single prescribed residue.  This universal
inequality, equivalently direct Kummer nonvanishing at the smaller
current factors, remains open.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-weighted-orbit-dichotomy} subsumes every
Euler derivative or weighted Frobenius-word escape: evaluating the word
at two either kills the selected power-residue class or returns an
invertible power of exactly that class.  It also proves that a scalar
identity for \(L(1)\) with a fixed integer Euler quotient is
normalization-ill-posed; changing the compatible primitive generator
while fixing the selected root rescales \(L(1)\) arbitrarily.  Only its
vanishing is intrinsic.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-mixed-jacobi-top} gives the exact
characteristic-zero counterpart: every first-order mixed Jacobi
coefficient outside the primitive top component vanishes, and every
primitive coefficient is a known nonzero multiple of \(L(1)\).  Thus a
single selected Fermat quotient decides the arm:
\[
 J_1\not\equiv-1\pmod{(\zeta_\ell-1)^2}.
\]
Remark~\ref{rem:cubic-higher-jacobi-no-go} proves that higher Jacobi
congruences cannot bootstrap this first coefficient: their terms have
distinct local valuations, so every deeper condition begins only after
the still-unknown \(L(1)\) has vanished.
Stickelberger ideal data see \(J_1\), but not this additive first-order
congruence for \(J_1+1\).  Corollary~\ref{cor:cubic-weighted-jacobi-resolvent}
packages it into one weighted product
\(\mathcal R_\lambda\not\equiv1\pmod{(\zeta_\ell-1)^2}\), while proving
that the corresponding unweighted product is identically flat.
Proposition~\ref{prop:jacobi-second-layer} gives the exact geometric
meaning of this congruence: cubic failure rationalizes
\(J[\pi^2]\) on the associated mixed Kummer-curve Jacobian, whereas
\(J[\pi]\) is rational unconditionally.  The theorem forces only the
second ramified layer (higher layers may also lift), and even full
rational \(\ell\)-torsion would fit inside the Weil and polarization
bounds.
Corollary~\ref{cor:cubic-primitive-crt-isolation} proves that the
exceptional residue is one of the three lifts
\(3^{k+1}-1,3^k-1,2\cdot3^k-1\), and isolates all success/failure
information in the top \(\Phi_{3^{k+1}}\)-component.  Thus every proper
cyclic projection is provably blind; its weight and mod-four consequences
give exact screens but do not evaluate the selected top coordinate.
Corollary~\ref{cor:cubic-augmentation}
shows that both the full Frobenius product and the apparent
\(\ell\)-th-power exponent test are unconditional: failure is an extra
proper-subproduct relation, so those identities alone cannot exclude it.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-block-compression} compresses that relation
to one reciprocal unit and proves that mask complementation forces the
prescribed exceptional residue rather than contradicting it.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-wendt-factor} gives the remaining exact
factor-selection problem: failure is the occurrence of the distinguished
minimal polynomial of \(\eta_k\), together with its reciprocal translate,
in a binary Wendt gcd.  Every Wendt factor already has the required
degree and a translation partner, so a closure must exclude this selected
pair rather than all such factors.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-kummer-descent} gives a complementary
degree-three descent: failure is exactly the existence over the preceding
field of one irreducible reciprocal coefficient pair satisfying two
explicit Dickson power-sum equations, and the degree-\(3\ell\) Capelli
composition then factors into \(\ell\) selected irreducible cubics.
This is lossless compression, not a contradiction: the two equations are
the same missing Kummer fibre.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-dickson-jacobian} proves that irreducibility is
already forced by those two equations.  Their geometric fibre is reduced
\(\acute{e}\)tale of size \(\ell^2\), with exactly zero or \(\ell\)
rational points according as the selected Kummer class succeeds or fails.
The universal Jacobian identity, discriminant ratio, reciprocal root,
selected \(\F_4\)-norm product, and Berlekamp trace all collapse to that
same class.  Hence ramification and cubic-discriminant methods supply no
second obstruction capable of emptying the rational fibre.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-wendt-singer} factors the gcd through Singer
coset intersections and gives their exact first two moments.  In the range
\(\ell-1<\sqrt q+1/\sqrt q\), every coset contains degree-\(n\)
Wendt factors, proving that generic factor exclusion is impossible there.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-wendt-s3} descends the selected pair to the
degree-\(3^{k-1}\) minimal polynomial of
\(\eta_{k-1}^2+\eta_{k-1}+1\); deciding whether that trace fiber lies in
the chosen multiplicative coset is the same Kummer evaluation.
Corollary~\ref{cor:cubic-reciprocal-selector} identifies this quotient
selector with \(\gamma_{k-1}^{-2}\), gives its irreducible cubic
recursion, and proves the exact order seam
\[
 \ord(z_k)=\ord(z_{k-1})\ord(\eta_k).
\]
Thus the descent is a lossless reciprocal form of \(C_k\), not an
induction from \(C_{k-1}\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-iterated-fibre} carries this descent through
the entire selected lower tower.  The unique lower \(\ell\)-th roots
are norm coherent; after fixing that norm, every geometric coefficient
fibre has \(\ell^2\) reduced points, each lower rational fibre is a
singleton, and the top rational fibre has zero or \(\ell\) points.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-trace-fibre-flag} gives the complementary
literal-trace version at every edge.  Its target is the actual reciprocal
coefficient pair
\((\eta_{j-1},\eta_{j-1}+1)\); the Jacobian numerator is
\(z_{j-1}\ne0\), every lower rational fibre is again a singleton, and the
top fibre has size zero or \(\ell\) according to the same Euler bit.
Thus the full trace recursion, symmetric source coefficients, discriminants,
and their ordinary norms are jointly failure-compatible.  A surviving
full-ancestry argument must be nonsymmetric across edges or introduce a
mixed additive--multiplicative correlation not fixed by these fibres.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-ancestral-jacobian-line} closes the homogeneous
part of that nonsymmetric derivative escape.  Every partial canonical
square-root Jacobian lies on the single lower-field line generated by
\(\gamma_k+1\), and every partial Jacobian lies on its square.  Hence any
nonzero homogeneous expression with arbitrary separate lower-ancestry
coefficients has either trivial current weight or an invertible power of the
original Euler phase.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-mixed-ancestral-presentation} closes the zero and
inhomogeneous part of the same derivative route.  The full formal mixed
ancestral algebra has the explicit presentation
\(K[T_1,\ldots,U_k]/I\simeq K[z]=L_k\), with a unique quadratic normal form;
in particular nonzero formal mixed polynomials can vanish, but every such
cancellation is generated by the selected translated cubic and the
linear/square relations.  Since \(z+1=\gamma_k\), the algebra contains the
original Euler endpoint rather than a second obstruction.  What remains is
direct selected nonvanishing of one quadratic remainder or a correlation
using genuinely different top-point data.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-coupled-radicals} closes the algebraic
chosen-root escape.  Compatible roots of
\(\eta_k,\varepsilon_k,\beta_k,\gamma_k\), together with every lower
selected radical, generate one Kummer extension and span one Kummer-class
line rather than independent Kummer classes.  Its three top weights are
units modulo \(\ell\), and
all root-choice invariants return to the coefficient field.  What remains is
a nonzero-weight correlation uniform on that one torsor, an external
character-sum estimate, or a direct weight-zero evaluation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-current-weight-collapse} closes the regular
pure-weight version of that escape.  Every evaluated nonzero weight has
full-field monodromy an invertible power of the original Euler phase, so
its field-of-definition test is precisely the same failure bit.  The
root-orbit product of a mixed polynomial is the weight-zero resultant
\(\operatorname{Res}(X^\ell-\tau_k,P)\): it detects whether some branch
vanishes but forgets which one.  Thus a mixed regular construction can
only move the problem to a selected weight-zero resultant that still
requires direct evaluation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-torus-torsor} identifies the top fibre with
one \(T[\ell]\)-torsor and its complete unresolved coordinate with
\[
 \Omega_{k,\ell}
 =\frac{z_k^{(q^2+q+1)/\ell}}{r_{k-1}}.
\]
Its exact affine Frobenius cycle structure shows that Jacobians,
ordinary norms, scalar characters, and linear determinant data erase
or merely re-express this one translation class; they do not create a
second obstruction.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-prescribed-trace} makes the last coordinate
one extremal value \(\Theta_\chi(\eta_{k-1})=3\).  Other Singer trace
fibres genuinely attain \(3\), so uniform character bounds cannot close
the selected fibre.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-trace-fourier-flag-collapse} shows that this
conclusion is unchanged after retaining every literal lower trace in the
Conway chain.  The complete selected flag is exactly the same three-point
immediate trace fibre, and arbitrary additive multi-Fourier weights formed
from those iterated trace coordinates collapse to one immediate-edge
character.  Combined with the Wendt criterion, failure remains the single
equality \(\Theta_\chi(\eta_{k-1})=3\), equivalently
\(P_{\eta,k}\mid G_{N/\ell}\).  This closes recursive trace-projector
refinements, but not top-point weights depending on non-trace data or
genuinely mixed nonsymmetric correlations with separate lower ancestors.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-self-polar-hyperplane} makes one genuinely mixed
additive--multiplicative correlation exact.  The selected trace hyperplane is
the projective line spanned by \(1,\gamma_k\), and the ratio of its character
sum to the unshifted hyperplane sum is precisely
\(\rho(\gamma_k)^{-1}\).  Thus equality of the two line sums is exactly the
original failure condition; all lower-ancestry affine translates span the
same line.  This recovers the selected phase without evaluating it.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-trace-zero} also proves that both selected
period coordinates have absolute trace zero and are not normal.  Thus
primitive-normal and normal-Gaussian-period theorems do not apply to the
actual cubic sequence; normality is not a missing route to the selected
Kummer value.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-previous-singer-lift} further proves that
every point of the preceding Singer set has exactly one current
three-point trace fibre.  Thus preceding Singer membership and
lower-level primitivity do not single out the selected fibre.  The full
cubic recursion remains rigid up to absolute Frobenius, so this is a
sharp obstruction to one-step descent rather than a recursive
countermodel.
Propositions~\ref{prop:singer-normal-translate} and
\ref{prop:cubic-two-normal-bases} nevertheless supply two selected normal
coordinates joined by an explicit determinant-one half-circulant.  Their
full Frobenius orbits lie in the current Kummer kernel simultaneously, so
normal-basis, Moore, and subspace-polynomial data again carry only the
original selected phase.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-two-normal-countermodel}
makes this compatibility exact at the actual degree (81) and current
prime \(2593\): the Singer, normalized inverse, and half-circulant identities,
absolute normality of \(\varepsilon\), and complete normality of \(\beta\)
all survive failure.  The model breaks exactly
at the next lower selected trace equation.  Thus a proof must use the full
recursive Conway ancestry, not the nonlinear incidence of these two normal
bases alone.

\item \emph{Exceptional arm.}
Prove \(D'_k\) for every \(k\), equivalently
\[
  \Psi_k\mid\ord(M_k)
\]
in Conjecture~\ref{conj:dk}.  Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}
gives Capelli and resultant formulations, while
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-partition-regulator} treats the large factors.
The antiunit, translated-binomial, semiprimitive, Dirichlet, Dickson,
and norm-blindness results show precisely why the remaining smaller
factors are not consequences of the present generic identities.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cd-selector-bridge} transports the complete
fibotomic coefficient through the selected cubic ancestry:
\[
 Z=\pi(M_k)
  =(\gamma_k^2+\gamma_k+1)^{2^{2h/3}-2}.
\]
The intermediate \(g_k=\gamma_k^2+\gamma_k+1\) form a norm-coherent
tower, and the corrected exceptional norm becomes one selected
Artin--Schreier root pair \(w,w+1\).  This still does not transfer
\(C_k\) to \(D'_k\): their current primes are disjoint, every
base-field coefficient is automatically an \(\ell\)-th power on the
\(D\) line, and the unevaluated datum is the unordered opposite
Kummer-class pair \(\{[w],-[w]\}\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-tower-countermodel} proves that the coarse
\(C\)-tower invariants alone do not select this coefficient.  The alternative
\(Z_k^\circ=\gamma_k^{-1}\) has trace one, full degree, norm-coherent
actual \(C\)-period ancestry, and is primitive under \(C_k\), yet its
Artin--Schreier root misses the entire current \(\ell\)-primary factor.
This does not disprove a special-form implication \(C_k\Rightarrow D'_k\);
such an implication remains open and must use the actual formula for \(Z\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-one-step-countermodel} strengthens that boundary.
At the actual \(k=4\) degrees and current prime \(163\), both a lower
coordinate and its cubic child are primitive, the exact equation
\(\gamma^3+\gamma=a\) holds, and consequently all three selected symmetric
coordinates of \(g=\gamma^2+\gamma+1\) have the exact one-step selected
form; nevertheless the resulting Artin--Schreier root is a \(163\)-rd power.
The sole mismatch is that \(a\) is not the Conway-selected preceding
coordinate.  Hence even one exact recursive step plus primitivity cannot
transfer \(C\) to \(D\);
the complete lower ancestry is indispensable.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-reflection-descent} puts that actual formula into
the same non-Galois reflection-norm normal form as the singleton-even arm:
\[
 D'_k\text{ fails at }\ell
 \iff
 2\in\Norm_{L_D/F(\zeta_\ell)}(L_D^\times),
 \qquad
 D^{\mathrm{cl}}_\ell(t)=2+\frac5{x^2+x-1}.
\]
This reflection field satisfies the Hasse norm principle and weak
approximation.  Its sole local norm obstruction is the original selected
prime over two, so the descent imports the exact \(C\)-coordinate but does
not couple the disjoint \(+2\) and \(-2\) current eigenspaces.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-normalized-root-norm} computes the actual
root's exact next lower norm; current \(\ell\)-powering is bijective in
that lower field, so even this stronger selected norm remains blind.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-alternating-translate-chain} makes this full-chain
boundary exact.  After alternating the \(\F_4\)-orientation, the actual
exceptional Kummer test is represented by
\(u_j=\gamma_j+c_j\), which obeys a norm-coherent twisted cubic at every
selected level.  Under hypothetical failure its \(\ell\)-th roots descend
uniquely and norm-coherently through the complete Conway chain to the
consistent terminal root \(c_0^2\in\F_4\).  Hence even full recursive
ancestry cannot close the arm through norms or corestriction alone; any
surviving proof must use a nonlinear interaction between the top Kummer root
and the twisted cubic equations.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-twisted-cubic-fibre} exhausts the resulting
unweighted nonlinear coefficient route.  The complete alternating ancestry
defines a reduced \'etale degree-\(\ell^2\) fibre with nonzero Jacobian
numerator \(\gamma_{j-1}\) at every level, singleton lower rational fibres,
and a top \(0/\ell\) rational-point dichotomy.  Under failure the Capelli
composition splits into \(\ell\) distinct selected irreducible cubics, all
carrying the same lower root chain.  Hence discriminant, resultant, and every
root-choice-invariant rational construction remain compatible with failure.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-weighted-fibre} closes the formal Kummer-eigenweight
escape as well: Fourier transformation recovers exactly the chosen root and
its conjugates, while every pure nonzero weight has monodromy an invertible
power of the original Euler symbol.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-mixed-weight-collapse} closes the formal
mixed-weight escape too.  The six Fourier modes generate only the standard
Kummer algebra and its root-scaling grading separates all weights.
Complementary mixed products recover the pseudonorm, while every weight-zero
monomial reduces to a power of \(u_k\).  Since the Artin--Schreier coefficient
and the complete lower root chain are fixed scalars, they do not couple
distinct weights.  Cancellation at one bad rational root is projection to a
chosen split factor and imports exactly the missing \(\ell\)-root
orientation.  The remaining theorem is direct nonvanishing of a selected
weight-zero phase, not another Fourier or ancestry relation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cubic-phase-trace} compresses that phase once more:
\(\Omega=1\) exactly when its immediate cubic trace is one.  Along the
selected twisted cubic this trace is the terminal value of an explicit
third-order recurrence with coefficients \(c_k,c_k,u_{k-1}\).  This replaces
the earlier six-term detector by three terms but does not evaluate the
terminal recurrence value; it is the same selected Dickson obstruction.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cubic-phase-orbit} proves that this compression is
lossless on the entire current phase orbit.  Its quadratic norm, orbit-cubic
discriminant, irreducibility, and shifted power-sum periodicity all distinguish
\(\Omega=1\) from \(\Omega\ne1\), but each is an equivalent restatement of
the binary failure condition on \(P_e\), not a determination of that value.
The complete trace \(\mathcal S\) records the phase orbit, while the named
binary tests do not refine the detector without evaluating it.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-phase-identification} then closes the normalization
gap between this detector and the earlier arithmetic constructions.  With
the conductor-five orientation fixed, the weighted-fibre phase is exactly
\(\xi_\ell^{L(1)}\), equivalently the \(G_1^{-1}C_1\) Jacobi phase, the
\(4^{-1}\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)\) antiunit phase, the half-block phase,
and the inverse selected-sextic phase.  Its cubic trace is \(P_e\).
Consequently these are exactly coordinated presentations of one scalar, not
independent constraints: the phase-level presentations recover \(L(1)\),
while \(P_e\) records its \(Q\)-Frobenius orbit and the reciprocal polynomial
records the corresponding selected factor test.  The remaining assertion is
still \(L(1)\ne0\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} adds an exact first-order
boundary: failure is simultaneous \(\pi^2\)-flatness of all mixed
Jacobi sums of conductor \(15h\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}
then proves that this vector has rank one: the single coefficient
\(C_1\) is a nonzero Gauss factor times
\(\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)\).  Thus the remaining first-order theorem
is exactly nonvanishing of the selected antiunit regulator, not a family
of independent Jacobi congruences.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-four-jacobi} compresses it further to the
canonical four-term cross-resolvent \(J_1J_4/(J_2J_3)\).  It is the
unique inversion-invariant, zero-augmentation detector on the
conductor-five phases, equivalently the selected \(\ell\)-character
tangent of one real quintic Jacobi antiunit.  The order-five norm sends
that antiunit to one and therefore discards the tangent rather than
evaluating it.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-eisenstein-determinant} descends this tangent
through the quadratic subfield and identifies it exactly with the
alternating determinant \(u_1e_2-u_2e_1\) of two relative Eisenstein
sums.  The complete order-five Hasse--Davenport product has only the
complementary symmetric first-order coordinate
\(u_1/e_1+u_2/e_2\); that relation is compatible with zero determinant
and cannot prove the required nonvanishing.
Proposition~\ref{prop:jacobi-second-layer} also identifies simultaneous
flatness with rationality of the second \(\pi\)-torsion layer of the
mixed Kummer-curve Jacobian.  Its exact size divides the Weil envelope
by a wide margin, so point counting and polarization do not exclude the
failure fibre.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-mixed-semiprimitive-no-go} proves that the
surviving primitive mixed character cannot enter the semiprimitive
Gauss--Jacobi regime: its current \(\ell\)-component requires an odd
minus-one exponent while its conductor-five component requires an even
one.  Hence the explicit semiprimitive tables stop exactly before
\(C_1\); the mixed decomposition index is in fact \(2(\ell-1)\), not
two.  Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-weighted-only} gives the complementary
product obstruction: every equal-weight Hasse--Davenport product is
automatically flat, and only the \(\lambda\)-weighted resolvent retains
the selected first line.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclotomic-derivative-no-go} closes the ordinary
cyclotomic-derivative escape: its reduction is unconditionally an
\(\ell\)-th power, while the \(\lambda^{-1}\)-weighted circular unit has
index exactly \(8hL(1)\).  The first generalized Bernoulli value is
identically zero for the relevant even character, including levels
where \(L(1)\ne0\).  Thus the weighted construction is an exact detector
but not an independent evaluation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity} gives the parallel
characteristic-two boundary: failure is a complement-of-zero submask
parity pattern modulo \(15h\), while its \(\ell\)-th-power consequence
is an unconditional idempotent identity.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-four-block-antiunit} identifies the complete
binary exponent before cyclic reduction.  Its four alternating
\(h\)-bit blocks evaluate at the selected conductor root to
\(R_k^{-d}\), exactly the inverse antiunit phase.  Thus the binary submask
and conductor-five regulator routes retain the same selected scalar and
cannot constrain one another; their common nontriviality is still open.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-half-block} splits the exponent into two
Frobenius-related blocks and proves that the resulting quadratic norm
equation, its Hilbert--90 condition, and its involution-fixed
coefficients are all compatible with failure.  The exact surviving
coordinate is Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-trace}: the selected
residue symbol must have nonzero relative trace.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-sextic} then identifies the phase with
one explicit reciprocal sextic over \(\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}\).  Equivalently,
failure is the selected divisibility
\[
 C^2Y^3+gCY^2+Y+g^2
 \mid D_{(2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}-2^{3^{k-1}}+1)/\ell}(Y).
\]
The Dickson polynomial factors completely as \(Y(Y+1)^2\) times
squares of irreducible cubics.  Thus degree, reciprocity, and factor
shape admit many false positives.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-norm-collapse} proves that even the
exact selected phase has trivial downward norm: corestriction
annihilates its full current \(\ell\)-primary line, while the lower
field has no \(\ell\)-torsion.  The recursive antiunit norm therefore
cannot transport a single bit of the required current order.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cubic-shape} proves more: every admissible
cubic has constant coefficient equal to the square of its trace
coefficient, exactly as \(G/C^2\) does.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-depressed-selected-cubic} then shows that even
the full affine depressed form is recursion-neutral: the selected cubic
is carried exactly back to \(U^3+U+g\).  Thus depressing the cubic does
not produce an independent discriminant or coefficient obstruction.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-dickson-hasse-jets} closes the suggested
higher-derivative escape: the Hasse jets through degree five are
universal rational functions of the root, and the sixth adds only the
common residue \(e\bmod8\).  More decisively,
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-depressed-class-multiplicity} constructs
symbolically \(Q/2-1\) distinct ambient Dickson cubics whose full affine
depressed equation is the same \(U^3+U+g\).  The selected cubic is only
one member; its unresolved content is placement in \(\mu_e\), not its
affine cubic class.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-fingerprint} evaluates the two
absolute traces
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(g/C)=1,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(C^{-2})=k\pmod2,
\]
and Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fingerprint-countermodel} proves
conclusively that their joint value is not an exclusion invariant: at
\(k=4\) a nonselected irreducible factor of the bad Dickson polynomial
has the same pair \((1,0)\).  Hence no argument using only these two
absolute traces can close the exceptional arm.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-constant-line} treats the weaker boundedness
question directly.  Its constant-extension moment proves
\(m_\ell<16(3^k)^4\) throughout this column, but the bound grows with
the level.  At any fixed Conway-subfield dimension, Riemann--Weil is
compatible with full line saturation; obtaining an absolute window
still requires uniform nonextremality of that selected moment.  The
complete-line product is unavailable as a substitute because
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-gap} proves it is unconditionally an
\(\ell\)-th power.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-adjacent-quartic} additionally reduces the D arm,
below Euler projection and in the original corrected-norm field, to the
selected adjacent-power assertion
\[
 D=\zeta^4+\zeta\in(K^\times)^\ell,
 \qquad D+1\notin(K^\times)^\ell.
\]
Equivalently,
\(X^{3\ell}+X^{2\ell}+X^\ell+\Phi_5(\zeta^3)\) is irreducible over
\(\mathbb F_{4^{3^{k-1}}}\).  Its norm is automatically an
\(\ell\)-th power, so this exact full-mark compression still does not prove
the required nonvanishing.
Remark~\ref{rem:plus-minus-tori} also shows that the cubic and
exceptional current groups have coprime orders, so no rational-point
homomorphism can transfer arm \((C)\) to arm \((D)\).
The remaining theorem is precisely nonvanishing of the recursively
selected antiunit index, equivalently exclusion of the recursively
selected cubic.
\end{enumerate}

Appendix~\ref{sec:provenance} records finite computations and external
factorization dependencies.  Those data
are valuable for falsification and audit, but none is used to promote a
uniform conjecture to a theorem.

\subsection{Conclusion}

The finite excess problem is therefore no longer diffuse.  The global
$0/1/4$ prediction is equivalent to four selected-value statements, each
with an exact algebraic detector and an explicit range of primary factors
already controlled by order bounds.  The remaining difficulty is not a
missing generic estimate: it is the arithmetic of the distinguished
Conway-compatible selector in each family.  Proving those four
nonvanishing assertions would establish the observed rule and hence a
uniform bound.  Conversely, a failure in any arm would identify the first
mechanism by which Lenstra excess escapes $\{0,1,4\}$.

\appendix
\section{Computational Evidence and Reproducibility}\label{sec:provenance}

This appendix separates exact finite computation from theorem and from
external data.  A row is called \emph{locally certified} only when the
checkout contains enough exact arithmetic and primality information to
verify it without trusting a probable-prime test or an external database.
A \emph{source-assisted} row has exact local field arithmetic but relies
on an identified external primality or factorization record.  A
\emph{consistent} row verifies only the currently known factors and does
not control an unfactored cofactor.  None of these finite labels asserts
a uniform theorem in the level.

\subsection{Source-pinned tower data}

The implementation vendors the complete OEIS A380496 b-file for the
\(126\) odd primes \(3\leq p\leq709\) and diffs it in full against the
source-pinned copy.  These integer rows determine the implemented
Kummer carries below the stated boundary; selected resulting ordinals
receive independent value tests.  In particular \(m_{359}=1\) is a
source-pinned table value and now also has the independent exact
Hilbert-root certificate of
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-359-hilbert-certificate}.
The first two rows beyond that b-file are independently certified.  The value
\(m_{719}=1\) is backed by the crossed certificate of
Propositions~\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-root-certificate} and
\ref{prop:ordinary-719-crossed-evaluation}; the self-contained
degree-\(2420\) binary-field certificate of
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-727-certificate} gives \(m_{727}=1\).
The implementation includes both rows separately and therefore crosses
\(\alpha_{727}\).  The next unsupported carry is \(\alpha_{733}\), where
it refuses to continue.  This is an intentional computational cutoff rather
than an invitation to extend the table prime by prime; the remaining ordinary
arm is the global assertion in the main-results table.

The contributor-linked extended A380496 auxiliary table~\cite{OEIS,Peeters}
also records
\(m_{1093}=m_{3511}=0\).  These are the two known base-two Wieferich
rows: \(v_{1093}(2^{364}-1)=v_{3511}(2^{1755}-1)=2\).
The external calculator applies Lenstra's exact exponent
\((2^E-1)/p\), so each zero asserts the full \(p^2\)-primary order,
not merely divisibility by \(p\).  The two rows lie outside the approved
126-term b-file, however, and the external repository stores no compact
nonidentity remainder certificate for them.  They are therefore
source-assisted external computations, not locally certified rows or
analytical instances of Proposition~\ref{prop:support}.

\subsection{Independent small-row oracle}

\path{experiments/ordinal_excess_probe.py} is a local term-algebra
oracle independent of the Rust production tower.  It implements the
impartial term algebra used by the calculators, multiplicative-order
tests for small component fields, and fixed-base root tests for larger
selected rows.  Its audited sample is:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrlc}
\toprule
\(p\) & \(m\) & \(\Qset\) & \(p\)-th root? \\
\midrule
\(7\)   & \(0\) & \(\{3\}\)  & yes \\
\(7\)   & \(1\) & \(\{3\}\)  & no \\
\(19\)  & \(1\) & \(\{9\}\)  & yes \\
\(19\)  & \(4\) & \(\{9\}\)  & no \\
\(73\)  & \(1\) & \(\{9\}\)  & no \\
\(89\)  & \(1\) & \(\{11\}\) & no \\
\(47\)  & \(1\) & \(\{23\}\) & no \\
\(179\) & \(1\) & \(\{89\}\) & no \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
These are exact finite checks, not evidence for an all-level
maximal-order theorem.

\subsection{Cubic finite evidence}\label{sec:cubic-evidence}

Proposition~\ref{prop:prime-step} proves \(C_1,C_2,C_3\) without an
order census.  The maintained script
\path{experiments/cyclotomic_3k_family.py} then performs exact
factor reconstruction and power tests.

For \(k=4,5\), every required factor is locally proved prime and the
script certifies that \(\gamma_k\) contains the full primary part of
\(2^{3^k}-1\); hence \(C_4\) and \(C_5\) are locally certified.  At
\(k=6\), the same exact field computations cover all listed factors,
but the \(42\)- and \(90\)-digit factors are only probable primes under
the local Miller--Rabin path.  FactorDB records them as proven primes,
but no certificates are vendored here.  Thus \(C_6\) is
\emph{source-assisted}, not locally certified.

At \(k=7,8\), every currently known prime factor of
\(\Phi_{3^k}(2)\) passes the required order test, while unfactored
composite cofactors remain.  These levels are only consistent with
\(C_k\).  The distinctions are summarized as follows:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ccl}
\toprule
levels & status & basis \\
\midrule
\(1\leq k\leq3\) & theorem & prime-step argument \\
\(k=4,5\) & locally certified & exact factorization, primality, and order \\
\(k=6\) & source-assisted & exact order; external primality for P42 and P90 \\
\(k=7,8\) & consistent & known factors only; unfactored cofactors \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection{Exceptional finite evidence}\label{sec:exception-evidence}

The maintained script
\path{experiments/exception_column_m4.py} checks the corrected norm,
the projected antiunit, and the current-factor power tests.  The fully
factored levels \(k=2,\ldots,5\) are locally certified and give
\(m_p=4\) for every prime with
\[
 f(p)\in\{18,54,162,486\}.
\]
At \(k=6\), the field computations are exact but the \(78\)-digit
factor is only a local probable prime; FactorDB records it as proven.
The complete \(k=6\) conclusion is therefore source-assisted unless a
primality certificate is supplied.  At \(k=7,8\), all currently known
prime factors pass, but the unfactored cofactors and their
multiplicities remain unknown; these checks are merely consistent with
\(D'_k\).

The small anchor rows \(m_{19}=m_{163}=m_{1459}=4\) are reproduced
rather than assumed.  An independent Artin--Schreier compositum model
also verifies \(\sigma(4)=5\), Proposition~\ref{prop:m4norm}, and the
direct power test on the smallest factor of each checked level.

\subsection{Reproducibility}

The four maintained entry points are
\path{experiments/ordinal_excess_probe.py},
\path{experiments/cyclotomic_3k_family.py}, and
\path{experiments/exception_column_m4.py}, together with the focused
certificate \path{experiments/cubic_two_normal_countermodel.py}.  The
additional programs
under \path{experiments/excess/} are exploratory and are not evidence
for the theorem statements in this paper.  Those statements depend only
on the displayed proofs and cited results; the maintained programs serve
finite certification, falsification, and audit.
